# THE 78 CONCEPTS — rev 76 workflow, IN FULL

**CARRIER (rule 16). This is the durable home of every concept the 26 directors produced.**
Generated from the workflow's own return value. The complete structured record, including the
grounding reports, expert plans and every audit verdict, is `WORKFLOW_rev76_output.json`.

Scores are the screener's 0–10. Audited finalists carry their verdicts in the JSON.

## TWENTY-FOUR SECTIONS — The bus sliced transversely every 150 mm and every slice drawn as a filled cross-section on one sheet — a CT scan of a taco stand, in which the three sections through the serving bays are dense with machinery and the sections through the engine bay are empty.  ·  screen 9.0  ·  `t1-2`

**THE IMAGE.** THE RISKY ONE. A cautious team rejects it because at thumbnail size it does not look like a bus.

The vehicle is cut transversely at a regular station interval and every slice is drawn as a filled closed shape, arranged in a grid and read left-to-right, nose to tail, like a contact sheet. Across the 4.065 m body (STATE.md: `dims L=4.065`), 150 mm gives 27 sections, 200 mm gives 21. Every section is printed at the same scale, on the same ground line, so the object's anatomy evolves as your eye travels the grid.

Each section is FILLED COLOUR, not line: the outer body a solid warm red band, the cream upper a solid band, glass a thin dark line, and everything the plane passes through drawn solid in the galley's own seven colours.

WHAT IT ACTUALLY REVEALS — this is the entire idea and it is three distinct acts:

ACT ONE, the cab. Sections forward of about x = +1.04 pass through a fully built interior: `seat_back`, `seat_base`, `seat_welt` and their passenger pairs, `wheel_rim` / `wheel_hub` / `wheel_horn` / two `wheel_spoke`, `dash`, `dash_grille`, `dash_glovebox`, three pedals, `gear_lever`, `mirror_int`. Dense, small, mechanical.

ACT TWO, the galley — and the body wall OPENS. The three sections at the measured bay centres (`BAY_CX = (0.6720, 0.0470, -0.5980)`) cut straight through the serving apertures. Each of those three shows: a break in the flank 515.5 mm wide; the counter slab in section, 107 mm thick (`CNT_ZT, CNT_ZB = 1.2540, 1.1470`) projecting 321 mm in plan (`CNT_Y_IN, CNT_Y_OUT = 0.8450, 1.1660`); the plancha or the worktop; a rack; the strip light near the roof line at z 1.788–1.802; and the back wall at y = −0.480. Three sections in the run are full of the machinery of feeding people. That is the cutaway, and it arrives as a rhythm rather than as a single reveal.

ACT THREE, the tail, and it is the punchline. The sections aft of about x = −1.30 pass through the engine bay of a 1963 Type 2 — and there is NOTHING IN THEM. An empty ring of sheet metal where on any other split-screen bus on earth there would be a dense air-cooled flat-four. That is Tacombi's own founding sentence rendered as a drawing rather than written as copy: "The engine was scrapped, transmission sold" (SPEC.md §0.1, graded S).

COMPOSITION: a 4 × 6 or 3 × 7 grid on warm paper. Each cell holds one section, labelled only with its station in millimetres from the front axle. Consistent ground line across every cell. No leader lines, no callouts, no perspective anywhere. Gold hairlines for the station labels and the cell rules only. One line of type at the foot in the brand's deadpan register — something at the exact temperature of "1963 Type 2 · transverse sections at 150 mm · station −1900: empty."

MOTION VERSION, and it is nearly free once the sections exist: the slices play in sequence nose to tail as a 3-second loop — the shape breathing open at the three bays and hollowing out at the tail. That is a title card, a social loop and a menu-screen ident from one asset. And it obeys the only motion law this vehicle has: it never drives. The SECTION PLANE moves; the bus does not.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** Awe first, then a small joke, and the gap between them is where the artefact lives.

The awe is the sheer measured-ness. You cannot fake this image. Twenty-seven consistent cross-sections of a specific vehicle is visibly the output of someone who knows the object to the millimetre, and it lands as evidence of care before it lands as information. That is the emotional payload of every good technical illustration and it is unusually strong here.

Then, six or seven seconds later, you notice the tail sections are empty — and if you know what a Type 2's tail contains, or you read the one line of type, the whole poster reorganises itself around an absence. It is the brand's origin story delivered as a punchline you find yourself, which is the strongest form.

And it is warm despite being a scan, because the palette is warm and the sections are hand-weighted: the reds and creams and ambers of a kitchen, arranged with the patience of a boat-lofting drawing.

**FORMATS.** B1 707 × 1000 mm, edition print, hand-numbered in pencil, authenticated with a blind emboss chop rather than a signature (there is no artist's hand on a render and a forged signature on CGI is embarrassing). Colophon at the foot in 6 pt naming revision, resolution, sample count and date — unfakeable provenance and completely anti-advert  ·  ALL-OVER APPAREL REPEAT. The sections tile beautifully: pattern at three metres, anatomy at thirty centimetres. This is the format that solves the walking-advert problem structurally — it reads as texture first and as a bus second, so only someone who knows sees the bus  ·  Wrapping paper, cup sleeves, and a menu's endpapers, from the same repeat  ·  3-SECOND LOOP, 1:1 and 9:16, for social and for the in-store menu screen — sections playing nose to tail  ·  SINGLE-SECTION MICRO-GOODS: station +0.047, the middle bay, the one with the griddle in it, as a 40 mm enamel pin, a woven label, or a 25 mm sticker. This is the entry point that teaches the poster  ·  Postcard set: three cards, one per bay section, at 148 × 105

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** Nothing in the VW-bus merchandise market is remotely like this, and the two nearest tropes are both refused on purpose.

It is NOT A BLUEPRINT. Dreamstime sells "Vw Van Blueprint Stock Illustrations" by the sheet, and the blueprint move is the obvious way to dramatise a 3D model — white lines on cyan, orthographic elevation, dimension arrows. This is filled colour shapes on warm paper with no elevation and no dimension arrows anywhere. And critically, a stock blueprint annotates a generic VW; every section here is of the CONVERSION.

It is NOT AN EXPLODED DIAGRAM. Nothing floats, nothing hovers, nothing is pulled apart on an axis. The vehicle is intact; the plane moves.

Its actual relatives are medical imaging and boat lofting, neither of which has ever been pointed at a food truck. And it can only exist because someone measured this exact object for seventy-five revisions — it converts the program's most exotic and least-used asset (a clean, manifold, 229-object mesh with `non-manifold edges (body) | 0`) directly into content, which nothing else in the program does.

And the punchline is proprietary. "The engine bay is empty" is a fact about ONE bus in the world. No competitor can print it, because every other bus in the category still drives — which is precisely the thing the category is selling.

**PRODUCTION.** WHY A CAUTIOUS TEAM REJECTS IT, said plainly so it is not discovered late: at thumbnail it reads as abstract pattern, and someone will say customers will not understand it. Ship it anyway. The pattern-at-distance / anatomy-up-close split is the feature, not the bug, and the single-section pin is the cheap entry point that teaches the poster. Do not sand this into a friendlier cutaway; the friendlier cutaway is Concept 1 and it already exists.

FILLED SHAPES, NOT LINE. The temptation will be to outline every section for legibility. Resist it — filled colour is what separates this from the blueprint category, and the sections have enough internal contrast (red band, cream band, steel, amber, green) to read without a keyline.

STATION LABELS FROM THE DATUM, GENERATED. Label from the front axle, and generate every figure from the constants at layout time.

THE ENGINE-BAY CAPTION MUST BE HONEST AND IT IS A TWO-PART FACT. The MODEL has no engine object — STATE.md's inventory contains none. The REAL VEHICLE has no engine, by the brand's own S-graded account. Those are two different facts that happen to agree, and the caption must not claim the model measured an absence it simply never modelled. Say it the true way: "no engine is modelled, because there is none in the vehicle." That sentence is better copy than the dishonest version anyway.

SPOT INKS AGAIN — five, per AUDIT_rev43's "seven inks printed; five if cut." The sections are large flat areas of a small number of colours, which is the ideal screen-print case and terrible CMYK material (large flat process fields band and mottle).

RENDER EACH SECTION AS ITS OWN TILE at high resolution. This entirely sidesteps the large-format stitch problem: no single frame needs to be 4677 px, because the sheet is assembled from 27 small ones. That is a real production advantage over both other concepts and it is worth saying out loud.

MOTION: 27 frames at ~5.5 min each is roughly 2.5 hours of render for the whole loop, and it never needs re-rendering because the sequence is the same asset as the poster. Do not fan out Blender — it is CPU-bound on four cores and two instances make both slower.

SEED THE GRAIN PER FRAME IF POST IS EVER APPLIED. post.py's grain is `seed=7`, fixed — identical grain on every frame reads as dirt on the lens and instantly betrays a still-image pipeline. For this concept the right answer is simply no grain at all.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** A SECTION CUTTER — and there is nothing like it in the tree. build.py step 3 issues windscreen, side, rear and gap cutters and nothing else; there is no clip plane, no slice, no section path anywhere. Needs a `T1_SECTION_X` boolean intersect against a thin slab, run N times. ⚠ AND THIS IS EXACTLY THE OPERATION THIS TREE HAS BEEN BURNED BY: STATE.md's own warning reads "The cab-door gap booleans passed at SUB=1 and collapsed the shell at SUB=2 for six revisions. Run both." Watch the slice fail before believing it.  ·  CAPPING, AND IT IS THE REAL ENGINEERING. A boolean slice leaves open boundary edges. The body shell is a thin skin — 2.8 mm per the source's own description of the galley as "a sealed 2.8 mm steel box" — so an UNCAPPED section renders as two hairlines rather than a solid band, and the entire visual idea collapses. Every cut surface must be capped and given the section's own fill material. Prototype this on ONE station before committing to twenty-seven.  ·  THE OFF-SIDE PROBLEM IS AT ITS ABSOLUTE WORST HERE, AND IT IS THE LARGEST PIECE OF NEW GEOMETRY ANY OF THESE THREE CONCEPTS NEEDS. Every transverse section crosses the backdrop plane at `GAL_Y_BACK = -0.4800` and continues to the off-side flank — and beyond that 24 mm slab there is NOTHING. The source is explicit that `gal_end_f` and `gal_end_a` exist "to stop a camera seeing past the backdrop into unlit body cavity." Sections aft of the galley will be honest; the three money sections THROUGH the galley will show a 24 mm slab floating in a void unless a real off-side liner and floor are built. Build them. They are worth having regardless, and both other concepts benefit.  ·  VERIFY THE SECTIONS AGAINST THE MESH, NOT AGAINST THE PICTURE. Each cut's cross-sectional area and its component count should be printed and checked, so a section that silently loses a capped face is caught by a number rather than by someone squinting at cell 14 of 27.  ·  NO ALPHA STRICTLY REQUIRED (each section renders on its own tile), NO EMBLEM WORK, NO NOSE WORK, and no dependency on the stitch chain at delivery size. The cab and galley objects benefit from Concept 2's form work but do not require it — at section scale a box reads as a box legitimately, because in section it IS a rectangle.  ·  ONE HONEST CHECK BEFORE ANY OF IT: render three trial sections — one cab, one bay centre at x = +0.047, one tail at x ≈ −1.90 — crop them and LOOK. If the tail section is not legibly, satisfyingly empty, the punchline does not land and the concept should be reconsidered rather than rescued.

**SCREENER.** The empty tail sections are the best idea here: a punchline the viewer finds unaided, proprietary to this one vehicle (STATE.md's inventory contains no engine object, matching the S-graded 'engine was scrapped'), unfakeable by any competitor with a stock shell, and it converts the project's least-used asset — a clean manifold 229-object mesh — into the content itself. Sections-as-contact-sheet is a boat-lofting/CT idiom nobody has pointed at a food truck. Real risk is engineering, not taste: the capping problem is the whole picture (an uncapped 2.8 mm skin renders as two hairlines), the off-side void behind GAL_Y_BACK = -0.4800 is worst exactly at the three money sections, and this tree has a documented history of booleans collapsing the shell at SUB=2 — but it proposes the right test (three trial sections, crop, look) before committing, and 27 small tiles sidestep the unproven large-format stitch entirely.

---

## EL COMBI — La Lotería — A 24-card lotería deck in which every card is an engraving of one part of this one vehicle — the game of archetypes played with a taco truck's components.  ·  screen 9.0  ·  `t8-0`

**THE IMAGE.** Pure black line on warm cream uncoated stock. No tone, no colour, no shading — a 19th-century trade-catalogue engraving crossed with a Mexican broadside. Card face 60 × 100 mm: a single object, centred, floating in cream, inside a 2 mm black rule with a 1 mm inner hairline; a number top-left in a slab face; the Spanish name in caps along the foot in hand-drawn condensed grotesque, English small beneath.

THE LINE SYSTEM, which is the whole idea: silhouette 0.5 mm, creases 0.3 mm, hatching 0.12–0.18 mm. No flat black mass wider than ~3 mm anywhere. Value comes ONLY from hatch density — four densities at roughly 8 / 4 / 2 / 1 lines per mm. And the hatch direction FOLLOWS THE SURFACE: it runs longitudinally along the flank, wraps circumferentially round the tyre, radiates from the pole of the hubcap dome, and cross-hatches only in true occlusion — under the counter, inside the wheel arches, behind the apertures. That is the difference between an engraving and a Photoshop filter, and it requires a normal pass, which is to say it requires the model.

THE DECK, 24 cards: 1 El Combi (the whole vehicle, lid propped, counter out — the full asymmetric 4.580 × 3.132 m propped silhouette, 1.462:1). 2 La Ventana (one serving aperture, 515.5 × 403.0 mm with its 55 mm corner radius, bobble fringe running the whole inner edge). 3 El Mostrador (the counter's radiused tail and brass nosing). 4 La Rueda (the hubcap bullseye: 274.0 mm cap, 86.9 mm badge). 5 El Farol (one festoon bulb on its flex). 6 La Plancha. 7 El Tablero (the mural lid, propped). 8 La Flor (one mural rosette, ten lobes, peace-glyph centre — carry it, don't sanitise it). 9 La Calidad (the sunburst, type at −19.7°). 10 El Sol. 11 El Espejo. 12 La Bocina. 13 El Sesenta y Tres (the empty chrome plate frame). 14 La Rejilla (the louvre block, ten slots at 21.111 mm pitch). 15 La Cinta (the bulb string alone, a single continuous line running edge to edge off both sides of the card). 16 El Toldo (the tail board on its stay, 0.7110 m chord at 38.0°). 17 La Carta (a menu card). 18 La Cortina (the bobble fringe alone, a scalloped run, nothing else). 19 El Faro. 20 El Pico (the nose blade and its two irons — CROPPED BELOW THE ROUNDEL). 21 El Vaso. 22 La Llave. 23 La Sombra (the cast shadow alone: a card that is only the shape the bus makes on the ground). 24 La Combi Cerrada — the same vehicle SHUT, which is a picture this project has never rendered in 76 revisions.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** The absurd dignity of giving a taco truck's parts the treatment normally reserved for the moon, the mermaid and death. A child gets a game and asks what La Cortina is. An adult gets the joke — this is a deck of archetypes with no archetypes in it — and then, a beat later, gets the affection underneath it: somebody decided every one of these things. Somebody chose the fringe. The engraving register does the second half of the work; engraving is how we draw objects we have decided are important, so drawing a bulb holder that way is a small act of love performed with a straight face. And it is unambiguously a thing families keep, without being a sticker.

**FORMATS.** Deck of 24 cards, 60 × 100 mm, 3 mm die-cut corner radius, 300 gsm uncoated CREAM board (not white — a warm 5–8% ground is what makes a line engraving read as a broadside rather than as clip art). Single black ink, offset or letterpress. Tuck box carrying the folk-art repeat in the same one ink.  ·  Six tablas (playing boards), 210 × 297 mm, 4 × 4 grid of 16 cards each, same stock — the deck only becomes a GAME when the boards exist, and the boards are where a family sits down.  ·  Individual cards blown up as A3 prints, 297 × 420 mm. The line is vector so it survives, but each size needs its own hatch pass — see production notes. La Sombra and La Cinta are the two that become genuinely strange at this scale.  ·  Kiss-cut sticker sheet, all 24 at 25 mm, A5. At 25 mm the hatching is gone and each card reduces to silhouette plus creases — draw that reduction, do not scale to it.  ·  Card backs and the tuck box as a one-ink folk-art repeat, built from the three components that carry 84.4% of the gold.  ·  Restaurant application: the 24 cards printed as a single 700 × 1000 mm uncut sheet, framed. It is a specimen sheet, and it is the cheapest wall piece in this whole programme.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** The VW-bus merch ocean is photographic, painterly, or minimal single-weight line art. Nobody engraves a bus, for a concrete reason: you cannot hatch a photograph convincingly, because convincing hatching has to follow the form, and following the form requires having the form. This is the one treatment that is only available to someone who spent 75 revisions building the geometry — it is the strongest possible argument for the model existing at all.

And 'a lotería deck made entirely out of one object's parts' exists nowhere, in any category. It is a deliberate category error: lotería is universal archetypes, and this deck refuses to have any. That is the joke and it is also the brand's actual position — this is not a bus that means freedom, it is a specific parked object with a fringe on it.

Trademark surface is near zero: El Pico crops below the bumper line, no card carries the roundel, and the hubcap card can be drawn as the ring geometry alone (STATE.md notes the badge's width fraction 'has never been compared to a frame', so drawing it from the model at card scale would be publishing an unverified proportion).

**PRODUCTION.** DELIVER VECTOR, NOT RASTER. At 0.12 mm a rasterised hatch will stair-step on the plate. Grease Pencil Line Art is the only route in this tree to editable strokes and therefore to SVG.

SET HATCH DENSITY IN FINAL MILLIMETRES PER SIZE AND NEVER SCALE BETWEEN SIZES. A 0.12 mm line scaled from A3 to a 60 mm card becomes 0.024 mm and disappears; scaled the other way it becomes a fat rule. Each of the four output sizes needs its own hatch pass. This is the single most likely way this concept fails silently.

Line floors: 0.12 mm for offset, 0.18 mm for letterpress (the bite closes finer lines). Cream stock, warm not neutral. Black only — a second ink would undo the entire premise.

This is a DIFFERENT ARTEFACT from F18 and must be offered as one. F18's style is locked to 'cartoon with rendered depth' and its scene to 'nothing but the bus, die-cut tight, plus the sun and the papel picado', and its trigger — 'build it after the model is done' — has not fired. This deck is neither that style nor that scene, so it is not gated by that trigger; say so in the proposal rather than letting the hold read as blocking it. Do not raise the papel picado question: he ruled it deferred until the sticker is actually being built.

THIS IS THE RISKY ONE AND I WILL SAY SO. A cautious team rejects it three ways: it is a game rather than a poster, so it does not look like merch; engraving looks nothing like any food brand's identity; and it is 24 finished illustrations before a single unit ships. I am proposing it anyway, because it is the only idea here that turns the project's real asset — 229 individually named, individually measured objects — into content, and because it is the only one a family would still have in a drawer in ten years.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** A LINE-EXTRACTION PASS. There is none: `grep -rln "freestyle|grease|gpencil|use_pass_|pass_index|cryptomatte" --include=*.py .` returns one unrelated probe. Build the Grease Pencil Line Art modifier route in preference to Freestyle — it emits EDITABLE VECTOR STROKES and is the only path in this tree to SVG. Crease angle 30–40°, NOT lower: `T1_body` is shade-smooth and a low angle will find the subdivision instead of the form.  ·  A NORMAL PASS and an AO/occlusion pass, to drive hatch DIRECTION and hatch DENSITY respectively. Copy `matte_tap`'s colour contract verbatim — `color_management='OVERRIDE'`, `view_transform='Raw'`, look 'None', gamma 1.0 — or every pass comes back AgX-curved and the density mapping is wrong everywhere. Note `probe_rev55_truenorm.py` already establishes that `Normal` collapses on the smooth-shaded shell and `True Normal` is the fix; use True Normal.  ·  PER-OBJECT ISOLATION RENDERS for 23 of the 24 cards. This is a visibility loop, not modelling — the objects are already separately named in STATE.md's inventory (`fringe | 3`, `menucard | 3`, `plate` + `plate_digits`, `louvres | 2`, `bulb_string`, `mir_head | 2`, `counter_nosing`, `cap`/`capring`/`capvw`, `tail_board` + `tail_board_stay`, `plancha`).  ·  THE SHUT VEHICLE, for card 24, and this is real source work rather than a render. `t1_shell.py` couples the lid's width to its angle: `LID_W = (LID_X0 - LID_X1) / LID_ASPECT / math.sin(math.radians(LID_OPEN_DEG))`. I computed the sweep from the live constants (LID_X0 0.9640, LID_X1 −1.0700, LID_ASPECT 1.7130): 76° → 1.2237 m (shipped), 45° → 1.6792, 30° → 2.3748, 10° → 6.8379, and 0° is a ZeroDivisionError. A naive angle sweep INFLATES the mural board to longer than the bus. Freeze LID_W at its shipped value behind an ablation, guard it, and WATCH THE GUARD FAIL on the coupled build before believing it green. The near-closed case will also trip `assert _top.co.y > LID_Y_HINGE + 0.010`, which is correct for a posed still and wrong for a swept one.  ·  NOT REQUIRED, and this is the concept's biggest practical advantage: no T1_ALPHA, no materials, no lighting rig, no colour management downstream, no large-format stitch, no 16-bit path, no emblem fix, no gloss work, no red decision. It is the only one of the three that ships without unblocking anything else in the pipeline.

**SCREENER.** The standout. 'A loteria deck with no archetypes in it' is a category error nobody has committed in any market, and three cards — La Cortina (a fringe alone), La Sombra (only the shadow), La Combi Cerrada (a picture this project has never rendered in 76 revisions) — are each better ideas than most whole concepts here. Culturally exactly right for taco+combi without costume-Mexican. The engraving argument is load-bearing and true: hatching that follows the normal needs normals, so this is the one treatment only available to someone who built the geometry. Honest about 24 illustrations before one unit ships.

---

## No Legible — A wall of everything on this vehicle that could not be read — printed at exactly the size and blur it was photographed at, with the measurement that failed set beside it — and one line, in the middle, perfectly clear.  ·  screen 9.0  ·  `t11-2`

**THE IMAGE.** B0, portrait, edge to edge, on the measured menu-strip yellow (172,144,17) — hue 48.0, a deep ochre, not a lemon. The field is a dense justified column of near-type: fifteen or twenty specimens of the vehicle's own lettering, each reproduced at the ACTUAL pixel scale and blur at which the record read it, then enlarged with the blur intact, so what you get is a page of beautiful, hopeless, half-resolved letterforms in dark red ink (72,46,6). Reading down: the mural lid's top strip, with the palm-frond occlusion left as a 695-pixel void between JUICES, and &, and beneath it in 7 pt caps 'THE THREE LEGIBLE WORDS AVERAGE 53.6 PX PER CHARACTER. THE GAP ACCOMMODATES 13.0 +/- 0.6 CHARACTERS. GOURMET TACOS IS 13 CHARACTERS. THAT IS A CONSISTENCY, NOT A READING.' Below that, the side strip's terminal block — three small lines that resolve at 16x to '?ANO M?' / '??O?A?' / 'Torta?', printed as exactly that, question marks and all, captioned 'REPORTED AS UNREAD; DRAWN AS ILLEGIBLE SMALL TYPE.' Below that, La Santa's seven merged downstrokes with the four perpendicular cuts drawn across them as hairlines and the counts 6 / 5 / 5 / 4 set in the margin, captioned 'LA SANTA REMAINS CONSISTENT AND REMAINS UNREAD. DO NOT REPORT IT AS VERIFIED.' Below that, the three pillar menu cards reproduced as what the model actually builds — five flat colour bands and a fine stripe field, no letters at all — captioned 'THE INDIVIDUAL WORDS ARE 1.5 PX TALL AND ARE NOT MEASURABLE.' Below that, '1963' as its seven-segment bars at 5 px, captioned 'FLAGGED.' And then, dead centre of the sheet, occupying a clean rectangle of untouched yellow with 40 mm of air on every side, one line set large, crisp, and completely legible, in silver with its keyline: GRACIAS POR SU PREFERENCIA. No image of the bus. No mark. No logo. Light: none — flat spot inks on paper. The only tonal event on the whole sheet is the sudden clarity in the middle.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** Recognition, then a small ache, then warmth. Anyone who has eaten at a taqueria has stood in front of a hand-painted board they could only half read, and ordered anyway, and been right to. The poster reproduces that exact experience and then rewards it: the one thing you CAN read is the thing the counter fascia actually says to you when you have paid. It reframes illegibility as intimacy rather than as failure — the signs are worn because the place is used. And for anyone inside the company it is quietly devastating, because it is a monument built entirely out of the project's own honest admissions of what it could not measure.

**FORMATS.** B0 wall poster, 1000 x 1414 mm, two spot inks (strip yellow, dark red ink) plus a silver for the one legible line, on uncoated. It must be BIG: the whole piece depends on the viewer walking up close, failing to read, and then finding the one line that works. At A3 it is just a texture.  ·  B1, 707 x 1000 mm, as the shop edition, numbered in pencil lower-left with a blind-emboss chop rather than a signature — there is no artist's hand to sign a piece assembled from measurements.  ·  Restaurant installation: the same wall, printed direct-to-substrate or wheat-pasted, in the queue line where people are already standing and reading.  ·  A 210 x 297 mm placemat version, with the legible line at the bottom where the plate does not cover it. This is where it earns its keep commercially — it is the cheapest object in the whole programme and the most likely to be photographed.  ·  Postcards, one specimen per card, in a set of twelve. Each card carries its own caption on the reverse verbatim from the record.  ·  NOT apparel. A shirt made of blurred type reads as a mistake at conversational distance and the joke does not survive being worn.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** It is not merchandise about a vehicle. It has no bus, no wheels, no silhouette, no VW mark, no sunset and no flowers, so it does not enter the category at all — it enters the category of the Mexican street sign, which nobody has mined. And its content is genuinely unavailable to anyone else: every specimen on the sheet exists only because lid_gen.py wrote down what it could not read instead of guessing, at 8-22x magnification, with the failure recorded as a result. A stock illustrator would have invented the missing thirteen characters. The whole poster is a monument to not having done that.

**PRODUCTION.** THIS IS THE RISKY ONE AND HERE IS EXACTLY WHY A CAUTIOUS TEAM KILLS IT. It contradicts the owner's own stated direction head-on: he ruled at rev 61 'senor Tacombi should be clearer in the render than in that photo. Well defined. I want this 3d model to look like new. Enhanced from the photo.' This poster does the opposite on purpose — it prints his signage as a smear. It also sits at an angle to SPEC section 10.10's 'ABSOLUTE REPLICATION OF ALL ARTWORK' hard bar, though not in breach of it: section 10.10's object is the MODEL's decals, and this is a derived artefact, but somebody will read it as the bar being abandoned and that argument has to be won in advance rather than after printing. It should be put to him as multiple choice with a printed A3 crop attached and the trade stated plainly — 'this piece is deliberately unclear, and here is why that reads as warmth and not as an error.' If he says no, it dies, and that is the correct outcome; do not soften it into legibility, because a half-legible version of this poster is just a badly printed menu. Production: the blur must be REAL and must be reproduced at its own scale — enlarge the actual measured crops, do not simulate a blur filter over clean type, or the whole honesty of the object collapses. Do not mix livery states on one sheet: rule 11 governs, and the Nolita chalkboard specimens (BIENVENIDOS, VENGA A NOLITA, TACOS DE HOY) belong to a different paint state from the Playa menu strips. If both are wanted, print two posters, not one. One factual check before anything goes to press: SPEC says the Nolita fascia reads '251 ELIZABETH STREET' while the rev-50 measurement record reads '267 ELIZABETH STREET' on two frames — those disagree, and a street number printed a metre wide is the worst possible place to inherit a stale figure.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** NOTHING ON THE 3D MODEL. This concept is the one that needs no render, no alpha, no export and no geometry — it is built entirely from the reference photographs and from lid_gen.py's and t1_detail.py's own recorded readings. That is a feature: it ships under the F191 and F193 holds without touching anything they gate.  ·  BUT IT EXPOSES ONE REAL MODEL GAP WORTH FIXING SEPARATELY: the three pillar menu cards are BUILT as geometry (CARD_W 0.0948 x CARD_H 0.3383 m at z 1.5667, on pillars measured at X +0.884 / +0.256 / -0.388) and carry NO LETTERING AT ALL — gal_menucard is five flat colour bands plus a fract stripe field, and its own docstring explains why: 'the individual WORDS are 1.5 px tall and are NOT MEASURABLE -- what is built is the band structure and the two measured colour statistics, not invented text.' That is the correct call and it should not be overturned by guessing. What would close it is a photograph, and PHOTOS_WANTED has never carried a menu-card item. Add one: one pillar menu card, square on and close.  ·  RESOLVE THE '251' vs '267' ELIZABETH STREET DISCREPANCY between SPEC and the rev-50 measurement record before any deliverable prints an address.  ·  IF the legible centre line is ever set from the vehicle's own hand rather than redrawn, it needs the same vector export named in concept 2 — there is currently no vector of any lettering in this tree.

**SCREENER.** THE BEST IN THE SET. A wall of everything unreadable, printed at the blur it was photographed at, with one legible line — GRACIAS POR SU PREFERENCIA — in the middle. Strange, and it works: it converts the project's own honest failures ('?ANO M?', 'Torta?', La Santa's 6/5/5/4 cuts) into the subject, which no stock illustrator could produce because they would have invented the missing thirteen characters. Emotionally exact — half-reading a board and ordering anyway. It contradicts the owner's 'clearer than the photo' ruling head-on and says so instead of hiding it. Correctly refuses apparel and demands B0 or nothing.

---

## TRES HUECOS — The Aperture Wall — Take the bus out entirely and repeat only the hole it has instead of windows: three hatches at life size, at the exact height they sit on the real vehicle, so standing at the wall is standing at the counter.  ·  screen 9.0  ·  `t18-1`

**THE IMAGE.** NO VEHICLE. The repeat unit is the serving side's architecture, drawn flat, at 1:1, from the constants rather than from any render.

THE UNIT, measured off t1_shell.py and STATE.md: three apertures, each 515.5 × 403.0 mm (`BAY_W = 0.5155  # equal, measured` and `Z_SILL, Z_HEAD, BAY_R = 1.3720, 1.7750, 0.0550`), corner radius 55.0 mm, on centres `BAY_CX = (0.6720, 0.0470, -0.5980)`. That gives pitches of 625.0 and 645.0 mm and therefore pillars of 109.5 and 129.5 mm — THE TWO PILLARS ARE NOT EQUAL, and the 20 mm difference is the single most important instruction in this concept. Every designer's hand will want to regularise it. Do not. It is measured, it is the hand of the man who cut the holes, and it is what stops the wall reading as a template. Total repeat span 1785.5 mm.

THE COURSES, every height machine-written and rendered at 1:1 ABOVE THE ACTUAL FLOOR: the aperture band sits 1.307–1.710 m (STATE.md: `band 1.372-1.775 un-dropped (1.307-1.710 AG)`). Below it, the belt line at 1.2070 m (`belt line @ x=0.962 (live) | 1.2070`) is the red/cream break — cream above, red below. Lower still, a fine horizontal stripe of ten slots at 21.111 mm pitch across 0.1900 m, running 0.895–1.085 m AG (t1_detail.py `LOUV_N = 10`, `LOUV_Z_TOP, LOUV_Z_BOT = 1.0850, 0.8950`, `LOUV_PITCH = 0.021111`) — the louvre band, which is the only regular repeat the real vehicle owns. The whole wall is therefore raked: the roof line falls at 17.75 mm/m (`rake 17.75 mm/m (locked 17.75)`), so every course is drawn on a 1.02° descent, front to back. It is imperceptible and it is why the wall breathes.

INK, three flats plus a scallop: field CREAM sRGB(206,208,200); the band below the belt in RED sRGB(196,49,36); the apertures themselves NEAR-BLACK, flat, no rendering, no interior — holes, not windows. Round the inside edge of every aperture runs a scalloped row of cream balls: the white bobble fringe, which t1_detail.py records as "CONFIRMED present in ref_side.jpg — a scalloped row of white balls runs round the inside edge of all three apertures". Optional fourth ink: brass `COUNTERTAN = (0.5870, 0.4930, 0.3060)     # = sRGB (201,186,150)` as a 20 mm rule at the sill, standing for the counter's nosing.

At small scales the same unit becomes a pure geometric repeat — three soft-cornered rectangles, two unequal gaps, a fine stripe below — and reads as mid-century modernist grid work until someone tells you what it is.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** BEING SERVED. This is the only pattern in the programme with a body position built into it: printed at 1:1 with the apertures at 1.307–1.710 m above the actual floor, an adult stands at the wall exactly as they stand at the hatch — the sill at the wrist, the head rail above the eye. You cannot walk past it neutrally; the wall puts you in a queue. The emotion is the anticipation of the moment before food arrives, which is the single strongest feeling this brand actually sells, and it is delivered by dimension rather than by picture.

Underneath that sits the origin story, told without a word. The $3,000 bus was "missing three of its windows" (SPEC §0.1, graded S — the founder's own account); the signature feature of this company is an ABSENCE. A pattern made only of that absence says it better than any image of the vehicle could. And the 20 mm pillar asymmetry is the tenderness in it: somebody cut these by eye and got them nearly, humanly, not-quite even.

**FORMATS.** 1:1 digital wall mural, non-woven wallcovering, 1.3 m drops, wall height 2.6–3.0 m. Horizontal repeat 1785.5 mm, so the repeat crosses drops — panel-print it per wall rather than sell it as a roll. THE PRIMARY USE: restaurant interiors, the Nolita/Playa dining rooms, event walls.  ·  1:4 — repeat 446.4 mm, apertures 128.9 × 100.8 mm, pillars 27.4 / 32.4 mm. Packaging wrap, shipper boxes, menu covers, tissue.  ·  1:12 — repeat 148.8 mm, apertures 43.0 × 33.6 mm, pillars 9.1 / 10.8 mm. Textile: shirting, jacket lining, bandana, scarf border, apron.  ·  Encaustic / cement floor tile, 200 mm: one bay per tile, three tiles plus grout = one repeat, the unequal pillars expressed as two different grout widths. The most Mexican object in the whole programme, and it needs no render at all.  ·  Perforated metal or laser-cut screen at 1:1 — the pattern as an actual architectural element, back-lit, in a doorway or a service pass.  ·  Tile-and-stripe combination for tabletop laminate and paper placemats, 1:6.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** There is no bus in it. That single decision puts it outside the entire VW-bus merchandise category — you cannot compare it to a sunset silhouette, a line drawing, a blueprint or a doodle tile, because it does not depict a vehicle at all. It also passes the logo-removal test completely: strip every word off it and it is still unmistakably one specific object's architecture.

The stock alternative — "repeat the windows of a VW bus" — would give you a row of EQUAL rectangles, because a factory bus has equal glass. Ours has three equal holes at UNEQUAL centres, which only exists because somebody cut them into a bus that was already missing its glass. The 625/645 mm pitch against the 515.5 mm bay is a fingerprint no stock asset can carry and no one can copy without the measurement.

And it carries zero trademark exposure on either claim: no VW roundel appears anywhere, and the trade-dress content — three cut apertures, a bobble fringe, a counter nosing, a louvre band — is entirely the conversion, none of which Volkswagen made.

**PRODUCTION.** THIS IS THE CHEAPEST AND LEAST GATED CONCEPT IN THE PROGRAMME. It needs no render, no Blender, no alpha, no stitch chain and no delivery-render permission — every figure above is read off t1_shell.py, t1_detail.py and STATE.md and can be drawn in Illustrator this week. That is not a compromise; the pattern is BETTER drawn than rendered, because a photoreal aperture would carry the render's salmon red (flank_compare: render (196,81,67) against the photograph's (158,16,6)) and the paint's measured gloss deficit (gloss_compare on out/r75_hero34f.png: render spread 0.495 against the photograph's 1.202 — 0.412 of it, FAIL against a 0.60 bar). A flat drawing has neither problem, and the real bus is hand-painted flat colour anyway.

THE ONE THING THAT IS NOT MEASURED, STATED PLAINLY: the bobble fringe's ball diameter and pitch. t1_detail.py says so itself — "Ball diameter and pitch are INFERRED (each ball is ~2 px)." So the scallop is a DRAWN DECISION, not a measurement. Draw it, declare it as a design choice, and do not let it acquire a false provenance line. Everything else on the sheet is machine-written.

IF YOU WANT DEPTH IN THE APERTURES — don't, for v1. Flat black is stronger and it is honest. If a later version wants the galley visible through the holes, note that F45 (roof-aperture interior) and F15 (unlit roofed body, 6.92e5 px², item 4 of 16 in visibility_budget) are BOTH ungated: nothing in the tree has ever measured whether the inside of this vehicle renders lit. Render one side frame and look through the bays before committing a wall to it.

THE FRINGE IS UNDER-BUILT BY ONE APERTURE. The model puts the bobble fringe round the three serving bays only (`for i, (xr, xf) in enumerate(S.BAYS)`, STATE.md `fringe | 3`), but the photographs put it round the WINDSCREEN too. Irrelevant to this pattern, which uses only the serving side — but it is a found element with no register row, and it should be recorded rather than lost.

PRINT: 3–4 spot flats, no process build. The near-black aperture wants a solid, not a rich black build, or the 55 mm corner radii will show registration fringing at 1:1. On the tile version, fire the aperture as a single glaze — the whole appeal is one uninterrupted dark field against cream.

SCALE DISCIPLINE: line weights must be specified in FINAL MILLIMETRES per product, never scaled from a master. The louvre stripe at 1:1 is 21.111 mm pitch and reads as ten strokes; at 1:12 it is 1.76 mm and reads as ten strokes; at 1:50 it is 0.42 mm and becomes a grey band. Decide at which product it stops being slots and becomes a rule, and draw that separately.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** NONE FOR v1. This is the concept's chief virtue and it should be stated as such in any proposal: every dimension is already machine-written and no capability in the tree needs to be exercised, fixed or unblocked to ship it.  ·  IF a later version wants real depth in the apertures: F45 (roof-aperture interior) and F15 (A7, unlit roofed body, 6.92e5 px²) are both UNGATED — never measured against a photograph. Render one `side` frame, crop it at the bays, and look through them before designing to what is behind the holes.  ·  THE BOBBLE FRINGE'S BALL DIAMETER AND PITCH ARE INFERRED, NOT MEASURED (t1_detail.py: "each ball is ~2 px"). If the scallop is going to carry a 1:1 wall, one close square-on frame of an aperture edge would close it. PHOTOS_WANTED has an existing carrier structure for exactly this kind of ask, and it has never had a fringe item.  ·  OPTIONAL AND CHEAP: nothing here reads the mesh, so add a small script that IMPORTS `t1_shell.BAY_W`, `BAY_CX`, `Z_SILL`, `Z_HEAD`, `BAY_R` and `t1_detail.LOUV_PITCH`, `LOUV_N`, `LOUV_Z_TOP`, `LOUV_Z_BOT` and prints the pattern's final millimetres per named scale. Re-derive; do not transcribe. A transcribed table goes stale silently and this repository has a rule about that.

**SCREENER.** The only one where the emotion is delivered by dimension instead of picture — printed 1:1 with the sill at 1.307 m the wall physically puts you in the queue; the 20 mm pillar asymmetry (109.5 vs 129.5, which the source's own BAY_CX confirms) is an uncopyable human fingerprint, it contains no vehicle so the whole category can't be compared to it, the founding fact is an ABSENCE and the pattern is made only of that absence, and it needs zero model work; the cement-tile format is the most culturally correct object anywhere in the set.

---

## PAPEL DE GRASA — The sheet that lights up while you eat — Deli paper printed on both faces in perfect back-to-front register: the bus dark and shut on top, the bus lit and open underneath — and the oil from your taco turns the paper translucent so the lights come on in the shape of where you ate.  ·  screen 9.0  ·  `free0-1`

**THE IMAGE.** A 305 × 305 mm square of natural-kraft greaseproof. 

TOP FACE, ONE INK, dark maroon (the folk art's own dark ink, ~#501010), coarse flexo line work, no halftones, no fills over ~15% coverage. A true orthographic side elevation of the bus at 1:14 — 290 mm long, so it runs almost the full sheet, floating with 8 mm of air top and bottom. The bus is SHUT and DARK: roof lid down, counter drawn as a single closed horizontal, the three serving apertures as empty outlined rectangles, the bulb string drawn as an unlit bead chain — small open circles on a hairline. Only the three largest folk-art masses are drawn, in outline, nothing shaded. Tiny type along the bottom edge, 5 pt, one line, no logo: *Playa del Carmen · 1963 · 4,065 mm*. It looks like a closed shop. On dry paper that is the entire design and it is deliberately a bit austere, a bit dull.

REVERSE FACE, ONE INK, warm amber-gold, printed in mirror so it registers back-to-front within ±1.5 mm. It carries ONLY the light: every bulb as a filled disc with a soft halo; the three apertures filled solid amber; the mural lid's eleven flowers as solid amber shapes standing UP where the top face shows the lid down; a spill of amber under the counter line. Nothing structural — no outline, no body, no wheels. On a dry sheet this face is invisible from the top.

THEN THE FOOD ARRIVES. Grease wicks outward from wherever the taco sits. Where the paper saturates it goes translucent and the amber blooms through the maroon line work — bulbs igniting one at a time along the roof, the hatches opening into light, the flowers rising out of the closed lid. The reveal spreads for the length of the meal and stops wherever you stopped eating. Every sheet is a different photograph of a different lunch. You cannot make two the same and you cannot make it happen on purpose.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** Delight first — an actual gasp from a seven-year-old, and a slower one from the adult opposite who works out what is happening. Then a small, good ache: to see it you had to ruin it. It is a magic trick that costs less than a cent and cannot be replicated at home, and it makes the customer a collaborator — the image is finished by the meal. It also lands the brand's real proposition without a word of copy: the bus opens and lights up when there is food. That is what the object DOES, expressed as a physical property of the paper rather than as a claim.

**FORMATS.** 305 × 305 mm DELI SHEET on 40–45 gsm natural greaseproof/glassine, food-contact, low-migration inks both faces. Grammage matters: heavier than ~50 gsm and the oil does not carry the reverse image through fast enough; lighter than ~35 gsm and it tears under a wet taco. This is the primary unit and it is given away, never sold.  ·  400 × 400 mm TRAY LINER, same artwork scaled, for sit-down locations — bigger sheet, more real estate for the bloom to travel across, and it survives a whole plate rather than one taco.  ·  #10 GREASE-RESISTANT BAG, the same two-face trick on the front panel only, so the reveal happens in the customer's hand on the walk home. Lower reveal reliability (less contact area) — treat as a bonus, not the promise.  ·  VELLUM EDITION, 305 × 305 mm on 110 gsm translucent vellum, screen-printed both faces, sold flat in a glassine envelope with a small brass clip so it can be hung in a window. Backlit by a window it shows the lit state permanently. This is the version for people who want the image without destroying it — and it quietly sells the trick to anyone who has not eaten with it.  ·  NAPKIN, 1-colour top face only, no reverse — do NOT run the trick on napkins. Napkins get used on faces. The trick belongs on the surface the food sits on.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** There is no VW-bus merchandise anywhere that is designed to be destroyed. Every sticker, tee, poster and print in the category is engineered for permanence; this one only completes when it is soiled, and its final composition is authored by an act of eating. It is also the only piece in this whole program that is native to the actual business — a taqueria's highest-volume printed object is greaseproof, and it is the one substrate a poster studio never touches. Trade-dress and trademark posture is the best available: one-ink line art at 1:14 on a side elevation, so the VW roundel is not in frame at all, the nose is edge-on, and the artefact is given away rather than sold. It contradicts nothing in F18 and IS NOT OFFERED AS F18 — F18 is a die-cut vinyl keepsake, locked to 'cartoon with rendered depth', for children to keep. This is the opposite artefact: paper, ephemeral, and it does not survive the meal. Ship them as a pair and they argue with each other productively.

**PRODUCTION.** THE PHYSICS IS THE RISK AND IT MUST BE TESTED BEFORE ANY DESIGN IS APPROVED. Greaseproof and glassine both go translucent under oil — that part is ordinary — but three things are unverified and only a physical test settles them: (1) whether amber ink on the reverse reads through a PARTIALLY saturated sheet or only a fully soaked one; (2) whether the top-face dark ink migrates or smears once wetted; (3) back-to-front registration tolerance on a web press, which is the whole trick — at 1:14 a bulb is roughly 1.5 mm and a 3 mm misregister puts every light off the roofline. Buy the paper, hand-screen ten test sheets, and put real food on them in week one. If registration cannot hold ±1.5 mm, the fallback that still works: abandon fine registration entirely and print the reverse as LOOSE AMBER FIELDS (a band along the roofline, three rectangles behind the apertures) that only need to land within ±6 mm. The trick survives; the precision does not. Reduction is measured, not guessed: folk_gen.py's own component census gives `COMP_TOP = {1: 47.8, 2: 73.7, 3: 84.4, 5: 87.8, 10: 93.5}` and a histogram in which 48 of the 66 gold components carry 2.29% of the ink — so drawing only the largest three masses reproduces 84.4% of the read at a fraction of the line count, which is exactly what a coarse one-ink flexo job needs. Inks must be food-contact compliant and low-migration; this is a regulated substrate, not a poster. Do NOT put the wordmark on the top face — the sheet is more interesting anonymous, and the reveal is the signature.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** A LINE PASS. Nothing in this tree produces vector or line art — the only .svg ever committed is `vw_canonical_2019.svg`, which the register grades a DIFFERENT OBJECT. Try the Grease Pencil Line Art modifier on the orthographic `side` view first: it produces EDITABLE VECTOR STROKES that export to SVG, which is exactly what flexo plates and screen positives want. The mesh is clean enough to trust it — STATE.md reads `non-manifold edges (body) | 0` and 229,489 quads against 1,040 tris. Fallback: Freestyle at a 30–40° crease angle, or a Sobel on the normal/Z passes.  ·  THE BULB STRING MUST BE SEPARABLE FROM THE VEHICLE. It is currently collapsed into one object — t1_detail.py does `ob = join([ob, flex], "bulb_string")` — and the reverse-face artwork is nothing BUT the bulbs. Keep the per-bulb objects addressable behind an ablation so nothing regresses, or trace the path by hand off the render.  ·  THE SHUT STATE HAS NEVER BEEN BUILT OR LOOKED AT IN 76 REVISIONS, and the top face is the shut bus. Worse, it is BLOCKED BY A REAL DEFECT: t1_shell.py computes `LID_W = (LID_X0 - LID_X1) / LID_ASPECT / math.sin(math.radians(LID_OPEN_DEG))`, so the lid's WIDTH is coupled to its angle as 1/sin — a naive angle sweep inflates the mural board past the length of the bus and divides by zero at 0°. Freeze LID_W at its shipped value behind `T1_LID_WCOUPLE`, add a guard that the panel's width does not move when the angle does, and WATCH IT FAIL on the coupled build first.  ·  THE MURAL LID ASSET IS CROPPED. F74(a) is open and correct: `tex/lidmural.png` is cut off at the bottom, flowers sliced mid-bloom, yellow side columns truncated, where ref_side.jpg shows a board framed on all four sides. The reverse face draws the flowers rising out of the lid, so this must be repaired in lid_gen.py and REGENERATED BY HAND — lid_gen.py is not called by build.py, so a stale texture ships silently.  ·  NOT MODEL WORK BUT ADJACENT AND FREE: F144 records that the render's bulbs "READ AS PEARLS, NOT LIGHTS". On this artefact the paper does the glow, so the 2D piece is correct before the 3D is — which is a reason to ship it early rather than a reason to wait.

**SCREENER.** The standout: deli paper printed both faces in register so the customer's own grease turns the bus from shut-and-dark to lit-and-open — an artefact designed to be destroyed, in a category that sells only permanence, on the one substrate a poster studio never touches. Native to a taqueria, not applied to it. It names the physics risk itself and carries a real fallback (loose amber fields at ±6 mm), and its reduction is grounded in folk_gen's own verified COMP_TOP census rather than in taste.

---

## Twelve Shadows — The bus is never shown. Only its shadow — the deployed silhouette, lid up, counter out, tail board propped — cast by the real sun at Playa del Carmen, once an hour, across one serving day. A vehicle that tells the time by not moving.  ·  screen 9.0  ·  `free4-2`

**THE IMAGE.** Single flat ink on bare paper. No object, no ground texture, no horizon — just a shape and a lot of nothing. THE SET, twelve panels, 11:00 to 22:00, one hour apart, from real computed sun positions at 20.6N 87.1W: at 11:00 the shadow is a compressed, dense, near-square mass with the counter's cantilever reading as one hard bar across it. At 14:00 it is short, heavy, pooled tight under the object, almost illegible — and that panel earns its place by being the one that gives nothing. By 17:00 it lengthens and the lid's shadow SEPARATES into a distinct wing riding above the body mass. By 19:00 it is a long raked lozenge running off the sheet edge, with the propped tail board a thin spear 515 mm past the tail (STATE.md: 'length excludes opened lids: 4.317 with them, 4.065 without'). The 21:00 panel has no sun shadow at all — and it is NOT blank: it is the festoon's own light, a scatter of small warm ovals pooled on the ground beneath the drip rail. The set ends with the bus lighting itself. THE ROSETTE, an alternative single sheet: all twelve overlaid from a common origin at low opacity in brand red. What comes out is a radiating burst — which IS the vehicle's own dominant ornament (the Calidad sunburst, the mural rosettes, the hubcap rings) arrived at by pure fact instead of by drawing a flower. The bus, by not moving, draws its own folk art. PALETTE: one ink, red on cream or black on natural; a second warm gold ink for 21:00 only. TYPE: one line, bottom left, hairline — the hour, the date, 20.6N 87.1W. Enormous white space. Nothing else.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** Patience, and then permanence. This is the only idea in the program that makes the brand's central fact — it stopped — into something you can WATCH HAPPEN. A shadow is physical proof of stillness; twelve of them are proof of a day's work. It is also quietly ruthless toward a merchandise category built entirely on motion: our vehicle's only movement is the sun's. And by panel three the viewer recognises the shape without ever having been shown the object, which is the most flattering thing a picture can do to a person.

**FORMATS.** TWELVE-PANEL SET, each 300 x 400 mm, one-colour screen print on uncoated 250 gsm, boxed — or issued one per month, which turns a print into a subscription and a subscription into a reason to come back.  ·  SINGLE ROSETTE PRINT, 700 x 700 mm, two-colour on heavy uncoated. Solid flat ink at this size is the ONE case where the chain's undeclared 8-bit truncation cannot hurt you (studio.py sets `color_depth = '16'`; stitch.py and post.py both read through PIL and truncate silently) — a hard-edged mask has no gradient to band. Trap 0.2 mm; consider a double hit against pinholing on uncoated.  ·  A PAINTED SUNDIAL ON THE TERRACE at a Playa site — hour marks laid where the actual bus's shadow actually falls. The graphic becomes a piece of ground the customer stands on, and the real object validates it every day at no cost. This is the version I would fight for.  ·  12-SECOND SCREEN LOOP, one frame per hour, static camera, no cut, no camera move: the shadow sweeps and stretches while nothing else in frame ever changes. The most anti-advert motion piece imaginable — it is a clock.  ·  ALL-OVER of the rosette for tissue, cup wrap, wrapping paper, endpapers, tote gusset. One tile, free across the whole range.  ·  FAVICON / APP MARK: the 14:00 shadow alone, solid, at 32 px. It survives because it is a single closed silhouette with no interior detail.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** There is no shadow-only VW-bus artwork anywhere, because a shadow is only interesting if the object is stationary — and the entire category's emotional stock is motion and escape. Every trope in it (sunset, surf, road, blueprint, line art) requires the vehicle drawn. This refuses to draw it and is still unmistakable, because the DEPLOYED silhouette — lid wing, counter bar, tail spear — is unlike any vehicle shadow anyone has ever seen. It is also the perfect anti-photoreal move for this specific asset: not one of the model's four failing photograph-comparison gates can touch a shadow. No salmon red, no matte paint, no emblem, no tread count, no gloss deficit. The piece is immune to every open defect in the register by construction.

**PRODUCTION.** The sun positions must be REAL or the whole piece is a lie: compute altitude and azimuth for 20.6N 87.1W on a named date, hourly, and PRINT THE EPHEMERIS IN THE COLOPHON so anyone can recompute it. That is the piece's own guard. Set `T1_SHADOW` (ships 9.0, with `T1_SHADOW_FLOOR` 0.030) by looking at the crop, not by inheriting it — studio.py's own comment says the shipped value is a presentation device for a bus sitting on white and that `T1_SHADOW=1.0` restores the floating arm exactly, while C3 fires at 20. Record that choice in the ledger as a GRADE FOR A DERIVED ARTEFACT, not a change to the delivery rig; rule 34 exists because a ruling about the studio RIG was once wrongly applied to a second deliverable for twelve revisions, and F259 ('Relight the studio and fix the ground if necessary') opens the rig anyway with 'DO NOT change RED' as its only constraint.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** THE ASSET IS ALREADY EMITTED BUT CURRENTLY UNREACHABLE. deliver.py writes `combi_%s_shadow_only.png` per view and the split is measured — shadow-catcher pixels near-black (max channel < 12/255) against a vehicle mean of 143, partitioning the footprint 37.8 / 62.2 %. It is blocked because every frame in out/ is colortype 2 with no alpha, `T1_ALPHA` appears in NO LEDGER since rev 62, and deliver.py correctly refuses: 'CONTROLS: 4 checked, 1 FAILED -- C1 ... PACKAGE IS NOT TRUSTWORTHY'. One env var and one render unblocks it.  ·  THERE IS NO SUN. studio.rig() takes a scalar `key` and builds a fixed cyclorama; no azimuth or elevation parameter exists anywhere in the tree. Build a small dedicated rig: one SUN lamp at a computed altitude/azimuth, one shadow-catcher plane (studio.py already carries `ob.is_shadow_catcher = bool(int(os.environ.get("T1_CATCH", "1")))`), alpha on, optics off.  ·  DO NOT ROUTE IT THROUGH `T1_SCENE=playa`. F57 records that path renders NO VEHICLE — 'a dark cylinder on a grey plane', reported and never diagnosed. Build the minimal sun rig instead.  ·  THE SHIPPED POSE IS ENOUGH AND THAT IS LUCKY. `t1_shell.py` computes `LID_W = (LID_X0 - LID_X1) / LID_ASPECT / math.sin(math.radians(LID_OPEN_DEG))` — the lid's WIDTH is coupled to its angle as 1/sin, so any variant that opens or closes the lid inflates the mural board past the length of the bus (and divides by zero at 0 deg). At the shipped `LID_OPEN_DEG = 76.0` it is correct, so this concept ships WITHOUT that fix — but freeze LID_W behind an ablation before anyone tries an animated lid.  ·  THE 21:00 PANEL NEEDS REAL WORK AND IT IS THE BEST PANEL. F144 stands: 'THE FESTOON BULBS READ AS PEARLS, NOT LIGHTS.' The bulbs must actually cast onto the ground plane. They are one joined object (t1_detail: `join([ob, flex], "bulb_string")`, STATE.md `bulb_string | 1`, `tb_bulbs | 1`, `tb_bulbflex | 1`) with an emissive material and live switches T1_BULB_STR / T1_BULB_BASEV — so it is a lookdev job, not a modelling one.  ·  Render each of the twelve at the SAME camera and the SAME ortho scale or the set is worthless. Use a top-down or fixed elevated camera on the ground plane; `topdown` exists (loc 2.60, 4.60, 6.40, lens 62) and has never been rendered by any preview list — look at one frame before committing.

**SCREENER.** The rosette — twelve real sun positions overlaid arriving at the brand's own sunburst ornament by pure fact instead of by drawing a flower — is the one moment of strangeness-that-works in this set, and the 21:00 panel where the bus lights itself is a genuinely great ending; immune by construction to every failing gate (no red, no gloss, no emblem), though the best panel is gated on F144's 'THE FESTOON BULBS READ AS PEARLS, NOT LIGHTS' and on a sun that studio.py says does not exist.

---

## THE FILAMENT — Delete the bus. What is left — one continuous warm line of ~115 lamps tracing a shape you already know — is the identity.  ·  screen 8.5  ·  `t2-1`

**THE IMAGE.** There is no vehicle. On black, a single drawn path of light: ~115 discrete beads on a hairline wire, running 3.5 m of drip rail as a long gentle horizontal, turning down and around the rear roof dome, then breaking away up the propped tail board's 38° diagonal on its own string. The wire SAGS in small catenaries between the beads — that sag is the whole difference between a festoon and a dotted rule, and it must be drawn, not scaled. One hard clean horizontal beneath, shorter, no beads: the counter's brass nosing. If the aperture surrounds resolve as beaded (see model work), three tall rounded rectangles of the same beads hang under the main line, on the measured equal pitch — STATE.md: 'bay widths 0.516 0.515 0.516'. Nothing else. SCALE BEHAVIOUR IS THE DESIGN: at 32 px the beads fuse into one continuous glowing stroke; at 300 mm they separate into individual lamps with visible wire between them; at 3 m they are objects. One graphic, three readings, all correct. TWO RENDERINGS, both shipped as masters: (a) LINE — one warm ink (the authored 1.000/0.760/0.442 flattened to a single amber) on pure black, no glow, no gradient, hard-edged, for embroidery, foil, vinyl and small sizes; (b) GLOW — the same path with real bloom, halation blooming into the black, for screen and for the animated bug. MOTION, and this is the logo animation: 1.4 s. Beads catch left-to-right but NOT in order — two or three fire early, one lags. At ~1.1 s a single flicker across the whole string, one frame dark. Then steady, and hold indefinitely. It loops by simply never resolving into stillness.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** Recognition without a name on it. Somebody who has eaten at Tacombi sees the bus instantly; somebody who hasn't sees a beautiful line and wants to know what it is. That asymmetry — rewards the insider, never punishes the stranger — is precisely what makes a mark get worn rather than displayed, and it is the difference between a badge and an advert. Emotionally the line is also the correct promise: it is the shape of the moment the place opens.

**FORMATS.** NEON OR LED SIGN, 1.2–2.4 m, for the restaurants themselves. This is the one that goes back into the physical world: the graphic is a fabrication drawing for a real light, and the bead pitch is not invented — 28.6 ± 1.0 mm, measured twice by FFT in ref_side.jpg. A brand whose logo is a real lamp on its own wall is a brand that is telling the truth.  ·  ANIMATED LOGO BUG 512 × 512 and 1080 × 1080; FAVICON at 32 px. The line survives the favicon; a bus silhouette does not — test this first, it is the strongest single argument for the idea.  ·  EMBROIDERY, 55 mm on a black cap front, single thread, CHAIN OR BEAN STITCH — a running stitch, so the ~1.2 mm satin-column floor does not apply and a hairline wire with bead knots is actually producible. One needle, low stitch count, cheap enough to run on everything.  ·  HOT FOIL, amber on black: matchbook, coaster, hang-tag, business card, 25–90 mm. Foil is the correct material because it is the only ink that changes with the light in the room, which is what the subject does.  ·  KISS-CUT VINYL STRIP, 200 × 12 mm, for a laptop edge or a shelf lip. The one sticker format the line is actually suited to — long, thin, and it does not want to be a bus-shaped die-cut.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** Every competitor mark in this category is a bus outline. This is not the bus and not an outline — it is the light the bus makes, which is a thing only an owner who lit one would ever think to draw. It passes the logo-removal test outright (remove the word 'Tacombi' and it is still unmistakably one specific vehicle) and it is the lowest-exposure mark available in the whole programme: no VW roundel, no factory pressing, no filled bus silhouette (the shape claimed as trade dress), no folk art, no red, no sunset, no wheels. It is an identity built entirely out of a feature Volkswagen never made and a hardware-shop festoon a founder screwed to a drip rail. It also cannot be mistaken for CGI, which matters: a photoreal render is a saturated technique and this is a drawing.

**PRODUCTION.** EXTRACT THE PATH FROM THE MESH, NOT FROM A RENDER. bulb_string() already computes every bead centre in closed form — x stepping from BULB_X0 = T._aft(-1.8000) to BULB_X1 = 1.7000 at BULB_PITCH = 0.0286, z = (T.ZT_ALL(x) - T.RT_ALL(x)) + 0.01512 off the drip-rail roll start, y = side*(T.WX(x)*T.G(z) + 0.0180). Write a ~30-line exporter that projects those centres through the orthographic `side` camera (studio.views()['side'] is `lens=None, ortho=5.90`, a true parallel projection) and dumps SVG. That gives a mathematically exact path with no tracing, no resolution ceiling and no render dependency — and it is the only vector asset this repository would then contain, because today the sole committed .svg is vw_canonical_2019.svg, which F168 records as a DIFFERENT OBJECT from the 1955–67 pressing. EMBRACE THE ASYMMETRY: the string is built show-side only, so the mark is inherently one-sided. Do not mirror it to make it 'balanced' — the vehicle is asymmetric when it is working (lid up one side, counter out the other) and that is the point. FOR THE GLOW MASTER, render at low key with `--bloom 1.0` (post.py defaults it to 0.0) and note that BLOOM_THR = 0.94 only behaves on a dark field. THE COLOUR IS AVAILABLE AND IS NOT THE PROJECT'S CONTESTED ONE: the amber comes from the authored emission (1.000, 0.760, 0.442), whose saturation F144 measured at 0.558 against the photographed bulb's 0.561 — 'the hue is right to 0.003'. That is the single best-grounded colour in the entire vehicle, unlike the red, which exists as four unreconciled numbers (t1_mats sRGB(196,49,36), lid_gen (159,45,22), cal_gen (214,46,30)) and images salmon in the render.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** RESOLVE THE APERTURE-SURROUND CONTRADICTION FIRST — THE SHAPE OF THE MARK DEPENDS ON IT, AND THE RECORD DISAGREES WITH THE BUILD. F144 states 'The bulb string runs the whole length of the drip rail AND forms the serving-aperture surrounds (F112)', and F112 (REFUTED-rev60b) says 'the aperture surround is a BEADED BULB STRING, which is what the render already builds'. But t1_detail.py's own measured note says the opposite and the build follows the note: 'The aperture surround is a matte white bobble fringe, not lamps, and the only lit string is on the drip rail OUTSIDE the skin, ~55 mm above the aperture heads' — and it is built by bobble_fringe() with material 'capwhite', unlit, at FRINGE_PITCH 0.0260 against the festoon's 0.0286. So the record says three rings of light and the machine builds three rings of matte white plastic. That is decidable by looking at ref_side.jpg at magnification and it decides whether the mark has three hanging rectangles or none.  ·  WRITE THE BEAD-CENTRE SVG EXPORTER. Small, no Blender needed at render time if the centres are dumped once, and it is the tree's only route to a vector asset.  ·  VERIFY THE STRING'S EXTENT BEFORE DRAWING IT LONG. The source's own comparison: 'the string runs model x +1.69 -> -1.64 photographed against -1.80 -> +1.70 built, +5 %.' The built string is 5 % longer than the photographed one. For a mark, decide deliberately which extent is authored and say which.  ·  ADD THE TAIL-BOARD RUN AND THE REAR DOME TURN AS PART OF ONE PATH. tb_bulbs and tb_bulbflex exist as separate objects on the board; the drip rail and the board string are currently unrelated geometry. For the mark they must be one continuous drawn stroke, which is an authoring decision in the exporter, not a model change.  ·  OPTIONAL BUT VALUABLE: FIX F144 IN THE MODEL SO THE GLOW MASTER CAN COME FROM A RENDER RATHER THAN BEING DRAWN. F144's two levers are both ablated and refuted (T1_BULB_STR=60 made the ratio WORSE, 1.40 → 0.88, by driving the bead toward clipping; T1_BULB_BASEV=0.30 moved it 1.38 → 1.50, nothing). Its own diagnosis is that the surround must be dimmer — i.e. the nocturne. Until that is measured, the LINE master is drawn and is not blocked by anything.

**SCREENER.** Strongest identity work: a mark that is the light the object makes rather than its outline, which only an owner who lit one would draw — survives a favicon where a bus silhouette dies, and the neon/LED format closes the loop by making the logo a fabrication drawing for a real lamp on the brand's own wall. Cheapest to build of all twelve (bead centres are closed-form in bulb_string; a ~30-line exporter through the true-parallel `side` camera yields the tree's only vector asset) and needs no relight. It also surfaced a genuine live contradiction — F144 says the string forms the aperture surrounds, while t1_detail builds FRINGE_PITCH 0.0260 matte 'capwhite' bobbles — and correctly notes that decides the mark's shape. Held off 9 only because string-light motifs are common in hospitality branding; the measured sag and 28.6 mm pitch are what keep it from reading as bunting.

---

## ROTULISTA — The bare red flank of the Nolita-state bus, painted onto by an invisible hand in the signwriter's real working order — three big gold masses first, then the tendrils, then the rosettes, then forty-eight tiny marks all at once, and the silver wordmark last with its dark shadow arriving one frame before the metal.  ·  screen 8.5  ·  `t5-1`

**THE IMAGE.** FRAMING: locked, no camera move at all. `hero34r`, the 3/4 rear-left — studio.py's own comment on it reads '3/4 rear-left, shows the counter wrap and the louvre block'. It puts the largest folk-art mass nearest camera (folk_gen's MASS-R1, the rear-most quarter, 73213 mm sq) and it keeps the VW roundel entirely out of frame. The flank bleeds off both edges. This is not a portrait of a bus, it is a wall being painted.

LIGHT: warm and LOW, raking across the flank from frame-left so the gold catches and the cream goes ivory. Not the flat white cyclorama. F259 licenses exactly this — the owner ruled 'Relight the studio and fix the ground if necessary'; the studio stays, the RIG is now open, and the single constraint travelling with it is DO NOT change RED.

PALETTE: opens on TWO colours — vermillion below the belt, cream above, a plain unornamented flank. Closes on FIVE: + folk-art gold, + dark maroon, + silver. The film is literally the palette assembling itself.

COMPOSITION: the counter's brass nosing runs as a hard bright horizontal on the lower third and never moves; the louvre block is the only fine texture in frame and it anchors the right. Everything that appears, appears in the band between the belt line and the arch.

MOTION — and THE ORDER IS A MEASUREMENT, not an invention. folk_gen.py's own connected-component census of ref_side.jpg: `COMP_TOP = {1: 47.8, 2: 73.7, 3: 84.4, 5: 87.8, 10: 93.5}`, and `COMP_HIST` whose first three bins are (24 components, 0.42%), (21, 1.32%), (3, 0.55%). Three components carry 84.4% of the gold; forty-eight carry 2.29%.
1. BARE RED FLANK. 1 s hold. It looks unfinished and slightly sad.
2. THE THREE MASSES, largest first, each laid down as ONE wet stroke over ~8 frames — the cab-door mass, then MASS-R1 over the rear quarter, then MASS-R2 over the arch. Two seconds in, 84% of the drawing exists.
3. THE TENDRILS — four dark-maroon S-curve commas with their dot rows, whipping on in 3 frames each.
4. THE ROSETTES — eight, popping on offbeats, centre-out.
5. THE FORTY-EIGHT. Every remaining tiny mark, in ONE FRAME, like dust settling. They add 2.29%.
6. THE SCRIPT LANDS LAST, silver, as one object — and its DARK KEYLINE ARRIVES ONE FRAME BEFORE THE SILVER, because that is the order a signwriter actually works: shadow, then metal.
7. 2 s hold. Loop by wiping back to bare red, or cut to black.

~14 s. The pacing is the argument: nothing to almost-everything in two seconds, then ten seconds spent on the last 16%. That is exactly what hand-painting feels like, and here it is derived from a census rather than felt.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** SOMEONE MADE THIS. The category's central lie is that a VW bus is a mood you can buy; this says a person stood in front of a red panel with a brush and a mahl stick and made decisions. The size-ordered pacing gives the viewer the specific, physical pleasure of watching craft — the big confident gestures land first and then the maker fusses for ten seconds over marks worth 2.29% of the ink, which is a thing every person who has ever made anything recognises in their body. And the last beat is the one that makes a signwriter nod: the shadow before the silver. It is a detail nobody outside the trade will consciously notice and everybody will feel.

**FORMATS.** 15 s vertical 1080x1920 for Reels/Stories. The flank crops to vertical well because the artwork is banded between the belt and the arch.  ·  6 s square 1080x1080 cutdown: three masses plus the script. The whole idea survives at 40% of the runtime.  ·  THE PROGRESSION POSTER — and the best stills in this concept are the UNFINISHED frames. Pull four: bare / three masses / plus rosettes / finished. Print 4-up at A2 420x594, or as a set of four A4s, uncoated, spot-colour. Nobody in the world sells 'a VW bus being painted'; this series is a genuinely new object.  ·  APPAREL, and the scale is measured not guessed: the THREE-MASS state alone at 1:1.5 on a back print, two inks. At that ratio the shipped tile's median gold stroke is 8.8 mm and its p10 is 1.8 mm — everything prints. The FULL flank at a 280 mm chest print (1:14.5) does NOT: p10 collapses to 0.18 mm and 95 of 196 components have their thickest point under 0.52 mm. Print one mass big, never all of it small.  ·  Packaging tissue, cup wrap, bag gusset: 'the forty-eight' alone as a scatter tile. One ink. The marks nobody notices, used as the pattern.  ·  20 s wall projection above the counter, looping, in store. The bus painting itself, above the bus.  ·  Title-card system for any future film: the wordmark's keyline-then-silver beat, isolated, 1 s, as the brand's sting.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** The market's flower-power VW is a decal somebody slapped on a van, and it is the single most saturated move in the category. This makes the OPPOSITE argument in the same visual vocabulary: it shows the florals being DRAWN, in Mexican signwriting order, which reframes them from Woodstock iconography to rotulismo. That reframing is the most valuable single thing this brand can do with its own artwork, and MOTION IS THE ONLY MEDIUM THAT CAN MAKE IT — a still flower on a bus is ambiguous and always readable as hippie; a flower being brushed onto a taqueria is not. It is also anti-CGI in a program built on CGI: it uses a photoreal 3D asset to stage a hand-painting event, so the technique argues against its own slickness. And there is no stock equivalent because the ordering is derived from a component census of this specific vehicle's actual paint.

**PRODUCTION.** CHEAP PROTOTYPE AVAILABLE TODAY: `T1_W_ART` is a live global opacity on the folk-art alpha (t1_mats.py multiplies the art mask by W_ART, shipped 1.00). Sweeping 0 to 1 gives a whole-flank fade-on in ~10 renders. That is NOT the concept — it fades everything together and loses the entire idea — but it proves the camera, the relight and the timing in one afternoon before anyone touches folk_gen.py.

STATE THE CEILING ON THE ORDERING. folk_gen's census is of ref_side.jpg, the PHOTOGRAPH. The shipped tile is measurably more fragmented — 196 components with the top three carrying 66.4%, against the photograph's 66 with the top three carrying 84.4%. If you animate the shipped tile you are animating a different drawing from the one the census describes. Say which one you used, in the ledger and in the client note.

folk_gen.py AND script_gen.py ARE NOT CALLED BY build.py. Regenerate the textures by hand or the render silently uses the old file. This has cost this project revisions before.

DO NOT 'IMPROVE' THE WORDMARK TOWARD THE PHOTOGRAPH. flank_compare's `Senor` row now scores a DELIBERATE DEPARTURE (F156) because the owner ruled the word to bright silver (F157: 'Bright silver, same as Tacombi'), and that ruling covers THAT WORD ONLY — the b flag, the i dot and the swash keep their measured tarnish. The gate reads the departure as improvement and will not warn you that you are undoing him.

LIVERY STATES ARE NOT MIXABLE. The bare opening state is Nolita/state-B; the finished state is Playa/state-A. Rule 11 and SPEC §7.1 permit rendering both because geometry transfers and paint does not — but any single frame that BLENDS them is a fake, and this has already killed three findings (F99, F100, F140). The transition here is legitimate precisely because it is temporal: you are watching state B become state A, not compositing them.

The relight must not go through `T1_SCENE=playa`. F57 records that path renders NO VEHICLE — 'a dark cylinder on a grey plane', reported and not diagnosed.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** THE SCRIPT HAS NO DARK KEYLINE AND BEAT 6 DEPENDS ENTIRELY ON IT. Measured on tex/senor.png (4096x1738 RGBA): among pixels with alpha > 200 the 1st-percentile luma is 168.3 and the fraction below luma 80 is 0.0000 — there is ZERO dark ink in the asset — while SPEC §3 requires 'SILVER with a dark keyline and drop shadow'. grep for keyline/shadow finds nothing in script_gen.py or t1_mats.silver_script. This is why the wordmark reads flat in the render and crisp in the photograph, and it is the cheapest visible improvement in the whole inventory. Fix in script_gen.py (lid_gen.py already authors the convention: `DARKLINE = (72, 26, 16)`), regenerate BY HAND, and add a guard that reads the asset's own sub-luma-80 fraction and goes red if it returns to zero.  ·  THE LOCKUP IS MIS-PLACED BY ~76-83 mm AND TWO INDEPENDENT ROUTES AGREE. flank_compare live on out/r75_side.png: 'the render's lockup has to move +76.2 mm forward and -47.6 mm down', cross-checked by a route sharing no step with it at +83/+80 mm aft with the ink top +61.1 mm high. Placement survives every downscale — on a 610 mm poster that is 11.4 mm of lateral error. Fix it in the SAME edit as the keyline.  ·  folk_gen.py MUST EMIT A PER-COMPONENT PAINT-ORDER RANK into an unused channel of swirl.png / swirl_b.png, so the animation is a threshold sweep rather than a hand-keyed matte. It already computes the components and their areas; nothing new has to be measured.  ·  `hero34r` CLIPS. I measured out/r75_hero34r.png: the subject's bbox runs to xmax = 1599 of 1600 — the counter, the object's strongest horizontal and the thing that makes it read as a taqueria rather than a van, runs off the right edge. Pull the camera or widen the aspect before designing to this view. It also has ONE rendered-file mention across LEDGER_rev66..75, against `side`'s 27 — the most Tacombi-forward angle is the one least looked at.  ·  A BARE-FLANK BUILD STATE. build.py binds the folk art, the script and the Calidad decal unconditionally. Beat 1 needs a `T1_LIVERY` (or at minimum the art masks driven to zero independently of each other), and the ablation must be watched producing a genuinely plain red flank, not a faint ghost.  ·  A WARM RAKING RELIGHT, under F259. The constraint is DO NOT change RED — that is the option he explicitly declined. Move the rig, not the paint, and re-read flank_compare / gloss_compare / probe_rev70_tyre afterwards so a relight cannot regress them silently.  ·  Same infrastructure debt as every motion piece: no keyframes, no export, one full rebuild per pose. This one is cheaper than most because NOTHING GEOMETRIC MOVES — it is a texture sweep on a fixed pose, so the build can be reused if T1_SAVE is used to persist one .blend.
