**SCREENER.** The paint ORDER is a measurement, not a mood — folk_gen's own census (COMP_TOP 84.4% in three components) makes the 2-seconds-then-ten-seconds pacing derived rather than felt, and 'dark keyline one frame before the silver' is trade knowledge nobody will name and everyone will feel; it also reframes the florals from Woodstock to rotulismo, which only motion can do, and it is honest that the shipped tile (196 comps) is a different drawing from the photograph it censused.

---

## The Same Camera, Twenty Years Later — Render the 2006 photograph from the 2006 photograph's own recovered camera, in its own measured light, with its own plants at their measured depths — and hang the two side by side.  ·  screen 8.5  ·  `t6-1`

**THE IMAGE.** A PAIR, or a dissolve between them, and the entire effect is that NOTHING SLIDES. LEFT / 2006: ref_rear34.jpg exactly as it is, 1200 × 824, warm and soft — the woman resting her cheek on her hand at a chrome-legged formica table, orange vinyl chairs, a green-checked cloth, the napkin dispenser, the palm trunk dead centre, the hand-lettered yellow sign FAVOR DE ORDENAR Y PAGAR AQUÍ, the mural lid up and blazing orange in the top right, the tail with its '1963' plate. RIGHT / 2026: the identical frame rebuilt. Same camera position, same lens, same light, same vegetation — and the bus rendered at full fidelity, sharp to the rivet. THE PEOPLE, THE CHAIRS AND THE TABLES ARE NOT THERE, and that absence is the content, not a cheat: what this project holds, measured, is the vehicle and the planting. LIGHT SPEC, EVERY NUMBER MEASURED AND QUOTABLE, all from studio.playa()'s own docstring and playa_env.py's header: NO sun, NO sky, NO haze, NO dapple gobo — all four are measured ABSENCES, not omissions. One large low LATERAL source (the palapa's single opening) under an absorbing ceiling. The signatures that identify it: the same red reads 3.95 : 1 between the flank facing the opening and the tail face 72° away; the palm trunk runs 12.5 : 1 across its own diameter; an up-facing cream surface is DARKER than a vertical one facing the opening (0.93 : 1) and brighter than one turned away (1.87 : 1). Shadows are WARMER, not bluer — the bright/dark ground split measures db* +2.4, which is the opposite sign to a sun/skylight pair. Foliage sits 52.1 L* below the cream (foliage L* 32.7, cream L* 84.8), and the band across the top of frame is a continuous polychrome FLOWERING mass — 55.1 % foliage, 13.4 % crimson heads, 5.5 % cream florets, jagged interpenetrating lower edge. Palette is therefore: one soft lateral wash, no specular highlight anywhere in frame, cream at the top of the range, a 52-unit drop into green, and warm shadow. Set beneath the pair in a hairline: the lens, the camera coordinates, and the two years. MOTION ARM: 4 s cross-dissolve, 3 s hold each end, looping. Because the camera is IDENTICAL the palm trunk lands on the palm trunk and the roof line lands on the roof line — that perfect registration is the whole trick, and it exists only because someone solved the pose.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** Vertigo, then reverence. You know this photograph — it is the founding image — and now you are standing inside it, and it is sharp, and everyone has gone home. It is the feeling of a measured survey drawing of a building you have eaten in: not nostalgia (nostalgia is soft and this is the sharpest thing in the room), but the quiet astonishment that somebody went and measured the afternoon. And it makes an argument no copy line could: the lunch ended, the people left, the bus is still there.

**FORMATS.** GALLERY PAIR, TWO PRINTS AT ~200 × 137 mm, hung 60 mm apart in matched frames. THE SIZE IS SET BY THE PHOTOGRAPH, NOT BY THE RENDER, and this is the honest constraint: ref_rear34.jpg is 1200 × 824 native, so 200 mm wide is ~152 dpi and 297 mm would be ~103 dpi. Print it INTIMATE. Match the render's apparent sharpness to the photograph's rather than letting the render out-resolve its partner — the pair only works if they are the same object seen twice, not a snapshot next to a CGI flex.  ·  THE FOUNDING-STORY WALL, one pair per location, by the till or the queue — the 'how we know' panel. This is where a brand puts provenance instead of an advert, and it satisfies the insider test: it rewards the person who has stood in that yard and says nothing embarrassing to anyone else.  ·  4-SECOND DISSOLVE LOOP at 1200 × 824 native — do not upscale. In-store screens, social, and the About page. The dissolve is the only motion this asset needs and it moves nothing in the frame.  ·  PRINTED SPREAD: photograph verso, render recto, gutter down the middle, in any book, menu insert or anniversary piece. The gutter does the dissolve's job in print.  ·  NOT A T-SHIRT AND NOT A STICKER. This artefact dies at small scale — its whole content is fine registration between two images. Do not let it be reduced.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** It is structurally impossible for anyone else to make. It requires all four of: the original photograph, a photorealistic measured reconstruction of that exact vehicle, a SOLVED camera for that exact frame, and a measured reconstruction of that exact environment. The camera is not estimated — studio.py records it as recovered from the rear rim flange conic fit (139.1 px = 0.440 m → 316 px/m), the horizon row and the wheel-ellipse axis ratio, and then CONFIRMED by a feature the solve never saw: it predicts the bus's far top corner at image column 554.6 and the cream lid panel is read at 555. Every VW-bus print in the market is a drawing of a generic bus. This is a photograph and its own forensic double, and the seventy-five revisions of measurement everyone treats as overhead become the product.

**PRODUCTION.** TWO THINGS GATE THIS AND BOTH ARE OWNER-SHAPED, NOT TECHNICAL. (1) F57 is OPEN and undiagnosed: 'T1_SCENE=playa RENDERS NO VEHICLE. Asked for a hero through build.py's own playa path, the frame comes back as a dark cylinder on a grey plane — the ground disc and nothing else … Found by LOOKING; every exit code was clean.' It has never been diagnosed because nobody has had a reason to. This concept is the reason. (2) F92 rules the Playa hero 'DEPRIORITISED, NOT CANCELLED' and HANDOFF_CARRIERS §4 is explicit that it licenses NOTHING TO DO NOW — 'Do not re-ask it; do not act on it either.' So this concept must be PUT TO HIM, as multiple choice with the pair mocked up, not started. It is also the strongest possible occasion to put it: F92 is the carrier of 'the emotional bar that sits ABOVE clinical accuracy', which is exactly what this asset is for. TECHNICAL NOTE THAT WILL BITE: studio._cull_foreground hides pl_* geometry standing between camera and vehicle, and its own docstring explains why — plants placed for the reference camera are correctly behind the bus from that bearing and wrongly in front of it from a hero bearing. For playa_ref specifically the culling must be OFF, because that camera is the one every mass was placed for. Verify it, watch it, and say which you ran. DO NOT publish a latitude/longitude in the caption: no coordinate for that yard exists anywhere in this repository and inventing one would be exactly the class of unverified figure this project has spent seventy revisions removing. Caption with what is measured — lens, camera vector, the two years — and let the owner supply the place line.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** DIAGNOSE F57. T1_SCENE=playa currently renders no vehicle at all. Reported at rev 57b, never diagnosed, and every other item here is downstream of it.  ·  RUN playa_env AND LOOK AT IT. It is 74 KB of measured, self-contained procedural planting with eleven masses placed by inverting the reference camera, and grounding shows ZERO rendered-file mentions for playa, playa_ref or playa_w across LEDGER_rev66..rev75. It has never been looked at in ten revisions. Render playa_ref once, crop it, and compare it against ref_rear34.jpg — that comparison IS the deliverable and it has never been made.  ·  CONFIRM _cull_foreground IS DISABLED FOR playa_ref, and add a row that says so in its own label. Culling on this one camera would remove the exact plants the frame exists to show.  ·  F74(a), OPEN AND DIRECTLY IN FRAME: 'tex/lidmural.png is cropped at the bottom — the bottom row of flowers is sliced mid-bloom and the yellow side columns are truncated, where ref_side.jpg shows a board framed on all four sides. The render is faithful to the asset, so the defect is in lid_gen.py's output.' The mural lid is the largest, loudest, most saturated object in the upper right of this exact frame. Regenerate BY HAND — lid_gen.py is NOT called by build.py, so a stale texture ships silently.  ·  RESOLVE WHICH FACE OF THE LID CARRIES THE MURAL. lid_gen.py says the OUTWARD/upper face; SPEC §4 says the underside; t1_shell.py's own comment says the mural faces the counter and calls that the one arrangement reproducing all three photographs. Unresolved in live source, and in this frame it decides what the biggest object in the picture is.  ·  THE TAIL BOARD'S DARK ANGLED RECESS IS n = 1 AND SHOULD STAY UNBUILT (F325: 30 × 12 px, 58 pixels below lum 90, in a 480 × 320 frame that is byte-identical to another reference — so it is one sighting, not two). It is visible in this composition. Leave it out and say why; that is a real result, not a to-do.  ·  COLOUR CEILING TO CARRY, NOT TO FIX: flank_compare on out/r75_side.png reads the render's red at median (196,81,67), G/R 0.422, against the photograph's (158,16,6), G/R 0.114 — it images salmon. In THIS concept the error is maximally exposed because the photograph is hanging beside it. The remedy is a print GRADE on the output file, which is outside F259's 'DO NOT change RED' (that governs the model's paint). Say which you did, in the ledger, in the same edit.

**SCREENER.** Turns 75 revisions of measurement overhead into the product — a solved camera confirmed by predicting a column it never saw — and the absent people are content, not a cheat ('the lunch ended, the people left, the bus is still there'); it correctly refuses to invent a lat/long or out-resolve its 1200x824 partner, but it is gated on F57 (playa renders no vehicle) and F92, so it must be put to the owner rather than started.

## AQUÍ ME QUEDO — The Still Point — THE RISKY ONE: the bus drawn as the only motionless, fully-coloured, hard-edged thing in a picture where everything else is moving — with a plant growing up through the front wheel arch.  ·  screen 8.5  ·  `t9-2`

**THE IMAGE.** FRAMING: wide, low, AT A CHILD'S EYE — camera around 0.95–1.00 m, which puts the horizon under the belt line and makes the bus loom kindly rather than sit like a product on a shelf. Shot from the FLANK or a rear three-quarter, never the nose (see model work). The bus dead centre, absolutely planted, filling the middle third.

THE CORE MOVE: the bus is drawn with a hard, clean, unbroken line and no motion cues of any kind. Everything else in the frame is moving and drawn accordingly — children running past with two or three ghosted repeats of their own outline, a dog mid-leap drawn three times, a scooter, a kite, a paper bag lifting off the ground, a ball in the air, blown leaves, birds, steam — all in soft edges and multiples. The bus alone is sharp. It has no speed lines because it does not move and never will again.

TWO DETAILS A CAUTIOUS TEAM WILL CUT: (1) each front tyre sits very slightly INTO the paving — a shallow settled dish under it, the object having been in one spot long enough to make its own hollow. (2) A small green plant grows up through the front wheel arch: four or five leaves and one flower, drawn with exactly the same care as the mural. Not a weed. A volunteer.

LIGHT: late-afternoon warm from behind and to the left. Long, soft, moving shadows on everything that moves; one hard, clean, still shadow under the bus that anchors it like a plinth. THE BULBS ARE ALREADY ON although the sky is still bright — the small true detail of a place that opened before it needed to.

PALETTE: the moving world is drawn in three washed-back tints. The bus carries all five inks at full strength and is the only fully-coloured object in the picture. Then the plant is THE ONLY GREEN IN THE FRAME — no other green anywhere — so a child's eye goes bus, then plant, then everything else, in that order, every time.

COPY, hand-lettered, small, low: *«Aquí me quedo.»* and beneath it at half size, 'This is where I stay.'

MOTION VERSION: a slow six-second loop in which ONLY the moving world moves. The bus never does — not a bob, not a settle, not a breath. The joke lands around second four, when a child realises the big thing in the middle is the one thing that is never going to do anything.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** The specific warmth of the fixed point — the shop that is always there, the grandparent's chair, the tree in the yard that was already old. For a child it is a promise rather than an adventure: *I will be here when you come back.* It inverts the entire emotional stock of the category, which is escape, and replaces it with the thing children actually need from the world, which is permanence. The plant is the tenderest possible way to say 'arrived and never left' — it says this has been here long enough for something to grow, and it says it without a word of copy.

**FORMATS.** Single large poster, A1 (594 × 841 mm), the wall piece  ·  DIE-CUT STICKER of the bus alone from this drawing — plant included, and the CAST SHADOW as the die-cut base rather than a contour cut around the tyres. That is AUDIT_rev43.md's own rule arriving from a different direction: 'DIE-CUT OUTLINE — bridge the underbody with the cast shadow, and let no feature narrower than 0.159 m touch the cut line.'  ·  Wraparound on a paper takeaway bag: the moving crowd runs round the sides, the bus sits still on the front face  ·  Child's tee, 240 mm chest, four inks — bus + plant + shadow only, no scene  ·  Slow six-second loop for social and the menu screen  ·  Postcard set: the same scene at four times of day, only the light and the crowd changing

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** It is the category's exact inverse. Every sunset-silhouette, surf-rack, road-trip VW print on the market sells motion and escape — and this vehicle cannot move: SPEC.md §0.1 grades it S, in Tacombi's own published words, 'The engine was scrapped, transmission sold, seats swapped for cooking equipment.' No competitor can honestly draw a VW bus that cannot leave, because no other seller owns one that had its engine taken out. It is also the correct MEXICAN reading rather than the American one: 'combi' is a pesero, a working microbus that hauls people to work, and Tacombi's own name is taco + combi. Every trope in the market — peace signs, daisies, tie-dye, sunset, surfboard — belongs to the Woodstock reading of the same body shell. And the composition removes the nose entirely, so the roundel that is simultaneously the most generic and the most enforced element on the object is simply not in the picture.

**PRODUCTION.** **WHY THIS IS THE RISKY ONE, SAID PLAINLY RATHER THAN SANDED OFF:** (a) it makes the hero product the least dynamic object in its own picture, which a cautious reviewer reads as 'boring hero'; (b) the plant and the settled hollow read as NEGLECT to anyone scanning quickly — they are the emotional core and they are the first two things a nervous reviewer deletes; (c) it contradicts the owner's own standard in the record. He ruled at rev 61: 'I want this 3d model to look like new. Enhanced from the photo.' A plant growing through the wheel arch is not new. It is not WEATHERED either — SPEC §3's lock is 'chalky, sun-faded, uneven, chipped edges, dusty lower body', which is decay. This is a third thing the record has no word for: TIME. F230 already names the collision between that rev-61 ruling and SPEC §3's WEATHERED lock as one of two owner questions that gate the render and CANNOT BE ANSWERED BY CODE. This concept forces that question instead of dodging it — so put it to him AS A PICTURE, as multiple choice with the crop attached, not as a paragraph. That is also the legitimate occasion to close F192, whose own condition is 'ask it once he names the medium'; (d) formally it refuses the category's only move, so if it fails it will fail as INERTNESS rather than as calm. That is a real risk and I am not going to pretend otherwise.

**IT CONTRADICTS F18 AND I PROPOSE IT ANYWAY.** F18's scene is locked to 'nothing but the bus, die-cut tight, plus the sun and the papel picado'. The die-cut here adds a plant, adds a shadow base and has NO SUN. It is therefore explicitly a DIFFERENT ARTEFACT from F18 and is not offered as the F18 deliverable — which also means it is not gated by F18's trigger, 'build it after the model is done'. Do not raise the papel picado: the owner ruled 'Leave it open, I'll decide when the sticker is actually being built. DO NOT RE-PUT IT UNTIL THEN.'

**NO SUN AS A RAYED DISC ANYWHERE IN THIS PROGRAMME.** AUDIT_rev43.md's reasoning is right and independently checkable: 'The vehicle already carries ELEVEN radial bursts, and it is the drawing's only light source.' The Calidad starburst, the mural rosettes, the hubcap rings, the headlamps and the wheel spokes all compete for it.

**HOLD THE GREEN.** One green in the whole picture and it belongs to the plant. If a second green appears, the idea is gone.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** **SHOOT THE FLANK, NOT THE NOSE — and the reason is measured.** At a 0.95–1.00 m camera the roundel lands dead centre of a child's eyeline: build.py carries `ROUNDEL_Z_AG = 1.0170`, and `ROUNDEL_D = 0.2800`. A five-year-old stands nose to nose with it. And it is wrong: probe_rev69_fitpose's P2 reads 0.8528 against P1b's ceiling of 0.9465, and probe_rev46_vw's C8 reads 'photo 3.39, built 2.84 -- the built cells are 1.19x TOO ROUND.' It is the owner's ninth report, F191 and F234 both stand, and it is also the most trademark-exposed element on the object. Composing it out is the same correct decision twice over.  ·  **NO CHILD'S-EYE CAMERA EXISTS.** studio.views() defines 14 cameras and the standing preview list is four (`front,side,hero34f,hero34r`). The closest is `low34` at eye 1.55 m, which no preview list has ever rendered. A ~0.95 m camera is a new dict entry and a frame nobody in 76 revisions has seen. Render it and LOOK before designing to it.  ·  **The cast shadow is both the composition's anchor and the sticker's cut path, and it is separable — but only on paper.** deliver.py writes `combi_%s_shadow_only.png` and `combi_%s_no_shadow.png`, with the split measured at rev 62: shadow-catcher pixels near-black (max channel < 12/255) against a vehicle mean of 143, the vehicle side 97.1 % fully opaque and 99.99 % ONE connected component. But **T1_ALPHA has not been run since rev 62** — no LEDGER contains the string, every frame in out/ is PNG colortype 2, and deliver.py currently prints 'CONTROLS: 4 checked, 1 FAILED -- C1' and 'PACKAGE IS NOT TRUSTWORTHY'. One render closes it and every format here depends on it.  ·  **post.py DESTROYS ALPHA** — measured RGBA in, colortype 2 out, and its docstring nowhere says so. Optics (bloom / CA / vignette / grain) and a cut-out are mutually exclusive from this repo. Decide the order before anything is graded, and note post.py's live default is `BACKDROP = "headroom"` (peak 252) while its own docstring still claims white — a 252 field inside a #FFFFFF margin is a visible rectangle on the sheet.  ·  The settled hollow under each tyre, the plant, the crowd, the ghosting, the steam and the birds are ALL DRAWN. Nothing in the tree does people, volumetrics or ground deformation.  ·  The bulbs-on-in-daylight beat needs F144 addressed or drawn around: 'THE FESTOON BULBS READ AS PEARLS, NOT LIGHTS.' For a flat illustration, draw them right; do not wait on the render.  ·  If the Calidad decal appears anywhere in the crop, draw its pink star from the PHOTOGRAPH, not the model: F16 is OPEN — 'the isolated star is built BELOW the burst where both red frames put it above; every flank rosette is drawn at the diameter of its gold core.' The same row means every shipped rosette is systematically undersized.  ·  **No close tyre crop in any large format.** t1_detail.py carries `TREAD_LUGS = 64  # DECLARED, inside the MEASURED bracket 48..84 (F308)` and `TREAD_CUT = 0.0060  # DECLARED groove depth, m.  NOT measured`, and probe_rev74_tread's T2 refuses to publish a count at all. At poster scale the lugs become countable and we would be publishing a specific checkable claim the project explicitly cannot defend. PHOTOS_WANTED has never carried a tyre item; one close square-on frame would close it.

**SCREENER.** The most emotionally precise idea in the brief and the only one that inverts the category's entire stock rather than decorating it — 'I will be here when you come back' is true of THIS object (engine scrapped, transmission sold) in a way no competitor can honestly claim. Plant as the only green so the eye goes bus-plant-world is a real compositional device. Best intellectual move in the set: it names TIME as a third thing the record has no word for, colliding the owner's 'look like new' with SPEC's WEATHERED lock, and forces F230 as a picture rather than dodging it. Real risk that 'everything blurred but the still thing' reads as inert, and it says so.

---

## THE UNCERTAINTY PLATE — A factory elevation drawing where every dimension line confesses how it was obtained — and the ones that cannot be obtained are drawn anyway, in the signwriter's hand, as sentences.  ·  screen 8.5  ·  `t12-0`

**THE IMAGE.** Landscape sheet, warm uncoated paper stock (not white, not cyanotype blue — the blue is the cliché). The vehicle is a TRUE orthographic side elevation, drawn as line only: a black-brown keyline at three declared weights (0.55 mm outer silhouette / 0.35 mm panel edges and shut lines / 0.20 mm hatch reveals, louvre blades, bulb sockets), with FOUR flat fills and no shading at all — body red, cream, folk-art gold reduced to its three largest masses only, and a warm near-black for tyres and glazing. The propped tail board runs off the top-right of the sheet on its measured 38.0-degree diagonal and is deliberately allowed to cross into the title block. Below and around it: full drafting furniture — extension lines, arrowed dimension rules, section flags, a scale bar, a north-style datum arrow at the front axle.

The subversion is entirely in the annotation, and it runs on TWO INKS AND TWO TYPE VOICES that mean two different things.

MEASURED dimensions are set in tight mono numerals, solid ink, hard filled arrowheads, exactly as a factory plate would: `2400 WHEELBASE` · `1369 / 1359 TRACK` · `664.9 TYRE` · `515.5 × 403.0 SERVING APERTURE ×3` · `17.75 mm/m RAKE` · `39.7 ARCH TO TYRE` · `109.5 | 129.5 PILLARS` (and that last one is the whole thesis in one figure — the three holes are equal but the two pillars between them are NOT, and no stylised bus drawing on earth gets that right).

UNMEASURED dimensions are drawn in a 40% tint, the arrowheads replaced by open square brackets, and the number replaced by hand-lettered script in the vehicle's own silver-leaf letterform — the same hand that painted 'Gracias por su Preferencia' on the counter fascia. These are the poems, and they are all true:
  · across the propped tail board — `[ 550 ] chosen, not measured. The board's plane contains the width, so it projects only through parallax. Upper bound 590. Lower bound: none.`
  · at the rear hatch's swing — `[ 64° ] a pose. No photograph we hold shows it open.`
  · at the roof — `[ ] height retired as a target, rev 22. What this vehicle stands at is open and unmeasured.`
  · at the tread — `[ 64 lugs ] declared. Two methods disagree; the honest answer is somewhere between 48 and 84.`
  · at the engine bay — `ENGINE: NONE. TRANSMISSION: SOLD.`
  · at the three apertures — `Arrived missing three windows. We left them that way.`
  · at the empty plate frame — `1963. The only date on the vehicle, and it is stamped on an empty plate.`

One further wrongness, small and deadly: the lid's own dimension line carries `WIDTH = f(ANGLE)` with a tiny inset graph, because in this model the mural board's width is genuinely a function of how far it is open (1.2237 m at 76°, 3.47 m at 20°). A blueprint annotating its own impossibility.

Title block bottom-right, six-point mono, unfakeable: `UNIT 01 · TYPE 2 · 1963 · PLAYA DEL CARMEN · 229 MESH OBJECTS · 376,383 VERTICES · 0 NON-MANIFOLD BODY EDGES · REV 76`.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** Recognition, then a small shock, then affection. You approach it as a spec sheet — the most emotionally cold object in graphic design — and at reading distance you discover it is being honest with you about what it does not know. The feeling is INTIMACY WITH A MACHINE'S HUMILITY. Every other technical poster asserts; this one admits, and admission is disarming in a way assertion never is. And the specific admissions are all about care: somebody measured the pillars between three holes to a tenth of a millimetre AND was unwilling to guess at the width of a board. That combination — obsessive where it can be, silent where it can't — is exactly what a person means when they say a thing was made with love. The '[ 550 ] chosen, not measured' line is the one people photograph and send to each other.

**FORMATS.** A2 landscape (594 × 420 mm) — THE PRIMARY, and it is chosen to fit the proven chain, not the ambition. 3840 px on the long edge is 488 mm at 200 dpi, so A2 sits inside what has actually been rendered once (3840×2640, rev 57). Ship A2 first.  ·  A1 (841 × 594 mm) as the LIMITED tier, and only after a proving run: A1 at 200 dpi wants 4677 px, 22 % above anything this chain has ever produced. Do not promise A1 before that run.  ·  A4 (297 × 210 mm) as a free insert at the restaurant, on the same uncoated stock, unnumbered. Same drawing, annotations reduced to five lines. This is the version people actually keep.  ·  A 1080 × 1350 crop set for social: five separate posts, each one a single dimension line and its confession, cropped tight. The 'ENGINE: NONE' one is the launch post.  ·  210 × 99 mm bookmark / bill-drop from a vertical slice through the rear quarter: louvre block, Calidad burst, rear wheel, and the '1963' annotation.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** The category already sells 'VW van blueprint' by the sheet — Dreamstime carries it as a standing stock shelf — and every one of those is a GENERIC bus, traced, annotated with plausible-looking numbers nobody measured. This sheet is unforgeable in three separate ways at once. (1) It dimensions the CONVERSION, not the vehicle: apertures, counter, tail board, louvre block. Anything you could draw from a factory manual is deliberately absent. (2) Its numbers come off a mesh that has been measured for seventy-six revisions and can be regenerated on demand — the wheelbase reads +0.0 mm to spec, the track +0.0 mm, the tyre −0.1 mm — so it is a document about a real object rather than a decoration in the shape of one. (3) Nobody else would ever print the uncertainties, because a stock seller does not HAVE uncertainties; they have a traced outline and a font. The confessions are the moat. It also escapes the trope structurally: a blueprint is the most SHAPE-forward treatment available and therefore the worst posture on the VW trade-dress question — this one solves that by dimensioning only the parts Volkswagen never made, and by using the side elevation, in which the VW roundel does not appear at all.

**PRODUCTION.** Four spot inks on uncoated: one declared red, one cream, one gold, one warm near-black. Five if the wordmark appears — silver, and specify it as a FINISH (foil or metallic ink), never as grey. DO NOT sample the red off a render: flank_compare on out/r75_side.png reads the render's flank at median (196,81,67) against the photograph's (158,16,6) — it images salmon, roughly ten just-noticeable differences off. And do not average the photographs either; probe_rev71_red.py prints its own prohibition, 'the photograph's red G/R is 0.0149 .. 0.0344, a 2.3x span. DO NOT QUOTE A SINGLE FIGURE FOR IT.' Choose one red as a design ruling, record its provenance, and reconcile it against the three authored reds that currently disagree (t1_mats sRGB(196,49,36), lid_gen (159,45,22), cal_gen (214,46,30)). A flat-ink artefact can be more chromatically correct than the render currently is, and should say so out loud.

Every figure on the sheet must be generated by a script that IMPORTS the constants (t1_shell.BAY_W, BAY_CX, Z_SILL, Z_HEAD, TB_TILT_DEG, TB_WIDTH, REAR_OPEN_DEG; t1_detail.LOUV_PITCH, LOUV_N, CAP_R; build.ROUNDEL_D) and reads STATE.md — never transcribed into the artwork file. A transcribed figure goes stale silently and this project has lost revisions to exactly that. Print the generator's own commit hash in the title block.

BRAND RISK, NAMED: a cautious team will ask to delete the confessions and keep the numbers. That kills it. The confessions ARE the concept, and they are also the reason this is the one artefact in the program that a customer reads for two minutes instead of two seconds. If the owner wants only the certainties, that is a different, weaker poster and should be labelled as one.

EDITION: open edition unnumbered on A4; A1 hand-numbered in pencil, authenticated by a BLIND EMBOSS chop rather than a signature — there is no artist's hand on a render and a forged signature on CGI is embarrassing. Archive the stitched master file as the edition master: Cycles sets no seed here and the run-to-run floor is ~2.04 % of pixels moving >8 levels, so a re-render is NOT a re-print.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** FIX THE ORTHOGRAPHIC CLIPPING — I measured it today and it blocks any elevation series. studio.views()['front'] and ['rear'] are ortho=3.55 with horizontal sensor fit, so at 1600×1100 the vertical extent is 3.55 × 1100/1600 = 2.4406 m, covering z 0.2997 to 2.7403, while the vehicle spans z 0 at the tyres to STATE.md's 'bbox top 3.132' with the lids up. out/r75_front.png proves it: 42 ink pixels on the top row and 738 on the bottom row, subject bbox y 0..1099 of 1100. Derive ortho_scale from build.py's own printed bbox with a stated margin instead of the typed 3.55, and add a verify_clone row asserting zero ink on all four borders of any ortho frame — WATCH IT GO RED at 3.55 before believing it green.  ·  out/r75_side.png is CLEAN — I measured 0 ink on all four borders, subject bbox x 212..1457, y 111..963 of 1600×1100, so the side elevation needs no reframing. It is the only camera in the tree that is already correctly composed. (For contrast: out/r75_hero34r.png carries 492 ink pixels on its right column — the counter runs off the edge — so the rear three-quarter needs reframing before it is used anywhere.)  ·  A LINE PASS. Nothing in this tree has ever extracted a line. Try the Grease Pencil Line Art modifier on the `side` ortho first, because it produces EDITABLE VECTOR STROKES that export to SVG, which is what a drafting sheet actually needs; Freestyle is the fallback for stroke quality; a Sobel on normal+Z passes is the cheap 2D prototype. Crease angle 30–40°, not lower — T1_body is shade-smooth and a low angle finds the subdivision. The mesh is clean enough to trust: 229,489 quad against 1,040 tri and 5,186 ngon, 0 non-manifold body edges, cutters rolled back none.  ·  RENDER WITH T1_ALPHA=1 for the flat-fill masks, and DO NOT route it through post.py — measured, post.py takes RGBA in (colortype 6) and writes colortype 2, so optics and cut-out are mutually exclusive and the silhouette is silently destroyed. T1_ALPHA appears in zero ledgers; it has not been exercised since rev 62. Run it once and watch deliver.py's C1 go from FAILED to PASS.  ·  FIX tex/senor.png's MISSING KEYLINE if the wordmark appears at size. SPEC §3 requires 'SILVER with a dark keyline and drop shadow' and the shipped asset has no dark ink at all — among pixels with alpha>200 the 1st-percentile luma is 168.3 and the fraction below luma 80 is 0.0000. lid_gen.py already authors the convention as DARKLINE = (72,26,16). script_gen.py is NOT called by build.py, so regenerate tex/senor.png by hand or the render silently keeps the old file. Cheapest visible improvement in the inventory.  ·  NOTHING ELSE. No new geometry, no emblem work, no gloss work. This sheet ships while F191's hold stands, because the side elevation does not contain the nose roundel — only the four hubcap badges, at 1.62 mm on an A2 sheet, below any scale at which their arrangement is judgeable. Ship the elevation FIRST and say why.

**SCREENER.** A factory plate where every dimension confesses its provenance, in two inks and two type voices — solid mono for measured, bracketed hand-script for posed. '[ 550 ] chosen, not measured. Upper bound 590. Lower bound: none.' and 'ENGINE: NONE. TRANSMISSION: SOLD.' The confessions are the moat: a stock seller has no uncertainties to print. Strongest detail is 'WIDTH = f(ANGLE)' with an inset graph — a blueprint annotating its own impossibility. Blueprint is the most saturated shelf in the category and it escapes structurally by dimensioning only what the factory never made. Sizes A2 to the proven chain instead of the ambition.

---

## AZULEJO 1963 — the bus fired into Puebla tin-glaze — The orthographic side elevation hand-painted in cobalt on tin-glazed talavera tile, grout lines running straight through the vehicle, and the three serving hatches left as bare unpainted glaze.  ·  screen 8.5  ·  `t13-0`

**THE IMAGE.** SOURCE VIEW: the true orthographic side elevation. `studio.views()['side']` is `ortho=5.90, lens=None` — a genuine parallel projection, and talavera does not do perspective, so the craft and the camera already agree. I measured out/r75_side.png at 77.8% x 77.5% frame fill with clean margin on all four sides and no clipping; it is the one existing frame that can be traced as-is.

PANEL: 6 tiles wide x 4 high, each 105 mm, = 630 x 420 mm. Vehicle at 1:8 — the propped silhouette (4.580 x 3.132 m, from STATE.md's `x range [-2.157, 2.160]` plus the tail board reaching x -2.420, and `bbox top 3.132`) lands at 572 x 391 mm inside it, lid up, tail board propped, counter cantilevered.

PALETTE, AND THIS IS THE WHOLE IDEA: Puebla talavera's historic six are cobalt blue, tin-white ground, copper green, manganese black-brown, antimony yellow, and one rare orange. THERE IS NO VERMILLION IN THIS CRAFT. The vehicle's dominant colour does not exist in the material. So: body in COBALT, the cream upper body and roof as raw tin-white glaze (warm off-white, never paper white), the folk art in antimony yellow with manganese outline, the counter's brass nosing in antimony, tyres in manganese. The single orange is spent on exactly one thing — the four hubcap domes. Four small warm discs in an otherwise blue-and-white panel, landing on the wheels, which is where the eye goes anyway.

LINE: everything outlined in manganese at ~1.5 mm on the panel, brush-loaded so each stroke starts fat and tapers to a hairline — which is already the flank art's own signature (I looked at ref_side.jpg cropped to the rear quarter: every gold acanthus stroke is thick-to-hairline, terminating in a tight rolled volute).

THE GROUT IS THE POINT. Tile joints run straight through the vehicle — do NOT compose to avoid them. One joint crosses the Senor Tacombi lockup; one crosses a wheel. Joints are 3 mm of unglazed warm-grey bisque. Each tile is a fraction of a degree off square, so the grid wanders. If the grid is perfect and the edges are crisp, it reads as a printed tile and the whole idea dies.

GLAZE BEHAVIOUR THE DIGITAL VERSION MUST FAKE: talavera glaze is convex and pools. Cobalt goes markedly darker where a stroke terminates and where two strokes cross, and it bleeds ~0.5 mm into the white so no edge is clean.

THE HATCHES: the three serving apertures are left as bare tin-white glaze, undrawn — the only three areas of the panel carrying no ink at all. STATE.md: `open serving apertures on +Y: 3`, `bay widths 0.516 0.515 0.516` — they are EQUAL, so the rhythm is measured, not eyeballed. Three empty rectangles in a blue drawing is the founding story stated in negative.

BORDER: one tile-wide, the flank's own acanthus in cobalt, running all four sides. Talavera panels are always bordered; ours borrows the vehicle's own ornament to do it.

LIGHT: photograph it, never render it. Single raking source from the left so the convex glaze catches, the grout falls into shadow, and it reads as a wall you could put your hand on.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** Permanence and civic weight. Tile is what a country puts on the front of a building it means to keep — it is the material of churches, market halls and fountains, not of trucks. Rendered in it, the taqueria stops being a vehicle that turned up and becomes a place that has always been there. And the palette does something nobody expects: stripped of its red, the bus stops reading as fun and starts reading as a monument. A person feels the same thing they feel in front of a tiled shopfront in a Mexican town square — that somebody built this on purpose, out of expensive materials, expecting it to outlast them.

**FORMATS.** 24-tile wall panel, 630 x 420 mm, bus at 1:8 — one per restaurant, hand-painted in Puebla, signed and dated on the reverse of the corner tile. This is the anchor object; everything else is derived from it.  ·  Compact retail panel, 4 x 3 tiles, 420 x 315 mm, bus SHUT at 1:11 (4.065 x 2.227 m per STATE.md's `overall height (vehicle max, lids excluded) 2.2268`). Framed and boxed. The shut state has never been rendered in 76 revisions, so this SKU doubles as the first sighting of the closed silhouette.  ·  Single-tile coasters, 105 mm, set of six, one motif each: an 8-lobed rosette, a dark comma-with-dots tendril, one acanthus volute, the wheel bullseye, the three hatches, and '1963'. Each motif drawn at ITS OWN scale rather than shrunk off the bus — the largest flank rosette is a 68 mm equivalent circle (folk_gen.FLANK_ROSETTES, 3632 mm2), so at 105 mm it is drawn UP, not down, and nothing collapses.  ·  Trivet, 2 x 2 tiles, 210 mm square: the wheel bullseye alone. Four concentric bands of the brand palette; STATE.md gives the badge at `4 rings R 0.043429` = 31.7% of the 274 mm cap, so it scales as a ratio.  ·  Printed derivative: the panel photographed square-on under raking light. A2 poster, tote, tissue wrap, cup sleeve. The photograph of a ceramic object is a fundamentally different image from a render of a bus, and it inherits none of the render's colour problems.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** Every VW-bus artefact on the market is a photograph, an illustration, or a line drawing, and essentially all of them are red or rainbow. This one is a fired ceramic object with NO RED IN IT, with grout running through the vehicle's face. The tile grid is a reduction rule the category has never used: it forces the drawing into flat, bounded, brush-loaded strokes and refuses gradients, gloss and photorealism outright — which is the exact opposite of the saturated 'sunset silhouette' and 'blueprint' shelves. It also sidesteps the project's largest measured defect rather than inheriting it: flank_compare on out/r75_side.png reads the render's red at median (196,81,67), G/R 0.422, against the photograph's (158,16,6), G/R 0.114 — it images salmon. A craft with no vermillion in its palette cannot get the red wrong. And there is a real, quotable geographic rhyme: talavera poblana is made in Puebla, and Puebla is where VW de Mexico built the Combi from 1970. One line of copy, no invention.

**PRODUCTION.** VENDOR: a certified talavera taller in Puebla (the craft carries a denominacion de origen; use a certified maker or do not call it talavera). Hand-painted maiolica on tin glaze, single firing after decoration. Lead times are long and per-panel variation is high — that is a feature for the restaurant anchors and a problem for retail, so cap the retail SKU at one panel size and accept that no two are identical.

COLOUR: do NOT hand the taller a hex value sampled off a render. Give them cobalt, antimony, manganese, copper green and the one orange as PIGMENT names and let the kiln decide. Every hex in this brief is an 8-bit display-space reading through PIL off warm, uncorrected frames — a mood reference, never an albedo. Separately, probe_rev71_red.py prints its own prohibition on the photograph side: 'the photograph's red G/R is 0.0149 .. 0.0344, a 2.3x span. DO NOT QUOTE A SINGLE FIGURE FOR IT.'

DRAWING SOURCE FOR THE ORNAMENT: draw the border acanthus from ref_side.jpg at 4-6x magnification, NOT from tex/swirl.png. The shipped tile is measurably more fragmented than the photograph — 196 gold connected components against the photograph's 66, with the top three carrying 66.4% against the photograph's 84.4% (folk_gen.COMP_TOP = {1: 47.8, 2: 73.7, 3: 84.4, ...}). The generator matches the coverage and misses the hand.

SIMPLIFICATION, WITH A MEASUREMENT UNDER IT: folk_gen.COMP_HIST's first three bins are (24, 0.42%), (21, 1.32%), (3, 0.55%) — 48 of 66 gold components carry 2.29% of all the gold. Delete all 48. That is a measured licence to reduce, not a taste call, and it is what makes the border cuttable in a brush.

DIGITAL PROOF BEFORE FIRING: build the panel as flat art with the bleed and the pooling faked, print it at size on matte, and pin it up at 2 m. Then fire one test tile and compare. Do not go to a 24-tile panel off a screen.

CEILING: nobody in this session has seen any current Tacombi merchandise, brand guideline, official colour or logo lockup — tacombi.com, shop.tacombi.com and fontsinuse.com all returned EGRESS_BLOCKED. Do not state a Tacombi brand colour anywhere in this programme until the owner supplies one.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** NOTHING BLOCKING — this concept can start today off out/r75_side.png, which already exists at 1600x1100 with clean margins. That makes it the fastest of the three to a physical proof.  ·  If any other elevation is used, FIX THE ORTHOGRAPHIC CLIPPING FIRST. studio.views()['front'] and ['rear'] are ortho=3.55 with horizontal sensor fit, so at 1600x1100 the vertical extent is 3.55*1100/1600 = 2.4406 m, covering z 0.2997 to 2.7403 — against a vehicle that reaches z=0 at the tyres and 3.132 at the top. out/r75_front.png has ink running off both borders. Derive ortho_scale from build.py's printed bbox with a stated margin instead of the typed 3.55, and add a verify row asserting zero ink on all four border rows/columns. Watch it go red on 3.55 before believing it green.  ·  DO NOT PAINT THE EMBLEM FROM THE MODEL. At 1:8 the nose roundel would be 35 mm and plainly judgeable (ROUNDEL_D = 0.2800 in build.py). It is the owner's ninth report: probe_rev69_fitpose's P2 reads 0.8528 against P1b's 0.9465 ceiling, and probe_rev46_vw's C8 reads 'photo 3.39, built 2.84 -- the built cells are 1.19x TOO ROUND'. The side elevation does not contain the roundel at all, which is the main reason this concept is unblocked by F191 — but the four hubcap badges do appear, at 34 mm each, and STATE.md warns 'hubcap badge is SELF-CONSISTENCY ONLY -- CAP_EMBLEM_WFRAC has never been compared to a frame'. Draw both from ref_playa_34.png, not from the render.  ·  F74(a) is open and would propagate into any lid artwork: tex/lidmural.png is cropped at the bottom — the bottom flower row sliced mid-bloom and the olive-yellow side columns truncated, where ref_side.jpg shows a board framed on all four sides. I opened the master and confirmed it. lid_gen.py is NOT called by build.py, so regeneration is a hand step or the render silently keeps the old file. Fix before the lid appears at 1:8.  ·  NOT GATED by F191/F193 — this is a hand-painted ceramic object, not the delivery render. Say so explicitly in the proposal so the hold does not read as blocking it.

**SCREENER.** The constraint does the creative work: talavera has no vermillion, so the vehicle's dominant colour cannot exist in the material, and stripped of red the bus stops reading as fun and becomes civic. Grout run deliberately THROUGH the face rather than composed around, the three hatches left as bare unglazed white — the founding story in negative, grounded in the measured equal bays — and the single rare orange spent only on four hubcaps. 'Render X in craft Y' is a known template; this escapes it by designing to the material's limits rather than skinning them. Slow, expensive, high per-unit variance.
