## Humo — The gold scrollwork was never decoration — it is the smoke, and in this one image it leaves the bodywork, rises through the serving hatch and off the top of the frame.  ·  screen 8.5  ·  `t19-1`

**THE IMAGE.** FRAMING: flat, measured, drawn — a true side elevation, no perspective, no horizon, no ground beyond a single hard shadow line. The vehicle occupies the lower two-thirds. The upper third is empty ground and is the whole point.

THE MOVE: the folk art on the flank is drawn exactly as it sits on the vehicle — the two big acanthus masses (the cab-door bouquet and the rear-quarter mass), the rosettes, the dark comma-tendrils — until it reaches the middle serving aperture. There, one gold stroke does not turn back. It carries THROUGH the hatch, up past the propped mural lid, and out of the top of the sheet: the same tapering thick-to-hairline acanthus line, the same rolled volutes and hooks, now unattached to anything, widening and thinning as it climbs. Two or three secondary fronds peel off it. A handful of rosettes float free in the upper air like sparks. Everything below the sill is still ornament; everything above the roof is unmistakably a plume.

The cut point matters: the vehicle's own measured rule is that the gold lives on the RED ONLY and stops at the rear corner (43.687% gold on the rear quarter against 0.006% on the flat tail face). The drawing breaks that rule ONCE, on purpose, at the hatch — which is the only opening in the bodywork. The smell comes out where the food comes out.

PALETTE — three inks plus the garment/paper as the fourth: gold (the authored GOLD is (0.8600, 0.5400, 0.0600), a chrome-yellow), cream, and a dark maroon near-black for the comma-tendrils. Body red is the SUBSTRATE, not an ink — print on red stock or a red garment, which deletes the largest screen and the ink slab that makes a shirt read like a sticker.

COMPOSITION: a heavy horizontal mass with one vertical line escaping it, running off the top edge. Deliberately unbalanced upward. On apparel, place it so the plume runs off the shoulder seam — the graphic leaves the garment.

MOTION (loop): 3 seconds. The vehicle is dead still. The scroll DRAWS itself off the flank, stroke by stroke, in the signwriter's real order — the three big masses first, then the tendrils, then the rosettes — and rises. Reverse and hold. It is a painting animation and a smoke animation at the same time, which is the joke, told twice.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** A joke landing. You read the poster as a nicely-drawn bus with flowers on it — the same reading you have given a thousand VW busses — and then your eye follows one curl off the bodywork and out of the frame and the whole image reorganises. The flowers were never flowers. Delight at a re-reading is the most durable reason anybody keeps a graphic, because it works again on the person you show it to.

Underneath that is something warmer and slower: it says the ornament on this vehicle came from the same place as the cooking. Somebody painted those curls by hand, with a brush, on a Tuesday, in the same yard where the comal was lit. The picture makes the decoration and the food one substance.

And on a garment it does the thing good graphics do — it refuses to be contained. A shirt where the smell runs off your shoulder is a shirt people ask about.

**FORMATS.** Screen print, 3 spot inks (gold / cream / dark maroon) on a RED garment — the body colour is the shirt. 280 mm chest, or a 340 mm shoulder-to-hem placement where the plume crosses the shoulder seam. Three screens, no white under-base, soft hand.  ·  500×700 mm poster, same three inks on heavy UNCOATED stock. Spot colour on uncoated hits a chroma CMYK cannot reach, and the flatness stops being a deficit and becomes the idiom — the real bus is hand-painted flat colour, not clearcoat.  ·  Tote, 380×420 mm panel, plume running off the top of the panel into the handle stitching.  ·  3-second motion loop, 1080×1350 and 1080×1920 — the paint-on/rise-off sequence.  ·  30 mm enamel pin of ONE scroll-plume alone, no vehicle. It reads at that size because it is a single tapering stroke with two volutes — this is the one element in the whole vehicle that survives at badge scale without reduction.  ·  Life-size embroidered rosette, 45–60 mm, left chest, two colours (~9k stitches). The largest measured flank rosette is a ~68 mm equivalent circle ON THE VEHICLE, so at 45–60 mm nothing is reduced and nothing collapses.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** This is the strongest anti-derivative move available anywhere in the program, because it takes the single most DANGEROUS element and converts it into the most differentiating one.

The category's third-biggest trope is hippie flower-power — daisies, sunflowers, peace signs. This bus genuinely carries hand-painted florals, so any careless crop of it gets read as Woodstock: the American counterculture meaning of a body shape whose Mexican meaning is a working microbus that hauls people to their jobs. That misread is the program's live hazard, not a theoretical one.

Giving the flowers a JOB fixes it in one gesture. Nobody has ever mistaken a plume of smoke for flower power. The moment the scroll comes off a griddle it is unmistakably rotulismo — Mexican signwriting — and the whole image relocates to the right country. It is the cheapest and most complete solution to the derivativeness problem in this brief, and it costs one drawing decision.

It is also formally unlike the category: no landscape, no sky, no sun, no vehicle in motion, flat spot colour rather than photoreal or watercolour, and a composition that deliberately runs off the sheet.

**PRODUCTION.** THREE SCREENS IS NOT A COMPROMISE, IT IS THE MEASUREMENT. folk_gen's own connected-component census of the reference flank reads COMP_TOP = {1: 47.8, 2: 73.7, 3: 84.4} and COMP_HIST's first three bins are (24, 0.42%), (21, 1.32%), (3, 0.55%) — 48 of 66 gold components carry 2.29% of the ink and the largest THREE carry 84.4%. Delete the 48. That is a licence to simplify computed on this artwork, not a design platitude.

DRAW FROM THE PHOTOGRAPH, NOT FROM THE SHIPPED TILE. The reference census is 66 components with the top three at 84.4%; a census of tex/swirl.png found 196 components with the top three at 66.4% — the shipped art is measurably more fragmented and smaller-scale than the reference, and folk_gen says so itself: 'the same 10% gold over the same flank can be one bouquet or five hundred commas.' SPEC §10.10's own method applies — read at 4–5x magnification, redraw at 2–4K, measure back.

SPECIFY LINE WEIGHTS IN FINAL MILLIMETRES PER SKU. Never scale one master. On the shipped 4096² tile the gold's stroke width runs p10 2.66 mm / p50 13.15 mm / p90 45.07 mm ON THE VEHICLE; reduce the whole flank to a 280 mm chest print (about 1:14.5) and the median becomes 0.91 mm and the p10 becomes 0.18 mm — below any reliable positive line on jersey. The plume is drawn at its own weight for its own size, and the flank art is kept to the three masses. Print a physical scale proof with a stepped wedge (2.0 / 1.0 / 0.7 / 0.5 / 0.35 / 0.25 mm) on the actual garment before any screen is cut.

DECLARE ONE RED AS THE SUBSTRATE COLOUR. There are four in the tree — t1_mats sRGB(196,49,36), lid_gen (159,45,22), cal_gen (214,46,30) — and the render's cannot be sampled: flank_compare reads it at (196,81,67), G/R 0.422, against the photograph's (158,16,6), G/R 0.114. It images salmon. Choose the ink and record it with its provenance; do not eyedrop a frame.

STATE THE ARTEFACT BOUNDARY IN WRITING. This contradicts the vehicle's own measured composition rule and it does so on purpose. Nothing on the model changes. SPEC §10.10's 'ABSOLUTE REPLICATION OF ALL ARTWORK' hard bar governs the MODEL's decals, not a derived stylisation — say so up front so this is not later audited against a rule written for texture bakes.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** ISOLATE THE FLANK ART AS ITS OWN LAYER. It is BOX-PROJECTED in the shader (t1_mats: projection='BOX'), so it has no UVs and does not export. T1_W_ART is a live global opacity on the folk-art alpha (ships at 1.00) — difference a W_ART=1.00 render against W_ART=0, or add an AOV. Either way it is a contained change.  ·  REGENERATE tex/swirl.png / swirl_b.png AT THE REFERENCE'S OWN LINE QUALITY, or accept that the poster is drawn by hand from ref_side.jpg and the tile is not the source. The census gap (196 components vs 66, top-3 66.4% vs 84.4%) is the measurement that decides which.  ·  FIX tex/senor.png's MISSING KEYLINE if the wordmark appears in any layout. Measured: among pixels with alpha>200 the 1st-percentile luma is 168.3 and the fraction below luma 80 is 0.0000 — there is no dark ink in the asset at all, against SPEC §3's 'SILVER with a dark keyline and drop shadow'. Silver on red with no keyline is mud at 200 dpi. script_gen.py is NOT called by build.py, so regenerate by hand or the render silently keeps the old file.  ·  DECLARE ONE BRAND RED as a design ruling with its own provenance line, reconciling the four authored values, and record that it is NOT taken from a render and NOT averaged from the photographs (probe_rev71_red.py prints its own prohibition: 'the photograph's red G/R is 0.0149 .. 0.0344, a 2.3x span. DO NOT QUOTE A SINGLE FIGURE FOR IT').  ·  OPTIONAL, FOR THE MOTION VERSION ONLY: teach folk_gen.py to write a per-component paint-order rank into an unused channel, so a threshold sweep paints the art on largest-first. It already computes the components. Regenerate by hand — folk_gen is not called by build.py.  ·  NOTHING ELSE. No geometry, no lighting, no render chain, no alpha, no stitch. This concept is the one that ships while every pipeline blocker in the program is still open.

**SCREENER.** The smartest single move here: it takes the program's live hazard — hand-painted florals misreading as Woodstock flower-power, wrong country entirely — and converts it into the differentiator with one drawing decision, because nobody mistakes a plume of smoke for a peace sign; the re-reading mechanism is the real reason people keep a graphic, the red garment deletes the ink slab, and folk_gen's own census (top 3 components = 84.4% of gold) is a measured licence to simplify rather than a platitude.

---

## BODEGÓN — THE HATCH AS A COTÁN NICHE — Photograph the serving window as a seventeenth-century Spanish still life: black ground, one raking light, a cabbage hanging on a string over the sill, and no bus visible anywhere.  ·  screen 8.5  ·  `free2-2`

**THE IMAGE.** Black. In the middle of a tall portrait sheet, one landscape rectangle: the serving aperture, dead square to camera, 515.5 × 403.0 mm with its 55 mm corner radius, floating in a black surround that is most of the page. The bay's white bobble fringe scallops the opening — the only ornament in the picture, catching light as a row of small moons. Inside the opening it is nearly dark. A single hard-ish source enters from upper left at roughly 25° above horizontal, warm, about 3000 K, and everything falls to black within 400 mm of depth. On the sill, arranged on Sánchez Cotán's hyperbolic curve — the swag that runs from the upper left of the niche down to the lower right — a whole cabbage and a bulb of garlic SUSPENDED ON STRINGS from the bulb wire above, then on the stone of the sill itself a stack of tortillas, one lime halved with the cut face turned to the light, one dried chile. Nothing else. Far back and barely resolved: 40 mm of the steel worktop's lit edge (GAL_Z_WORK 1.5010) and one bulb burning as a small orange sun, deliberately out of focus. No wordmark, no folk art, no roundel, no vehicle. Palette by area: 70 % black, then warm cream, one cold green (GAL_GREEN 0.1450,0.3050,0.1750 is already in the tree), one red-orange, brass. Motion version: a 6-second silent loop in which only two things move — the hanging cabbage rotating maybe 15° and back, and the bulb's filament breathing. The camera is locked. Nothing else in the frame moves at all. Three prints, one per bay, hung as a triptych.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** Reverence, and the shock of being taken seriously. This is the emotional opposite of every food-truck image ever made: no sizzle, no crowd, no sun, no smiling person holding a taco. It is quiet, dark and formal, and it says that the ingredients — a cabbage, a lime, a stack of tortillas — deserve the treatment Spain gave to game birds and quinces in 1600. For anyone who knows Cotán it is a jolt of recognition. For everyone else it is simply an unaccountably serious picture of a lime, in a category where nothing is ever serious, and that is why they stop. It also does something no other asset in this program can: it makes the hole in the side of the bus, which exists only because the donor was missing three windows, into a sacred architectural frame. The cheapest fact about the vehicle becomes the most beautiful thing in the picture.

**FORMATS.** Gallery-format print series of three, 500 × 700 mm portrait, on uncoated rag, with a spot gloss varnish on the bulb only and nothing else.  ·  In-restaurant installation: the same three at 1:1 — 515 mm wide each — hung with their sills at the real 1.307 m, so they read as three more hatches cut into the restaurant wall.  ·  Menu cover and wine-list cover, 210 × 297 mm, the niche small in a large black field.  ·  6-second loop for social, 4:5 and 9:16. It will be the only dark, still, silent thing in the feed.  ·  Packaging: the black niche as the face of a takeaway box or bag, printed on kraft so the black goes matt and warm.  ·  Christmas / seasonal card, 148 × 210 mm — the composition takes a seasonal ingredient swap without any redesign at all.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** It is not a VW-bus picture. It is a still life that happens to be framed by one, so it does not compete in the ocean at all — no silhouette, no sunset, no palms, no daisies, no blueprint, no line art, none of the seven documented saturation tropes, and no vehicle to be generic about. The lineage is exact rather than borrowed: the bodegón is the Spanish still-life tradition that crossed to New Spain and became the Mexican one; Cotán's signature format is literally a dark rectangular window niche with produce hung on strings and set on the sill; and this vehicle has three of those, 515 × 403 mm, with the glass removed and a dark galley behind. I am not applying a style to a subject — the subject already is the format. It also happens to be the only concept here that is immune to the project's two worst measured defects: gloss_compare's paint-spread failure is irrelevant because there is no paint in frame, and the salmon red is irrelevant because the red body is outside the crop.

**PRODUCTION.** THIS IS THE RISKY ONE AND I WANT THAT ON THE RECORD. A cautious team rejects it in the first review for four reasons, all fair: it is dark, it does not show the product in the appetising way, it does not show the bus, and it cites a painter nobody in the room has heard of. Propose it anyway — the owner's stated bar is 'emotion evoking' and the record carries, in his own words via F92, 'the emotional bar that sits ABOVE clinical accuracy'. Two hard production constraints. FIRST, build this as a SEPARATE NAMED RIG, never as an edit to the studio. F259 licenses a relight — 'Relight the studio and fix the ground if necessary' — but its constraint travels with it (DO NOT change RED, and flank_compare / gloss_compare / probe_rev70_tyre must not regress), and a low-key single-source rig will make every one of those photograph-facing gates unreadable. If it is a second rig, nothing goes red for the wrong reason. SECOND, read everything in linear and refuse clipped data: photometry.py enforces this and its own rules apply hardest to a picture that is 70 % black — a single blown highlight on the lime destroys the whole conceit. Print: the black must be a rich four-colour black or a spot, never 100K, or the fringe's small moons disappear into it. Ceiling to carry: nothing about how this renders is verified, because F45 leaves the aperture interior unmeasured — though F98's measurement that the render's interior sits at 0.705 of exterior cream value against the photograph's 0.774 says the interior is already darker than reference, which is the direction this concept wants.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** THE FOOD DOES NOT EXIST. This is the only concept of my three that needs genuine new geometry: five to eight hero produce props — cabbage, garlic bulb, halved lime, dried chile, a stack of tortillas — with real subsurface scattering. Nothing edible exists among the 229 objects. Budget it honestly as the cost of the idea, not as a detail.  ·  INTERIOR CAMERAS AND A MEASURED APERTURE INTERIOR — same precondition as concept 2. F45's roof-aperture half is OPEN, UNGATED and never measured, and this composition is entirely an interior lit through a hole.  ·  A SINGLE-SOURCE LOW-KEY RIG, built as a named second rig so the existing photograph-facing gates are not disturbed. Do not touch the studio.  ·  SUSPENSION HARDWARE. gal_hook ×6 exist and bulb_string / tb_bulbflex give a flex curve to hang from, but the actual strings, knots and the way a cabbage hangs need building. In Cotán the string is half the picture.  ·  GROUND THE BOBBLE FRINGE OR RETHINK IT. It is the picture's only ornament at 500 mm print size, and t1_detail records 'Ball diameter and pitch are INFERRED (each ball is ~2 px)'. This is the one element that would embarrass us at gallery scale.  ·  Confirm the show-side glass really is absent in the render — t1_shell's OPEN_BAYS = (0, 1, 2) says all three panes are removed on +Y, which is exactly right for a niche and should be verified by looking, not by grep.  ·  F72 sits just outside the crop and will creep in on any wider frame: the counter props read within 2.4 DN of the painted body where the photograph shows 60+ DN, and gal_caddy has no dark frame member at all.

**SCREENER.** Cotán's format literally IS a dark niche with produce hung on strings over a sill, and this vehicle has three of them only because the donor was missing three windows — so the subject already is the format rather than a style applied to it. 'The cheapest fact about the vehicle becomes the most beautiful thing in the picture' is the best line in the batch. Understands F259's constraint well enough to demand a SEPARATE named rig so the photograph-facing gates don't red for the wrong reason. Costs the most new geometry (5-8 SSS produce props) and food-photography has had a bodegón moment, so it is slightly less unclaimed than it believes.

---

## COUNTER HEIGHT — Shoot the whole brand from 0.95 m — a four-year-old's eyeline — where the counter lip is above your head and the taco arrives from the sky.  ·  screen 8.5  ·  `free3-0`

**THE IMAGE.** Camera at z = 0.95 m, 0.55 m out from the counter's outer edge, 28–35 mm, tilted up about 12°, standing where a child stands at bay 3. FRAMING, bottom to top: the lower 55% of the frame is nothing but cream counter fascia, dull, sharp, filling the eye the way a wall does. At 62% frame height a single hard brass hairline runs edge to edge — the nosing at 1.1890 m — and it is dead level with the horizon of the picture, which is the whole compositional gag: the brand's strongest horizontal is a thing you cannot see over. Above it, a band of undercut shadow, then the aperture sill at 1.307 m, then bay 3's opening with the white bobble fringe scalloped along its edge like a row of small moons, then the bulb string at roughly 1.765 m, then the painted lid leaning out over the entire top of the frame at 76° like a ceiling of flowers. Entering top-right, foreshortened and enormous, a forearm and a paper-wrapped taco coming down. LIGHT: focus locked on the brass line at f/2.8 — everything below it sharp and matte, everything above it soft and warm, so the picture is literally divided into the reachable and the unreachable. Key comes from the bulbs and from the 1.110 × 2.034 m sky hole above the counter; the cream fascia is fill-lit and slightly cool. PALETTE: cream occupies the bottom half, one brass line, red only as a 40 mm sliver of flank at frame left, gold folk art dissolved into bokeh, amber bulbs as the top light. MOTION VERSION (vertical, 15 s): locked camera, nothing moves for 9 seconds except the bulbs breathing — then the hand comes down, sets the food on the brass line, and leaves frame. No cut. No music.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** The exact physical memory of being small at a counter: the wood or the metal at your eye level, the impossibility of seeing what is happening up there, and then the arrival of something wonderful from above. It is awe produced by geometry rather than by styling, and it lands on two audiences at once — children feel it as the present tense, adults feel it as a recovered memory. It is also the only frame in this whole program in which the viewer is not a spectator of a vehicle but a customer standing at a hatch.

**FORMATS.** Children's picture book, 24pp, 210 × 210 mm — the bus is NEVER shown whole; every spread is at 0.95 m, and the reveal on the final spread is the side elevation at adult height. This is the F18 audience served in a format F18 never named, and it is a different artefact from F18.  ·  Growth chart, 300 × 1600 mm, matte paper, with the REAL heights ruled on it: counter 1.19 m, serving sill 1.31 m, aperture head 1.71 m, bulb string ~1.77 m, roof 1.99 m. A child measures themselves against the actual bus.  ·  Restaurant paper placemat, 420 × 297 mm, one colour + cream, printed so it reads correctly from a seated child's angle.  ·  Kids' tee, 90 mm left-chest: brass line, fringe, one hand. Two inks on natural.  ·  Die-cut sticker sheet, A6 kiss-cut: the fringe run, one bulb, the brass corner, a hand and a taco.  ·  15 s vertical film, 1080 × 1920, silent or with room tone only.  ·  3 m restaurant wall graphic, printed at 1:1, so a real child stands beside a rendered counter at the same height as the real one.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** Every VW bus in the merchandise category is a three-quarter portrait shot from adult eye height, because that is how you photograph a vehicle you admire. This is not a portrait of a vehicle — it is a point of view from inside a transaction, and it is only available because this bus is a building with a counter on it. Structurally it also cannot be mistaken for VW merchandise: `ROUNDEL_X = 2.1290`, `ROUNDEL_Z_AG = 1.0170` — the roundel is four metres away, behind the camera's shoulder, out of frame in every shot in the series. And the reason it is unclaimed is measurable: `studio.views()` defines fourteen cameras and the lowest z in any of them is 1.52 m. In 76 revisions and 793 commits nobody has once rendered this object from the height of the person the original deliverable was written for.

**PRODUCTION.** Print the growth chart and the placemat as one-colour-plus-substrate; do not attempt process red, since `flank_compare` on out/r75_side.png reads the render's red at median (196,81,67) against the photograph's (158,16,6), G/R 0.422 vs 0.114 — it images salmon, and this composition happily uses almost no red. The brass nosing is the only element that must hold a hard edge at every size, so specify it in final millimetres per SKU (0.6 mm minimum) and never scale one master. For the book, shoot every spread from the same z = 0.95 and vary only x/y — the constancy of the eyeline is the whole device. This is NOT the delivery render and is not gated by F191/F193; say so in the proposal. F192's 'ask once he names the medium' condition is met by the growth chart, which has a real trim size.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** Add a `child` camera to `studio.views()` at z = 0.95 with tgt z ≈ 1.30 — trivial, but nothing at that height exists and the composition cannot be previewed without it.  ·  THE GALLEY INTERIOR IS UNGATED AND THIS FRAMING POINTS STRAIGHT AT IT. F45 (roof-aperture interior, 2.91e5 px²) and F15 (A7 unlit roofed body, 6.92e5 px²) have never been measured against a photograph. Render one child-height frame and LOOK before designing to it — `t1_detail.py` records the photographed truth as 'NOTHING inside any aperture is darker than display luma 73 … The galley is a bright room.'  ·  RESOLVE A STALE SOURCE COMMENT AGAINST THE MACHINE. `t1_detail.py` says 'THE SHELL DOES NOT CUT THAT HOLE … Until it is cut, the opening is stood in for HERE … `gal_ceiling` is an emissive panel at the roof line.' STATE.md says 'roof aperture: open, and solid fore / aft / both sides' and reports no roof above 1.710 m at |y|<0.30. The machine outranks the prose; establish which is live before lighting anything through that hole.  ·  THE BOBBLE FRINGE IS INFERRED AND THIS SHOT MAKES IT A HERO. `t1_detail.py`: 'Ball diameter and pitch are INFERRED (each ball is ~2 px)' in the reference. At this camera each ball is hundreds of pixels. Either measure it from a better frame or add a PHOTOS_WANTED item — one close square-on frame of one aperture edge closes it.  ·  F144 STANDS AND IT IS NOW THE KEY LIGHT: 'THE FESTOON BULBS READ AS PEARLS, NOT LIGHTS.' `BULB_PITCH = 0.0286`, `BULB_R = 0.0110` — 22 mm bulbs on a 28.6 mm pitch. Fix the emission and the bloom threshold or the top of every frame is dead.  ·  No human figures exist anywhere in the tree. The hand is the single most important element and it must be built, photographed and composited, or drawn.  ·  `T1_ALPHA` has not been run since rev 62 and no ledger contains the string; every frame in out/ is colortype 2. The hand composite needs a real alpha, and `post.py` destroys alpha (measured: RGBA in, colortype 2 out), so the optics pass cannot be used on this route.

**SCREENER.** The sharpest single image here: at 0.95 m the brass nosing IS the horizon, and the picture divides into the reachable and the unreachable by geometry alone rather than by styling. The growth chart ruled with the bus's actual measured heights is the best product in all ten — it is the only idea that converts 76 revisions of measurement into an object a family uses. Real cost honestly named: no human figure exists in the tree and post.py destroys alpha, so the descending hand is genuinely awkward to composite.

---

## Touched — The bus rendered as nothing but the marks people left on it — every worn, polished, rubbed and tarnished place glowing on black, and the vehicle legible only from its own damage.  ·  screen 8.0  ·  `t0-2`

**THE IMAGE.** A BLACK SHEET. Out of it, in the exact silhouette and pose of the vehicle, only the WEAR is rendered. No body colour. No cream. No folk art. No wordmark. No glass. No outline. What you see is:

· the polished halo around the cab door handle where thumbs go;
· the brass counter nosing burnished bright along the ~1.8 m where plates and forearms cross it, going dull at both ends where nobody reaches;
· three bright bands on the aperture sills where cooks lean, one per bay, on the measured 625.0 / 645.0 mm centres;
· the rubbed patch low on the cab door where a knee goes in and out;
· the worn arch lips, front and rear;
· four kerb scuffs on four hubcap domes;
· the grime tide along the rocker, rising and falling with the road;
· the tarnish blooms on 'Señor', on the b-flag and ascender, and on the i-dot — the exact three places script_gen.py measured them;
· the polish on the engine-lid handle;
· the wear on the tail board's stay, where it is propped and unpropped every day of service.

Everything renders as a faint warm luminance on black — bone-white through amber, NEVER cyan-to-magenta, because the moment the palette goes scientific the piece becomes a diagram and the feeling dies. Think a long exposure of where hands have been, not a thermogram. No caption inside the image; full bleed, the shape running nearly edge to edge. One line of type at the very foot, small, Spanish first: 'Todo lo que ves es donde alguien puso la mano.' / 'Everything here is where someone put a hand.'

MOTION VERSION — and this is the better version. It ACCUMULATES. Open on black. The marks appear in the order a day of service makes them: the door handle first, then the sills as the cooks come on, then the nosing as plates start crossing it, then the rocker's tide as the street throws up at it. 20-30 seconds, no cuts, no camera move, one continuous build. The last frame is the whole vehicle in wear. Hold. Fade. Begin again.

And this is the ONLY motion vocabulary this object can honestly have: Tacombi's own account, graded S in SPEC §0.1, says 'The engine was scrapped, transmission sold'. It cannot drive. So nothing turns, nothing travels, and the only thing that moves is accumulation.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** This is the one that can make somebody's throat go tight.

Every other object in this category is about escape — the road, the sunset, the going. This one says a thing gets worn where it is loved, and here is the map. It reframes damage as a record of service: twenty years of people being fed, written into brass and paint.

And the mechanism is that every single mark is HUMAN CONTACT rather than weather. The bus's shape is being drawn by its customers. Somebody who ate at that counter, or leaned on that sill, or pushed that door handle, is literally in the picture — not represented, PRESENT, as the place where the metal went bright. That is a thing a photograph cannot say and a slogan cannot say, and it is why this concept exists.

It is also, quietly, an argument about the brand: we are not selling you a vehicle, we are showing you twenty years of hands.

**FORMATS.** THE HERO: a large print, black ground, A1 (594 x 841 mm) or bigger, landscape. On a soft-black uncoated or a matte-laminated heavyweight. This one WANTS scale and it wants dark — at small sizes the shape does not resolve and the whole trick fails. Below A2 do not make it.  ·  MOTION: 20-30 s, no audio dependency. 16:9 for the screen at the counter, 1:1 and 9:16 for social. It loops natively because the last frame fades to the first.  ·  THE COMMERCIAL DERIVATIVE, AND IT IS THE STRONGEST APPAREL ITEM IN ANY OF THESE THREE CONCEPTS: a tee or a tote carrying ONE mark on black and nothing else — the counter-nosing wear band alone, ~200 mm long, a single warm smear. No bus, no logo, no words. It passes the logo-removal test absolutely, because there was never a logo.  ·  SCREEN PRINT ON BLACK in a warm metallic or a glow ink — one plate, one pull. A metallic on black under a restaurant's warm lighting does physically what the image is about, which no CMYK reproduction can.  ·  Restaurant wall, large, behind the pass. This piece belongs in the building it is about.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** Nothing in the VW-bus market looks remotely like this, and — the stronger claim — nothing in that market COULD. Every trope in the category is a silhouette treatment: sunset silhouette, line art, blueprint, pop-art fill. This is the ANTI-silhouette, a shape defined by the absence of shape, assembled in the viewer's eye out of disconnected marks.

More importantly it cannot be made from a photograph at all. You cannot separate wear from paint in a JPEG — the two arrive in the same pixel. It requires a 3D asset with separable material passes and authored, causally-located wear. Which is to say: this is the first idea on the table that MAKES THE MODEL EARN ITS EXISTENCE. Seventy-five revisions have produced an asset whose every output so far is something a good photographer could have shot. This one is not.

And it contains no VW roundel, no VW body colour, and arguably no VW shape — only the marks Tacombi's own customers made on a vehicle Tacombi owns.

**PRODUCTION.** THIS IS THE RISKY ONE AND I AM NOT SANDING IT DOWN. A cautious team rejects it in the room: a food brand's hero image with no food, no bus, no colour, no logo and no legible product, made entirely of damage. Propose it anyway.

IT IS A PASS-COMPOSITE, DONE ALMOST ENTIRELY IN NUMPY, so it iterates in seconds rather than 5.5 minutes a look once the passes exist. It needs a wear mask as a renderable channel, an object/material ID pass to isolate brass from chrome from paint, and an AO/curvature pass. NONE of these exist — grep for pass_index / use_pass across the tree returns nothing outside the denoiser config.

COPY matte_tap's COLOUR CONTRACT VERBATIM on every File Output slot: color_management='OVERRIDE', view_transform='Raw', look 'None', gamma 1.0. Otherwise a slot inherits AgX/Punchy and every 'linear' pass comes back S-curved. matte_tap's docstring records exactly that trap, and probe_rev54_aov.py is the working precedent for driving an arbitrary internal shader socket to an output by graph walk.

WATCH THE KILL: render with every wear mask at zero and confirm the frame is IDENTICALLY black. matte_tap already did this class of negative control and got cover 0.0000 % against 0.267 mean alpha. A wear pass that is not watched failing is a glow of unknown origin.

16-BIT OR IT BANDS, AND THIS IS THE CONCEPT WHERE IT ACTUALLY BITES. A soft warm gradient falling into black across a metre of print, at 8 bits, will show contour rings — and studio.setup_render writes 16-bit while stitch.py and post.py both truncate silently through PIL. Nothing in the record names this as a print risk. Fix the read path or dither deliberately; do not discover it at the printer with plates cut.

STATE THE HONEST CEILING, IN THE PIECE IF IT CARRIES A CAPTION AND CERTAINLY TO THE OWNER: WHERE THE HANDS GO IS AN INFERENCE, NOT A SURVEY. I looked at ref_side.jpg at 3x — a customer's forearm is on the cab door sill and a cook in white is working the hatch, so the sill and the door are grounded. The counter nosing, the door handle and the engine-lid handle are common sense about a serving counter, not measurement. Nobody has ever photographed this bus's door handle close up, and PHOTOS_WANTED has never carried a touch-point item. 'It cannot be recovered from what we hold' is the true statement, and the ask that would close it is a set of close frames of the touch points — a ten-minute job for anyone standing next to the vehicle.

AND SAY WHAT IT IS NOT: it is not F18. F18's style is locked to 'cartoon with rendered depth' and its scene to 'nothing but the bus, die-cut tight, plus the sun and the papel picado', and its trigger — 'build it after the model is done' — has not fired. This is a different artefact and must be offered as one. F191 and F193 hold the DELIVERY RENDER; this is look-development and should be labelled that way, which is also honest, because 'get the bus ready' is precisely what authoring real wear would be doing.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** AN AUTHORED WEAR SYSTEM, WHICH DOES NOT EXIST, AND THIS IS THE BIG BUILD. Today weathering is ONE shared noise field — MOTTLE_AMP 0.55 plus the dust ramp plus the fade — applied everywhere at once, so the roof and the door handle wear identically. I cropped out/r75_side.png at the rocker and looked: there is no grime tide, no chip, no scuff, no handprint anywhere; the arch lip is as clean as the roof. TOUCHED needs wear with CAUSES: per-material, per-location masks keyed to contact, hand-painted in the same pipeline as swirl.png. This is also the fix the entire ARTIFACT territory needs, so it is not a cost of this concept — it is the reason to do this concept.  ·  THE CHIP GATE IS DEAD ON THE RED AND BOTH PROCEDURAL ROUTES ARE ALREADY REFUTED IN SOURCE (Pointiness is mesh-density-dependent; True Normal 'detects TESSELLATION, not folds' and was withdrawn). Stop trying to derive it. Author it. Same conclusion as Relic, reached from the other end.  ·  A PASS KIT — passes.py, ~80 lines. use_pass_object_index, use_pass_material_index, use_pass_normal, use_pass_position, use_pass_diffuse_color, AO, and cryptomatte. Drive pass_index off build.py's own ASSIGN list, which gives 17 stable material keys (paint, chrome, chrome_d, glass, tyre, rubber, dark, steel, bumpercream, countercream, capred, capwhite, lidmural, lidsign, roundelred, amber, bulb) so the ID islands map to design intent for free. Z and cryptomatte to OPEN_EXR_MULTILAYER.  ·  A WEAR AOV — the authored masks driven out through an Emission AOV so the composite can read them directly, rather than being inferred from the beauty. probe_rev54_aov.py is the precedent.  ·  T1_SENOR_TARNISH=1 for the three tarnish blooms. They are the only marks in the piece that are already measured to six figures, which is worth saying out loud: 'Señor' median (85,46,35) against clean silver (126,123,127), warm oxide at a* +14.0 against +6.9.  ·  THE BRASS NOSING NEEDS A LENGTHWISE WEAR PARAMETER — bright in the middle 1.8 m, dull at the ends. It is the single most important mark in the image and the model has no handle for it.  ·  16-BIT READ/WRITE THROUGH stitch.py AND post.py, or an explicit dither on export. On black-ground work this is not a nicety.  ·  IF THE MOTION VERSION IS MADE: prototype T1_SAVE (build.py's only persistence, and it appears in ZERO handoff documents). A wear accumulation is a mask animation, not a geometry animation, so it does not need keyframed hinges — but it does need one saved scene rather than a rebuild per frame, or the flicker floor eats it. And take the flicker seriously: the render is not run-to-run deterministic, ~2.04 % of pixels move >8 levels on hero34f and 7.90 % on the body, which on a sequence is a visible boil rather than a stills noise floor.

**SCREENER.** Highest emotional voltage and the only concept that makes the model earn its existence — you cannot separate wear from paint in a JPEG, so this is the first output a good photographer could not have shot. 'A thing gets worn where it is loved' is a real line and the accumulation film is the only honest motion vocabulary for a vehicle that cannot drive. Two things keep it off 9: the whole claim rests on inferred touch points ('where the hands go is an INFERENCE, not a survey' — it says so, which is to its credit, but the emotional payload is exactly the invented part), and the warm-on-black glow map sits one bad palette decision away from reading as a thermogram. Also the largest build: authored wear, a pass kit, and an AOV that grep finds nowhere in the tree.

---

## AS MEASURED — Six macro specimen plates of parts VW never made — each printed at a stated magnification and captioned with its own dimension AND its own certainty, so the sheet publishes its doubt alongside its beauty.  ·  screen 8.0  ·  `t7-0`

**THE IMAGE.** SIX SQUARE PLATES, 320 x 320 mm, subject dead centre on paper-white with a 40 mm margin. Light is a COPY STAND, not a studio: two large sources at plus/minus 45 degrees elevation, no visible key, no glamour, no environment. Everything sharp corner to corner — f/22 — and the caption prints the near/far limits so the sharpness is a stated fact rather than a look. No cast shadow; a 2 mm contact darkening only, so each object floats on the sheet like a pressed specimen.

PLATE 1 — THE BULB, 1:1. A single 22.0 mm festoon bulb reproduced at EXACTLY life size, alone, in the middle of a 320 mm sheet. 298 mm of empty paper around a thumbnail. This is the shock plate and it opens the set: the viewer's hand goes to it before their eye does.
PLATE 2 — THE BADGE, 6.9:1. The hubcap roundel filling the frame: cream V over W, the 8.08 mm ring band, the 6.0 mm plate, on the dome's red, the dome's curvature reading as one slow vertical gradient behind the glyph.
PLATE 3 — THE NOSING, 30:1. 19.9 mm of brass in elevation, a single horizontal band running edge to edge. Tan laminate above, cream below, one specular line along the crown of the roll. A Barnett Newman zip that happens to be a counter lip.
PLATE 4 — THE EDGE, 23:1. The tail board's 26 mm edge stack square-on: near-black band, cream band, red band. Three horizontal stripes and nothing else.
PLATE 5 — THE LUG, 18:1. One transverse tread groove, 6.0 mm deep, the crown falling away either side.
PLATE 6 — THE LOUVRE, 28:1. Two blades and the 7.0 mm aperture between them, the dark box receding behind.

PALETTE: paper white; the object's own colours; ONE caption ink (warm near-black); ONE hairline rule in brass. Nothing else on the sheet.
TYPOGRAPHY: 6 pt condensed caps, bottom-left, THREE LINES ONLY — WHAT IT IS / HOW BIG IT IS / HOW SURE WE ARE. Examples, verbatim from source: "TAIL BOARD EDGE · 26.0 mm · BOARD THICKNESS NOT MEASURED"; "TREAD LUG · 64 PER REVOLUTION · DECLARED — THE MEASUREMENT ADMITS 48 TO 84"; "COUNTER NOSING · 19.9 mm IN ELEVATION · MEASURED, 113 COLUMNS · PROJECTION 7.0 mm INFERRED"; "HUBCAP BADGE · 86.9 mm · PROPORTION NEVER COMPARED TO A PHOTOGRAPH". The third line is the entire idea.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** BEING TRUSTED. The pleasure of a museum label written by someone who declined to bluff. Every other object in a shop tells you it is perfect; this one hands you a beautiful thing and, in six-point type, tells you exactly which part of it nobody has checked. Reading "22.0 mm · NOT MEASURED" under a lovely object produces a specific and rare feeling — you are being addressed as an adult. It also converts seventy-five revisions of stubborn honesty into the product's actual content, so the thing that made this project slow becomes the thing that makes it unbuyable elsewhere.

**FORMATS.** SET OF SIX PLATES, 320 x 320 mm, matte uncoated 300 gsm, litho or giclée. Uncoated and matte because a specimen sheet must not glare — the object supplies all the gloss in the picture and the paper must supply none.  ·  MASTER SHEET, 700 x 1000 mm, all six in a 3x2 grid with one shared scale bar serving the whole sheet. This is the wall piece; the jump between 1:1 and 30:1 across one sheet is the experience. DO NOT PROMISE THIS UNTIL THE STITCH CHAIN IS PROVEN ABOVE 3840 px (see production notes).  ·  POSTCARD / BOOKMARK SET, 105 x 148 mm, same six plates, same captions at 4.5 pt. The cheap tier, and the one that goes in a pocket at the counter. Caption legibility must be proof-printed, not assumed.  ·  WEB SCROLL — one page, six plates, the magnification ratio counting up as you scroll from 1:1 to 30:1. Free, and it is the argument for the paper.  ·  NOT APPAREL. The caption is the concept and 6 pt type does not survive a screen or a needle. Anything that drops the third line is a different, worse idea.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** The VW-bus merch ocean sells THE WHOLE VEHICLE — sunset silhouette, surf, palms, minimal line art, blueprint elevation. Every single one of those is a picture of a van. THERE IS NO VAN IN THIS. The nearest trope is the blueprint, which is a standing stock category sold by the sheet, and the difference is exactly the one that matters: a stock VW blueprint annotates the FACTORY object, drawn from a manual anyone can buy, and every dimension on it belongs to Volkswagen. Every subject here belongs to the CONVERSION — a brass counter lip, a tail-board edge, a festoon bulb, a louvre — parts VW never pressed and no catalogue holds. It is also the only merchandise I know of that PRINTS ITS OWN UNCERTAINTY. An advert cannot do that; a stock asset has no uncertainty to print because nobody measured anything.

**PRODUCTION.** MAGNIFICATION LADDER, computed from live source constants, at a 600 mm print with the subject filling the frame: bulb 22.0 mm = 27.3:1; counter nosing gold 19.9 mm = 30.2:1; louvre pitch 21.1 mm = 28.4:1; tail-board edge stack 26.0 mm = 23.1:1; hubcap badge 86.9 mm = 6.9:1; hubcap 274.0 mm = 2.2:1.

RESOLUTION IS FINE; THE CAMERA IS NOT. A 320 mm plate at 300 dpi is 3780 px, INSIDE the 3840 x 2640 the MARGIN chain was proven at (rev 57, one view, 106.8 min). The 700 x 1000 mm master sheet needs ~8270 px and is 2.15x above anything ever stitched — that one needs a proving run and stitch.py's exit code READ, not assumed.

EVERY CAPTION FIGURE MUST BE READ FROM SOURCE AT BUILD TIME, NEVER TYPED. A plate whose dimension went stale is worse than no plate, and it would be the exact failure this whole set is about. The grade line likewise: pull it from the constant's own comment or from OPEN_FINDINGS, do not paraphrase.

Substrate: uncoated, matte, warm-white rather than blue-white — the cream and brass both die against a cool white. Caption ink warm near-black, never 100% K.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** A MACRO CAMERA CLASS. This is the whole blocker and it is not a resolution problem — it is a camera problem, which is much better news. `studio.views()` has no macro entry; the nearest is `detail_f` at loc (4.90, 2.15, 1.85) to tgt (1.95, 0.05, 1.16), a ~3.5 m standoff. And nothing can be cropped out of what exists: I measured the subject bbox in out/r75_side.png at x 212-1457 (1245 px on a 1600 px frame), which puts a 22.0 mm bulb at 5-7 px and the 86.9 mm badge at 24-27 px. Build a `macro_*` family: 100-200 mm lens, 0.15-0.5 m standoff, one per plate.  ·  USE `aim()`'s EXISTING DOF REPORTING AS THE CAPTION'S OWN HONESTY. It already reads `T1_FSTOP`, sets `aperture_blades = 9` and `aperture_rotation = radians(11)`, and `dof_limits()` returns near/far/hyperfocal. Set f/16-f/22 for the all-sharp specimen look and PRINT the returned limits in the caption. Nothing in this project has ever used this machinery.  ·  FIX THE HUBCAP BADGE'S BLACK RELIEF HALO BEFORE PLATE 2. I cropped out/r75_side.png at the rear hubcap at 6x and looked: every stroke of the render's badge carries a heavy dark self-shadow and the glyph reads as a dark blob, where ref_side.jpg at 6x shows clean cream strokes on red with no dark at all. Cause is almost certainly `CAP_EMBLEM_DEPTH = 0.0060` — a 6.0 mm plate on an 86.9 mm badge — plus the `capwhite` albedo. Ablate the depth and look before tuning anything.  ·  THE MOTTLE / DENOISER SPECKLE IS THE PICTURE AT 6.9:1. I looked at out/r75_hero34r.png at 2x: the body red carries visible dark pepper across the whole panel. `MOTTLE_AMP` defaults to 0.55 via `T1_MOT_AMP`. At specimen magnification that speckle stops being texture and becomes dirt on the plate. Render the ladder and choose deliberately.  ·  PLATE 2 IS THE ONE THAT COULD BE WRONG AND THE RECORD SAYS SO. STATE.md: "hubcap badge is SELF-CONSISTENCY ONLY -- CAP_EMBLEM_WFRAC has never been compared to a frame; see PHOTOS_WANTED item 7." Printing a proportion nothing has ever checked at 6.9:1 is the wrong direction. Either close item 7 (ONE HUBCAP, SQUARE ON AND CLOSE — still open) or let the caption's third line carry it, which is the honest and interesting option.  ·  EXPORT `T1_FX=0` BY HAND FOR ANY STITCHED PLATE. `grep -c T1_FX hq_render.py` returns 0, while hero.py — the chain it replaced — forces it off with the docstring "Per-strip optics band at the seams." And pass `--backdrop white` explicitly: post.py's live constant is `BACKDROP = "headroom"` with peak 252, so the default drops a 252-grey field inside a white margin.

**SCREENER.** The third caption line — printing the object's own uncertainty beside its dimension — is genuinely unseen on merchandise, and it converts 75 revisions of stubbornness into the product's actual content, which is the rarest kind of alignment available here. Plate 1 (a 22 mm bulb life-size on a 320 mm sheet) is a real physical shock. Held below the top because 'museum specimen plate' is an established genre and 'being trusted' is a designer's feeling more than a customer's; the audience for a 6 pt provenance grade is narrow. Honest that the macro camera class does not exist.

---

## ABIERTO — Two inks, a coarse form-following halftone, and the only light in the picture is the bulb line and three lit holes — the bus as the thing you spot across a plaza when you are hungry and it is late.  ·  screen 8.0  ·  `t8-2`

**THE IMAGE.** The vehicle three-quarter from the serving side, at DISTANCE, from a standing customer's eye height of 1.55 m — which is the reference photograph's own derived camera height (`REF_MEASUREMENTS.md`: 'a pinhole model with camera height ≈ 1.55 m, horizon y ≈ 340'), not the 3.55 m the shipped hero sits at. The vehicle occupies roughly 45% of the frame width and sits LOW in the composition, with a great deal of dark above it. No landscape. No sky. No ground plane you can name.

TWO INKS: a deep near-black warm (≈ #1A1210) and one warm light (a cream-gold, ≈ #E8C87A), on natural uncoated stock that carries the mid-tone.

The tonal structure is brutal. The body is almost entirely the dark ink. The ONLY light in the picture is:
— the FESTOON BULB LINE, tracing the drip rail nose to tail, wrapping the rear roof dome, and running the free edge of the propped tail board. One continuous stroke of light describing the whole silhouette in a single gesture.
— the THREE SERVING APERTURES, glowing, each 515.5 × 403.0 mm with its 55 mm corner radius, the bobble fringe reading as a scalloped rim of light, and the galley's shapes inside them as dark cut-outs against the glow.
— the COUNTER'S TOP SURFACE catching that glow as one hard horizontal band — the strongest line in the composition.
— the MURAL LID lit from below by the apertures, so its rosettes read as a faint patterned field rather than as a graphic.

EVERYTHING IS HALFTONE. No smooth gradient anywhere. And here is the trick that makes it geometry rather than a filter: THE SCREEN ANGLE FOLLOWS THE SURFACE NORMAL. The dot grid wraps the body's curvature like a mesh stocking — the roof's screen runs one way, the flank's another, the wheel arch's dots curve round it. On the flat counter slab the screen is dead straight, which is what makes the counter read as the only flat thing in the picture. A halftone taken off a photograph cannot do this; it needs a normal pass.

Dot spec: 34 lpi at A1 (a 0.75 mm cell), 55 lpi at A3. Coarse on purpose, so the dot is a visible object at one metre and dissolves into tone at four.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** You are hungry, it is late, and you see the lights are on. That is the whole payload, and it is the most primitive good feeling a food brand can own. The bus is not being presented to you as a product — it is being presented as a destination you have just spotted, at the distance from which you spot it. Everything about the composition serves that: low in frame, far away, dark above, and the only information is where the light is.

The coarse halftone does the second half of the emotional work in a way a clean render structurally cannot. Big visible dots read as distance, as memory, as a printed thing — as something that already happened to you rather than something being sold to you now.

**FORMATS.** A1, 594 × 841 mm, two-ink screen print on natural 300 gsm. The flagship, and it wants to be big: at 34 lpi the dot is a texture you walk up to and then lose. This is a wall piece, not a poster.  ·  A3, 297 × 420 mm, at 55 lpi for the shop. Re-screen for the size; do not scale the A1 file.  ·  305 × 305 mm album format, cropped to the aperture band alone — three lit rectangles, a counter line, and dark. Almost abstract, and the strongest thing in the set at small size.  ·  RESTAURANT APPLICATION, and this is where it earns its keep: the same image at 1.2 × 1.7 m as a two-colour wall vinyl or a hand-painted mural behind the counter, halftone scaled to a 3 mm dot so it reads from the street. Two colours is within a signwriter's reach.  ·  Window decal derivative, single ink: the BULB LINE ALONE, gold on clear vinyl, 900 mm wide. One continuous stroke that describes the entire vehicle — it works at 900 mm and it works at 32 px, which makes it the natural logo-scale device and the natural favicon.  ·  Motion derivative, if and when animation is authorised: the same frame with the bulb line igniting nose to tail. It is a single path, so it animates for free. Flagged only — there is NO owner ruling on animation anywhere in the record.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** The market's night VW is a campfire silhouette against a starfield — a vehicle parked in wilderness, meaning escape. This has no landscape, no sky, no horizon, no stars, and no vehicle-as-freedom. It is a BUILDING WITH ITS LIGHTS ON, seen across a distance, in two inks. That is a completely different proposition from the same body shape, and it is the proposition Tacombi's own founding account supports: the engine was scrapped and the transmission sold, so this thing does not go anywhere and never will.

The form-following screen is the technical differentiator and it is not decoration: no halftone derived from a photograph can rotate its screen with the surface, because a photograph has no normals. Anyone can see that this was drawn from an object that was modelled, which is the only honest reason for this project to exist in the first place.

Honest flag: a night piece brushes against territory the record parks. F92 holds the Playa hero as 'DEPRIORITISED, NOT CANCELLED' and licenses nothing to do now — but its object is a photoreal environment DELIVERY RENDER via `playa_env.py`, not an illustrated two-ink print, and F57 records that the in-tree playa path renders no vehicle at all. Meanwhile F259 explicitly opens the lighting rig: 'Relight the studio and fix the ground if necessary', with the single constraint 'DO NOT change RED' — which this concept honours by having no red ink in it. State that distinction and put it to him rather than assuming it.

**PRODUCTION.** BUILD THE SEPARATION FROM A LINEAR LUMINANCE, NOT FROM THE TONE-MAPPED FRAME. Read the 16-bit master through `photometry.read_png`, never PIL — PIL has no 16-bit RGB path and truncates silently (F263 / rule 58), and the whole downstream chain, `stitch.py` and `post.py` alike, already throws the low byte away at its first line. An 8-bit source will band across the very large dark body areas, and a halftone AMPLIFIES banding rather than hiding it. This is the single most likely way this concept fails at the printer.

SCREEN ANGLES AND MOIRÉ. Run the light ink at 45° base and the dark at 15°, with the normal-driven rotation on top. Then check against the two regular repeats already on the vehicle: the louvre block at `LOUV_PITCH = 0.021111` m (21.1 mm) and the tyre tread at π × 0.6649 / 64 = 32.6 mm. At A1 those land at roughly 3.1 mm and 4.8 mm on the sheet — four to six halftone cells, squarely in the moiré band. PROOF AT FINAL SIZE ON THE ACTUAL STOCK; moiré cannot be judged on screen. If the louvres beat, break the screen locally or crop them into shadow.

Trap the light ink 0.15 mm into the dark. And do NOT let the bulbs resolve into discrete dots — they must merge into a continuous line, which means the emissive has to bloom BEFORE the screen is applied, not after.

Avoid a close tyre crop in any large format. `TREAD_LUGS = 64` is carried in the source as 'DECLARED, inside the MEASURED bracket 48..84', and `probe_rev74_tread`'s T2 refuses to publish a count at all — six estimates spanning 48 to 84. At A1 the lug pitch is about 4.8 mm and countable. In this composition the wheels are in shadow, which resolves it for free; keep them there.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** THE BULBS MUST BECOME LIGHTS. `OPEN_FINDINGS` F144 stands: 'THE FESTOON BULBS READ AS PEARLS, NOT LIGHTS.' The whole concept rests on this one element. The emitters exist (STATE.md: `bulb_string | 1`, `tb_bulbs | 1`, `tb_bulbflex | 1`), `t1_mats` has an `emissive(name, colour, strength=8.0, …)` builder, and `T1_BULB_STR` / `T1_BULB_BASEV` are already ablation switches — so this is lookdev, not modelling. Push the emission above the bloom threshold and drop the key.  ·  THE INTERIOR MUST BE LIT, AND THIS IS THE REAL RISK — RENDER ONE FRAME AND LOOK BEFORE COMMITTING. The composition depends entirely on the three apertures glowing with legible galley shapes inside them. The galley is built (roughly thirty `gal_*` meshes plus `plancha`, `galley_top`, `galley_backdrop`), but F45 (roof-aperture interior, 2.91e5 px²) and F15 (unlit roofed body, 6.92e5 px², item 4 of 16 in `visibility_budget`) are BOTH ungated and have never been measured against anything. Whether the bays render as anything but dark holes at low key is unknown. This is the go/no-go test and it costs one frame.  ·  A CAMERA AT 1.55 m THAT DOES NOT EXIST. `hero34f` sits at 3.55 m; `hero34r` has ONE rendered-file mention across ten revisions and it clips — I measured `out/r75_hero34r.png` and the subject's bounding box reaches x = 1599 of 1600, so the counter, the object's strongest horizontal, runs off the right edge. New view dict entry, then check the framing by looking at it before designing to it.  ·  A NORMAL PASS (True Normal, per `probe_rev55_truenorm.py`) to drive the screen rotation, plus a linear luminance pass. No `use_pass_*` exists anywhere in the tree. Copy `matte_tap`'s colour contract verbatim — `color_management='OVERRIDE'`, `view_transform='Raw'`, look 'None', gamma 1.0 — or the 'linear' pass arrives S-curved and every dot size is wrong.  ·  A 16-BIT READ PATH, or at minimum a stated triangular dither at export, before any A1 goes to plate. `studio.setup_render` writes `color_depth = '16'` and both `stitch.py` and `post.py` discard it silently through PIL.  ·  LARGE-FORMAT PROVING. Nothing above 1600 × 1100 exists on disk; the MARGIN chain has been proven once, at 3840 × 2640, at rev 57, and that frame is gone. A1 at 200 dpi wants 4677 px — 22% above proven. Also export `T1_FX=0` BY HAND: `grep -c T1_FX hq_render.py` returns 0, while `hero.py`, the chain it replaced, forces it off with the docstring 'Per-strip optics band at the seams.' And read `stitch.py`'s exit code, not the redirect's.  ·  NOT REQUIRED: the emblem (the nose is turned away and in shadow — so this concept is not gated by F191/F234), the red decision (there is no red ink), the gloss deficit (a halftone has no specular), the cream's mottle (it is under the dark ink).

**SCREENER.** Strongest single payload in the brief — hungry, late, the lights are on — and that IS a food brand's whole business. The form-following halftone is a genuine technical differentiator with the same normals argument as the loteria. The window-decal derivative (bulb line alone as one continuous stroke, working at 900 mm and at 32 px) is probably the most commercially valuable single item anywhere in this set and doubles as the logo-scale device. Marked down because two-ink night-poster-of-a-lit-place is a known register, and it correctly flags that the lit interior is an unmeasured go/no-go (F45 and F15 both ungated).

---

## ¿QUÉ HAY ADENTRO? — The Cutaway — The near flank removed and the whole vehicle drawn as a doll's house in section — cab, galley, counter, tail board, everything at once, everything at the same scale, everything equally loved.  ·  screen 8.0  ·  `t9-1`

**THE IMAGE.** FRAMING: true orthographic side elevation with the +Y skin gone. No perspective anywhere. Left to right the drawing reads as four rooms:

THE CAB — dash, dash_grille, dash_glovebox, dash_knob, speedo_bezel / speedo_face / speedo_glass, the steering wheel (wheel_rim, wheel_hub, wheel_horn, wheel_spoke ×2), gear_lever + gear_knob, pedal_a/b/c, seat_base / seat_back / seat_welt and their mirrored pair, mirror_int, cab_floor. Every one is in STATE.md's object inventory. A cap on the passenger seat. Nobody driving, because nobody can.

THE GALLEY — STATE.md carries 32 `gal_*` rows: gal_hook ×6, gal_bot ×4, gal_botcap ×4, gal_band ×3, gal_can_u ×3, gal_caddy ×2, gal_can_l ×2, gal_rack_up ×2, gal_stack ×2, gal_tool ×2, plus gal_worktop, gal_warmer, gal_warmer_tap, gal_appliance, gal_sqbottle, gal_pale, gal_wrap, gal_upright, gal_rail, gal_shelf_b, gal_rack_hi/lo/shelf — and `plancha`, `galley_top`, `galley_backdrop`. Bottles on shelves, tools on hooks, cans stacked. This is the density that makes the drawing worth a second Tuesday.

THE COUNTER — counter, counter_nosing (the brass lip), counter_top, cantilevered out past the body (STATE.md: 'length excludes opened lids: 4.317 with them, 4.065 without').

THE TAIL — the propped board with its bulb row (tail_board, tail_board_stay, tb_bulbs, tb_edge_red, tb_edge_dark).

COMPOSITION: the three serving apertures are three windows onto the action, and their MEASURED rhythm is the grid — each 515.5 × 403.0 mm (t1_shell.py `BAY_W = 0.5155`, `Z_SILL, Z_HEAD, BAY_R = 1.3720, 1.7750, 0.0550`), corner radius 55.0 mm, on centres `BAY_CX = (0.6720, 0.0470, -0.5980)`. So the pitches are 625.0 and 645.0 mm: the BAYS ARE EQUAL AND THE PILLARS ARE NOT — 109.5 mm and 129.5 mm. Draw that asymmetry. It is the whole difference between an observed drawing and a styled one, and a child will not know why it looks true.

POPULATION: a cook at the plancha, a second person reaching for a hook, a child on tiptoe at the middle hatch showing only fingertips and forehead (the sill is 1.307 m above ground and a five-year-old's eye is around 0.95–1.00 m — the child physically cannot see over it), a dog under the counter, the cat back on the tyre, a bird on the drip rail. Twelve things to find, listed along the bottom of the placemat version.

LINE AND COLOUR: flat colour, all line in lid_gen.py's `DARKLINE (72, 26, 16)`, one weight throughout — the gear knob gets exactly the same line as the mural. Tiny hand-lettered Spanish labels on hairline leaders: *la plancha · el mostrador · las bombillas · el techo pintado*. English beneath at half size, or not at all.

LIGHT: flat, even, picture-book noon. No hero light, no drama, no rim. The wonder comes from DENSITY, not illumination — and a flat scheme is also the only honest one, because this interior has never been lit.

PALETTE: this is the one concept that uses everything — red sRGB(196,49,36), cream sRGB(206,208,200), gold, `COUNTERTAN = (0.5870, 0.4930, 0.3060)  # = sRGB (201,186,150)` for the brass and nosing, plus a family of steel greys for the galley and the amber of the bulbs.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** The pleasure of the cross-section — a child's first realisation that things have INSIDES. Ladybird cutaways, Richard Scarry, an ant farm, the doll's house with the front wall off. It does not ask to be admired; it asks to be searched. The specific joy is finding the eleventh thing after you thought you had found everything, and it renews: a poster you have looked at a hundred times still has a bottle you never noticed. That is the mechanism by which an object becomes 'something families keep' — not sentiment, but inexhaustibility.

**FORMATS.** Search-and-find poster, 700 × 500 mm, matte uncoated, with a 'find these 12 things' strip along the foot  ·  WIPE-CLEAN PAPER PLACEMAT, A3, at the restaurant — this is the one that actually gets a child through a meal, and it is the format the owner's audience clause was written for  ·  500-piece jigsaw — statistically the format most likely to become a family object rather than a purchase  ·  Fold-out concertina card, 6 panels, so the bus unfolds in the hand  ·  Cotton panel: tea towel, or a cot-sized wall hanging  ·  Long-thin web/social crop: the three apertures alone as a triptych banner

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** Nobody else has the inside. Every VW-bus print in the market is exterior-only, because no stock asset models a galley — and this repository holds 32 `gal_*` inventory rows, a complete cab, a plancha and three menu cards, and HAS NEVER SHOWN ANY OF IT TO ANYONE. The oldest live row in the register is F14, gal_end_f's sight lines, graded INHERITED-rev52 and un-re-measured for more than twenty revisions. The single thing this project is uniquely rich in is the single thing it has never rendered. It also sidesteps the category's whole visual language by having no silhouette move at all: no sunset, no palm, no road, and — crucially — no nose, so the VW roundel the owner has now rejected nine times (F191 and F234 both stand) never enters the frame, and neither does the most trademark-exposed element on the object.

**PRODUCTION.** UNCOATED STOCK, ALWAYS. A dense flat-colour line drawing dies on gloss.

HAND-LETTER THE LABELS IN SPANISH FIRST. The vehicle's own lettering hierarchy already models this — the Nolita counter fascia carries '267 / ELIZABETH STREET / NUEVA YORK' in small condensed caps above 'Gracias por su Preferencia.' in red cursive. Spanish large, English small.

DRAW FROM THE PHOTOGRAPHS AT MAGNIFICATION, NOT FROM THE FLANK TEXTURE. folk_gen.py's own opening says why: 'Coverage is a MARGINAL; a generator can match it exactly and still be wrong, because the same 10 % gold over the same flank can be one bouquet or five hundred commas.' And its component census disagrees with the shipped tile — the photograph reads 66 gold components with the top three carrying 84.4 % (`COMP_TOP = {1: 47.8, 2: 73.7, 3: 84.4, ...}`), which is the read to reproduce.

THE SACRIFICE RULE IS MEASURED AND IT APPLIES HERE. `COMP_HIST`'s first three bins are (24, 0.42 %), (21, 1.32 %), (3, 0.55 %) — 48 of 66 gold components carry 2.29 % of the ink. On a placemat or a jigsaw, delete all 48 and lose nothing. AUDIT_rev43.md states it as a sticker rule; it is a general licence to simplify, and I reproduced the arithmetic from folk_gen.py's live constants rather than trusting the audit row.

DO NOT MIX LIVERY STATES. Folk art belongs to the Playa state; the chalkboard lid and the painted glass lettering belong to Nolita. CLAUDE.md rule 11 and SPEC §7.1 forbid carrying artwork across states, and the project has already killed three findings (F99, F100, F140) on exactly this. A cutaway that blends them is a fake and a customer who has eaten at Nolita will see it.

THIS IS NOT F18 — F18's scene is locked to 'nothing but the bus'. This drawing is full of people, animals and labels. Different artefact; say so.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** **THE INTERIOR HAS NEVER BEEN LIT OR LOOKED AT.** F45 (roof-aperture interior, 2.91e5 px², item 6 of 16 in visibility_budget and dead centre of the hero frame) and F15 (unlit roofed body, 6.92e5 px²) are BOTH UNGATED — never measured against a photograph, no instrument on either. Render one `side` frame, look through the three bays, and report what is actually visible before a line is drawn. The galley may render dead.  ·  **There is no cutaway and it is not a camera move.** The +Y flank is a shell with three holes in it. Cheapest honest route: a material override making the near skin transmissive while keeping the aperture surrounds, the drip rail and the bobble fringe OPAQUE — the fringe is what tells the eye where the missing wall was (`FRINGE_R, FRINGE_PITCH, FRINGE_INSET = 0.0090, 0.0260, 0.0130`, and the source comment records '65 fringe balls per aperture are one object, not 65').  ·  F14 stands and it is the oldest live row in the register: gal_end_f's sight lines, INHERITED-rev52, never re-measured. Whether the galley reads correctly THROUGH the apertures is precisely the open question this concept depends on.  ·  No people, no animals, no steam. All drawn on top.  ·  **deliver.py has no sub-crop concept** — every output is the whole vehicle. Adding named presets (cab / galley / counter / tail) would let each room be drawn from its own frame at its own scale instead of one designer hand-cutting boxes. It already writes four subdirectories `("full_frame", "trimmed", "layers", "web")`, which is the place to hang them.  ·  **No 3D export path exists.** `grep` for export_scene / gltf / usd_export / fbx across the tree returns nothing; the only persistence is build.py's `if os.environ.get("T1_SAVE"): bpy.ops.wm.save_as_mainfile(...)`, and T1_SAVE appears in ZERO handoff documents. An illustrator cannot be handed this interior — only PNGs of it. This is the single largest structural blocker on any illustration programme.  ·  **tex/senor.png has no dark keyline.** SPEC §3 requires the wordmark 'SILVER with a dark keyline and drop shadow'; measured on the shipped master, among pixels with alpha > 200 the 1st-percentile luma is 168.3 and the fraction below luma 80 is 0.0000 — there is no dark ink in the asset at all. lid_gen.py already authors the right colour for it (`DARKLINE = (72, 26, 16)`). script_gen.py is NOT called by build.py, so regenerate by hand.

**SCREENER.** Uses the single most underexploited asset in the repository: 32 gal_* objects, a full cab, a plancha, all built and NEVER SHOWN TO ANYONE, with F14 inherited since rev 52. The observed detail carries it — bays equal at 515.5, pillars NOT (109.5 vs 129.5 mm) — which is the whole difference between a drawn cutaway and a styled one. Placemat/jigsaw/search-and-find is the correct ladder for the owner's own 'something families keep'. Formally the least original of the set (Scarry/Biesty is a well-worn genre), and it rests entirely on an interior nobody has ever lit.
