**SCREENER.** Inverting the viewer from in-front-of to underneath is the sharpest positional idea here and the clipped-daylight strip that makes print read as SHADE is a real designer's move; the packaging-lid interior is free brand equity — but strip the vehicle out and a folk-floral scarf is a hundred-times-seen object, and it ships a known defect at a metre wide until F74(a)'s crop is fixed in lid_gen.py, which build.py does not call.

---

## Sold — A parts plate of everything Tacombi TOOK OUT — engine scrapped, transmission sold, seats replaced, three panes already missing — with the surviving bus reduced to a ghost hairline behind them. The category's only anti-restoration piece.  ·  screen 8.0  ·  `free4-1`

**THE IMAGE.** A technical parts plate, flat, two inks, no render visible in the final. BACKGROUND: the bus as a 1:20 orthographic side elevation drawn in a SINGLE HAIRLINE of brand red at ~10% visual weight — present, ghostly, almost paper. FOREGROUND, solid and full-strength in warm near-black, laid out on a strict catalogue grid with thin leader lines running back to where each came from: the flat-four engine, the transmission, the front bench seat, the middle bench, and THREE PANES OF GLASS drawn as three empty rounded rectangles. Each carries a caption in small condensed caps — a DISPOSITION, not a part number: ENGINE — SCRAPPED. TRANSMISSION — SOLD. SEATS — REPLACED WITH COOKING EQUIPMENT. GLASS x3 — ALREADY MISSING. ROOF — CUT AND REELED BACK. At the foot, same weight as everything else: PURCHASE PRICE $3,000. MEXICO CITY. And one last line, the only sentence on the sheet with a person in it: SERVING SINCE 6 FEBRUARY 2006. THE DOUBLE INVERSION: (1) the REMOVED parts are printed solid and heavy while the surviving vehicle is a ghost — absence rendered as presence. (2) The three 'missing' panes are drawn at exactly the serving-aperture proportion, 515.5 x 403.0 mm at 55.0 mm radius — so the missing-parts diagram accidentally draws the brand's own three-hatch mark, and only a reader who has looked hard finds it. PALETTE: warm near-black + brand red on uncoated cream or manila. No light, no shading, no gradient. Nothing on this sheet is photographed.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** A small, cold thrill, followed by warmth. It is a death certificate for a beloved object, set with total composure, and out the other side of it a restaurant exists. It says: we killed a classic on purpose, we would do it again, here is the receipt, and now come and eat. Nobody in this category has the nerve to print the word SOLD next to a transmission. That nerve is the whole emotional payload — the reader feels let in on something the brand could have hidden and chose not to.

**FORMATS.** POSTER, ISO A2 (420 x 594) and A1 (594 x 841), two-colour screen print on uncoated 250 gsm. CRITICAL SUBSTRATE LOGIC: because the elevation is a VECTOR hairline rather than a photoreal render, this format escapes the project's resolution ceiling entirely — nothing above 1600x1100 exists on disk, and the stitch chain has been proven exactly once, at 3840x2640 (rev 57, 106.8 min, worst seam z=1.62). A1 at 200 dpi would want 4677 px, 22% beyond proven. A vector plate does not care.  ·  TEE, one colour dark on natural, chest 280 mm — THE PARTS ONLY, no bus at all. A shirt covered in the components of a vehicle that isn't there.  ·  HANG-TAG SERIES, 60 x 100 mm, one removed part per tag, five tags across the range. Collect them. Free brand equity attached to goods people already bought.  ·  PLACEMAT REVERSE / BACK OF MENU — the plate at 300 x 220 mm, read while waiting.  ·  SITE HOARDING for a new opening, 3 m wide, vector, unlimited scale. 'This is what we are about to do to a bus.'

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** The entire VW-bus category — sunset, surf, restoration, blueprint, 'keep it original', officially licensed nostalgia — is a RESTORATION culture. Every image in it sells the vehicle intact and going somewhere. This one sells the vehicle gutted and staying put. It is the only piece in this whole program that a VW enthusiast would actively dislike, and that reaction is the proof it is not stock. It also inverts the blueprint trope rather than joining it: a stock blueprint annotates what a VW IS; this annotates what this one no longer HAS. And every caption on the sheet is the owner's own published sentence, graded S in SPEC.md §0.1 — 'The engine was scrapped, transmission sold, seats swapped for cooking equipment, and the roof was reeled back' — so it cannot be argued with.

**PRODUCTION.** THIS IS THE RISKY ONE AND HERE IS THE RISK, UNSANDED. (a) A purist reads it as vandalism of a '63 splitscreen. That is the intended cost. (b) It DRAWS VOLKSWAGEN-MADE COMPONENTS AND CAPTIONS THEM, which is a HEAVIER trademark posture, not a lighter one, and it must go past a lawyer before it goes near a press — this is not legal advice. My design-side mitigations, which also make the piece better: draw every part as a generic period component with NO VW marks, no part numbers, no roundel anywhere on the sheet, and never name the manufacturer in copy. The brand's own account never names VW either; it says 'a 1963 combi bus.' (c) It is not warm, and this brand is warm — which is why the last line at the foot must stay, and must be the only sentence with a date and a person in it. CONTRADICTS PRIOR ART, STATED PLAINLY: this sits hard against the shop's deadpan-leisure voice, and it draws objects that are not on the vehicle and have never been photographed, which is against the spirit of SPEC §10.10's replication bar. §10.10's OBJECT is the model's own decals and this is a derived artefact, so it is not bound by it — but say so in writing rather than letting someone discover the tension later. DO NOT print a year taken from the '1963' number plate: SPEC records that `PLATE_W = 0.3300` has no provenance anywhere in the repo, and SPEC's own §0.1 carries an unresolved tension between the buy date and the 6 February 2006 opening. Confirm the date with the owner before it goes on paper.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** NONE OF THE REMOVED PARTS EXIST IN THE MODEL, AND THAT IS CORRECT — they were removed in 2006. STATE.md's 229-object inventory carries `englid_handle` and a `gap_englid` shut line but no engine, no gearbox, no bench. So they must be DRAWN FROM FACTORY LITERATURE, and that route is explicitly open by owner ruling: F229 / rule 52, 'This has to be a commonly available measurement', with four sources named by name in HANDOFF_CARRIERS §0.06 (thesamba's split-bus dimensions pages, coolairvw's bumper guide). All four were EGRESS_BLOCKED in two consecutive containers — retry, the policy is per-environment, and do not budget the piece on it. THIS IS THE ELEGANT PART: the parts we must source from the catalogue are exactly the parts that were taken out. The model holds the conversion; the catalogue holds the loss.  ·  THE THREE PANES NEED NO MODEL WORK AT ALL — the whole sub-diagram is four constants from t1_shell.py: `BAY_W = 0.5155`, `BAY_R = 0.0550`, `Z_SILL, Z_HEAD = 1.3720, 1.7750`, `BAY_CX = (0.6720, 0.0470, -0.5980)`.  ·  THE GHOST ELEVATION MUST BE VECTOR, AND THERE IS NO VECTOR PATH IN THIS TREE. The only .svg committed is `vw_canonical_2019.svg`, which F168 records as a DIFFERENT OBJECT from the 1955-67 pressing. Route: the `side` ortho camera (lens=None, ortho=5.90 — the best-instrumented camera in the tree, 27 rendered-file mentions across rev 66-75) through a Grease Pencil Line Art modifier or a Freestyle pass, then SVG export. The mesh is clean enough to trust a line renderer: 0 non-manifold body edges, 229,489 quads against 1,040 tris.  ·  CHECK THE ORTHO FRAMING FIRST. The front/rear orthos are ortho=3.55 with horizontal sensor fit, covering only z 0.2997..2.7403 at 1600x1100, while the vehicle spans z 0 to STATE.md's `bbox top 3.132` — both ends clip. `side` at 5.90 is the safe one; verify before tracing.  ·  NO EMBLEM ANYWHERE ON THIS SHEET. That is a design decision, not an omission, and it happens to route around the owner's ninth report (probe_rev69_fitpose P2 0.8528 against P1b's 0.9465 ceiling; probe_rev46_vw C8 'the built cells are 1.19x TOO ROUND') and around the standing delivery hold, F191 and F193.

**SCREENER.** Anti-restoration is the only position in this category nobody occupies, and the missing panes accidentally drawing the brand's own three-hatch mark at BAY_W 0.5155 / BAY_R 0.0550 is the kind of buried detail that earns a second look; fatal risk is that the hero content is not in the model and must come from catalogue sources that were EGRESS_BLOCKED twice — the piece is hostage to a network policy.

---

## CERO — the view from the driver's seat — The only automotive image ever made that is about having arrived: first person from the cab of a bus that has not moved since 2006, needle at zero, and the rear-view mirror showing a kitchen instead of a road.  ·  screen 8.0  ·  `free5-0`

**THE IMAGE.** First person, seated. Camera at the driver's eye, roughly 1.15–1.20 m above the cab floor, short lens (~35 mm) at a wide aperture so the world outside goes soft. Top two-thirds: the split windscreen — two real panes, 585 x 377 mm each with a 26 mm centre divider and a 50 mm corner radius (t1_shell WS_PANE_W/WS_PANE_H/WS_DIV/WS_R), so the divider is a genuine vertical compositional line, not a texture. Through the glass, NOT a road: the palapa, pale paving, a queue of feet, the counter's awning edge — the restaurant. Bottom third, in sharp focus and cool shadow: the dash's long horizontal, the big thin-rimmed steering wheel with its horn ring and two spokes crossing frame, the gear lever standing in neutral with its knob, three pedals cut off by the bottom edge. Dead centre-left on the column: the speedometer, chrome bezel, dark face, needle resting at zero. Top left, the killer detail — the interior mirror, and in it not traffic behind but the galley: racks, the plancha, a cook's shoulder. Light: warm bounce coming UP through the windscreen off the paving, cool skylight dropping in from the open roof aperture behind, the drip-rail bulbs just breaking into the top corners of the glass as warm points. Palette split deliberately down the middle of the frame — inside is cool, still, grey-green and black and chrome; outside is vermillion and gold, hot, busy, and a stop overexposed. Every emotional pound is carried by two objects: the needle at zero, and a mirror full of kitchen.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** Relief, then tenderness. Every car image ever made — every advert, every poster, every VW-bus tee in the ocean — is about GOING. This is the only one about having got there. The needle at zero is not a failure; it is the punchline of the brand's own S-graded origin ("The engine was scrapped, transmission sold, seats swapped for cooking equipment"). A person reads the stillness first, then notices the mirror, and the second beat lands: this machine gave up moving so it could feed people. It is also, quietly, the most flattering possible image of a customer — you are what the driver arrived to see.

**FORMATS.** 500 x 700 mm print, matte UNCOATED stock, 4c plus one spot warm-black. Uncoated because the composition is 60% glass and chrome — a coated sheet turns those into gloss and the image reads as a car advert. One per location, hung at eye height near the counter so the wall repeats the view.  ·  THE HERO PRODUCT — A WALL CLOCK whose face is the combi's own speedometer. The real bezel is 152 mm across (speedo_bezel R 0.0760) with a 136 mm face; make it either life size at 152 mm as a desk piece or 250 mm (1.6x) for a wall. Chrome ring, dark face, and the joke does the selling: a working clock in a dial that reads zero speed forever. This is the one item here a person buys who has never eaten at a Tacombi.  ·  22 mm hard-enamel pin: the speedo at zero, four colours, butterfly clutch. Cheap, high margin, and it is the insider test passing — a stranger sees a nice dial, a customer sees the whole story.  ·  Backlit lightbox, 900 x 1200 mm, mounted above the pass so the staff work under the driver's view. Staff-facing artefacts are the ones people photograph.  ·  Phone lock screen / OG card at 1290 x 2796 — the vertical crop works because the windscreen divider gives it a spine.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** The VW-bus merchandise category is EXTERIOR and it is about escape: sunset, surf, palms, open road, the bus going somewhere. There is essentially no bus merch shot from the driver's seat, and there is a structural reason — a stock bus's driver's seat looks at a road, and that picture is just a car advert. Ours looks at a taqueria, which no stock asset can do. It is also uncopyable by anyone without this specific model: it needs the cab BUILT, and STATE.md's inventory has it — dash, dash_grille, dash_glovebox, dash_knob, wheel_rim/wheel_hub/wheel_horn/wheel_spoke x2, speedo_bezel/face/glass, gear_lever + gear_knob, pedal_a/b/c, both seats with backs and welts, mirror_int, mirror_stem, doorcard x2, doorback x2, cab_floor. And it carries ZERO VW roundel: the nose emblem — the owner's ninth reported defect, and the single most trademark-exposed element on the object — is behind the camera.

**PRODUCTION.** Ship the clock and the pin FIRST; they need only the dial artwork and no render at all. The print needs a proving run: 300 dpi on a 500 mm edge is ~5900 px, and nothing above 1600x1100 exists on disk — the only large-format run this chain has ever made is one 3840x2640 frame at rev 57 and that file is gone. Do NOT sample the brand red off this frame (or any frame): flank_compare on out/r75_side.png reads the render's red at median (196,81,67), G/R 0.422, against the photograph's (158,16,6), G/R 0.114 — it images salmon. Here that matters less than anywhere else, because all the red is out-of-focus background. Two defects this composition dodges for free: there is no large flat body panel in shot, so the measured gloss deficit (gloss_compare: paint spread 0.412 of the photograph against a 0.60 bar) has nothing to show on; and the white cyclorama is not in frame at all. Grade it a third of a stop under — the image is about stillness, and stillness is darker than a product shot.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** ADD AN INTERIOR CAMERA. All 14 entries in studio.views() are exterior; the two closest, 'counter' (loc 3.40, 5.20, 1.98) and 'playa' (loc 3.15, 5.75, 1.60), both stand outside the vehicle. Nothing in this tree has ever rendered from inside the cab. Add a 'cab' view at the driver's eye, ~35 mm, wide aperture.  ·  BUILD THE SPEEDOMETER DIAL — it does not exist. t1_detail builds three bare discs: speedo_bezel (chrome_d), speedo_face (material 'dark'), speedo_glass (glass). There is no needle, no numerals, no odometer; grep for needle/dial/odometer across the tree returns nothing on this object. The dial face is a new texture (and the hero product's artwork), the needle is one new object, and the reading MUST be declared a POSE, not a measurement — nothing in the reference set shows the instrument.  ·  CHECK mirror_int ACTUALLY REFLECTS AND THAT THE GALLEY IS IN ITS CONE. It is built as a solid prism in t1_detail; if its material is not reflective, or the reflection cone points at the roof, the frame's best detail is dead and the whole concept softens to a still life.  ·  LIGHT THE INTERIOR — it has never been lit or looked at. F15 (unlit roofed body, 6.92e5 px^2) and F45 (roof-aperture interior, 2.91e5 px^2, item 6 of 16) are both recorded UNGATED, i.e. nothing in the tree has ever measured them. Budget for the cab rendering dead black on the first attempt. F259 is the licence: the owner ruled 'Relight the studio and fix the ground if necessary', with the single constraint 'DO NOT change RED' — which this never touches.  ·  BUILD TWO PHOTOGRAPHED, UNBUILT THINGS THAT ARE BOTH IN THE TOP OF THIS FRAME. (a) The WHITE BOBBLE FRINGE around the WINDSCREEN aperture: t1_detail's bobble_fringe loops over S.BAYS only — three bays, and STATE.md confirms 'fringe | 3' — while ref_playa_34.png shows an unmistakable row of white balls running the windscreen's full inner edge. (b) The INTERIOR RED SCALLOPED VALANCE WITH GOLD LOOPED FRINGE hanging inside the top of the windscreen, visible in ref_playa_34.png and present in NO source file and NO carrier in this repository — grep for curtain/valance/pelmet finds only the body's rear valance PANEL, a different object. Both are cheap, both are the frame's charm, and (b) is a fourth ink in the vehicle's palette that nothing in the record names.

**SCREENER.** 'Mirror full of kitchen' is the single best detail proposed anywhere in this set and the speedo clock is the one object a stranger buys — but the dial does not exist (t1_detail builds only speedo_bezel/face/glass as bare discs, no needle, no numerals), so the hero product's artwork is invented in a project whose entire ethic is measurement, and instrument-cluster clocks are already automotive-merch furniture.

---

## TURN OFF THE LIGHT — A keepsake that is deliberately dull in daylight and only becomes the bus when the room goes dark — phosphorescent stickers, a black tee, and a small pierced nightlight that is literally a lamp shaped like the reason the brand exists.  ·  screen 7.5  ·  `t2-2`

**THE IMAGE.** TWO STATES, and the design brief is that BOTH must be beautiful. DAYLIGHT STATE: a die-cut of the vehicle, near-monochrome, black on black — deep matte black phosphor over a gloss black substrate, so at 30 cm you read a rich, deliberate, almost-invisible object with a sheen difference doing all the drawing, and at 2 m you read nothing at all. Small type across the lower edge, set in the brand's condensed civic voice the way the real counter fascia carries '267 / ELIZABETH STREET / NUEVA YORK': APAGA LA LUZ / TURN OFF THE LIGHT. Spanish first, English under it, both small. DARK STATE: the light comes up over about three seconds and resolves into the vehicle's own lights only — ~115 beads along the drip rail at their measured 28.6 mm pitch (reduced for the physical piece, see notes), the three serving bays as three soft filled rectangles, the tail board's string on its 38° diagonal, and one thin line for the counter's brass edge. The BODY IS NEVER DRAWN. It is a constellation in the shape of a taco truck, and the viewer's eye closes the silhouette. THE PHYSICAL OBJECT, which is the real product: a small nightlight, 150–200 mm long — a die-cut black steel or smoked-acrylic profile of the vehicle, pierced with a row of pinholes along the drip-rail line and three cut apertures for the bays, lit from behind by a single warm LED at roughly the festoon's own colour temperature. Off, it is a black plate. On, it throws the bead line and three trapezoids of amber onto a child's bedroom wall — the exact light pattern the real vehicle throws onto a pavement. CAMPAIGN FILM, 8 seconds, no music, no logo: a lit kitchen, the sticker on a fridge, unremarkable; a hand hits a switch; cut to black and HOLD FOR TWO FULL SECONDS — long enough to be uncomfortable — then the glow resolves. End on the type: APAGA LA LUZ.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** The delayed gift. Children keep things that DO something, and a thing that only performs after they are in bed is a secret they own. That is the most direct possible answer to the standing audience brief — 'For children at the restaurant; should spark joy and be something families keep' — without being that deliverable. For the adult buying it, the feeling is different and better: it is a small light in a dark room, given to you by a restaurant, which is the same transaction the vehicle itself performs. The product is not a picture of the brand's promise; it is the promise, at 1/25 scale, on a wall.

**FORMATS.** DIE-CUT PHOSPHORESCENT STICKER, ~90 mm overall. Substrate: matte black vinyl with a phosphorescent screen layer confined to the lit elements. SOLD IN A BLACK ENVELOPE printed TURN OFF THE LIGHT — the packaging solves the retail problem the product deliberately creates.  ·  KISS-CUT A6 SHEET, 105 × 148 mm: the hero at ~90 mm plus satellites harvested at their OWN scale, not shrunk — one single bulb, one serving bay, the '1963' plate, one bay trapezoid of light. A constellation kit a child places on a ceiling. Design to a 4 mm web between cut paths and no concave notch under 3 mm so the negative lifts in one pull.  ·  BLACK COTTON TEE, phosphorescent plastisol on the lit parts only, chest width ~200 mm. Accept the material honestly: phosphor is green-cast and low-chroma, so the glowing state has NO brand red in it at all. Print the daylight state as a near-black tonal so the shirt is wearable as a plain black tee and the glow is a private joke.  ·  THE NIGHTLIGHT, 150–200 mm long, black powder-coated steel or smoked acrylic, single warm LED, USB. This is the flagship and the one with real margin. It is the only product in the entire programme that is itself a light source.  ·  GLOW-IN-THE-DARK KIDS' PLACEMAT / COLOURING SHEET for the tables — cheap, disposable, and it demonstrates the sticker before anyone buys one.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** Nothing in the VW-bus merchandise ocean is a light. The category is exhaustively 2D — sunset prints, line art, blueprints, tie-dye — and every one of them is a picture of a vehicle. This is an object that performs, and it performs the one thing this specific bus does that a stock bus cannot: it lights a place up after dark. It also drops the entire trope set by construction — no sky, no sun, no palms, no daylight red, no VW roundel (there is no nose in it), no filled bus silhouette. The competitor cannot copy it, because a stock VW bus has glass where this one has three holes and has no festoon at all; a phosphorescent stock-bus sticker would just be a glowing van, which is a novelty. This is a glowing FLOOR PLAN OF A LIT ROOM that happens to be bus-shaped.

**PRODUCTION.** THIS IS NOT THE F18 DELIVERABLE AND I AM PROPOSING IT ANYWAY. It contradicts F18 on both locks: F18's style is locked to 'cartoon with rendered depth' and its scene to 'nothing but the bus, die-cut tight, plus the sun and the papel picado' — this has no sun, no papel picado and is not a cartoon. It is a DIFFERENT ARTEFACT, so F18's own trigger ('build it after the model is done', restated at rev 62 as 'once i determine the model is done') does not gate it, and neither do F191/F193, which hold the delivery render. Say that in the proposal explicitly, in one sentence, or the holds will read as blocking it. Do NOT re-put the papel-picado question — the owner ruled 'Leave it open, I'll decide when the sticker is actually being built.' WHY A CAUTIOUS TEAM REJECTS THIS, stated plainly rather than argued away: (1) glow-in-the-dark reads as novelty-shop, and the brand's existing voice is deadpan leisure-outfitter — 'purpose-built', 'expo', 'monument'; (2) it is unmerchandisable on a shelf, because a customer cannot see it under retail lighting; (3) phosphor is green and dim, so the brand's own red is unavailable in the state that matters; (4) it is the only concept here that requires manufacturing a physical object rather than printing one. MITIGATIONS THAT KEEP THE RISK INSTEAD OF SANDING IT OFF: sell it in the printed black envelope; put it in the takeaway bag and hand it to children at the table, which is the channel the audience brief names anyway; and specify aqua-to-amber phosphor rather than the standard yellow-green if the budget survives it, because it is the only phosphor that lands anywhere near the authored bulb colour. CUT-PATH ENGINEERING, and it is non-negotiable: do not contour-cut the true silhouette. STATE.md gives 'rear arch lip above hub 0.3722 m (ARCH_R 0.3735) -> tyre gap 39.7 mm', which at a 90 mm sticker is 0.88 mm of vinyl and tears on weeding; the propped tail board is a ~22 mm blade on a 0.7110 m chord and would read ~0.3 mm thick. Close the alpha mask morphologically at a radius chosen in FINAL MILLIMETRES (r ≈ 1.5 mm ÷ scale), run the cut along a ground line so the vehicle sits on a plinth, and let nothing narrower than ~3 mm reach the cut. The tracer is already written and selftested (trace_outline.trace_with_holes; its header: 'selftest() checks the tracer on shapes whose area and perimeter are known BY CONSTRUCTION'), and AUDIT_rev43 reached the same rule independently: 'bridge the underbody with the cast shadow, and let no feature narrower than 0.159 m touch the cut line.'

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** THE SILHOUETTE AND THE GLOW PASS CANNOT CURRENTLY COME OUT OF THE SAME FILE, AND THIS IS THE ONE HARD BLOCKER. deliver.py's trimmed/ output is the measured die-cut geometry ('the vehicle side is 97.1 % fully opaque and 99.99 % ONE connected component'), but it needs alpha — and every frame in out/ is colortype 2, T1_ALPHA appears in no LEDGER since rev 62, and deliver.py currently prints 'CONTROLS: 4 checked, 1 FAILED -- C1 … PACKAGE IS NOT TRUSTWORTHY'. Meanwhile post.py, which owns bloom, reads `rgba[..., :3]` and returns a 3-channel image — I would be asking for a cut-out AND a glow from a chain that can produce one or the other. Fix post.py to carry alpha through, or produce the two masters from two renders and register them by hand.  ·  EXPORT THE BEAD CENTRES AND THEN DECLARE A REDUCTION. bulb_string() gives every centre exactly, but 28.6 mm pitch on a 175 mm nightlight is ~1.2 mm between pinholes — unmanufacturable in steel and illegible in acrylic. The physical object needs every third bulb. That is a DESIGN DECISION and must be recorded as one, in the same way the source records 'TREAD_LUGS = 64 # DECLARED … NOT measured' — do not let a reduced pitch be mistaken later for a measurement of the vehicle.  ·  GET THE THREE BAYS AS THEIR OWN MASK. The glow artwork needs the aperture openings as clean shapes, not as a hole in a render. They are measured and equal (STATE.md: 'open serving apertures on +Y: 3' and 'bay widths 0.516 0.515 0.516', band 1.307–1.710 AG), so they can be authored from constants rather than traced — which is better, because a traced aperture inherits whatever the galley behind it happened to be doing.  ·  DECIDE THE APERTURE SURROUND (same open question as concept 2): three rings of light or three rings of matte bobbles. On a glow product it is the difference between three bright rectangles outlined in beads and three plain rectangles.  ·  NO NEW GEOMETRY IS OTHERWISE REQUIRED — and that is the argument for doing this one first. It is the only concept in this territory that does not need a relight, does not need interior emitters, does not need F144 closed, and does not need the large-format chain (nothing above 1600 × 1100 exists on disk and hq_render.py's MARGIN path has been proven once, at 3840 × 2640, at rev 57, on a frame that is gone). A 90 mm sticker at 600 dpi wants ~2100 px; the existing preview frames clear that with room.

**SCREENER.** The sticker and tee are novelty-shop territory and it knows it, but the NIGHTLIGHT is the standout object in all twelve — a pierced die-cut that throws the vehicle's actual light pattern onto a child's wall is the brand's promise as a manufactured thing, not a picture of it, and it is the only proposal that answers the standing 'something families keep' brief without touching F18's locked style or trigger. Best production thinking in the set (the 39.7 mm tyre gap becoming 0.88 mm of vinyl that tears on weeding is the kind of note that saves a print run). Unsolved and named: phosphor is green and dim, so the state that matters carries none of the brand's colour, and post.py's rgba[..., :3] makes cut-out and glow mutually exclusive today.

---

## CUT OPEN — The mark is never printed — it is removed. A bus-shaped hole punched through coasters, bags, menus and cards, so the brand is literally an opening you look through.  ·  screen 7.5  ·  `t4-1`

**THE IMAGE.** A flooded field of one colour — vermillion on a coaster, kraft on a bag, cream on a menu cover — and the deployed silhouette DIE-CUT CLEAN THROUGH IT. There is no second ink, no line, no shading and no illustration anywhere on the object. What you see through the hole is the table, the shirt, the next page, the daylight. Composition: the hole is deliberately oversized against its carrier, 70-85 % of the short dimension, and it BREAKS ONE EDGE — the counter spur or the mast runs off the die so the shape reads as bigger than the object containing it, which is what stops it looking like a decal and makes it look like damage. The three serving apertures are handled as the identity's fingerprint rather than hidden: each is retained as an island bridged top and bottom by a visible 1.5 mm tab, exactly the way a signwriter's stencil is bridged, so the mark carries six small deliberate interruptions that repeat across every product and become recognisable in their own right. Orientation fixed as in HANDBRAKE, rear right, plinth baseline flat to the object's own bottom edge or bleeding off it. On a sticker sheet the logic inverts: the bus is the NEGATIVE — everything around it peels away and the bus is what stays on the backing, so a child ends up holding the hole in one hand and the bus in the other.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** It stages the founding sentence physically instead of describing it. Dario Wolos bought a green bus for $3,000 that was 'missing three of its windows' and 'figured out how to cut open the Tacombi' (SPEC.md §0.1, graded S — the brand's own account). The signature feature of this vehicle is an ABSENCE, and every competitor draws a presence. Held up to the light a punched coaster does the whole brand story in one gesture with no copy at all, and it does it for a five-year-old at a table as readily as for a designer. It also passes the keeper test the hard way: an object with a hole in it is more interesting than an object with a print on it, so it does not get thrown away.

**FORMATS.** Letterpress coaster, 90 x 90 mm, 2 mm greyboard, flooded one side, bus die-cut through at ~72 mm wide. At 72 mm the pillars come out 1.72 / 2.04 mm — BELOW the 3 mm web a die needs, so the pillars are thickened to a declared 3.0 mm on the die and the departure is written down. A hole in a coaster is also structurally the most robust die-cut you can make, because the board carries it.  ·  Kraft carry bag, shape punched through the upper gusset at ~160 mm wide so the ROOF LINE of the hole doubles as the hand hole. This is the concept's best object: the die is functional, so the brand mark is the thing you carry the bag by.  ·  A6 sticker sheet, 105 x 148 mm, bus as the NEGATIVE at ~95 mm wide. One kiss-cut pull removes the field and leaves the bus. Kiss-cut web >= 4 mm between any two paths so the whole negative lifts in one piece — that single pull is the product's entire play mechanic.  ·  Menu cover, shape cut through to the first page at ~180 mm wide, so the menu's own first line shows through the hole and changes with the menu. The only format where the hole carries live copy.  ·  Hang tag, 40 x 58 mm, hole at ~34 mm. Below the true-pillar floor, so this size uses the NOTCH resolution: the three apertures are opened downward to the sill edge and become a comb of three slots rather than three islands — no bridges needed and the mark stays legible.  ·  Laser-cut 3 mm acrylic keychain, 60 mm, in one flood colour. Bridges at 1.5 mm are comfortable in acrylic and the cut edge catches light, which a printed sticker cannot do.  ·  Floor stencil at the restaurant entrance, 600 mm, cut from 0.5 mm mylar and sprayed. The same bridged path, scaled — which is why the master must be VECTOR from the trace, not a scaled bitmap.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** The category has one move: print a bus. Nobody removes one. And here the removal is not a graphic conceit borrowed from Swiss poster design — it is the literal founding action and the literal signature feature, so the strangeness is documentary rather than decorative. Three further separations. (a) A hole has no colour, so this concept routes entirely around the programme's largest measured defect: the render's red images salmon (G/R 0.422 against the photograph's 0.114) and there is nothing to match. (b) A hole has no shading, no gloss and no highlight, so gloss_compare's failing paint (spread 0.412 of the photograph against a 0.60 bar) is irrelevant. (c) The bridges. Every stencil-bridged mark in the world reads as a stencil; almost none makes the bridges a deliberate, repeated, positioned signature. Six tabs in the same six places on every product is a recognisable device that cannot be lifted from a stock silhouette, because a stock silhouette has no apertures to bridge.

**PRODUCTION.** THE HARD CONSTRAINT: a hole must be ONE CONNECTED REGION of removed material and everything left behind must be attached. The three apertures are islands and would fall out. Two legitimate resolutions, and pick per size: (a) NOTCH — open each aperture down to the sill so the flank becomes a three-tooth comb (use below ~50 mm, where bridges would be finer than the die can hold); (b) BRIDGE — retain each island on a 1.5 mm tab top and bottom and make the tabs an owned feature (use at 60 mm and above). DIE RULES: minimum 3 mm of material between any two cuts, internal radius >= 1.5 mm, no web narrower than 3 mm, and every acute corner relieved. That means the measured pillars (109.5 / 129.5 mm true, i.e. 1.72 / 2.04 mm at a 72 mm coaster) MUST be thickened to 3 mm and the tail board's 22 mm blade MUST be thickened — write both departures down in the artefact's own spec with the true figures beside them, so nobody later mistakes the die for a measurement. Note the scope: SPEC §10.10's 'ABSOLUTE REPLICATION OF ALL ARTWORK' hard bar governs the vehicle's own painted decals, not a derived die, and this artefact is derived — say so up front so a creative track is not audited against a rule written for texture bakes. THE MASTER MUST BE VECTOR. Nothing above 1600x1100 exists on disk (out/ is untracked and starts empty), which at 4.580 m across 1244 px of ink is 272 px/m — fine for a 90 mm coaster, useless for a 600 mm stencil. Trace once to a path, then every size is free. CEILING: I did not run a die. Every millimetre threshold above is standard trade practice, not a measurement off this tree, and a vendor's own minimums override mine — get them in writing before art is approved.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** Same prerequisite as HANDBRAKE and it is the only real one: one T1_ALPHA=1 render of `side`, not routed through post.py. Everything else in this concept is 2D.  ·  Trace to VECTOR with trace_outline.trace_with_holes() — this concept depends absolutely on the holes being returned as holes, which is the exact defect trace() has and the module documents.  ·  A BRIDGING / CLOSING PASS: morphological closing of the alpha mask at a radius chosen in FINAL millimetres (r = 1.5 mm / k) to weld the underbody into the plinth and kill the 39.7 mm arch gaps, then a min-feature measurement on the produced path, then the six bridge tabs placed by hand rather than generated — their positions are a design decision and should be identical on every product.  ·  A DEPARTURE REGISTER for the die: one short file listing every place the cut path leaves the measured geometry (pillar 109.5 -> 3.0 mm at 72 mm, tail board 22 -> declared thickness, arch gaps deleted) with the true value beside it. This is cheap and it is what stops the die becoming a source of false measurements later, which is this project's most-repeated failure mode.  ·  NOT required: the emblem, the red, the gloss, the large-format chain, any export, any lighting work. This is the cheapest concept in the set and the only one whose whole risk sits with the die vendor rather than with the model.

**SCREENER.** Best fact-to-feeling weld in the set — the founding sentence is literally 'cut open' and 'missing three of its windows', so a punched hole stages the brand instead of describing it; the bag whose hand hole IS the roofline is the single best object here and the bridged tabs as an owned signature is a real designer's move, but negative-space die-cut is a known idiom and thickening the pillars to 3 mm quietly discards the measured asymmetry that made it feel found.

---

## OPENING HOURS — A locked orthographic side elevation in which a plain 1963 panel van, over four mechanical beats and nine seconds, becomes unmistakably a taqueria — and then cuts back to being a van.  ·  screen 7.5  ·  `t5-0`

**THE IMAGE.** FRAMING: locked orthographic side elevation, `studio.views()['side']` — ortho 5.90, camera at z 1.52 m, `lens=None`. Parallel projection: no perspective, no lens character, no bokeh. This is deliberately a DRAWING, not a photograph, and it is a projection no camera can make. Master at 2.2:1, with 16:9 / 1:1 / 4:5 crops pulled from it. The vehicle sits at ~78% of frame width (measured on out/r75_side.png: 77.8% x 77.5%, clean margin all four sides — the only one of the four r75 frames that is not clipped).

LIGHT: flat, even, frontal. No key, no drama, no environment. The whole frame is cream paper (post.py's live `BACKDROP = "headroom"`, peak 252) and the only dark value in the picture is the contact shadow, which is a pool under the tyres, not a ground plane. Five values total.

PALETTE: vermillion below the belt line, cream above it, warm grey shadow, cream paper, and — only in the last beat — amber. That is the entire colour script.

COMPOSITION: the belt line is the horizontal that organises the frame and it sits almost exactly on the lower third. The counter's brass nosing is the second horizontal. Everything that moves, moves ABOVE the belt line or behind the rear axle; the front two-thirds of the vehicle is static for the whole loop. Type, when present, hangs off the counter's brass line as a baseline and off the drip rail as a cap line — the object supplies its own grid.

MOTION — five beats, stepped at 12 fps with holds, NOT eased at 24:
1. CLOSED AND DARK. 24-frame hold. It reads as a generic split-screen van. Nothing in frame says food, Mexico, or Tacombi.
2. THE TAIL BOARD DROPS. `TB_TILT_DEG` to 38.0 deg over 6 steps, with 2 frames of overshoot and settle — that hitch IS the stay taking load.
3. THE REAR HATCH SWINGS. `REAR_OPEN_DEG` 0 to 64.0 deg, 5 steps.
4. THE ROOF LID RISES. `T1_LIDDEG` to 76.0 deg over 8 steps, overshooting ~3 deg and settling for 2 frames — that IS the strut clacking home. This is the big move: STATE.md gives `roof at tail = 1.988` closed against a printed `(bbox top 3.132)` open, so the silhouette grows 57% in eight frames. (Note studio.py's rev-44 comment still says 3.046; STATE.md is the live figure. Name the one you use.)
5. THE BULBS IGNITE nose to tail over 10 frames. Then a 36-frame hold on the deployed vehicle, and a HARD CUT back to beat 1.

~110 frames, ~9 s, of which roughly 5 s are holds. The film is almost entirely stillness punctuated by four mechanical events.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** RELIEF. Every VW bus in the merchandise category is going somewhere — sunset, coast, highway, escape. This one structurally cannot: Tacombi's own account, graded S in SPEC.md §0.1, says the engine was scrapped and the transmission sold. So the only motion it owns is opening, and opening is what a shop does when it is ready for you. The stepped 12 fps cadence with overshoot-and-settle makes it feel like an object with weight and hinges rather than a smooth CG tween — a person watches it and believes there is metal in it. And the argument of the whole brand is delivered in nine seconds without a word: closed, it is anybody's van; open, it is nobody else's. The conversion is the identity.

**FORMATS.** 9 s loop, 1080x1080 and 1080x1350, MP4 + WebP. Social feed. The 57% silhouette change survives the feed's autoplay-muted-thumb-scroll problem because it is a SHAPE change, not a detail change.  ·  1920x1080 in-store screen loop, mounted above the actual counter — the bus opening above the thing it opens onto. This is the format the piece is really for.  ·  DIPTYCH POSTER, A1 594x841 portrait, uncoated 200-250 gsm, litho or 4-ink spot. Closed elevation above, open elevation below, at IDENTICAL scale with one shared scale bar. The identical-scale claim is only honest because the camera is orthographic and the mesh is measured — a photographic pair could never make it. Uncoated because the vehicle's real finish is chalky paint, not clearcoat.  ·  3-frame animated favicon and web loading state at 32 px: closed, open, lit. It reads at 32 px because 57% of the height changes.  ·  FLIPBOOK CARD for the counter, 12 cards at 85x55 mm, one beat per card, stapled. NOTE: this is a kids' object and it is adjacent to F18's audience, but it is NOT offered as F18 — F18's style is locked to 'cartoon with rendered depth' and this is photoreal orthographic. Different artefact, said plainly.  ·  6 s cutdown, beats 4-5 only (lid + bulbs), 1080x1920 vertical, as a story/ad unit.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** Nothing in this category transforms. Search the VW-bus merchandise market and every asset is a static bus pretending it is about to leave: sunset silhouette, surf, palms, peace signs, minimal line art, 70s wash, blueprint. All of them sell MOTION AND ESCAPE, and all of them are the American counterculture reading of this body shape — while 'combi' in Mexico is a pesero, a working microbus that hauls people to work. Tacombi's own name commits it to the workhorse reading. This piece refuses the category's entire emotional stock by showing the handbrake. It also uses a projection the category cannot obtain: a true parallel-projection elevation exists only because someone spent 75 revisions measuring the object, and a stock asset has no measurements behind it. Finally, the closed state has NEVER BEEN BUILT OR LOOKED AT in 76 revisions — the piece's first beat is a picture of this vehicle that does not yet exist anywhere.

**PRODUCTION.** CADENCE: 12 fps stepped, not 24 fps eased. The overshoot-and-settle on the lid and on the tail-board stay IS the sound design — clack, creak, clack, tick, no engine. Animate to those events even in a silent loop.

RENDER ECONOMICS: 110 frames is not 110 renders. The stepped design means ~26 UNIQUE poses; holds repeat ONE rendered frame. At the brief's own quoted ~5.5 min/frame at 1600x1100/96 spp plus a ~20 s build, that is roughly 2.5 h sequential. DO NOT FAN OUT BLENDER — CLAUDE.md is explicit that it is CPU-bound on 4 cores and two instances make both slower. One backgrounded queue launched with `setsid`, analysed in the foreground.

FREEZE THE HOLDS. The render is not run-to-run deterministic and the measured floor is ~2.04% of pixels differing by >8 levels whole-frame and 7.90% ON THE BODY (F308). Denoising is OPENIMAGEDENOISE, spatial only, no temporal pass — so a re-rendered hold will BOIL on the large flat cream panels. Render each held pose once and repeat the file.

GRAIN: post.py's `grain(..., seed=7)` uses a fixed seed. Identical grain on every frame is not film grain, it is a dirty gate, and it is the most amateur possible tell. Seed from the frame index or turn grain off for motion. Nothing in the tree currently has a frame index to seed from.

ALPHA vs OPTICS: post.py reads `rgba[..., :3]` and saves 3 channels — measured, colortype 6 in, colortype 2 out. You can have optics or a cut-out, never both. For this piece take the optics and skip the cut-out.

POSTER: nothing above 1600x1100 exists on disk and `out/` is untracked. A1 needs a proving run of hq_render + stitch, and `grep -c T1_FX hq_render.py` returns 0 where the chain it replaced forced optics off per strip — export `T1_FX=0` by hand or the vignette bands at every seam, and then post.py lays a SECOND optics pass over the stitch. Read stitch.py's printed worst-z and its exit code (2 on a seam), not the redirect's.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** BLOCKING, AND IT IS ONE LINE: `LID_W` IS COUPLED TO THE LID ANGLE AS 1/sin. t1_shell.py: `LID_W = (LID_X0 - LID_X1) / LID_ASPECT / math.sin(math.radians(LID_OPEN_DEG))`. I computed the sweep from the live constants (LID_X0 0.9640, LID_X1 -1.0700, LID_ASPECT 1.7130): 76 deg -> 1.2237 m (shipped), 60 -> 1.3711, 45 -> 1.6792, 30 -> 2.3748, 20 -> 3.4717, 10 -> 6.8379, 5 -> 13.6238, and 0 deg is a ZeroDivisionError. A naive T1_LIDDEG sweep does NOT close the lid — it inflates the mural board to longer than the bus and then crashes. FIX: freeze LID_W at its 76 deg value as a constant, add `T1_LID_WCOUPLE=1` to restore the coupling, and add a guard row that the built panel's width does not move when the angle does. WATCH THE ROW GO RED on the coupled build before believing it green.  ·  The lid's `assert _top.co.y > LID_Y_HINGE + 0.010` picks the topmost vertex, which is degenerate near 0 deg where the panel is flat and float ordering decides the winner. Correct for a posed still, wrong for a swept sequence. Needs a closed branch with the cause named in the row's own label, not a relaxation.  ·  `TRUNK_OPEN_DEG = 0.0` is a hardcoded constant on a lid whose lateral hinge is already solved and printed. Two lines gives `T1_TRUNK_OPEN` and a fifth beat, for free.  ·  THE BULBS CANNOT IGNITE IN SEQUENCE. t1_detail.py collapses them with `join([ob, flex], "bulb_string")` and build.py binds one `"bulb"` material. Keep them addressable behind an ablation. `BULB_PITCH = 0.0286` on a rail from `T._aft(-1.8000)` to `1.7000` — that is a dense bead, so the ignition reads as a line sweeping, not as dots popping.  ·  F144 stands: 'THE FESTOON BULBS READ AS PEARLS, NOT LIGHTS.' Beat 5 is the payoff and it currently has no glow. `t1_mats` has an `emissive(name, colour, strength=8.0)` builder and `T1_BULB_STR` is a live ablation; push the emission above post.py's `BLOOM_THR = 0.94` and look at a crop.  ·  THERE IS NO ANIMATION INFRASTRUCTURE AT ALL. Zero hits for frame_start / keyframe_insert / render(animation=True). Every hinge BAKES its rotation into vertices, so one pose == one full rebuild. The real unlock is `T1_SAVE` — build.py's `bpy.ops.wm.save_as_mainfile(...)` — which appears in ZERO handoff documents (grep returns 0 in the brief, HANDOFF_CARRIERS.md, CLAUDE.md, README.md, START_HERE.md and OPEN_FINDINGS.md). Save one .blend, parent each lid to an empty on its already-computed hinge axis instead of baking, and you get real keyframes, one build instead of N, and a file another artist can open.  ·  NO 3D EXPORT PATH EXISTS. grep for export_scene / gltf / usd_export / fbx returns nothing. No motion designer outside this repo can touch the asset.  ·  THERE IS NO OWNER RULING ON ANIMATION ANYWHERE IN THE RECORD. `grep -rli animat` across every .md and .txt returns ONE file, PANEL_rev61.md, and its single hit is a persona description. This is ungrounded territory and must be put to him as multiple choice with the closed and open frames attached — not inferred. Do NOT re-ask the emblem weight (F314), the nose (F214/F215), or the papel picado (F18).

**SCREENER.** The strongest strategic inevitability — the only motion this bus owns is opening, and the identical-scale diptych is honest ONLY because the camera is orthographic; it also earns its keep by catching the LID_W 1/sin coupling (verified: a naive T1_LIDDEG sweep inflates the mural board past the bus and ZeroDivisionErrors at 0), though 'the product assembles itself' is a common brand-motion shape.

---

## ENGINE: NONE — The bus made one journey under its own power and then became freight — so file the route as a shipping document, not a road trip.  ·  screen 7.5  ·  `t17-1`

**THE IMAGE.** A dockside customs plate. True orthographic side elevation — no perspective, no vanishing point, no romance — the vehicle laid flat across the upper two-thirds of a portrait sheet, cut out clean on a warm off-white ground with a single 0.4 pt hairline ground rule under the tyres and no cast shadow at all. The vehicle is rendered as flat two-tone with the folk art present but printed as ONE ochre ink, not modelled: no gloss, no highlight, no reflection, no film grain. It reads as a technical illustration of a crate's contents. Below it, a caption block of small condensed caps, ranged left, in three columns, set at 7/10 pt with generous leading and enormous white space around it. Column 1 — ORIGEN: PLAYA DEL CARMEN, QUINTANA ROO, MX. DESTINO: 267 ELIZABETH STREET, NUEVA YORK, NY. Column 2 — the dimensions, every figure lifted live from STATE.md and none typed: LARGO 4.065 m (4.580 m con la tabla trasera abierta) · ANCHO 1.7497 m · DISTANCIA ENTRE EJES 2.4000 m · VÍA DEL. 1.3690 m / TRAS. 1.3590 m · NEUMÁTICO ø 0.6649 m · TECHO 1.9833 m. Column 3, and it is the whole poster — MOTOR: NINGUNO. TRANSMISIÓN: NINGUNA. AUTOPROPULSADO: NO. Below the columns, one line in a hairline rule box: 'Rodó una vez. 2006.' — It rolled once. Palette: exactly two inks. One warm near-black for the entire drawing and all type, and one declared brand red used on the body two-tone and NOWHERE else, so the red is the only warm thing on the sheet and reads as a heartbeat. Optionally a third: a genuine rubber-stamp chop in red, off-square and slightly over-inked, bottom right. Light: flat, even, documentary. Typographic register is deadpan equipment catalogue — the voice Tacombi's shop already uses ('purpose-built', 'expo', 'monument') — with Spanish set first and English half-size beneath.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** A small shock, then affection, then reverence. You scan the plate the way you scan any spec sheet — bored, competent — and then you hit MOTOR: NINGUNO and the object reorganises itself in your head. The thing whose entire cultural meaning is freedom of movement cannot move. And a beat later you understand that this is not a tragedy, it is the point: it stopped, on purpose, so that you could sit down and eat. That turn — from 'this is broken' to 'this is a building' in the space of two words — is the strongest single emotional mechanism available anywhere in this brand, and it is achieved with type, not with imagery. It also inverts the category's whole promise: everyone else sells you the departure; we are selling you the arrival, and we have the paperwork.

**FORMATS.** Poster, A2 420 × 594 and A1 594 × 841, screen-printed 2 inks on heavy uncoated 300 gsm. Portrait. Limited edition, hand-numbered in pencil lower-left with a BLIND EMBOSS chop rather than a signature — there is no artist's hand on a render and a forged signature on CGI is embarrassing; a chop is the correct authentication for an authorless work and gives the buyer a reason to touch the paper.  ·  A6 105 × 148 mm card in the taco box — the entire plate reduced, which works because it is type and flat shape, not a photograph. Free distribution, and the highest-volume brand contact the company has.  ·  Four-page uncoated newsprint insert, 280 × 400 mm — page 1 the plate, page 2 the SPEC §0.1 origin quote set large, page 3 the folk-art rosette repeat, page 4 the two addresses. Cheap, generous, keepable.  ·  Tote panel, screen-printed 2 inks, plate reduced to 240 mm wide across the face.  ·  Enamel or engraved brass plate, 120 × 80 mm, fixed to the counter at each location — the object naming itself in its own restaurant.  ·  Web / editorial hero at 1600 × 2263, which is inside what the existing preview chain already produces.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** The category sells escape, and every route piece in it is a departure. This is the only one that is a CONSIGNMENT. It also beats the one adjacent trope it stands near — the blueprint/exploded-diagram poster, which is a standing stock category sold by the sheet — on the single axis that matters: a stock VW blueprint annotates a generic factory vehicle and could be drawn from a manual, whereas this plate dimensions the CONVERSION and the ABSENCES. 'MOTOR: NINGUNO' is not available to anyone else on earth. And it is the only merchandise idea I can name that turns seventy-five revisions of obsessive measurement into the actual content of the artwork rather than into invisible substrate — the dimensions ARE the poster, and they are machine-written and defensible. Finally it is the strongest anti-advert of the three: it never once says the product is good.

**PRODUCTION.** THIS IS THE RISKY ONE AND A CAUTIOUS TEAM WILL KILL IT. The objection will be 'you are telling customers our hero bus is a broken van in a crate.' The answer is that Tacombi's own About page tells this story with pride and SPEC grades it S: 'The engine was scrapped, transmission sold, seats swapped for cooking equipment, and the roof was reeled back to produce a taco serving stand.' We are quoting the founder. Put the concept to the owner as multiple choice with a printed A3 proof attached rather than as a description — the tone lives or dies on the paper stock and the size of the white space, and it cannot be judged on a screen. TWO INKS ON UNCOATED is not a cost saving, it is the concept: flat spot colour makes every measured weakness of the render irrelevant at once — the salmon red, gloss_compare's 0.412-of-photograph spread against a 0.60 bar, the 8-bit truncation in stitch.py and post.py, and the run-to-run non-determinism. FIGURES DISCIPLINE, and this is where the piece can embarrass us: (1) STATE.md's headline row reads 'overall length (ex counter) 4.5800 | +525.0 mm OUT' — that 525 mm is the PROPPED TAIL BOARD, not an error; print 4.065 m as the body and 4.580 m only with 'con la tabla trasera abierta' attached. (2) DO NOT dimension the tail board's width: t1_shell.py says 'TB_WIDTH = 0.5500 # POSE CHOICE, NOT MEASURED' with an admissible upper bound of 0.59 m and NO lower bound. (3) DO NOT call out or crop close to the tyre tread: TREAD_LUGS = 64 is commented 'DECLARED, inside the MEASURED bracket 48..84' and probe_rev74_tread's T2 refuses to publish a count at all — at poster scale the lugs become countable and we would publish a claim the project cannot defend. (4) The roof height is RETIRED as a target (H_ROOF 1.960, rev 22); quote the measured 1.9833 at the rear axle with its station named, never an absolute roof height. Use the address I read off the frame, 267, not SPEC's 251. THIS IS NOT THE F18 STICKER and the proposal must say so in its first line: F18's style is LOCKED to 'cartoon with rendered depth' and its trigger is 'build it after the model is done', which the owner has held four times. This is a different artefact and is not gated by that hold.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** RUN T1_ALPHA=1 ON ONE `side` FRAME. This is the single dependency and it is one environment variable. Every PNG in out/ today is colortype 2 with no alpha, deliver.py correctly refuses them ('CONTROLS: 4 checked, 1 FAILED -- C1' / 'PACKAGE IS NOT TRUSTWORTHY'), and no LEDGER contains the string T1_ALPHA — so the compositing capability is a rev-62 result carried forward thirteen revisions unexercised. Watch C1 go green; that is the watched kill this capability has never had on a live tree.  ·  DO NOT ROUTE THE RESULT THROUGH post.py. Measured: RGBA in (colortype 6), colortype 2 out — post.py reads `rgba[..., :3]` and saves three channels. Optics and cut-out are mutually exclusive from this repo and no document says so. This poster wants neither bloom nor grain nor vignette, so the constraint costs nothing here, but it must be known.  ·  NO NEW LIVERY WORK. The plate uses the FOLK-ART state, which is the only textured state that exists — and that is defensible on the record rather than convenient: SPEC §7.2 says the red folk-art bus is the one that stood in Nolita, having been shipped from Mexico, and explicitly warns that 'livery colour is NOT an era discriminator and must never be used as one.'  ·  IF ANY ELEVATION OTHER THAN `side` IS USED, FIX THE ORTHO CLIP FIRST. studio.views()['front'] and ['rear'] are ortho=3.55 with horizontal sensor fit, giving 3.55 × 1100/1600 = 2.4406 m of vertical coverage against a bbox top of 3.132 m — both ends are cut, and out/r75_front.png shows ink running off the top and bottom borders. Derive ortho_scale from build.py's own printed bbox with a stated margin instead of the typed 3.55, and add a verify row asserting zero ink on all four borders — watched failing on 3.55 before it is believed passing.  ·  REGENERATE STATE.md AFTER any geometry edit and take the dimension block from it, not from this document. Every figure on the plate must be re-derived by a script that imports the constants, never transcribed — a transcribed table goes stale silently and the plate is a permanent object.  ·  OPTIONAL, HIGH VALUE, SEPARATE JOB: the wordmark's missing dark keyline. SPEC §3 requires 'SILVER with a dark keyline and drop shadow' and the shipped tex/senor.png has no dark ink at all — of pixels with alpha>200 the fraction below luma 80 measures 0.0000. Silver on red with no keyline is mud at print resolution. script_gen.py is NOT called by build.py, so regeneration is a hand step. Cheapest visible improvement in the inventory and it serves every other concept too.

**SCREENER.** 'MOTOR: NINGUNO / TRANSMISIÓN: NINGUNA / AUTOPROPULSADO: NO' plus 'Rodó una vez. 2006.' is the best copy line in the whole set and the turn from broken-van to building is real; but the technical-spec/blueprint poster is a genuinely crowded shelf that it sells by the sheet, deadpan-catalogue voice is now a house style rather than a discovery, and one line of type carries the entire concept — its figures discipline (4.065 body vs 4.580 with the board propped) is exemplary.

---

## Schlieren — The bus rendered with forensic precision everywhere except one column of air, where the heat off the griddle bends the picture — heat drawn as optical error, not as orange.  ·  screen 7.5  ·  `t19-0`

**THE IMAGE.** FRAMING: the orthographic show side, or a near-flat 3/4 from the serving side, vehicle filling ~78% of frame width with clean margin all round. Locked, level, absolutely still. Cropped so the counter's horizontal runs edge to edge and the roof line sits high — this is a wide, calm, almost architectural frame.

LIGHT: cool, flat, high, even. No key glare, no sunset, no warm bounce. The cream reads near-neutral (the authored CREAM is G>R, a cool off-white — sRGB(206,208,200)), the paving grey, the shadow a soft contact ellipse. Deliberately undramatic, because the drama is a defect.

THE EFFECT: one vertical column of air, rising from the plancha (t1_detail builds it at y −0.460..0.100 up to z 1.455) and the stainless counter warmer (top 1.4950), climbing through the serving band (STATE.md: 1.307–1.710 AG) and out over the roof (1.988). Inside that column, and ONLY there, everything behind is displaced: the louvre slots go wavy, the aperture head-rails kink, the drip-rail bulb string breaks into beads and rejoins, the far edge of the cream flank shears by 2–4 px and comes back. The displacement is sub-pixel to 4 px, turbulent, vertically stretched, denser at the base and dissipating at the top. Nothing glows. Nothing is orange. There is no steam, no wisp, no smoke.

The only colour in the effect is dispersion — a hairline red/cyan fringe on the high-contrast lines the ripple crosses, because refraction is wavelength-dependent and that is physically true.

PALETTE: cream, warm grey, near-black, the body red held DOWN — desaturated toward its own shadow, so the picture reads almost monochrome and the eye has nothing to look at except the flaw. The bulb string is the one warm accent, at (1.000, 0.760, 0.442) — the authored emissive colour.

COMPOSITION: rule of the vertical intruder. A wide horizontal object with one narrow vertical event crossing it, off-centre, at the third. Enormous quiet, one disturbance.

MOTION (loop version): 6 seconds, 12 fps, seamless. The vehicle is bit-still. The ripple boils — turbulence advected upward at ~0.35 of frame height per second, regenerating at the base. That is the entire animation. It is also the sound design: the only thing moving is the air, so the viewer supplies the hiss.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** Recognition of something you have seen ten thousand times and never once looked at. Everyone has stood in front of a griddle and watched the wall behind it go liquid; nobody has ever seen it drawn. It lands as the physical memory of standing CLOSE to hot food — the half-second before you eat, when you can feel the heat on your face and haven't tasted anything yet. That is anticipation, not appetite, and anticipation is the more durable feeling to hang on a wall.

The second, colder pleasure is compositional: the image is exact everywhere except in one place, and that one place is where the life is. On a project whose entire method is measurement, the picture says the thing that matters is precisely what the measurement cannot hold.

**FORMATS.** A2 landscape print (420×594), matte or satin UNCOATED — ships now. 3840 px is 325 mm at 300 dpi / 488 mm at 200 dpi, and 3840×2640 is the only size the current stitch chain has ever been proven at (rev 57, one view, 106.8 min).  ·  A1 (594×841) after ONE proving run — A1 at 200 dpi wants 4677 px, 22% above anything ever stitched. Do not promise it before the run.  ·  NOT gloss, NOT varnished, NOT foiled. A gloss coat puts a real specular event on the paper that competes with the drawn optical event. This is the one concept in the program that must be flat.  ·  6-second silent loop, 1080×1080 and 1080×1920, 12 fps — social and in-store screen. Seed the film grain from the frame number: post.py's grain() is seed=7 fixed, and identical grain on every frame reads as a dirty gate, not as film.  ·  24–32 mm enamel pin or embroidered patch is WRONG for this concept — the effect dies below about 200 mm. Do not stretch it into small goods.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** The named ocean is sunset silhouette, surf, palms, peace signs, daisies, 70s wash, minimal line art and blueprint. Every one of them sells the bus GOING somewhere, at golden hour, in a landscape. This image has no sky, no sun, no horizon, no landscape, and the vehicle does not move — which is also the brand's own published fact: the engine was scrapped and the transmission sold.

And the technique does not exist in the category. There is no schlieren VW bus. Refraction is not a filter you can buy; it requires knowing where the heat source physically is, which requires a measured model — so this is the one image in the whole program that is genuinely UNAVAILABLE to anyone working from stock photography or a traced silhouette.

It also dodges the blueprint trap by inches and deliberately. A blueprint annotates a generic VW and Dreamstime sells it by the sheet. This annotates nothing; it is a photograph of a physical effect that happens to be about a taqueria.

**PRODUCTION.** BUILD IT IN post.py, NOT IN BLENDER. There is no volumetric anywhere in the tree — playa_env.py's own header states 'no volumetric fog / no world volume: airlight bound dY < 0.004'. post.py is pure numpy and already operates on the LINEAR image before the display transform is inverted, with bloom(), chromatic(), vignette() and grain() in that order. A schlieren(lin, mask, amount, sigma, octaves) function slots in beside them at roughly 50 lines: an fBm turbulence field, vertically stretched and advected, sampled as a 2D displacement, masked to the heat column. No new render machinery, seconds per iteration.

USE THE DISPERSION THAT IS ALREADY THERE. post.py carries CA_COEF = 0.0011 in chromatic(). Run the displacement per-channel at slightly different amounts rather than bolting on a separate fringe — that is what refraction actually does and it costs nothing.

APPLY IT ONCE, ON THE STITCHED FRAME — never per strip. hq_render.py has no T1_FX=0 where the chain it replaced forced optics off per strip precisely because they band at the seams.

THE REAL PRINT RISK IS BANDING, NOT THE EFFECT. A smooth sub-pixel displacement gradient across the largest flat cream surface in the frame, at 8 bits, on a metre-wide sheet, is textbook banding — and studio.py writes 16-bit while stitch.py and post.py both truncate through PIL silently. Either add a 16-bit read path or apply a triangular dither at export. Settle this before any large proof leaves the building.

PAINT THE MASK AND LOOK AT IT BEFORE PUBLISHING ANY FIGURE OFF IT. The heat column is a projection of real world coordinates through the built camera, not a rectangle drawn by eye.

CEILING TO CARRY: the displacement amount has no measurement under it. Nothing in the reference set shows the shimmer, and no probe can score it. It is an ART DIRECTION CHOICE and must be labelled one — do not let it acquire a false grade.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** WRITE THE DISPLACEMENT PASS. There is no displacement function in post.py and no volumetric in the tree. ~50 lines of numpy alongside bloom/chromatic/vignette/grain, operating on the linear image before the display transform.  ·  BUILD THE HEAT-COLUMN MASK GEOMETRICALLY. Project the plancha (y −0.460..0.100, top z 1.455), the counter warmer (top 1.4950) and the aperture band (1.307–1.710 AG, three bays at 0.516/0.515/0.516) through the built camera. Paint the mask, look at it, and only then read anything off it.  ·  LOOK THROUGH THE HATCHES FIRST. This composition stares straight into the bays and F45 (roof-aperture interior, 2.91e5 px², item 6 of 16) and F15 (unlit roofed body, 6.92e5 px²) are BOTH UNGATED and have never been measured against a photograph. Render one side frame, crop the three bays at 3x, and look before committing to the frame.  ·  FIX F144 — 'THE FESTOON BULBS READ AS PEARLS, NOT LIGHTS.' The ripple is only legible where it crosses a bright hard line, and the drip-rail festoon is the best such line available. The lever exists: M['bulb'] is emissive('bulb', (1.000, 0.760, 0.442), strength=T1_BULB_STR, default 9.0).  ·  RESOLVE post.py's ALPHA DESTRUCTION or accept a baked ground. Measured: RGBA in, colortype 2 out — rgba[..., :3] at the top, three channels at the save. Optics and cut-out are mutually exclusive today, so either carry alpha through or composite onto the final ground before post.  ·  SETTLE THE 8-BIT PATH. stitch.py and post.py both read through PIL, which has no 16-bit RGB path and truncates without warning, on a chain whose renderer writes color_depth = '16'.  ·  PROVE THE STITCH ONCE ABOVE 3840 before anything larger than A2 is offered, and READ stitch.py's exit code and printed worst-z rather than the redirect's (F49: 'a guard whose exit code nobody reads is not a guard').

**SCREENER.** Most original technique in the set — refraction isn't a filter you can buy, it needs the plancha's real coordinates, so it is genuinely unavailable to anyone working from stock, and 'exact everywhere except the one place where the life is' is a sophisticated argument for this particular project; marked down because it's craft-awe rather than feeling, it dies below ~200 mm by its own admission, it stares straight into bays F45/F15 have never gated, and it lists F144's bulb fix as a lever the register grades exhausted.

---

## 1:1 — the wall that is a bus — Print the side elevation at exactly life size on a wall, so the serving hatch lands where a real serving hatch lands and you order through the drawing.  ·  screen 7.5  ·  `free1-1`

**THE IMAGE.** A flat, life-size orthographic side elevation applied edge-to-edge on a wall. 4.065 m long (`dims L=4.065`, machine-measured). The serving band sits at 1.307–1.710 m above the floor — STATE.md's own above-ground figures — which is real shoulder height, so in a Tacombi location the graphic is set out so the room's ACTUAL service window falls inside bay 1 of the drawing. You order through the bus.

NOT photoreal. Flat, line-free, no outline anywhere: five inks — body red, cream, folk-art gold, dark maroon, and one pale grey for the ground shadow, which is the only soft edge on the wall. Matte, colour-matched, no border, no frame, no white margin: it must read as a wall that was painted, not a poster that was hung. Because it is orthographic, there is no vanishing point and no fake depth — you walk along it and it stays true, which is a physical sensation a photographic mural cannot produce.

THEN TWO THINGS COME OFF THE WALL AND BECOME REAL. (1) The bobble fringe: `FRINGE_R 0.0090`, `FRINGE_PITCH 0.0260` — 18 mm balls at 26 mm pitch is an off-the-shelf ball-fringe trim. Buy it, tack it round the three printed apertures. (2) The bulb string: `BULB_PITCH, BULB_R = 0.0286, 0.0110` — 22 mm bulbs at 28.6 mm pitch, emission authored at (1.000, 0.760, 0.442) — that is a commercial warm festoon, near enough to spec from a catalogue. Run it along the printed drip-rail line and switch it on.

So: 95% ink, 5% object, and the 5% is exactly the two elements the render is worst at. We stop trying to render light and install it instead.

HOME SCALE: one bay alone, 515 × 403 mm with a 55 mm corner radius, as a window cling — a serving hatch for your own kitchen window. And a 1:20 strip of the whole elevation at 203 mm, with a printed scale bar, so even the small one carries the claim.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** Standing beside it, you are the right size. A poster makes the bus a picture and you a viewer; a 1:1 bus makes you a person standing next to a bus. The first beat is a small physical shock — it's actually that big — and the second is the involuntary urge to measure yourself against it, which is why children will stand in front of it and be photographed. 'Something families keep' relocated from a sticker to a wall: the photograph they take beside it is the keepsake, and we didn't have to print it.

**FORMATS.** Wall graphic 4065 mm long, matte self-adhesive vinyl, five panels with butt seams falling on the vehicle's own panel lines, never mid-artwork. Height: body-only to ~1.98 m (roof at rear axle 1.9833) or lids-up to 3.132 m (STATE.md bbox top) — choose deliberately, they are different rooms  ·  Dado variant for low ceilings: cut at the belt line, `Z_BELT` 1.2070 — a red-only lower band carrying the wheels and the counter, at exactly waist height  ·  Counter-front application: `CNT_ZT/CNT_ZB` 1.2540/1.1470 give a 107 mm slab and `CNT_OVERHANG = 0.3150` a real overhang — a bar front can be graphically continued into the drawing  ·  Window cling, one bay, 515 × 403 mm, r55  ·  1:20 hang-tag / bookmark, 203 × ~112 mm, with a scale bar and '1:1 ELSEWHERE' set in 6 pt  ·  Physical parts kit shipped with the graphic: 12 m of 18 mm white ball fringe, one 22 mm warm festoon run, a fitting diagram

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** Every VW bus print on the market is a picture of a bus at an arbitrary reduction, because nobody knows how big the bus is. A photograph cannot tell you — it is a projection — and the market's blueprint-style prints are decorative pastiche at whatever size the paper happened to be. This is the only VW bus graphic in the world whose SCALE is a verified claim, and the claim is checkable in the corner of the sheet: L 4.065 m, W 1.7497 m (−0.3 mm to spec), wheelbase 2.4000 (+0.0 mm), tracks 1.3690 / 1.3590 (+0.0 mm), TYRE_D 0.6649 (−0.1 mm), bays 0.516 / 0.515 / 0.516 equal. Seventy-six revisions of measurement, spent on the one thing measurement is actually for. It also carries no roundel — the side elevation has none — so the owner's ninth-reported defect and the sharpest trademark surface are both simply absent.

**PRODUCTION.** THE HONEST BLOCKER, STATED UP FRONT: this cannot be delivered as a raster from the current chain. 1:1 at 4065 mm wants ~16,000 px on the long edge at a 100 dpi wall convention; the large-format chain has been proven ONCE, at 3840 × 2640 (rev 57, 106.8 min, one view), nothing since rev 70 exceeds 1600 × 1100, `out/` is untracked so every large frame ever made is gone, both `stitch.py` and `post.py` truncate 16-bit to uint8 through PIL, and `grep -c T1_FX hq_render.py` returns 0 where the chain it replaced forced per-strip optics off. So route it as VECTOR — flat colour separations off a material/ID pass — which is resolution-free, is the correct medium anyway, and sidesteps every one of those defects. That decision is the project, not a workaround.

SECOND: at 1:1 every artwork defect is also at 1:1. The wordmark is 1276 mm long on the wall and `tex/senor.png` contains no dark ink at all — of pixels with alpha > 200 the fraction below luma 80 measures 0.0000, against SPEC §3's requirement of 'SILVER with a dark keyline and drop shadow'. Silver on red with no keyline, a metre and a quarter wide, will read as a grey smear from across the room.

Also: naming a medium and a size is precisely the precondition F192 states for asking the owner the delivery-dimension question ('Do NOT re-ask it cold; ask it once he names the medium'). This concept is the legitimate occasion to close a ruling open since rev 64.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** An ID / material pass, so a flat-colour separation exists at all. There is none — `grep -n "pass_index\|use_pass"` returns nothing outside the denoiser config. Drive `pass_index` off `build.py`'s own `A(obj, key)` assignments so the islands carry design intent, and copy `matte_tap`'s colour contract verbatim (OVERRIDE / Raw / look None / gamma 1.0) or every pass comes back S-curved through AgX.  ·  Fix the orthographic clipping and guard it. `front`/`rear` at ortho 3.55 cover z 0.2997–2.7403 on a vehicle spanning 0 to 3.132, and `out/r75_front.png` carries ink on its top and bottom border rows. Derive `ortho_scale` from the build's printed bbox with a stated margin, then add a row asserting zero ink on all four borders — and watch it go RED on 3.55 before believing it green.  ·  Regenerate `tex/senor.png` with the dark keyline and drop shadow SPEC §3 requires. `script_gen.py` is NOT called by `build.py` — this is a hand regeneration or the render silently uses the old file. Add a guard that reads the asset's own sub-luma-80 fraction and reds if it returns to zero.  ·  Regenerate `tex/lidmural.png` — F74(a) is OPEN, the board is cropped at the bottom with the flower row sliced mid-bloom and the yellow side columns truncated where `ref_side.jpg` shows it framed on all four sides. `lid_gen.py` is not called by `build.py` either.  ·  Move the pink star. F16 is OPEN: the isolated star is built BELOW the Calidad burst where both red frames put it above-left. At 1:1 that error is roughly hand-sized on a wall.  ·  Decide the hubcaps. At 1:1 each badge is 86.9 mm (`CAP_EMBLEM_D = 0.3170 * CAP_D`, STATE.md's badge radius 0.043429), and STATE.md says in terms 'hubcap badge is SELF-CONSISTENCY ONLY — CAP_EMBLEM_WFRAC has never been compared to a frame'. Either close PHOTOS_WANTED item 7 with one square-on hubcap photograph, or draw the cap as rim geometry with no glyph.

**SCREENER.** The smart move is that 95% is ink and the 5% that becomes real object — ball fringe and festoon bulbs bought from a catalogue — is precisely the 5% the render is measurably worst at (F144, bulbs read as pearls). Scale-as-verified-claim is the strongest commercial positioning offered. It correctly kills its own raster route and reroutes to vector, and it caught the senor.png keyline defect, which I verified at frac<80 = 0.0000. But life-size vehicle wall graphics are an established museum and showroom device, so the idea is sound rather than strange.

---

## Condition Report — The bus catalogued the way a conservator catalogues an accessioned object — an orthographic elevation on museum board, where the caption names not its dimensions but its LOSSES.  ·  screen 7.0  ·  `t0-0`

**THE IMAGE.** PORTRAIT SHEET. True orthographic side elevation — no perspective, no vanishing point, the vehicle dead level and parallel to the paper. It occupies the upper two-thirds; the lower third is caption apparatus. This is the `side` camera (studio.py: `ortho=5.90, lens=None`), and I checked it is the only frame that can carry a plate: out/r75_side.png has ZERO ink on all four borders and fills 77.8% x 77.5%, while out/r75_front.png runs off the top (39 px, median 145,143,133 — cream bodywork) and off the bottom (736 px, median 24,22,22).

LIGHT: a copy-stand light, not a hero light. Broad and EVEN across the full 4.065 m — an elevation with a hotspot is a photograph, an elevation with even light is a document — raked about 15-20 degrees off the flank so three things read as shallow relief: the press ribs, the panel's own waviness, and the directional brush mottle in the silver leaf. A soft, tight contact shadow under the tyres only, so it sits rather than floats. No ground plane, no environment.

PALETTE: the vehicle's own five inks (the chosen brand red, cream, folk-art gold, dark maroon, silver leaf) floating on a warm neutral board — oyster or bone, never white. The paper is the second-largest area in the composition and it should read as museum board.

COMPOSITION: below the vehicle, a hairline rule, then the apparatus, set small and quiet: accession line, materials, dimensions, then the CONDITION column, which is the whole point. Six to nine leader lines — 0.25 mm, ending in a small open circle at the actual mark on the vehicle. Every callout is a real sourced fact, not a caption written to sound good:

· SILVER LEAF, CHALKED AND LOCALLY TARNISHED. Concentrated on the word 'Señor', the b-flag and ascender, the i-dot and part of the swash. Warm oxide, not sulfide — darkest quartile a* +14.0 against lightest +6.9. 'Señor' median (85,46,35) against clean silver (126,123,127). [script_gen.py]
· BRUSH DIRECTION LEGIBLE IN THE LEAF. Mottle correlation length 13-16 px along the stroke against 3.5-5.0 px across; long axis within ~10 degrees of the baseline, which rises 11.6 degrees. It is a signwriter's hand, measured.
· ORNAMENT TERMINATES AT THE REAR CORNER RADIUS. 43.687 % gold on the rear quarter against 0.006 % on the flat tail face, with a positive control in the same rows. [t1_mats.py tail gate]
· THREE GLAZING APERTURES REMOVED. 515.5 x 403.0 mm each, 55.0 mm corner radius, on centres of 625.0 and 645.0 mm — so the pillars are 109.5 and 129.5 mm and are NOT equal. Donor vehicle acquired without the glass.
· NUMBER-PLATE FRAME, EMPTY. 330 mm. '1963' embossed on the upper rail; chrome dulled and pitted.
· BRASS COUNTER NOSING, POLISHED BRIGHT BY USE.
· ROAD FILM, LOWER BODY.
· And one honest blank: NOSE ROUNDEL — CONDITION NOT ESTABLISHED. Because it isn't.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** Reverence without nostalgia. It produces the feeling of standing in front of a well-labelled object in a good museum: someone looked at this closely, for a long time, and told you the truth about it INCLUDING what is broken. It flatters the reader by assuming they want facts rather than mood.

The specific gut-punch is the wordmark callout. A brand that will describe its own logo as a material in decay — 'silver leaf, chalked, warm oxide' — is a brand with nothing to prove. That reads as confidence, and confidence is the emotion the category never sells, because a sunset poster is by definition an appeal.

It is also the only concept here that gets MORE moving the longer you stand in front of it, which is the entire economics of a poster earning wall space.

**FORMATS.** A2 portrait, 420 x 594 mm — litho on 300 gsm uncoated warm-white or oyster board. Image band ~340 mm wide, i.e. about 1:12 on the 4.065 m subject. THIS SIZE IS CHOSEN BY THE MEASURED CEILING, NOT BY TASTE: visibility_budget.py printed 'scale: 734 px/m' at a 3840 px frame, so the vehicle is ~2984 px and 340 mm lands near 220 dpi. Uncoated because a document should absorb light, not bounce it.  ·  A1, 594 x 841 mm — ONLY after the stitch chain is proven above 3840 px. At 1:5 (813 mm image) the same 2984 px gives ~93 dpi, which is a poster you can see the pixels in. Do not sell this size until a proving run exists.  ·  Museum-label card set, 105 x 148 mm, one callout per card, boxed as a pack of nine on 350 gsm board with a blind-embossed chop.
