These go on the restaurant tables — the caption apparatus becomes the product.  ·  Restaurant wall vinyl at 1:5 or 1:4 (0.81-1.02 m image), where viewing distance covers the resolution honestly. Matte laminate; a document must not glare.  ·  Web/deck version at 1400 px long edge — which is deliver.py's own `--web-edge` default, so it costs nothing.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** The blueprint move is a standing stock category — Dreamstime sells 'Vw Van Blueprint Stock Illustrations' by the sheet — and it is the obvious way to dramatise a 3D model, so it is the trap this concept walks straight past. The difference is not style, it is SUBJECT. A blueprint annotates the FACTORY VEHICLE: dimensions anyone can take from a manual, of a bus half a million of which were made. A condition report annotates DAMAGE, which exists on exactly one object in the world and cannot be drawn from a manual, from a stock model, or from a photograph — only from a measured asset plus a record of what was actually looked at. Which is the one thing 75 revisions of this project have produced and nothing has yet spent.

Second escape: the side elevation contains no VW roundel at all. The only VW marks in frame are four hubcap badges at 86.9 mm, which at 1:12 print at 7.2 mm. So the piece is built almost entirely out of the CONVERSION — the three cut apertures, the cantilevered counter, the propped lid, the signwriting, the folk art — none of which Volkswagen made. That is the same answer for distinctiveness and for trademark exposure, which is how you know it is the right answer.

Third: the category's entire emotional stock is escape — sunset, surf, palms, the open road. This one is stationary, level, and indoors. It is the handbrake as a graphic posture.

**PRODUCTION.** CAMERA: use `side`. I verified it is the only clean frame of the three in out/. Do NOT crop a three-quarter to fake an elevation — the whole claim is parallel projection, and a viewer who knows will see the vanishing point.

RED: choose the ink, do not sample it. flank_compare on out/r75_side.png reads the render at median (196,81,67), G/R 0.422, against the photograph's (158,16,6), G/R 0.114 — it images salmon. And the photograph cannot supply a single figure either: probe_rev71_red.py prints its own prohibition, 'the photograph's red G/R is 0.0149 .. 0.0344, a 2.3x span. DO NOT QUOTE A SINGLE FIGURE FOR IT.' Three authored reds also disagree (t1_mats sRGB(196,49,36), lid_gen (159,45,22), cal_gen (214,46,30)). Pick one, record its provenance, and note in the ledger that correcting red on a PRINT FILE is a grade and is outside F259's 'DO NOT change RED', which governs the model's paint.

TYPOGRAPHY: the caption is the artwork. Set it in the brand's own display family for the header and a plain grotesque or a Bookman for the apparatus — but the Cooper Black / Davida / ITC Bookman attribution is a search summary of a page that was EGRESS_BLOCKED and NOBODY IN THIS PROGRAM HAS SEEN A TACOMBI BRAND GUIDELINE. Confirm with the owner before setting a single word. Never a generic 'Mexican restaurant' face — that is the counterfeit tell.

DATES: if the sheet states a year, use SPEC §0.1's S-graded 'Opened 6 February 2006' and check it with him first — SPEC's own buy-date and open-date sit suspiciously close and public sources contradict each other (one search summary says founded in a Kombi in Mexico City in 2005).

EVERY CALLOUT CITES ITS FRAME. If a number came off out/r75_side.png, say so on the sheet or in the colophon. That discipline is the piece's credibility and it costs nothing.

COLOPHON: a 6 pt line naming revision, resolution, sample count and date. Unfakeable provenance, and it turns the project's method into content.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** RE-DERIVE THE ORTHO FRAMING FROM THE BBOX, NOT FROM A TYPED CONSTANT. `side` is 5.90 and `front`/`rear` are 3.55; at 1600x1100 with HORIZONTAL sensor fit the front's vertical coverage is 3.55*1100/1600 = 2.4406 m, and STATE.md gives 'bbox top 3.132' with the lids up plus tyres at z=0. I measured the consequence directly: out/r75_front.png carries 39 ink px on the top row (median 145,143,133 — cream bodywork) and 736 on the bottom (median 24,22,22). Derive ortho_scale from build.py's own printed bbox with a stated margin, and ADD A VERIFY ROW asserting zero ink on all four border rows/columns of any ortho frame — and WATCH IT GO RED on `front` at 3.55 before believing it green.  ·  RELIGHT FOR EVENNESS ACROSS 4 m. The rig is a hero softbox; a copy-stand elevation needs no falloff nose to tail. F259 is the licence — the owner ruled 'Relight the studio and fix the ground if necessary', the studio stays, the RIG is open. The standing constraint travels with it: DO NOT change RED, that is the option he declined.  ·  THE WORDMARK HAS NO DARK KEYLINE, AND ON A PLATE THAT IS FATAL. SPEC §3 requires 'SILVER with a dark keyline and drop shadow'; the shipped tex/senor.png has effectively no dark ink — among pixels with alpha>200 the 1st-percentile luma is ~168 and the fraction below luma 80 is 0.0000. At 1:12 the 1278 mm lockup prints at ~106 mm and the keyline is the whole reason it reads. script_gen.py is NOT called by build.py — regenerate by hand or the render silently uses the old texture. lid_gen.py already authors the convention as DARKLINE = (72, 26, 16).  ·  MOVE THE LOCKUP. flank_compare on out/r75_side.png: 'the render's lockup has to move +76.2 mm forward and -47.6 mm down', with an independent route agreeing at +83/+80 mm aft. Placement is judged against the panel, not against fine detail, so it survives every downscale — on a document it is the error a reader will actually catch.  ·  DROP THE CREAM'S MOTTLE AMPLITUDE. MOTTLE_AMP ships at 0.55 and in out/r75_side.png the upper body reads as blotchy galvanised zinc rather than chalked paint — the largest surface in the frame (2.08e6 px^2, item 2 of 16 in visibility_budget) rendering as the wrong material. The switch exists (T1_MOT_AMP) and has never been laddered and looked at.  ·  IF THE LID APPEARS: regenerate tex/lidmural.png. F74(a) is open — the asset is cropped at the bottom, flowers sliced mid-bloom and the yellow side columns truncated, where ref_side.jpg shows the board framed on all four sides. lid_gen.py is also not called by build.py.  ·  16-BIT THROUGH TO PRINT. studio.setup_render writes color_depth '16' and both stitch.py and post.py read through PIL, which has no 16-bit RGB path and truncates silently. A large flat cream field with post.py's 5.5 % corner vignette across it, at 8 bits, on a metre of paper, is textbook banding. Nothing in the record names this as a print risk.

**SCREENER.** The conservator's condition report is a smart escape from the blueprint trope it names — annotating LOSS rather than dimension is a subject only one object in the world has — and 'a brand that will describe its own logo as a material in decay' is a genuinely good emotional read. Two drags: several callouts are code facts wearing conservation clothes (43.687% gold on the rear quarter is a gate result, not something a conservator observed), and the premise is quietly circular, because the model carries almost no authored damage to report — the losses would have to be built before they could be catalogued. Competent and dignified rather than startling; a jaded AD has seen the specimen-sheet register.

---

## SECTION AA — The show-side flank turned to a red ghost so you can see the kitchen through it — a true orthographic technical plate of the 2.34 m galley, annotated only with numbers the machine actually measured.  ·  screen 7.0  ·  `t1-0`

**THE IMAGE.** True orthographic side elevation, no perspective anywhere — studio.views()['side'] is `lens=None, ortho=5.90` at camera height z 1.52, which is within 30 mm of the camera height REF_MEASUREMENTS.md recovers for the reference photograph itself ("a pinhole model with camera height ≈ 1.55 m"). The vehicle sits on warm off-white paper: no cast shadow, no vignette, no grain.

The +Y flank skin — rocker to drip rail — is rendered as a 12–15% wash of the body red (t1_mats.py `RED = (0.5520, 0.0294, 0.0176)   # = sRGB(196,49,36), hue 5.0, sat 0.816`), carrying its own artwork as hairline engraving rather than paint: the folk art reduced to its three largest connected components only (folk_gen.py's own census, `COMP_TOP = {1: 47.8, 2: 73.7, 3: 84.4, ...}` — three shapes carry 84.4% of the gold, and 48 of 66 carry 2.29%), the Señor Tacombi lockup as an outline, the Calidad burst as a ring of ticks.

Behind that ghost, at full saturation and full contrast, sits the galley: t1_detail.galley_dressing()'s fit-out running `X0, X1 = -1.3000, 1.0400` — 2.34 m of it. Plancha, worktop, warmer and tap, the hanging rail with six hooks and their tools, two racks, four capped bottles, the cling-film roll on its spindle, two condiment caddies with their fills, the pail, the strip light. Behind them the back wall, split at its measured material change: `GAL_SEAM_X = -0.6140`, neutral white aft (`GAL_WHITE = (0.7300, 0.7300, 0.7355)`), warm cream forward (`GAL_CREAM = (0.7500, 0.7040, 0.6420)`).

The three serving apertures are the ONLY places where the skin is genuinely absent — hard-edged, full colour, the white bobble fringe intact. So the eye reads three sharp bright windows punched in a soft red field. That is the vehicle's founding fact drawn as a composition: SPEC.md §0.1 grades it S that the $3,000 donor bus was "missing three of its windows."

LIGHT — and this is where the warmth comes from, free, without inventing anything. The model's own light logic is already argued in source: down through the roof opening, which at the shipped 76° lid measures x −1.0700…+0.9640 by y −0.5450…+0.6787, i.e. 2.03 × 1.22 m and about 2.49 m² (computed from `LID_W = (LID_X0 - LID_X1) / LID_ASPECT / math.sin(math.radians(LID_OPEN_DEG))`), plus one warm-white practical, `gal_tube`, emit (1.000, 0.918, 0.790), running x +0.890…−1.190 at z 1.788–1.802. t1_detail.py states the consequence outright: "NOTHING inside any aperture is darker than display luma 73, and the 2nd percentiles are 91 / 106 / 127. The galley is a bright room." So the plate is top-lit, the counter's underside sits in warm bounce, and no lighting is invented.

COMPOSITION. The counter slab is the sheet's baseline horizontal — `CNT_ZT, CNT_ZB = 1.2540, 1.1470` (a 107 mm slab) with `CNT_OVERHANG = 0.3150` past the tail — and the type sits on it. Leader lines in gold (`GOLD = (0.8600, 0.5400, 0.0600)`) at 0.5 pt, all at 30°/60°, each ending in a 1.2 mm dot on the object and a caption in a side gutter. Annotate ONLY measured things: three equal bays at 515.5 mm (`BAY_W = 0.5155  # equal, measured`; STATE.md prints `bay widths 0.516 0.515 0.516`), band height 403 mm (`Z_SILL, Z_HEAD = 1.3720, 1.7750`), roof aperture 2.03 × 1.22 m, counter overhang 315 mm, galley 2.34 m, wall material change at x −614 mm. A tone bar at the foot carrying the seven galley albedos as swatches.

PALETTE: warm paper ground; ONE red at 12% for the ghost; the galley at full strength in its own seven colours; gold for the rule system and nothing else.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** The specific pleasure of being shown how something works by someone who knows it and likes it. An advert withholds; a section discloses — that is structurally the opposite move, which is why it cannot read as advertising however beautifully it is printed.

For a Tacombi customer the feeling is sharper and more personal: *oh — that is what is behind the window I order at.* You have stood at that counter and seen 515 mm of it. The plate hands you the other 2.3 metres.

And it earns a wall the way technical plates do: it rewards walking up to it. At three metres it is a red bus; at thirty centimetres it is a griddle, a rack of bottles, a rail of tools and a strip light.

**FORMATS.** A2 594 × 420 landscape, offset litho on uncoated 200 gsm — the shipped frame is 1600×1100 = 1.4545:1 against ISO landscape's 1.4142:1, so it is a 3% trim, not a new camera  ·  A1 594 × 841 for the restaurant wall, IF and only if the stitch chain is proven at that size first (see production notes — it is not today)  ·  FOLDED A3 BROADSHEET, given away with orders. This is the strongest version: it makes the technical plate a thing families keep, which is F18's own emotional brief ("should spark joy and be something families keep") arriving in a completely different artefact. Say plainly in the proposal that it is NOT the F18 sticker and is therefore not gated by F18's trigger  ·  Tee, back print, 300 mm wide: the ONE-COLOUR GOLD LINE reduction only — ghost wash and full-colour galley both die on cloth  ·  Menu inside cover / endpaper at 1:1 with the A3 fold  ·  Postcard set at 148 × 105, one bay each, cropped to a single aperture

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** Every VW-bus print in the market shows the OUTSIDE of a bus that is going somewhere — sunset, palms, surfboards, a road. This shows the inside of a bus that stopped. Tacombi's own account, graded S in SPEC.md §0.1: "The engine was scrapped, transmission sold, seats swapped for cooking equipment, and the roof was reeled back to produce a taco serving stand." The object is a building. Nobody sections a building they are trying to sell you a road trip in.

The adjacent trope is the Haynes cutaway, and this dodges it on content: every Haynes cutaway in existence opens onto an engine and a drivetrain. This one opens onto a griddle, a warmer with a tap, and a rack of bottles. It also declines the BLUEPRINT move entirely — Dreamstime carries a standing "Vw Van Blueprint" category, and this is filled colour under real light, not white lines on cyan.

Structurally it cannot be copied: it requires a measured 229-object interior. A stock VW bus asset is an empty shell, so the competition literally has nothing to reveal.

It also routes around the program's three worst exposures at once. The nose roundel is not in a side elevation, so the owner's ninth-reported emblem defect is out of frame. The VW mark appears only as four 1.6 mm hubcap badges. And it is the lowest-red-per-unit-area composition available, which minimises exposure to the largest measured colour error in the frame (flank_compare on out/r75_side.png: render median (196,81,67) G/R 0.422 against the photograph's (158,16,6) G/R 0.114).

**PRODUCTION.** PRINT THE GHOST, DON'T SCREEN IT. A 12% red flat tint over a full-colour galley is a halftone collision waiting to happen: the louvre block at `LOUV_PITCH = 0.021111` m is 1.45 mm at A2 and beats against a 55 lpi screen. Either run the ghost as a spot ink at a flat percentage, or drop the louvres from the ghost layer entirely.

SPOT, NOT PROCESS. AUDIT_rev43.md's surviving colour row reads "the whole vehicle is ONE 70° hue wedge plus four neutrals. Seven inks printed; five if cut." Five spot inks on uncoated will beat CMYK on chroma and sidesteps the salmon-red problem entirely: you CHOOSE the red rather than inheriting the render's. Note the interior constants (`GAL_WHITE`, `GAL_CREAM`, `GAL_STEEL`, `GAL_DARK`, `GAL_RED`, `GAL_GREEN`, `GAL_AMBER`) are LINEAR albedos in source, not sRGB hex — convert before writing any ink spec, and do not eyedropper them off a render.

NO OPTICS PASS. post.py is right to skip here anyway (a technical plate wants no bloom, no CA, no grain) — and it must be skipped, because post.py measurably destroys alpha (RGBA colortype 6 in, colortype 2 out) and its live default is `BACKDROP = "headroom"` at peak 252, which would put a faint grey rectangle around the picture on every sheet while its own docstring still claims the default is white.

SIZE CEILING, STATED HONESTLY. Nothing above 1600×1100 exists on disk and out/ is untracked. The MARGIN chain has been proven ONCE, at 3840×2640 (rev 57, 106.8 min, worst seam z=1.62), and that frame is gone. 3840 px is 325 mm at 300 dpi and 488 mm at 200 dpi — so A2 is comfortably servable and A1 (594 mm at 200 dpi wants 4677 px) is one 22% proving step beyond anything ever run. Prove it on `side` specifically: orthographic is the safest thing to band-render, no DoF and no perspective drift across strips.

AND THE 8-BIT TRAP NOBODY IN THE RECORD HAS NAMED AS A PRINT RISK. studio.setup_render sets `color_depth = '16'` and every PNG in out/ carries IHDR depth=16 — then stitch.py and post.py both read through `Image.open(...).convert("RGBA")`, which has no 16-bit RGB path and truncates silently. A soft tonal falloff across the large flat cream upper body at A1 is textbook banding territory. Test for it on the proving stitch — count distinct levels per 100 px across the falloff — before plates are cut.

CAPTIONS MUST BE GENERATED, NOT TYPED. Pull every dimension from the constants at layout time (BAY_W, Z_SILL/Z_HEAD, CNT_ZT/CNT_ZB, GAL_SEAM_X, the LID_W expression). A typed figure on a printed plate goes stale silently and this project has a rule about exactly that.

STATE THE CEILING ON THE SHEET. t1_detail.galley_dressing()'s own docstring says: "DEPTH (Y) IS NOT MEASURABLE… Y is therefore chosen, not measured." A cutaway publishes depth. Put a one-line note in the colophon saying the fore-aft and vertical positions are measured and the depths are the model's choice. That note makes the plate MORE credible, not less — it is the difference between a technical drawing and a picture pretending to be one.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** A `T1_GHOST_SKIN` SWITCH — a material override on the show-side flank shell only, driven off build.py's existing `A(obj, key)` ASSIGN list. Must be a material swap, NOT a boolean cut, so the folk art, script and Calidad textures survive as engraving on the ghost rather than disappearing with the geometry.  ·  CUT ONLY FROM +Y. THE OFF SIDE DOES NOT EXIST. `galley_backdrop` and `gal_backdrop_f` are 24 mm slabs at `GAL_Y_BACK = -0.4800`, and `gal_end_f` / `gal_end_a` exist explicitly — the source says so — "to stop a camera seeing past the backdrop into unlit body cavity." Any ghost or cut placed outboard of y = −0.480 publishes a hollow shell.  ·  A LINE PASS. There is no NPR infrastructure in the tree at all — grep finds no `use_pass_*` or cryptomatte outside the denoiser config. Two routes: Freestyle silhouette+crease (set the crease angle at 30–40°, NOT lower — T1_body is shade_smooth and a low angle will find the subdivision), or the Grease Pencil Line Art modifier, which produces EDITABLE VECTOR STROKES that export to SVG and has never been tried here. The mesh is clean enough to trust either: STATE.md reads 229,489 quad / 1,040 tri / 5,186 ngon body faces and `non-manifold edges (body) | 0`.  ·  RUN `T1_ALPHA=1` AND VALIDATE IT. Every frame in out/ today is IHDR colortype=2 with no alpha, deliver.py refuses them (`CONTROLS: 4 checked, 1 FAILED -- C1`, `PACKAGE IS NOT TRUSTWORTHY`), and no LEDGER contains the string T1_ALPHA — so the compositing capability is a rev-62 result carried thirteen revisions on paper. The plate needs to sit on chosen paper, not on post.py's 252-grey field.  ·  F45 AND F15 MOVE FROM BACKGROUND TO HEADLINE. The roof-aperture interior (F45, 2.91e5 px², item 6 of 16) and the unlit roofed body (F15, 6.92e5 px²) are both UNGATED — nothing has ever measured them against a photograph. This concept makes the interior the subject, so those two open findings become the picture's own risk. Render the galley at scale and LOOK at a crop before committing to the composition.  ·  AND CHECK WHAT THE ROOF'S UNDERSIDE ACTUALLY IS. `gal_ceiling` was deleted at rev 13 on an owner ruling recorded in source: looking down through the opening you see "the BARE INSIDE OF THE BODY'S OWN RED EXTERIOR PAINT -- no separate interior colour, no headlining, no pale ceiling." A section that shows a pale ceiling is wrong by ruling, not by taste.

**SCREENER.** The content saves it: every cutaway ever drawn opens onto a drivetrain, and this one opens onto a griddle, a warmer with a tap and a rack of bottles — which no stock asset can follow, because the competition has an empty shell. 'That is what is behind the window I order at' is precisely the right customer-shaped feeling, and it routes cleanly around the emblem and the largest colour error. But the form is a cutaway poster, and the world has many cutaway posters; it will be read as one at ten paces. Feasible, well-sequenced, and honest about the ceiling that matters (t1_detail's own 'DEPTH (Y) IS NOT MEASURABLE… Y is therefore chosen') — solid rather than arresting.

---

## HANDBRAKE — The deployed silhouette as the brand's single mark — one ink, one filled shape, welded to the ground line, with a mast, two spurs and three punched holes; the shut bus is never drawn again.  ·  screen 7.0  ·  `t4-0`

**THE IMAGE.** Orthographic side elevation, rear to the RIGHT (the orientation the tree's own `side` camera already gives: I read out/r75_side.png and the tail board and counter are on the right). One flat ink on paper. No outline stroke — the mark is a fill, not a drawing. No wheels-as-circles, no glazing, no folk art, no emblem, no interior. The whole identity is four events that survive any reduction: (1) THE MAST — the mural board propped at 76 deg standing 1.144 m above the roof line (roof at tail 1.988, bbox top 3.132), a near-vertical dark blade over the front half, 2.034 m long by 1.224 m wide (t1_shell.py: `LID_W = (LID_X0 - LID_X1) / LID_ASPECT / math.sin(math.radians(LID_OPEN_DEG))` = 1.2237 at the shipped 76 deg); (2) THE SHELF — the counter, a horizontal spur running off the tail at belt height; (3) THE SPLINTER — the tail board, a 38.0 deg diagonal spur off the rear roof reaching x -2.420, tip at z 2.2087; (4) THE THREE HOLES — the serving apertures punched clean through the flank, each 515.5 x 403.0 mm with a 55.0 mm corner radius, on unequal pillars of 109.5 and 129.5 mm (BAY_CX 0.6720 / 0.0470 / -0.5980 against BAY_W 0.5155 — draw the asymmetry, it is what makes it read as observed rather than styled). The bottom edge does NOT sit on four tyres: the underbody is bridged into a solid plinth that meets a dead-flat ground line, with the two wheel arches drawn as shallow scoops in that plinth rather than as daylight gaps. The mark therefore has one continuous baseline and no floating. Overall proportion 4.580 x 3.132 m = 1.462:1 — and I confirmed it independently on the render: the ink bbox of out/r75_side.png is x 213..1457, y 111..963, i.e. 1244 x 852 px = 1.460:1, clear of all four borders. Motion, when it moves: four beats, assemble only — body, mast rises, splinter drops, holes punch. It never rolls.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** Arrival, and the specific relief of it. A shut bus is a shape that is going somewhere; this shape has its arms out. The mast reads as an awning, the shelf as a counter, the three holes as places a face appears — so a person reads OPEN before they read VEHICLE, and reads BUILDING before they read VW. That is the whole feeling the brand owns and the category does not: everyone else sells escape, this sells having got there. The three unequal pillars do the quiet work — a perfectly regular mark reads as designed, a slightly irregular one reads as measured off a real object, and that is what makes it feel like a thing rather than a logo.

**FORMATS.** Woven neck label, 20 mm wide (13.7 mm tall at 1.462:1). The reduction floor: apertures come out 2.25 x 1.76 mm, pillars 0.48 / 0.57 mm — below any reliable woven or printed positive. At this size the T3 kill list fires: pillars thickened to a declared 0.8 mm, arch scoops (0.17 mm) deleted entirely, tail board thickened from its true 0.096 mm to 0.5 mm. Ship the mast + shelf + splinter + three holes only.  ·  Enamel pin, 40 mm (27.4 mm tall). Apertures 4.50 x 3.52 mm, pillars 0.96 / 1.13 mm — hard enamel holds a 1.0 mm metal wall, so this is the smallest size where the three holes are true. Recessed enamel, one colour, polished metal for the plinth so the mark sits on a bright line.  ·  Embroidered left chest, 65 mm. Satin column floor ~1.2-1.4 mm: pillars land at 1.55 / 1.84 mm, so they survive; the arch scoops (0.56 mm) do not and become a straight baseline. Four needles maximum; the plinth is the same colour as the body so the mark is one field.  ·  76 mm die-cut sticker (52 mm tall). Apertures 8.55 x 6.69 mm, pillars 1.82 / 2.15 mm, arch scoop 0.66 mm — the smallest size at which the mark can be drawn true with no kill list. Matte white PVC, matte laminate, cut on the plinth so the cut path has one flat baseline and no fragile notch.  ·  150 mm sticker / hangtag / letterpress card (103 mm tall). Everything survives: apertures 16.9 x 13.2 mm, pillars 3.59 / 4.24 mm, arch scoop 1.30 mm. This is the only size where the louvre pitch (0.69 mm) is even arguable — it is still not drawn, because the mark's rule is four events and nothing else.  ·  Foil-blind deboss on uncoated menu stock, 120 mm. A silhouette is one flat region, which is exactly what a deboss die wants; the three holes emerge as the only unembossed paper. No ink at all — the strongest premium expression of the mark.  ·  App icon / avatar, 1024 px square. DO NOT shrink the 1.462:1 mark into a square. Re-cut it: crop to the rear 55 % — shelf, splinter, one aperture, one arch — so the square is filled by mass rather than by margin. The crop is the same asset, framed differently, and it is what makes the icon not look like a shrunken poster.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** The entire VW-bus merch category draws a SHUT vehicle: profile or three-quarter, wheels as circles, usually implying motion, at roughly 1.83:1 (this bus body-only is 4.065 x 2.227 = 1.826:1 and that is precisely the ocean). This mark is 1.462:1 with a 1.144 m mast on the roof, a horizontal spur off the tail and a 38 deg splinter off the back — four appendages Volkswagen never made and no stock asset can carry. Two further arguments that are structural, not stylistic. First, the side elevation contains NO VW roundel at all: the emblem is five nose objects (vw_ring, vw_disc, vwbar x2, plus the hubcap capvw glyphs) and none of them is in this silhouette, so the mark is not gated by F191/F234 and is not carrying the mark the owner has now rejected nine times. Second, rule 11 says geometry transfers between reference frames and livery states while paint and artwork do not — so a pure silhouette is the ONE deliverable in this whole programme that is simultaneously true of the Playa folk-art bus, the Nolita plain bus, the chalkboard state and the green workshop conversion. Every other artefact has to pick a state. This one is true of all of them, which is what a brand mark has to be. And the rule the mark lives by is a single sentence any designer can hold: NEVER DRAW THE SHUT BUS.

**PRODUCTION.** REDUCTION LADDER, derived from live constants (overall width 4580 mm; scale k = W_mm/4580) — do not transcribe this, re-derive it in a script that imports t1_shell.BAY_W / BAY_CX / Z_SILL / Z_HEAD and t1_detail's constants, because a typed table goes stale silently. SURVIVAL ORDER, worst first: louvres (21.1 mm pitch) die everywhere below ~150 mm and are never drawn; the arch-to-tyre gaps (39.7 rear / 40.7 front mm) die below ~76 mm and become the plinth; the tail board's 22 mm thickness must be thickened at every size below 150 mm and that departure must be declared; the three apertures and their unequal pillars are the last true detail and die below ~40 mm; the mast, shelf and splinter survive to 20 mm and are therefore the mark. MINIMUM FEATURE RULES for any cut version: internal radius >= 1.5 mm, no notch narrower than 3 mm, no material web under 3 mm. COLOUR: the programme must DECLARE one brand red before this ships — the tree holds four unreconciled reds (t1_mats RED = sRGB(196,49,36), lid_gen RED = (159,45,22), cal_gen RED = (214,46,30)) and the render's red cannot be the source: flank_compare on out/r75_side.png reads the render's median (196,81,67), G/R 0.422, against the photograph's (158,16,6), G/R 0.114. Nor can the photograph be averaged — probe_rev71_red.py prints its own prohibition: 'the photograph's red G/R is 0.0149 .. 0.0344, a 2.3x span. DO NOT QUOTE A SINGLE FIGURE FOR IT.' Pick it as a design ruling with stated provenance. TRADEMARK, stated honestly and not sanded off: a silhouette programme is the MOST shape-exposed thing this brand can make, because VW claims the bus shape itself and enforces against merchandise. The mitigation is the mark's own logic — tune the composition so the non-VW mass (mast, shelf, splinter, three holes) dominates the frame, and never crop to the bare T1 profile. That is a design posture, not clearance; a lawyer decides per product per market. CEILING: nothing in this concept has been produced. Every PNG in out/ is colortype 2 (I read the IHDRs), so no alpha silhouette exists anywhere in the repository today, and deliver.py refuses the current set.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** Render `side` (and `hero34r`) with T1_ALPHA=1 and confirm the file comes back colortype 6. I verified today that out/r75_side.png and out/r75_hero34r.png are 1600x1100 depth=16 colortype=2 — RGB, no alpha — so the silhouette source does not exist. T1_ALPHA has not been exercised since rev 62 and appears in no ledger; watch deliver.py's C1 go from FAILED to PASS, which is the watched kill this capability has never had on a live tree.  ·  DO NOT route the alpha frame through post.py. It reads `rgba[..., :3]` and saves three channels — optics and cut-out are mutually exclusive from this repo and post.py's docstring nowhere says so. For a flat mark you want neither bloom nor grain anyway.  ·  Trace with trace_outline.trace_with_holes(), NOT trace(). The module records exactly this failure on the VW glyph — 'filling the outer contour alone fills those cells too ... IoU against its own source was 0.6254' — and the three serving apertures are holes in a single connected component, so trace() would silently fill them. Run selftest() first and watch it pass ('checks the tracer on shapes whose area and perimeter are known BY CONSTRUCTION').  ·  Add a MINIMUM-FEATURE GUARD in the same edit: measure the produced path's narrowest notch and smallest internal radius in FINAL millimetres at a stated reproduction width, and WATCH IT GO RED on the raw silhouette (the 39.7 mm arch gap and the 22 mm tail board will fail it) before believing it green on the plinth-closed path. Anchor it on arithmetic, not on a grep for a function name.  ·  Freeze LID_W before any variant touches the lid angle. `LID_W = (LID_X0 - LID_X1) / LID_ASPECT / math.sin(math.radians(LID_OPEN_DEG))` couples the board's WIDTH to its angle as 1/sin: at 76 deg it is 1.2237 m, at 30 deg it would be 2.37 m, and at 0 deg it is a ZeroDivisionError. A 'shut bus' comparison frame — which this concept needs precisely so it can be rejected on evidence — cannot be rendered until LID_W is frozen with an ablation (T1_LID_WCOUPLE=1) and a guard that the panel width does not move with the angle.  ·  Nothing else. This concept needs no emblem work, no folk art, no gloss, no red, no large-format chain and no 3D export. It is the one deliverable in the programme that the model can serve TODAY, one env var away.

**SCREENER.** 'NEVER DRAW THE SHUT BUS' is one ownable sentence that inverts the whole category, the reduction ladder is real work, and it is the one deliverable the model can serve today — but the creative core is still a bus silhouette, the most-drawn object in the market, and the concept honestly names its own trade-dress exposure as the highest of the set.

---

## SOMBRA — The bus is never in frame — only its shadow, cast long across pale ground at four in the afternoon, with the three serving hatches burning through it as the picture's only light. THE RISKY ONE.  ·  screen 7.0  ·  `t4-2`

**THE IMAGE.** No vehicle. A pale warm ground — limestone paving, not white paper — filling the frame, and lying across it one long dark shape running toward the viewer and out of the bottom edge. Low raking key from the rear quarter, sun maybe 15-20 degrees up, so the cast is sheared and stretched: the mast becomes a long dark blade laid across the ground, the counter spur a fin, the tail board a splinter, the body a broad slab. Proportions are deliberately WRONG — this is not the 1.462:1 elevation, it is that shape thrown and distorted, and the distortion is the point. Punched through the slab: three bright lozenges where the serving apertures let the light straight through to the ground. They are the only light in the image and they are the whole reading — a dark mass with three lit openings in it is a place that is open. Ink: ONE warm near-black (never neutral grey — a shadow on limestone is warm), on a warm pale ground. Edge soft along the length, hardening to a hard contact at the four tyre patches and at the plinth, which is the only place the object touches the world. No outline, no detail, no colour, no wordmark. THE SERIES: three casts of the same object — morning long the other way, noon compressed almost to a footprint, late afternoon long toward you — printed as a triptych or used as a timed identity that changes through the day. MOTION: six seconds, the shadow sweeps a whole day across the ground, compressing and stretching, and on the last frame the three lozenges invert from lit to glowing as the bulbs come on and the sun goes.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** A shadow is proof that something stopped and stayed. It is the quietest possible statement of presence and the exact opposite of every escape image in the category — a moving object has no shadow you can study. The brand's own S-graded account says the engine was scrapped and the transmission sold; a raking shadow is the visual definition of a parked thing, so this is the one treatment that CANNOT be misread as a road trip. And the emotional trick is the absence: the customer's own memory supplies the bus. Someone who has eaten there sees the counter and the propped board instantly; someone who hasn't sees a beautiful strange dark shape with three windows of light in it and is not punished. That asymmetry — better if you know, fine if you don't — is exactly what makes something get kept rather than worn once.

**FORMATS.** Blind deboss or brass foil, 12-30 mm, on a card, a bill fold, a bag base. A shadow is one flat region with no interior detail, which is the ideal deboss subject; the three lozenges come up as the only untouched paper. At 12 mm this out-performs any detailed mark in the programme.  ·  Floor vinyl at a location entrance, 1:1 or larger, laid so the cast points INTO the room. The one piece of brand material that is site-specific by nature: it is the bus's shadow, on the floor, where the bus is not. Anti-slip laminate, one warm black on the existing floor colour.  ·  Triptych of A2 prints (420 x 594 mm), morning / noon / late — one object, three marks, one scale bar. At A2 the proven part of the render chain is comfortable; at anything above A1 it is not (see production notes).  ·  Coaster, 90 mm, shadow printed in a warm grey and the three lozenges left as bare paper. The apertures being PAPER rather than ink is the whole design — the light is the substrate.  ·  Six-second loop, 1:1 and 9:16, for social and for a screen above the counter. The shadow sweeps a day; the lozenges light on the last frame. It is an entire brand film with no vehicle, no camera move and no music cue.  ·  App splash / loading state: the shadow lengthens while the app loads and stops when it is ready. The load time becomes the afternoon.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** The VW-bus merchandise market has never once shown the bus absent — every asset in it is a portrait. This is the only treatment in the whole programme where the subject is not depicted, and that is unrepeatable by anyone selling generic bus art, because a generic bus casts a generic shadow with no mast, no fin, no splinter and no three holes: it would read as a van-shaped smudge and nothing else. Two further separations. The category's entire stock is MOTION (sunset, surf, road, palm-flicker); a raking cast shadow is motionlessness made visible, so this concept is the strategy line 'the bus that stopped' rendered rather than written. And on trade dress it is the least exposed silhouette treatment available: a sheared, foreshortened, ground-projected cast is not the vehicle's profile, and it is the four appendages plus the three holes — none of them Volkswagen's — that carry all the recognition.

**PRODUCTION.** WHY A CAUTIOUS TEAM KILLS THIS, said plainly: the shape is deliberately distorted, so it fails the 'unmistakably THIS bus at a glance' bar that my whole territory is supposed to serve. It is an insider mark that gets better with familiarity, and it should run BESIDE HANDBRAKE, never instead of it — HANDBRAKE is the mark, SOMBRA is what the brand does with it. Take the bet knowingly or not at all; a hedged version of this (a slightly-tilted, mostly-legible shadow) is worse than either pole. THE OWNER RULING THIS RUNS ON, and it should be quoted when proposing it: F259, rev 71 — 'Relight the studio and fix the ground if necessary.' The studio stays and playa_env.py stays out, but the RIG IS OPEN, and this is the only concept in my three that needs it. The one constraint travelling with F259 is DO NOT change RED — which this concept does not touch, because it has no red. DO NOT reach for the environment path: F57 records that T1_SCENE=playa 'RENDERS NO VEHICLE ... a dark cylinder on a grey plane', open and undiagnosed, and F92 parks the Playa hero as deprioritised-not-cancelled with nothing to do now. This is a studio relight, not an environment. CEILING ON RESOLUTION: nothing above 1600x1100 exists on disk and the current large-format chain has been proven ONCE, at 3840x2640, at rev 57, on a model many revisions old, and that frame is gone. 3840 px is 325 mm at 300 dpi — so A2 is available after one proving run and A1 (594 mm) needs 22 % more than has ever been stitched. Also note the chain is 8-bit end to end (stitch.py and post.py both read through PIL) and a long soft shadow gradient across a metre of pale ground is textbook banding: fix the read path or dither on export BEFORE any large print is committed. And the shadow's own shape should be captured as VECTOR from a top-down orthographic pass, so the small formats never depend on the stitch at all.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** THERE IS NO CAST SHADOW IN THIS TREE AND THIS IS THE HEADLINE BUILD. studio.py's ground is a shadow CATCHER whose shadow lives in ALPHA — `ob.is_shadow_catcher = bool(int(os.environ.get("T1_CATCH", "1")))` — and the module's own comment says the open item is 'the catcher's shadow survives the alpha-over at a few code values'. T1_SHADOW ships at 9.0 and studio.py documents it as a presentation gain, with 'T1_SHADOW=1.0 restores the floating arm exactly'. So what exists is a contact-shadow presentation device, not a rendered cast.  ·  BUILD A LIT GROUND PLUS A LOW KEY. The switch for the first half already exists and is measured: studio.py records that a real lit surface (T1_CATCH=0) 'does put a shadow under the body -- 175.2 mean'. The second half is new: a directional key at ~15-20 deg elevation from the rear quarter, with the existing rig's fill pulled back. Render the SAME view twice at one setting to establish the two-render floor before publishing any A/B — the render is not deterministic and the recorded whole-frame floor is ~2.04 % of pixels differing by >8 levels.  ·  WATCHED KILL, in the same edit: with the new key removed, the shadow mask must be EMPTY; with T1_CATCH=1 on the lit-ground path it must be empty too. A shadow instrument that has never been watched failing has not been tested, and this concept's entire output is that one mask.  ·  CAPTURE TWO WAYS IN ONE PASS: the raking perspective frame is the picture; a top-down orthographic of the ground plane gives the shadow as a flat traceable region for the small formats. Trace the top-down with trace_outline.trace_with_holes() so the three lozenges come back as holes rather than being filled.  ·  VARY THE SUN, NEVER THE LID. The three-times-of-day series must move the key only. `LID_W = (LID_X0 - LID_X1) / LID_ASPECT / math.sin(math.radians(LID_OPEN_DEG))` inflates the mural board as the angle closes (1.2237 m at the shipped 76 deg, and a division by zero at 0), so any temptation to 'close the lid a bit for the noon frame' silently produces a board longer than the bus. Freeze LID_W first if the series ever wants a different pose.  ·  NOT REQUIRED, and worth saying because it is what makes this concept cheap despite the rig work: no emblem (F191 does not gate it — there is no nose in a shadow), no folk art, no script, no red, no gloss, no export, and no fidelity gate applies to it at all.

**SCREENER.** A dark slab with three lit openings punched through it is a genuinely arresting image and 'a shadow is proof something stopped' is the right argument for a bus whose transmission was sold; docked because it needs lookdev that does not exist (studio.py's ground is a shadow CATCHER whose shadow lives in alpha) and because it admits it fails its own territory's legibility bar and must ride beside HANDBRAKE.

---

## The Wall Was Already Doing It — Tacombi hand-screened wallpaper of its own bus onto boards in the Playa yard in 2006; finish the job properly, print it, and park the bus in front of it — the bus never moves, the place moves past it.  ·  screen 7.0  ·  `t6-2`

**THE IMAGE.** I found this by cropping the reference rather than reading the record, and it is in TWO frames — ref_side.jpg cols 800–1024 and ref_playa_34.png cols 60–500, the same wall from two bearings. Behind the bus, on white boards, are two hand-screened repeats side by side: (a) a TERRACOTTA/RUST grid of interlocking psychedelic letterforms, screened in square tiles, dense enough to read as ornament rather than as words; and (b) a loose grid of BLACK, high-contrast, photocopy-halftone SCREEN-PRINTED VW BUS FRONT-ENDS — six or more of them, Warhol repeat. They already made bus wallpaper, by hand, in 2006. THE IMAGE: camera dead level at the belt line, near-orthographic side elevation, bus at ~78 % of frame width with clean margin (measured on out/r75_side.png: 77.8 % × 77.5 %, no clipping — the only one of the four live cameras that frames cleanly; front clips both ends and hero34r runs to xmax 1599). Behind it, edge to edge and top to bottom, a wall of ITSELF: the same silhouette flat-inked at roughly 1/6 scale in a five-across grid, deliberately off-register the way a hand pull is, ink broken over the board texture, one tile half-cut by the frame edge on each side. The real bus is the ONLY object in the picture carrying light — everything behind it is dead flat, no gradient, no shadow, no falloff. PALETTE: two inks on the wall — a rust/terracotta pulled from the measured wall, not invented, on unbleached warm white — against the bus's full five-token palette (red, cream, folk gold, mural burnt-orange, brass). The joke, and it is a real design idea rather than a gag: the wall is a REDUCTION of the object standing in front of it, so you can see exactly what survived the flattening — and folk_gen.py's own component census tells you what will (largest three gold components carry 84.4 %; 48 of 66 carry 2.29 %). MOTION: 2 s seamless loop. The bus is locked and does not move a pixel. THE WALL SCROLLS one tile-width behind it. That is the only honest way this vehicle can be shown travelling — the engine was scrapped and the transmission sold, so the bus never moves and the world moves past it.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** Delight, then a half-second double-take. Wit rather than warmth, which is the register the other two concepts do not cover and which the brand's own copy voice already lives in — 'purpose-built', 'expo', 'monument', deadpan leisure-outfitter. It rewards anyone who has stood in that yard and seen the actual boards, and it costs nothing to a stranger, who just sees a very good graphic. And the motion arm carries the whole positioning in one gesture that needs no line of copy: everything moves except this.

**FORMATS.** WALL COVERING — 520 mm repeat, screen or digital, specified as a fitting-out asset for the restaurants themselves. This is the format the concept is actually for: it turns the environment into a manufacturable SKU rather than set dressing, and it is the only idea in this brief where PLACE is the product.  ·  HERO PRINT, A2 420 × 594 mm landscape at 200 dpi off the rev-57-proven 3840 chain. A1 (594 mm) wants ~4677 px at 200 dpi, which is 22 % above anything ever stitched — reachable after ONE proving run, and stitch.py's exit code 2 on a seam is the specific thing to watch (F49: 'THE GUARD FIRED AND THE RUNNER IGNORED IT').  ·  TISSUE PAPER 500 × 750 mm, TOTE LINING, CUP WRAP ~250 × 90 mm, BAG GUSSET, MENU BACK PANEL. The repeat is one flat ink, so it survives every one of these substrates and every reduction — unlike the folk art, whose p10 stroke measures 0.18 mm at a 280 mm chest print.  ·  2-SECOND SCROLLING LOOP, 1080 × 1350 and 1920 × 1080. Seamless by construction because the wall is a true repeat; only one tile-width of travel is ever rendered.  ·  PHONE / DESKTOP WALLPAPER, given away free. Literally wallpaper. The name is the format.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** The entire VW-bus merchandise category puts the bus on a SUNSET. This puts the bus on ITSELF, and it makes the background — the part every competitor treats as disposable atmosphere — the manufactured object. It also has a provenance no stock asset can claim: it is not an invented conceit, it is finishing something the brand already did by hand in its own yard, visible in two independent reference frames. And the reduction is honest rather than decorative — you can hold the flat repeat against the lit bus in the same picture and see precisely what a flattening keeps and what it throws away, which is a designer's argument made visible instead of asserted.

**PRODUCTION.** THIS IS THE RISKY ONE AND HERE IS EXACTLY WHY A CAUTIOUS TEAM KILLS IT. Repeating a recognisable VW bus profile at scale, as decoration, on saleable goods is the single highest trade-dress exposure in this whole programme: Volkswagen enforces against merchandise and claims the bus shape itself, and 'Officially Licensed' competitor product exists on the shelf. THE MITIGATION IS ALSO THE BETTER DESIGN, so take it: repeat the CONVERSION silhouette — lid up, tail board propped, counter cantilevered past the tail, three hatches — not the donor body. That outline is a shape Volkswagen never built, it is exactly the geometry deliver.py's trimmed/ output already produces, and it is unmistakably Tacombi's. Note the sharp irony to put in front of the lawyer: Tacombi's own 2006 wall repeats a PLAIN split-screen bus front, which is precisely the version we must NOT copy. Design-side risk reduction is not clearance; a qualified trademark lawyer signs off per product, per market, with the actual artwork in front of them. SECOND HONEST FLAG: this reads as gimmick if the repeat is too tidy or too dense. It must look hand-pulled — off-register, broken ink, uneven pressure — or it becomes stock wallpaper. THIRD: state in the proposal that this is a DIFFERENT ARTEFACT from F18. It contradicts nothing, but F18's style is locked to 'cartoon with rendered depth' and its trigger ('build it after the model is done') has not fired; offering this as F18 would walk it into a hold it is not subject to. Do NOT sample the rust ink off a render — flank_compare shows the render images salmon; pull it from the measured wall in ref_playa_34.png, the one frame HANDOFF_CARRIERS §0.1 records as neutrally balanced on the paving (116,119,120), and declare it once with its provenance.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** T1_ALPHA EXERCISED AND deliver.py's C1 WATCHED GOING GREEN. This is the whole dependency. Every frame in out/ is colortype 2 with no alpha; T1_ALPHA appears in ZERO ledgers, so 'transparency works' is a rev-62 result carried forward thirteen revisions unrun; and deliver.py currently prints '4 checked, 1 FAILED -- C1' and 'PACKAGE IS NOT TRUSTWORTHY' on the live set. One env var, one render, and the flat silhouette this concept is built on exists.  ·  KEEP post.py OUT OF THIS PATH. Measured: RGBA in, colortype 2 out — post.py reads rgba[..., :3] and saves three channels, so optics and cut-out are mutually exclusive from this repo and its docstring nowhere says so. The wall repeat needs the silhouette, not the grain.  ·  A CONVERSION-STATE SILHOUETTE: lid up, tail board propped, counter out. That is the shipped pose already (STATE.md: 'rear hatch: glass_rear swung +64.00 deg', tail board at 38.0°, counter among what projects past the body) — but note 64° is a DECLARED POSE, not a measurement, and TB_WIDTH 0.5500 is labelled in source 'POSE CHOICE, NOT MEASURED. Inside the 0.59 bound.' The repeat therefore publishes two pose choices at wallpaper scale. That is acceptable for a derived graphic and must be said out loud rather than discovered.  ·  SUB-CROP AND FLAT-FILL PRESETS IN deliver.py. It writes full_frame / trimmed / layers / web but has no sub-crop concept and no flat-ink output. The layers it already emits — combi_*_no_shadow.png and combi_*_shadow_only.png — are exactly the two inputs the owner's own lost style clause asks for ('vector line and flat colour, shading and occlusion sampled from the 3D asset', NEXT_CONTEXT_PROMPT_rev39 §7.3, dropped by rev 43 and missing from every carrier since). Restore that clause to a carrier in the same edit; rule 16.  ·  NOTHING ELSE. The wall is flat art and needs no lighting work, no relight, no environment build, and no emblem fix — the side elevation does not contain the nose roundel at all, only the four hubcap badges (~1.6 mm at sticker scale, ~10 mm at A2). This is the only one of my three concepts that is entirely unblocked by the owner's ninth report.

**SCREENER.** Provenance nobody else could claim, and I verified it myself rather than taking it on trust: Tacombi really did hand-screen bus wallpaper in that yard, visible in two independent frames, recorded in no repo document. 'The bus never moves, the world moves past it' is the honest motion gesture for a vehicle with no engine, and the trade-dress mitigation (repeat the CONVERSION silhouette, a shape VW never built) is a better design than the thing it protects. Held at 7 because putting an object in front of a repeat of itself is a known art-director move — the concept names Warhol itself — and the emotional register is wit rather than warmth, which is thinner.

---

## BUENOS DÍAS — The Bus Wakes Up — A four-panel wordless strip in which the bus opens: no face, no road, no driving — the character is the unfolding, and the light migrates from outside the vehicle to inside it.  ·  screen 7.0  ·  `t9-0`

**THE IMAGE.** FRAMING: four panels, left to right, ONE camera that never moves — the true orthographic side elevation (studio.py: `"side": ... lens=None, ortho=5.90`, eye at 1.52 m, a genuine parallel projection). Nothing changes between frames except the bus itself. The locked camera is what makes the transformation read.

PANEL 1 — BEFORE LIGHT. Everything shut: roof lid down, tail board folded, bulbs cold, counter bare. Palette collapsed to three cool values — the red pulled toward lid_gen.py's `DARKLINE = (72, 26, 16)` rather than toward grey, the cream to a blue-grey, the ground one flat band. Exactly ONE warm accent in the whole panel: a lit window in a building far off, four pixels wide. A cat asleep on the front tyre (`TYRE_D=0.6649` in STATE.md — a 665 mm tyre is a cat-sized bed).

PANEL 2 — THE CRACK. The roof lid lifts about 20°. A single seam of warm light escapes from under it and lands as a hard wedge on the ground. Nothing else has changed. This is the panel children lean into.

PANEL 3 — THE UNFOLD. Lid at full open (t1_shell.py `LID_OPEN_DEG = 76.0`), both struts visible, and the mural board's flowers swing round to face the viewer and blaze — the reveal is a PAINTED CEILING. The tail board props out (`TB_TILT_DEG = 38.0`, `TB_CHORD = 0.7110`). The bulbs ignite in sequence left-to-right: the leftmost dozen warm and haloed, the rest still cold pearls. Warm light begins spilling through the three hatches.

PANEL 4 — OPEN. Full warmth, steam off the plancha, the bobble fringe swinging. Small people: a cook's forearms in one hatch, and at the middle hatch a child on tiptoe of whom you see only ten fingertips and the top of a head — because the serving sill sits at 1.307 m above ground (STATE.md: `band 1.372-1.775 un-dropped (1.307-1.710 AG)`) and a five-year-old's eye is around 0.95–1.00 m. The child genuinely cannot see over the counter. That is the funniest and truest gag available and it is measured, not invented.

LIGHT: one warm source per panel, growing, and it MIGRATES — panel 1's light comes from outside and is cold, panel 4's comes from inside the vehicle and is warm. That migration is the whole emotional engine.

PALETTE: five flat inks plus line. All line in `DARKLINE (72,26,16)` — a warm near-black, never #000. Red, cream, gold, a night blue-grey, and a bulb amber that exists only in panels 3–4. The single gradient permitted in the entire strip is the bulb halo.

MOTION (loop version): stepped at 8–12 fps with HOLDS, not tweened at 24. The lid overshoots 2–3° at the top and settles — that overshoot IS the sound of the strut clacking home, and it costs one extra frame.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** The privilege of watching something wake up — the feeling of being the first customer of the day. For a child specifically: a machine that is also a house that is also a shop, opening like a music box. The delight is structural rather than decorative: you are shown a closed box and then shown that it was never a box. Warmth arrives as literal light, panel by panel, and the last thing to appear is other people. Nobody's face is drawn, so nothing is cute at you — the affection is earned by the object doing something generous.

**FORMATS.** 4-panel horizontal poster, ~840 × 250 mm, matte UNCOATED (this drawing dies on gloss), 'Buenos días' hand-lettered small in one corner  ·  KISS-CUT STICKER STRIP: four 90 mm stickers in a row on an A6 sheet (105 × 148 mm), so a child peels them in order and sticks them in sequence on a window — the sticker becomes a toy with a rule. Minimum 4 mm web between cut paths so the negative lifts in one pull; no concave notch under 3 mm  ·  3-second stepped loop, 8–12 fps, for social and the in-restaurant menu screen  ·  Printed on the back of the kids' paper placemat: panels 1–2 as line only (colour them in), panels 3–4 in full colour as the answer  ·  Concertina card, 4 panels, so the strip physically unfolds in the hand

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** Every VW-bus product on the shelf shows a bus GOING somewhere — sunset, palms, surfboards, a road. This one shows a bus that has arrived and is opening, which is the only thing this vehicle can honestly do: SPEC.md §0.1 grades it S in Tacombi's own words, 'The engine was scrapped, transmission sold, seats swapped for cooking equipment, and the roof was reeled back to produce a taco serving stand.' No road, no horizon, no motion. And the money shot — a mural lid rising to reveal a painted ceiling — is a gesture no stock VW asset can make, because no stock VW bus has a hinged painted lid. It also deliberately refuses the one anthropomorphism convention everybody reaches for: eyes in the windscreen is Pixar's Fillmore (a VW Type 2), which is simultaneously the most derivative and the most legally exposed move available. There is no face anywhere in this concept.

**PRODUCTION.** TECHNIQUE — use the owner's own words, which have been missing from every carrier for 32 revisions. NEXT_CONTEXT_PROMPT_rev39.md §7 item 3: 'Style LOCKED by me: cartoon with rendered depth — vector line and flat colour, shading and occlusion sampled from the 3D asset.' Rev 43 dropped everything after the em-dash. That clause is a buildable pipeline and deliver.py already emits both its inputs: `combi_%s_no_shadow.png` (flat-fill reference) and `combi_%s_shadow_only.png` (occlusion pass). Restore the clause to a carrier.

THIS IS NOT F18. F18 is a SINGLE die-cut with scene locked to 'nothing but the bus, die-cut tight, plus the sun and the papel picado' and a trigger of 'build it after the model is done'. This is a four-panel sequence with no sun. Say so in the proposal and F18's hold stops applying.

PICK ONE RED AND DECLARE IT WITH PROVENANCE. The tree holds four that disagree — t1_mats.py `RED = (0.5520, 0.0294, 0.0176)  # = sRGB(196,49,36)`, lid_gen.py `RED = (159, 45, 22)`, cal_gen.py `RED = (214, 46, 30)` — and the render's red cannot be the source: flank_compare on out/r75_side.png reads the render at median (196,81,67) against the photograph's (158,16,6). Nor can the photographs be averaged: probe_rev71_red.py prints its own prohibition, 'the photograph's red G/R is 0.0149 .. 0.0344, a 2.3x span. DO NOT QUOTE A SINGLE FIGURE FOR IT.'

Hand-letter 'Buenos días'; do not set it. No system face touches this brand — the owner rejected one by name at rev 8.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** **THE LID CANNOT BE SWEPT AS BUILT — THIS BLOCKS PANELS 1 AND 2.** t1_shell.py: `LID_W = (LID_X0 - LID_X1) / LID_ASPECT / math.sin(math.radians(LID_OPEN_DEG))`. I computed the sweep from the live constants (LID_X0 0.9640, LID_X1 −1.0700, LID_ASPECT 1.7130): 76° → 1.2237 m (ships), 45° → 1.6792, 20° → 3.4718, 10° → 6.8379, and 0° is a ZeroDivisionError. Panel 2's 20° lid would render a mural board 3.47 m wide — wider than half the bus. FIX: freeze LID_W at its 76° value with `T1_LID_WCOUPLE=1` restoring the coupling, plus a row that reads the BUILT panel's width across three angles — and watch that row go red on the coupled build before believing it green.  ·  The near-closed 'leans over the counter' assert will fire: `assert _top.co.y > LID_Y_HINGE + 0.010`, where `_top` is the topmost vertex, which is degenerate on a near-flat panel. Correct for a posed still, wrong for a swept sequence. It needs a closed branch with the cause named in the row's own label — not a relaxation.  ·  **Panel 1 requires the vehicle SHUT, a picture that has never existed in 76 revisions.** Render it, crop it and look at it before a single line is drawn.  ·  Sequential bulb ignition needs the bulbs addressable. t1_detail.py currently collapses them: `ob = join([ob, flex], "bulb_string")`, one object, one material. The source records the count and pitch — '211 px/m -> pitch 28.6 +/- 1.0 mm, ~115 bulbs', `BULB_PITCH, BULB_R = 0.0286, 0.0110`. Keep them separable behind an ablation. Note F144 already says the render fails this element — 'THE FESTOON BULBS READ AS PEARLS, NOT LIGHTS' — so the DRAWN treatment can be right before the render is.  ·  **tex/lidmural.png is CROPPED AT THE BOTTOM (F74a, OPEN)** — 'the bottom row of flowers is sliced mid-bloom and the yellow side columns are truncated, where ref_side.jpg shows a board framed on all four sides.' Panel 3 IS that board; it is the payoff of the whole strip. Fix lid_gen.py and REGENERATE BY HAND — lid_gen.py is not called by build.py, so a stale texture ships silently.  ·  **Which face of the lid carries the mural is contradicted inside the tree and nothing resolves it.** lid_gen.py says 'FRONT lid, OUTWARD (upper) face'; SPEC.md §4 says 'underside of the raised roof lid painted with flowers'. Panel 1 (shut) and panel 3 (open) show different pictures depending on the answer. One multiple-choice owner question with both crops attached.  ·  No people, no animals and no volumetrics exist anywhere in the tree (`grep` for smoke/volume finds nothing). The cook's arms, the child, the cat and the steam are DRAWN, not rendered.  ·  T1_ALPHA has not been run since rev 62 — no LEDGER contains the string, every frame in out/ is PNG colortype 2, and deliver.py refuses with 'CONTROLS: 4 checked, 1 FAILED -- C1 ... PACKAGE IS NOT TRUSTWORTHY'. One render closes it, and every panel needs the cut-out. Do NOT route the result through post.py, which discards alpha (RGBA in, colortype 2 out).

**SCREENER.** The structural idea is good and specific: light MIGRATES from outside-and-cold to inside-and-warm across four panels, and the money shot — a mural lid rising to reveal a painted ceiling — is a gesture no stock VW asset can make. The measured gag (a five-year-old physically cannot see over a 1.307 m sill) is exactly what makes a drawing feel true, and refusing eyes-in-the-windscreen is right on both craft and legal grounds. It also recovers the owner's own style clause, dropped from every carrier for 32 revisions. But 'the vehicle opens across panels' is a familiar children's-book device and panel 4 is conventional warmth.

---

## THE PASS — the serving hatch, printed life size — One aperture, square on, cropped so the 515.5 × 403.0 mm serving hatch IS the poster edge — a lit interior, a tray of tacos coming forward onto the sill, and a brass line under it; printed at 1:1 and hung at counter height so it reads as a hole cut in your wall.  ·  screen 7.0  ·  `t10-0`

**THE IMAGE.** FRAMING: dead square-on to the +Y flank, one bay only (BAY_CX gives centres at 0.6720 / 0.0470 / −0.5980 — use bay 1, the forward one). The 515.5 × 403.0 mm aperture (BAY_W = 0.5155 in t1_shell.py, 'equal, measured'; band 1.307–1.710 AG from STATE.md) fills the sheet to a 12 mm cream reveal on all four sides, so the poster's own edge and the bus's sheet metal are the same line. BAY_R = 0.0550 corner radius. The white bobble fringe scallops all four inner edges — built, three of them, STATE.md 'fringe | 3'.

COMPOSITION, bottom to top: the brass counter nosing runs the full width as a hard warm horizontal at the very bottom, 53 mm below the sill (CNT_ZT = 1.2540 against the sill at 1.307 — my arithmetic on those two constants). On the counter, pushed toward camera and CUT BY THE POSTER'S BOTTOM EDGE: a shallow paper-lined tray, three tacos, a wedge of lime, one squeeze bottle standing beside it. Behind, through the aperture, the galley falls away dark and warm — the plancha (top z 1.455), the stainless warmer (top 1.4950) with its tap at 1.3100, the rack. Filling the upper-left third, defocused and CROPPED BY THE APERTURE HEAD: a cook as a BRIGHT WHITE SHIRT SHAPE with one forearm coming down to the tray. Not a rendered face. Not a rendered hand with fingernails. A bright block, out of focus, the way he actually reads in ref_side.jpg (240,290)–(620,420), which I cropped and looked at — the cook is a backlit white shirt behind a dark interior, and that is both the truthful read and the forgiving one.

LIGHT: this is the one frame where the studio's white void is wrong and the record already licenses fixing it (F259, rev 71, owner: 'Relight the studio and fix the ground if necessary' — the rig is open, RED is not). Interior warm and low — plancha glow from below-left, a practical over the rack — against a cool, flat, slightly overexposed exterior. The aperture becomes a lit rectangle in a field of cream. Steam catches the rim light coming through the hatch head.

PALETTE: cream reveal (206,208,200, t1_mats CREAM) → white fringe → warm dark galley → brass nosing (COUNTERTAN sRGB 201,186,150 for the top, brass for the strip) → and the only saturated colour in the entire frame is the food and one salsa cap. Everything else is desaturated so the meal is the only thing that shouts. Note F74(b) is OPEN and directly in shot: GAL_RED ≈ sRGB(196,163,153), 'a dusty beige-pink', where ref_rear34.jpg shows saturated red and yellow. I confirmed it by eye in my own crop.

MOTION (if used): 3 seconds, locked camera, no push. The tray arrives on the sill, the hand withdraws up out of frame, the steam keeps rising. That is the whole cut. It is the only loop in the entire program where the vehicle does not move — which is correct, because Tacombi's own S-graded account says the engine was scrapped and the transmission sold.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** BEING SERVED. Not 'look at this beautiful van' — the specific two-second feeling of a tray landing on a counter and being pushed toward you. Square-on framing puts you at the hatch rather than beside the vehicle, so the picture has a second person in it: you. And the life-size print turns that from a depiction into a small physical trick — a person walks past a poster hung with its bottom edge at 1.254 m and gets a half-second of thinking there is a window there. That flinch is the product. It works because the aperture is a MEASURED size, not a composed one; nobody else can print this at 1:1 and be right.

**FORMATS.** POSTER, 515 × 403 mm — the aperture at 1:1, on uncoated matte stock. MATTE IS LOAD-BEARING: gloss makes it read as a photograph of a hole; matte lets it read as a hole. Hang instruction printed on the back: bottom edge at 1254 mm from the floor (CNT_ZT), which puts the brass line exactly where a real counter lip is.  ·  IN-RESTAURANT WALL PIECE, 1030 × 806 mm (2:1 of life size) or a three-up of all three bays at 1:1 — 1546 mm of wall, which is the real bay run. Hang it in the Nolita room and the wall has three hatches in it.  ·  SOCIAL 4:5 (1080 × 1350) — crop into the tray and the fringe, lose the aperture's top edge. The fringe scallop survives at thumbnail size; the tacos do not, so this crop leads with the fringe rhythm and the brass line.  ·  STICKER, 90 × 70 mm die-cut on the aperture's own rounded rectangle (55 mm radius scaled to 9.6 mm at that size — comfortably above any weeding threshold). Gloss here, because a sticker wants to be an object. This one is NOT offered as the F18 deliverable and does not touch F18's trigger.  ·  MENU HEADER / table tent: the aperture proportion (1.279:1) as the standing image box for every printed piece — a house crop derived from the building.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** Every VW-bus print in existence is shot from OUTSIDE the vehicle looking at it. This one is shot from the customer's side of a hole and the vehicle is barely in it — you see 12 mm of cream, a fringe, and a kitchen. It cannot be mistaken for stock because a stock 1963 Type 2 HAS GLASS THERE. The three hatches exist only because the $3,000 donor bus was 'missing three of its windows' (SPEC §0.1, graded S) — so the single feature this composition is built on is the one thing the whole merchandise category structurally cannot carry. It also has no VW roundel in frame at all, which sidesteps both the trademark question and the owner's ninth-report emblem defect in one crop. And it is a FOOD picture: nobody sells a VW-bus poster with a taco in it, because nobody else's bus is a taqueria.

**PRODUCTION.** ⚠ THE HONEST CEILING FIRST, AND IT GOVERNS ALL THREE CONCEPTS: THERE IS NO FOOD ANYWHERE IN THIS PROJECT — not in the 229-object mesh (I read the whole STATE.md inventory: plancha, warmer, caddies, cans, squeeze bottles, tools, and zero edibles), not in the eight textures, and NOT IN THE PHOTOGRAPHS. I cropped ref_side.jpg (560,280)–(1010,470) and looked: the counter is fully dressed — five napkin caddies, a stainless warmer, a chrome condiment stand, the brass nosing — AND COMPLETELY EMPTY OF FOOD. So every taco, tray, lime and steam wisp in all three concepts is INVENTED. That is a real result, not a gap to paper over. The precedent for sanctioned invention is exact and it is the owner's own: SPEC measures the papel picado ABSENT from the reference ('Bunting in that render would be an invention') and he locked it into the sticker scene anyway. There is NO owner ruling on food anywhere — I grepped OPEN_FINDINGS and every 'taco' hit is the word 'Tacombi'. PUT THE FOOD QUESTION TO HIM before building any of it, as multiple choice with this crop attached: three tacos on paper / a torta / a cup of juice / nothing, keep it empty.

CAMERA: `studio.views()['counter']` already exists — loc (3.40, 5.20, 1.98), tgt (−0.55, 0.75, 1.26), lens 90, focus (0.20, 1.05, 1.22), f/6.3 — commented in source as 'the shot that says taqueria'. It has NEVER BEEN RENDERED (zero rendered-file mentions across LEDGER_rev66–75). On studio's own dof_limits formula at its COC = 0.030 that gives sharp 4.73–6.03 m at a 5.30 m focus distance — a real 1.3 m field, so the tray is sharp and the far bay goes soft. This concept needs a squarer variant of it, not a new lens family.

RENDER ONE `counter` FRAME AND LOOK THROUGH THE BAYS BEFORE DESIGNING ANYTHING ELSE. F45 (roof-aperture interior, 2.91e5 px², item 6 of 16 in visibility_budget) and F15 (A7, unlit roofed body, 6.92e5 px², item 4) are BOTH UNGATED and unmeasured — nothing in the tree has ever scored the interior against a photograph. The galley may render dead black, in which case this concept's entire subject is a hole.

STEAM: do it in 2D over the render for the first proof. There is no volumetric anywhere in the tree for the vehicle, a smoke domain breaks the one-build-per-frame model (a sim needs frame continuity), and on 4 CPU cores with no fan-out allowed that is a revision, not an afternoon.

PRINT: 515 mm at 300 dpi wants 6083 px. Nothing above 1600 × 1100 exists on disk and the MARGIN chain has been proven once, at 3840 × 2640 (rev 57). BUT — because this crop is ONE BAY, not the whole vehicle, a 3840 px frame cropped to the aperture still delivers well over 300 dpi at 515 mm. This is a concept the existing chain can actually serve, and that is not an accident of luck: cropping is the resolution strategy.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** FOOD GEOMETRY, ~8 objects, all buildable from the existing toolkit: tortillas as T.revolve discs (folded = two arcs), a paper-lined tray as T.solid_prism over T.rrect (the same call t1_shell.tail_board uses), a lime wedge as a partial revolve, fillings as displaced masses. No new primitive is needed.  ·  SUBSURFACE — AND THIS IS AN ARCHITECTURAL CHANGE, NOT A MATERIAL TWEAK. t1_mats.py line 16 states it as policy: 'NO SUBSURFACE ANYWHERE. Every material sits at Subsurface Weight 0.0', and t1_detail.py sets it to 0.0 explicitly under a comment naming 'verify row 6b'. Lime flesh, tomato, crema and cooked masa are all subsurface materials. Adding the first one in the tree will interact with a verify row. Name the row, watch it, and re-base it with a companion per the project's own rules.  ·  INTERIOR LIGHTING. Owner-licensed by F259 ('Relight the studio and fix the ground if necessary'; the constraint that travels with it is DO NOT change RED). The galley currently has no dedicated practical — a plancha glow and a rack light are new. F45 and F15 must be measured, not assumed.  ·  THE COOK AS A DEFOCUSED WHITE-SHIRT SHAPE — a simple blocked form at the plane of the rack, not an anatomical figure. There are no human figures anywhere in the tree and a badly modelled hand would sink the frame faster than any of the project's open defects. Say so in the brief rather than discovering it in review.  ·  FIX F74(b) IF ANY BOTTLE IS IN SHOT: GAL_RED = (0.5350, 0.3600, 0.3120) ≈ sRGB(196,163,153) is a dusty beige-pink where the photograph shows saturated red and yellow. OPEN since rev 58, and it is the only food-adjacent colour the model owns.  ·  STEAM: 2D for the proof; a Blender volume domain only if it survives an owner review.

**SCREENER.** Builds on the one feature the merch category structurally cannot carry — the three apertures exist because the donor bus arrived missing its windows — and the 1:1 print hung at 1254 mm turns a depiction into a small physical trick. Square-on framing genuinely puts a second person in the picture. But nearly everything in frame is unbuilt: no food anywhere in the mesh, textures or photographs; an interior nobody has ever rendered that may come back dead black; a human figure in a tree with no human figures; and subsurface against an explicit no-subsurface policy. Honest about all of it, which is why it scores here and not lower.

---

## MONOPOLIO — the bus, out of focus, behind the food (THE RISKY ONE) — A 24×36 print in which the sharpest object is a 200 mm tray of tacos at 0.8 m and the photorealistic bus is a soft red-and-cream shape eleven metres behind it, dissolved into nine-sided bokeh off its own bulb string — the meal in front, the vessel behind, and 76 revisions of measured fidelity deliberately thrown out of focus.  ·  screen 7.0  ·  `t10-2`

**THE IMAGE.** FRAMING: 85–100 mm at f/2.8, focused at 0.8 m, camera at 1.15 m — seated height at a folding table, not standing. NEAR FIELD, sharp and filling the lower third: a paper-lined tray at a slight angle, three tacos, a cut lime, a squeeze bottle with a genuinely saturated red cap, condensation on a bottle at the right edge cut by the frame. Off-white paper, a scuffed enamel tabletop. MIDDLE: nothing — a deliberate empty 3–8 m of ground, which is what makes the depth read. FAR: the bus at 11–13 m, angled so the SHOW SIDE and the propped tail board are presented, lid up, counter deployed. It is entirely soft. It is recognisable by three things only: the two-tone break sweeping down the flank, the propped board's 38° diagonal (STATE.md: 'principal-axis chord 0.7110 m, angle 38.0 deg'), and the bulb string.

THE BULB STRING IS THE LIGHT AND THE COMPOSITION. Shot at dusk with the festoon lit, every out-of-focus bulb becomes a NINE-SIDED disc — studio.aim() already sets aperture_blades = 9 and aperture_rotation = radians(11) so 'out-of-focus speculars are 9-sided, not perfect discs', and that machinery has never been used, because hero34f runs 78 mm at f/8 with a hyperfocal I compute at 25.4 m on studio's own COC = 0.030, i.e. everything is sharp and the DOF is decorative. Here the string draws the vehicle's whole silhouette as a broken line of amber discs and nothing else needs to be legible.

PALETTE: warm and narrow. Amber bulbs, warm dark ground, and the food carrying the only fully saturated colour — the salsa red and the lime green are the two highest-chroma objects in frame and everything else is pushed toward dusk. The bus's cream reads as the second-brightest mass and the red goes almost to brown at that exposure.

COMPOSITION: classic near/far, but inverted against every expectation for this asset. The vehicle occupies perhaps 35–40 % of the frame and is NOT the subject. It is context. The tray is the subject and it is unmeasured, invented geometry sitting in front of the most measured object this project owns.

MOTION: 6 seconds, locked. Steam off the tray. The bulb string flickers once on the inverter. A soft shape crosses behind the counter and is gone. Nothing else. A rack focus from the tray back to the bus would be the obvious move and it is WRONG — pulling focus to the bus makes the bus the subject again and undoes the entire idea. Hold the focus on the food for the whole six seconds and let the viewer never get a sharp look at the vehicle.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** YOU ARE ALREADY EATING. Every other image this program can make positions you as someone LOOKING AT A BUS. This one positions you as someone who has already ordered, paid, sat down, and is about to pick up a taco — and the bus is the pleasant thing in your peripheral vision. That is the actual customer experience and no other composition in the brief contains it. The emotional mechanism is the empty middle ground: the gap between your table and the bus is the distance you already walked. And the withheld focus does something a sharp hero cannot — it makes the viewer want to look at the bus and refuses them, which is exactly the appetite you want a poster to leave behind.

**FORMATS.** POSTER, 610 × 915 mm (24 × 36). The near-field food carries the detail; the bus is soft, so it carries almost no resolution demand at all.  ·  LANDSCAPE HERO for web and menu-board headers, 1200 × 800 and up — the widest crop, more empty middle, more of the bulb line.  ·  4:5 and 9:16 SOCIAL crops (1080 × 1350, 1080 × 1920) — this is the only concept in the brief that is natively a phone image, because it is the composition every customer already takes with their own phone.  ·  POSTCARD, 148 × 105 mm, given with the bill. Small, warm, personal, and it is the format the image was born in.  ·  6-SECOND LOOP for social and for the in-store screen.  ·  NOT APPAREL. The image dies below about 200 mm — the bokeh turns to mush and the food loses its read. Say so and do not let it be squeezed onto a tee.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** THIS IS THE PHOTOGRAPH THE REFERENCE SET ALREADY CONTAINS AND THE RECORD HAS ONLY EVER FILED AS A NEGATIVE. I opened ref_nolita_flank.jpg and looked at it: it is a hand holding a bottle of Cerveza Monopolio, sharp, in the near field, with a tray at the lower right, and the bus SOFT BEHIND IT. It is the only consumer-POV image in the whole set, and the project's own records list it purely as 'state B, Nolita, plain red flanks, no folk art, no script' — as evidence about livery, never as a composition. Somebody at Tacombi already took the best photograph of this vehicle and the register catalogued it as a livery sample.

Against the merch category: the entire VW-bus market sells the vehicle SHARP — sunset silhouette, surf wagon, line drawing, blueprint, exploded diagram. Every one of those tropes requires the bus to be legible. Defocus it and none of them can attach. And the thing in sharp focus instead is a taco, which no VW-bus print on earth contains. It also refuses the category's other reflex: this bus is stationary at dusk with a meal in front of it, not driving into a sunset — which is the truth, since the engine was scrapped and the transmission sold.

**PRODUCTION.** ⚠ SAY THE RISK PLAINLY, BECAUSE IT IS REAL. This composition throws away the project's entire investment. Seventy-six revisions of measurement produce an object that is deliberately out of focus, and the sharpest thing in the frame is invented geometry with no reference behind it. A cautious team rejects this in one sentence: 'we spent five months measuring a bus and you blurred it.' That objection is correct on its face and I am proposing it anyway, because the business sells tacos.

⚠ IT IS ALSO A DODGE AND I WILL NOT PRETEND OTHERWISE — BUT IT IS A DODGE THAT PAYS. Three of the four instruments in this tree that compare the model to a photograph fail on properties of a LARGE, SHARP, FLAT BODY, and this frame does not have one: gloss_compare fails at spread 0.412 of the photograph against a 0.60 bar (out-of-focus paint has no specular structure to miss); flank_compare reads the render's red at G/R 0.422 against the photograph's 0.114 (a defocused red at dusk exposure loses most of that gap's visibility, though NOT its truth); and the emblem — the owner's ninth report, with F191 and F234 both standing — is off-axis and dissolved. That is a strategic argument for shipping this concept FIRST, and simultaneously an argument that must be stated out loud rather than smuggled, or it will look like we hid three failing gates behind a lens.

IT INVERTS THE RESOLUTION BLOCKER, WHICH IS THE UNDERRATED PART. Nothing above 1600 × 1100 exists on disk and the MARGIN stitch chain has been proven exactly once at 3840 × 2640 (rev 57, one view, 106.8 min). Every other poster concept in this program is gated on proving that chain at a larger size. THIS ONE IS NOT: the bus is soft, so it needs almost no pixels, and the sharp subject is a 200 mm tray photographed at 0.8 m — a separate, small, near render. This is the only large-format piece here that the existing proven chain can serve today.
