DOF, computed on studio's own dof_limits formula at its COC = 0.030: 85 mm f/2.8 at 0.80 m gives a sharp band of roughly a centimetre — normal food-photography depth, focus on the near edge of the front taco and let the back go. 100 mm f/2.0 at 0.55 m gives a 3 mm band, which is a macro problem, not a food shot; do not go there. aim() reads T1_FSTOP from the environment and PRINTS its own near/far/hyperfocal every frame, so the numbers are reportable rather than claimed.

THE FOOD IS INVENTED — see THE PASS's note; there is no food in the mesh, no food in the textures, and no food in the photographs (I cropped ref_side.jpg's counter and it is dressed and empty). In this concept the invented object is the SHARPEST thing in frame, at maximum size, in a print. If the food is bad the whole picture is bad, and there is no fallback crop. That is the concentrated version of the risk and it should decide whether this concept gets built at all. PUT IT TO HIM WITH A PROOF, not a description.

AND ONE CLEAN INHERITANCE: use the folk-art Playa livery, not the Nolita state the source photograph shows. The two states may never be mixed (rule 11; SPEC §7.1), so this is a picture that has never existed rather than a reconstruction of one that has.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** NEAR-FIELD FOOD, BUILT TO A HIGHER STANDARD THAN CONCEPT 1 — it is the subject and it is sharp at print size. Tray, three tacos, lime, a bottle, a table surface. Expect this to be the expensive item and budget it as such.  ·  SUBSURFACE, MANDATORY HERE. t1_mats.py line 16: 'NO SUBSURFACE ANYWHERE. Every material sits at Subsurface Weight 0.0.' At 0.8 m and f/2.8 on a 610 mm print, a lime with no subsurface reads as painted plastic and the frame dies. This is the single hardest technical requirement in the whole brief.  ·  A NEAR CAMERA. `counter` (90 mm f/6.3, focus 5.30 m) and `playa` (42 mm f/3.5, 'standing AT the counter, eye height... the counter lip in the near field') both exist in views() and NEITHER HAS EVER BEEN RENDERED. Neither is near enough. Add one at 85–100 mm focused at 0.7–1.0 m, and note the rig's own frame-culling helper `_cull_foreground` exists precisely because near geometry gets in the way — read it before placing a table.  ·  FIX F144 — 'THE FESTOON BULBS READ AS PEARLS, NOT LIGHTS'. In every other concept this is cosmetic. Here the bulb string is the background's only light source and the entire bokeh field, so the frame does not exist until the bulbs actually emit. STATE.md has bulb_string | 1, tb_bulbs | 1, tb_bulbflex | 1 and t1_mats has an emissive() builder — push the emission above post.py's own BLOOM_THR and look at it.  ·  A DUSK RIG. Licensed by F259 ('Relight the studio and fix the ground if necessary'), constrained by the same ruling's DO NOT change RED, and NOT to be routed through the in-tree environment path — F57 records that T1_SCENE=playa renders no vehicle at all, 'a dark cylinder on a grey plane', reported and never diagnosed. Build the dusk in the studio rig or composite via T1_ALPHA.  ·  T1_ALPHA if the near field and the vehicle are rendered separately — which they probably should be, since one wants a 0.8 m camera and the other an 11 m one. Note it has not been run since rev 62, every frame in out/ is colortype 2 with no alpha, deliver.py correctly refuses them, and post.py DESTROYS alpha (measured: RGBA in, colortype 2 out), so optics and cut-out are mutually exclusive today.  ·  F74(b) — the squeeze-bottle caps are a dusty beige-pink where the photograph shows saturated red and yellow. Open since rev 58, and in this concept a salsa cap is one of the two most saturated objects in the frame.

**SCREENER.** Contains the sharpest act of seeing in the whole set, and I verified it: ref_nolita_flank.jpg IS the consumer-POV shot and the record only ever catalogued it as a livery sample. The refusal to rack focus is real direction. Two problems. The premise overstates its own source — I opened the frame and the bus is a fairly sharp indoor phone snap, not soft, no dusk, no lit festoon. And it is the least buildable idea here: invented food as the sharpest object at 610 mm, mandatory subsurface, F144's unlit bulbs carrying the entire bokeh field, and alpha compositing that post.py destroys.

---

## No Es Una Fuente — A type-foundry specimen sheet for a wordmark that is not a typeface and can never be one — nine hand-measured glyphs blown up to a metre, each one carrying the coordinates it was traced from.  ·  screen 7.0  ·  `t11-0`

**THE IMAGE.** Full-bleed body red — t1_mats.py's authored RED, sRGB(196,49,36), hue 5.0, sat 0.816 — flat, uncoated, no gradient, no vehicle anywhere in frame. On it, a 3x3 grid of nine cells with generous gutters, each cell a single glyph of the Señor Tacombi lockup lifted straight from script_gen.py's traced outlines and set at roughly 220 mm cap height on an A1: the swash T, the swash ribbon alone, a, c, o, m, b, i, and in the bottom-right cell the whole lockup at small size, arced. The glyphs are metallic silver — script_gen.py measures the real ink at median RGB (127,122,125), saturation 0.080, so this is NEUTRAL grey-silver, never white and never warm gold — and each carries the dark keyline and offset drop shadow SPEC section 3 asks for and the shipped texture does not have. Over each glyph, in 6 pt condensed black caps at 30 percent opacity, its own measurement, verbatim from the source: over the o, "counter x 141-161 y 49-73 / 224 px in a 21x25 box / 43 % fill -- a groove, not a bowl"; over the a, "TWO counters: 69-74 x 65-71 and 72-80 x 76-83"; over the i, "no separate dot -- one stroke, (248,20.6) to (274,39.2), neck half-width 2.2 at (252.8,25.3)"; over the b, "left stem 5.9-7.2, over the counter 1.8-2.8"; over the T, "stem x 33-60, lowest point of the lockup at y 103". Hairline registration crosses at every cell corner, in the same silver. Along the foot of the sheet, one line of 9 pt caps: the per-glyph IoU scores against the photograph, printed as a row of figures — T .981 SWASH .955 A .955 C .964 O .964 M .939 B .948 I .935 LOCKUP .942. Light: none. This is flat spot colour on paper, not a render — the only dimensionality is the physical foil and the deboss.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** Reverence, and the specific pleasure of being trusted with the working. A person feels they are holding the evidence rather than the advertisement — the way a good specimen book makes you want to touch the letter. The emotional turn is in the bottom-left cell: the a, which the record shows has TWO counters where any mechanical construction can only make one. That is the moment the viewer understands a human being painted this with a brush, on a bus, in Playa del Carmen, and that nobody has ever been able to reduce it to a rule. Pride without a superlative — which is exactly the brand's existing deadpan register.

**FORMATS.** A1 poster, 594 x 841 mm portrait, on heavy uncoated 300 gsm warm white. UNCOATED IS THE WHOLE POINT: a spot vermillion hits full chroma on uncoated stock in a way CMYK on gloss cannot, and it sidesteps the render's measured red problem entirely (flank_compare on out/r75_side.png reads the render at G/R 0.422 against the photograph's 0.114 — it images salmon). Two spot inks plus a metallic silver foil block for the glyphs. The foil is not decoration: the real ink is silver leaf and the owner ruled it 'Bright silver, same as Tacombi' (F157).  ·  A2 open edition, 420 x 594 mm, same separation without the foil — silver ink rather than foil — for the shop.  ·  16 pp saddle-stitched specimen booklet, 148 x 210 mm, riso or two-colour litho: one glyph per spread, its measured control points on the verso, the glyph on the recto. This is the object a family keeps.  ·  Left-chest apparel: ONE glyph only, at 90-110 mm, printed at final size and never scaled down from the poster art. The lockup's own stroke widths on the vehicle are p10 3.95 mm / p50 19.54 mm; at a 240 mm chest print (1:5.3) the p10 is 0.74 mm and everything survives, but at left-chest scale the whole lockup dies and a single glyph does not.  ·  Hard-enamel pin, 22-28 mm: the o's spiral counter alone. Two colours, and the 43 percent fill reads as a real void at that size.  ·  Inside-neck woven label and a hem tag carrying the swash T alone.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** The VW-bus merchandise market is entirely pictorial — sunset, palms, surfboards, line-art vans, blueprints. There is no typographic entry in it at all, because a stock bus has no lettering worth blowing up. This poster contains no vehicle, no silhouette and no VW mark, so it is invisible to both the trope and the trademark question at once. And it cannot be copied: the artwork is nine traced outlines whose control points were read off ref_side.jpg at 6-14x magnification, which is 75 revisions of somebody's actual labour. A competitor can buy a VW bus vector in ten seconds; they cannot buy this a with its two counters.

**PRODUCTION.** Spot colours must be DECLARED, not sampled off a render — the project holds four unreconciled reds (t1_mats sRGB(196,49,36); lid_gen (159,45,22); cal_gen (214,46,30)) and probe_rev71_red.py prints its own prohibition, 'the photograph's red G/R is 0.0149 .. 0.0344, a 2.3x span. DO NOT QUOTE A SINGLE FIGURE FOR IT.' Pick one, record it as a design ruling with provenance, and stop. Silver must be foil or a metallic ink, never grey ink at saturation 0.080 — flat grey on red is the exact failure the missing keyline already produces. Set the caption figures in a neutral grotesque; do NOT set them in a display face, and do not set the wordmark itself in any font: the owner rejected a system script by name at rev 8 ('That script i see on the p9 hero is NOT it') and SPEC section 10.10 names 'a system font with flourishes bolted on' as the failure mode. Artwork is vector-from-outline, so this ships with zero dependency on the render chain, on T1_ALPHA, or on any resolution above 1600x1100. Honest ceiling: the per-glyph IoU figures are the generator's own acceptance scores against ref_side.jpg, not a claim about the printed sheet; and flank_compare's live region scores on out/r75_side.png read i at 0.686 of its own ceiling and b at 0.747, both under the 0.75 bar, so those two glyph outlines should be re-checked against the photograph before they are printed a metre tall.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** RESTORE THE DARK KEYLINE AND DROP SHADOW TO script_gen.py, and regenerate tex/senor.png BY HAND (script_gen.py is not called by build.py, so the render otherwise silently keeps the old texture). This is not new design work — it was authored and then lost: the REJECTED font-based generator sign_gen.py still carries KEYLINE = (46, 44, 46), KEY_HC = 0.030, THICK_HC = 0.014 and SHADOW_DX_HC, SHADOW_DY_HC = 0.020, 0.026, and its docstring opens 'Silver hand-painted script with a dark keyline and a drop shadow.' When rev 10 replaced it with traced outlines the keyline did not survive the rewrite. Measured on the shipped tex/senor.png (4096 x 1738 RGBA): of pixels with alpha > 200 the 1st-percentile luma is 168.3 and the fraction below luma 80 is 0.0000 — there is no dark ink in the asset at all, against SPEC section 3's 'SILVER with a dark keyline and drop shadow'. Add the guard in the same edit: a row that reads that sub-luma-80 fraction and goes red if it returns to zero.  ·  FIX THE LOCKUP'S PLACEMENT ON THE FLANK. flank_compare on out/r75_side.png prints 'the render's lockup has to move +76.2 mm forward and -47.6 mm down', with an independent cross-check agreeing at +83 / +80 mm aft and the ink top +61.1 mm high. Placement survives every downscale, so it is visible on any product that shows the flank.  ·  SCOPE F157 EXPLICITLY IN THE COMMIT: the owner ruled the WORD 'Senor' to bright silver (luma 117.1 -> 201.1 against a clean silver of 210.9) and TARNISH_ZONES is not 'Senor' — the b flag, the i dot and the swash keep their measured tarnish. Brightening those to match would exceed the ruling. And do not tune the wordmark toward the photograph: F156 records that flank_compare's Senor row now scores a DELIBERATE DEPARTURE, so 'improving' it reverses an owner ruling and no gate in the tree will say so.  ·  NOTHING ELSE. No render, no Blender, no alpha, no stitch. This concept is deliberately built to be shippable while F191 and F193 hold the delivery render.

**SCREENER.** A specimen sheet for a mark that can never be a typeface is a genuinely good fit for a hand-painted wordmark, and the 'a' with two counters where any mechanical construction makes one is a real emotional turn — the moment a viewer understands a person painted this with a brush. Ships flat under the hold and repairs a measured defect (the keyline lost when traced outlines replaced sign_gen.py). Marked down because the specimen sheet is a designers'-in-joke genre a jaded AD has seen plenty of, and the Magritte title is borrowed wit rather than invented.

---

## LA COMBI PICADA — the bus as a hole — Hand-chiselled papel picado in which the vehicle is entirely negative space, strung across the taqueria, and the real deliverable is the coloured bus-shadow it throws on the floor at four o'clock.  ·  screen 7.0  ·  `t13-1`

**THE IMAGE.** THE RULE IS THE DESIGN: papel picado is SUBTRACTIVE. A chisel drives through 40-50 stacked sheets of tissue at once. Every mark is a hole; nothing floats; anything not connected to the sheet falls on the floor. So the whole drawing has to be re-authored as a connected web — every stroke of the folk art becomes a bridge holding the sheet together.

HERO FLAG: 400 x 300 mm tissue, landscape, hung from a twine hem. The bus in negative — the windows, the wheel arches, the gaps between the folk-art strokes, and the shadow under the vehicle are all cut AWAY; what remains as paper is the body. From two metres it reads as a bus; from ten it reads as lace. Vehicle at 1:10.2.

THE THREE HATCHES ARE THE LARGEST HOLES ON THE SHEET and they sit dead centre. STATE.md: `open serving apertures on +Y: 3`, `bay widths 0.516 0.515 0.516`. At 1:10.2 each is 50 x 40 mm of pure absence with a 55 mm-radius corner reading 5.4 mm — comfortably cuttable, and the biggest single void in the flag.

THE SEQUENCE ALONG THE STRING, alternating: (1) the bus, side elevation; (2) the three hatches ALONE — three rounded rectangles in an otherwise plain sheet, the most abstract flag on the string and the one that becomes the brand's mark; (3) the wheel bullseye, four concentric rings; (4) one rosette at 200 mm, i.e. drawn UP four times from its real 68 mm (folk_gen.FLANK_ROSETTES, largest 3632 mm2) so every petal survives; (5) the bulb string as a wavy line of round holes; (6) '1963' cut as four numerals inside a chrome-frame outline. Repeat.

COLOUR: the vehicle's own five, run down the string in fixed order so the sequence reads as a repeating colour bar — vermillion, cream, gold, dark maroon, cobalt. DO NOT go rainbow. Standard-issue rainbow tissue is exactly what makes papel picado read as generic party goods, and it is the one decision that would sink this.

THE ASYMMETRY TELL: real papel picado is folded before cutting, so it is nearly always bilaterally symmetric. Ours cannot be — a bus has a front and a back. Lean into it. Cut flat, unfolded, one sheet at a time if that is what it takes. The asymmetry is what makes a passer-by look twice.

MOTION, AND THIS IS THE PAYLOAD: hang the string in a window facing west. At 4 pm the sun throws the flags onto the floor and the wall — a four-metre bus, in coloured light, 200 mm tall, wobbling in the draught every time the door opens. The holes are the drawing; the shadow is the drawing inverted. Film it: locked-off camera, a taqueria floor, fifteen seconds, one shot, no cut, no music, room tone only — chairs, a griddle, Spanish. Coloured bus-shadows sliding across tile as the string turns. That is the animation, and it needs no 3D animation whatsoever.

SECOND MOTION BEAT, two seconds, for a logo sting: a single flag turns edge-on and the bus vanishes to a line.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** Fiesta — and the particular melancholy inside fiesta, which is that papel picado is made to be destroyed. It goes up for a festival and comes down in rags a week later. Rendering the brand's founding object in the most temporary material available says something no permanent object can: we are open today, this is lunch, nothing here is a monument. It is the deliberate inverse of the tile concept. And the shadow does the emotional work — a child eating a taco looks down and there is a red bus on the floor moving slightly, and it is not on a screen, and it will be gone when the sun moves. People photograph that. Nobody photographs a t-shirt.

**FORMATS.** Real strung banners: 400 x 300 mm flags on cotton twine, 10 m runs, in every restaurant, made by a papel picado taller in San Salvador Huixcolotla — the town that is the craft's centre and holds its denominacion. Chisel-cut tissue, not laser.  ·  Retail box: a 3 m string, five flags, tissue, in a flat card sleeve with the cutting explained on the inside. Cheap, giftable, and the box is where the origin story goes.  ·  One flag mounted between two sheets of low-iron glass, 500 x 400 mm framed. Turns a disposable object into a keepsake, which is precisely the tension the material already carries — and it is the SKU that justifies a real price.  ·  Print: the flags photographed BACKLIT on white so the paper glows and the holes go pure white. A2 poster, tote, tissue wrap, cup sleeve, menu cover. A backlit photograph of cut paper is unmistakably a photograph of an object.  ·  Motion: the 15-second floor-shadow loop (locked-off, one shot) plus the 2-second edge-on logo sting. Both are camera work, not render work — they cost a tripod and an afternoon.  ·  Die-cut vinyl derivative at 100 mm, cut path derived from the same connected web. STATED PLAINLY: this is NOT the F18 sticker and does not re-put the F18 papel-picado question.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** The entire VW-bus merchandise market is ADDITIVE — printed, drawn, embroidered, foiled onto something. This one is a hole. And the match is earned rather than decorative: the vehicle's signature feature is three MISSING windows (SPEC.md §0.1, graded S — the donor bus was 'missing three of its windows' and Wolos 'figured out how to cut open the Tacombi'), and papel picado is a craft that consists of nothing but what has been removed. Nobody has ever made VW-bus merchandise you can see through. It also refuses the category's whole emotional stock in one move: sunset, surf and open-road art all sell permanence-of-freedom, and this object is explicitly disposable. The floor-shadow film in particular has no competitor — there is no VW-bus asset anywhere whose deliverable is a shadow.

**PRODUCTION.** HONESTY ABOUT PRIOR ART, STATED UP FRONT: F18 locks papel picado into the die-cut sticker's scene and the owner ruled 'Leave it open, I'll decide when the sticker is actually being built. DO NOT RE-PUT IT UNTIL THEN.' THIS IS NOT THE F18 STICKER. It is a banner, a poster and a film, it is offered as a different artefact, and it does not re-put that question. Separately and more importantly: SPEC.md measures that there is NO papel picado in the reference photograph — 'The band across the top of the reference is a continuous flowering mass — 55.1 % foliage, 13.4 % crimson heads, 5.5 % cream florets, jagged interpenetrating lower edge, no flag silhouettes. Bunting in that render would be an invention.' So this concept IS invention. That is legitimate for a derived craft object and it must NEVER be composited into a render of the vehicle in its scene, or we have faked the reference.

CUT ENGINEERING — the numbers that decide whether it can exist: at 1:10.2 the arch-to-tyre gap (STATE.md, `rear arch lip above hub 0.3722 m ... -> tyre gap 39.7 mm`) is 3.9 mm of tissue, which a chisel can hold. The propped tail board is a 22 mm-thick blade on a 0.7110 m chord (t1_shell.py TB_T, TB_CHORD), reading 2.2 mm thick and 70 mm long — marginal, and it must be tied to the body at both ends or it tears. Bridge the underbody with the ground line, exactly as AUDIT_rev43.md's surviving sticker row already says: 'DIE-CUT OUTLINE — bridge the underbody with the cast shadow, and let no feature narrower than 0.159 m touch the cut line.'

THE FOLK ART MUST BE REDUCED, AND THE MEASUREMENT SAYS BY HOW MUCH: the gold's stroke width on the vehicle runs p10 2.66 mm / p50 13.15 mm / p90 45.07 mm. At 1:10.2 that is p10 0.26 mm — a chisel cannot clear a 0.26 mm bridge in tissue, so those strokes are not thin lines, they are torn paper. Delete the 48 components that carry 2.29% of the gold and keep the three that carry 84.4% (folk_gen.COMP_HIST, COMP_TOP). Measured licence, not taste.

PROTOTYPE ON PAPER BEFORE ANY VENDOR CALL: cut one flag by hand at 400 mm with a scalpel and hold it up to a window. Everything about this concept is decided by whether the web holds and whether the shadow reads. Both are answerable in an afternoon.

TISSUE: standard papel picado stock is thin and fugitive — colours shift in a week of direct sun. That is correct for the restaurant banners and wrong for the framed SKU; use an archival tissue or a light Japanese kozo for the framed one and say on the label that the restaurant version is meant to fade.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** RUN T1_ALPHA=1 ONCE — this is the single blocking dependency and it has been un-run for thirteen revisions. Every PNG in out/ is colortype 2 with no alpha; studio.py only sets RGBA `if _alpha_delivery()`; no LEDGER_rev*.md contains the string T1_ALPHA. Run live, deliver.py prints 'CONTROLS: 4 checked, 1 FAILED -- C1' and 'PACKAGE IS NOT TRUSTWORTHY'. One env var, one side render. Watch C1 go green — that is the watched kill this capability has never had on a live tree.  ·  DO NOT ROUTE THE ALPHA THROUGH post.py. Measured: RGBA in (colortype 6), colortype 2 out — it reads `rgba[..., :3]` and saves three channels. The optics chain and the cut-out chain are mutually exclusive in this repo and post.py's docstring nowhere says so. For this concept you want the raw alpha and no optics at all.  ·  BUILD THE CUT PATH AS A MORPHOLOGICAL CLOSING, NOT A CONTOUR TRACE. Close the alpha mask at a radius stated in FINAL millimetres (r = 1.5 mm / scale), then trace. trace_outline.py's `trace_with_holes()` already exists and its header says 'selftest() checks the tracer on shapes whose area and perimeter are known BY CONSTRUCTION'. Add a guard that MEASURES minimum notch width and minimum internal radius along the produced path in final millimetres, and watch it go RED on the raw silhouette before believing it green on the closed one.  ·  NO EMBLEM WORK NEEDED — the side elevation does not contain the nose roundel, so this concept ships while the owner's ninth report is still open. Do not add a front-facing flag to the string.  ·  deliver.py's trimmed/ output is already the die-cut geometry (F161) and its shadow/vehicle split is measured — the vehicle side is 97.1% fully opaque and 99.99% ONE connected component. That single-component property is exactly what papel picado requires. Reuse it rather than re-solving it.

**SCREENER.** 'The deliverable is the coloured bus-shadow on the floor at four o'clock' is a genuinely good inversion, the subtractive craft rhymes honestly with a vehicle whose signature feature is three MISSING windows, and the folded-symmetry asymmetry tell is a real craftsman's observation. But papel picado is the most generic possible signifier of 'Mexican restaurant' and the concept is fighting its own material; the best flag (three hatches alone as the brand's mark) is buried at position 2 behind the obvious one; and its gold stroke-width triple (2.66/13.15/45.07 mm) appears in no file I could find.

---

## HORARIO — The opening hours of a taqueria, drawn as sixteen identical elevations of the same vehicle in which only the lid, the bulbs and the light have moved.  ·  screen 7.0  ·  `t14-0`

**THE IMAGE.** A grid — 4×4 on a landscape sheet, or a single column of sixteen on a tall one — of the SAME orthographic side elevation, repeated once per hour from 06:00 to 21:00. Registration is perfect to the pixel, because `studio.views()['side']` is `lens=None, ortho=5.90` at camera z 1.52: a true parallel projection of one unmoved mesh, so every cell is mathematically superimposable on every other. Nothing about the body, the wheels, the script or the folk art shifts by a millimetre across the sheet. Four things, and only four things, change from cell to cell: (1) LID ANGLE — 0° shut, cracking at 07:00, propped at 76° from 08:00, back down at 21:00; (2) TAIL BOARD — stowed, then out at its measured 38°; (3) BULB STRING — dark until 18:00, then the ~115 bulbs at 28.6 mm pitch lit warm amber (the shipped emission is (1.000, 0.760, 0.442)); (4) THE LIGHT — cool and flat and shadowless at dawn, neutral and overhead at noon with the contact shadow pulled tight under the tyres, low and warm at 18:00 with the shadow raking out of the cell, and after 20:00 the bulb line as very nearly the only source, the cream going blue-grey and the red going almost black except where the string touches it.

Composition: one flat ground colour across the whole sheet, cells separated by a 0.4 pt hairline, no boxes. Beneath each cell, the hour in small condensed caps — 06:00 · 07:00 · 08:00 — nothing else. Two cells carry one extra word set in the same size: the first reads CERRADO, the cell at 08:00 reads ABIERTO. The vehicle's palette is held absolutely constant so the eye reads the light changing rather than the paint; the ground and the shadow do all the temporal work.

Motion version: the same sixteen cells as a 16-frame loop at 1 fps, hard cuts, no tweening — a stepped time-lapse where the subject is nailed down and the day slides over it.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** Recognition, and the specific pleasure of a timetable. You do not look at this and think about a van; you look at it and find the hour you go. It is the feeling of a shop you know the hours of — belonging rather than aspiration. And because the bus is pinned identically in all sixteen cells, the sheet quietly says the thing the brand's own founding account says: the engine was scrapped and the transmission sold. This object does not go anywhere. The day goes past it.

**FORMATS.** B1 poster, 707 × 1000 mm, portrait, single column of sixteen — uncoated 200 gsm, litho or spot-colour screen. Uncoated because the piece is a document, not a photograph, and a matte surface kills laptop/wall glare on a large flat field.  ·  A2, 420 × 594 mm, 4×4 grid — the shop-wall and shipping-friendly size. At A2 each cell is roughly 95 mm wide, which puts the whole 4.065 m vehicle at about 1:43; at that scale the lid, the tail board, the counter and the bulb line all read and the folk art correctly reduces to two gold masses, which is what folk_gen.py's own component census says it should (three components carry 84.4 % of the gold).  ·  Tea towel, 500 × 700 mm, 16 cells, 3-colour flat screen on natural cotton — a genuinely useful object (Test 2: it dries dishes), and the timetable is at home in a kitchen.  ·  Apron bib panel, single row of six cells across 300 mm — the staff wear the hours.  ·  16-frame loop at 1 fps, 1080 × 1350, for social and for the in-store screen.  ·  Woven hem label / cuff tape: the bulb line alone as a repeat, 12 mm deep.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** Nobody makes a timetable of a VW bus because no other VW bus has opening hours. The entire category — sunset, surf, palms, peace signs, blueprint — sells motion and escape; this sells a shift. It is also structurally impossible to photograph: a camera cannot hold sixteen exposures over sixteen hours with zero registration error, and no photograph is orthographic, so the cells could never be genuinely comparable. The grid is only available to someone who has spent seventy-five revisions measuring the object, which converts this project's actual asset — measurement — into the reason the picture exists. And it carries no VW roundel: the side elevation does not contain the nose mark at all, only four 1.6 mm hubcap badges at sticker scale, so this ships while the emblem is still the owner's ninth open report.

**PRODUCTION.** Hold the vehicle's palette FIXED across all sixteen cells and let only the ground, the shadow and the bulb emission move — if the body colour drifts cell to cell the whole conceit collapses into sixteen different pictures. Do not sample the brand red off a render: flank_compare on out/r75_side.png reads the render at median (196,81,67) against the photograph's (158,16,6), i.e. it images salmon; declare one red as a design ruling with its own provenance and print that. Set the hours as figures only, in a condensed grotesque at 7–8 pt, and resist adding any caption — the piece dies the moment it explains itself. On the tea towel and apron, reduce to three inks (cream ground, red, dark) and let the garment carry the fourth; on the poster, spot colour on uncoated will reach a chroma CMYK cannot, which matters because the whole sheet is one large flat field. Print the 20:00–21:00 cells as a genuinely dark ground with the bulb line as the only bright ink — do not compromise them to match the daylight cells' legibility.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** **LID_W MUST BE DECOUPLED FROM LID ANGLE — this is the blocker and it is one line.** `t1_shell.py` derives `LID_W = (LID_X0 - LID_X1) / LID_ASPECT / math.sin(math.radians(LID_OPEN_DEG))`, so sweeping `T1_LIDDEG` INFLATES the mural board as it closes and divides by zero at 0°. Freeze LID_W at its shipped 76° value, add `T1_LID_WCOUPLE=1` to restore the old behaviour, and add a guard row that reads the built panel's width off the mesh at three angles. Watch it go red on the coupled build first.  ·  The lid's `assert _top.co.y > LID_Y_HINGE + 0.010` ("leans over the counter") is degenerate near 0° — the topmost-vertex pick becomes float ordering on a flat panel. It needs a closed-case branch with the cause named in the row's own label, not a relaxation.  ·  A BULBS-OFF ARM. `M["bulb"]` is `emissive("bulb", (1.000, 0.760, 0.442), strength=_bs)` with `T1_BULB_STR` scaling it; strength 0 leaves an emissive shader at zero rather than an unlit glass bead. Needs a real dark material so the 06:00–17:00 cells read as bulbs that are off, not as bulbs that are missing.  ·  A COLOUR-TEMPERATURE AXIS ON THE RIG. `rig(key=...)` and `T1_KEY` scale POWER only; there is no colour parameter, and `studio.py`'s own docstring records that the studio has **NO SUN** — "The brightest ground patches sit only +5 L* over their shadows ... there is no sun/skylight pair." Sixteen hours of light needs a directional key whose azimuth, elevation and colour all move. F259 is the licence — the owner ruled "Relight the studio and fix the ground if necessary" — with its own constraint attached: DO NOT change RED.  ·  `T1_TB_TILT` already exists (ships 38.0) so the tail board sweep is free; `T1_TRUNK_OPEN` does not exist and `TRUNK_OPEN_DEG = 0.0` is hardcoded on a lid whose lateral hinge is already solved — two lines would add a fourth moving element.  ·  Render budget, arithmetic on the brief's own ~5.5 min/frame at 1600×1100/96 spp: sixteen cells ≈ 90 min sequential. Do NOT fan out Blender — it is CPU-bound on four cores and two instances make both slower.

**SCREENER.** Nobody makes a timetable of a bus because no other bus has hours — and the perfect ortho registration converts this project's actual asset into the reason the picture exists, which is the best justification-of-medium in the set. But 4x4 grids of one subject under changing light is a well-worn conceptual device, sixteen ~95 mm buses at A2 is small, the emotional claim is clever rather than moving, and it needs the LID_W decoupling plus a colour-temperature axis the rig does not have plus ~90 min of render.

---

## THE DAY IS A SHADOW — One frame. The bus rendered once and razor sharp; underneath it, a whole day's worth of its own shadows accumulated into a single soft fan, so the ground becomes a sundial and the vehicle becomes the gnomon.  ·  screen 7.0  ·  `t14-1`

**THE IMAGE.** Square, or 4:5. Flat ground, no horizon, no backdrop furniture. The vehicle sits centred and slightly low in the frame, deployed — lid propped, tail board out, counter dressed, bulbs faintly warm — occupying about 55 % of the frame width so there is real air around it. It is rendered ONCE, at one exposure, with one neutral key, and it is completely sharp.

Beneath and around it: a fan. Twelve to twenty-four separate contact-shadow passes, each cast from a different sun azimuth across the service day, summed and normalised into a single accumulated field. Directly under the vehicle every hour's shadow overlaps and the ground is dense and dark; as the fan sweeps out east and west it thins to almost nothing at the extremes. The edges are soft where the count is low and hard where it is high, so the fan has visible grain to it — you can read individual hours near the extremes and only a mass near the middle. The sweep is deliberately ASYMMETRIC: opening is not the mirror of closing, and the fan should be wider on one side.

Palette: three things. The ground is a single warm neutral, taken from the one reference frame in the set with a genuinely neutral white balance — ref_playa_34.png's paving reads (116,119,120). The shadow accumulation is a multiply of a warm dark, never black — it is a shadow on a hot pavement, not a hole. And the vehicle keeps its own declared palette at full chroma, so it sits inside the fan like an object dropped into water.

No type on the image at all. If anything is set, it is one line of small figures in a bottom margin outside the picture: the hours the fan spans.

Motion version: fifteen seconds, no camera move whatsoever. The frame opens empty and grey. The first shadow lands with a soft fade. Then the next, and the next, each one arriving on a slow beat, the fan thickening under the stationary vehicle. When the fan is complete it holds. Then, last, the bulb line ignites nose-to-tail and the frame goes warm. Cut.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** Stillness — and then, half a second later, the small shock of realising what you are looking at. A whole day passed across this thing and it did not move. That is the brand's entire thesis delivered without a word of copy, and it lands as calm rather than as a claim. It also carries a working person's fact underneath the poetry: shadows accumulate like that because somebody stood there all day.

**FORMATS.** 1:1, 1000 × 1000 mm, gallery print, limited edition on heavy uncoated rag — hand-numbered in pencil, authenticated by a BLIND EMBOSS chop rather than a signature (a render has no artist's hand and a forged signature on CGI is embarrassing). Spot gloss varnish restricted to the bulb line only, so the one warm element catches the actual light in the actual room.  ·  4:5 and 1:1 digital, 2160 px, for social and for phone lockscreen — the composition survives cropping to a tall phone because the fan is horizontal and the vehicle is centred.  ·  A2 open edition, 420 × 594 mm landscape, matte-coated litho — the affordable tier.  ·  15-second loop, 1080 × 1350 and 1920 × 1080, for the in-store screen and for social. Silent; the sound design, if it ever gets one, is a bulb inverter ticking.  ·  Beer mat / coaster, 95 mm round: the FAN ALONE, no vehicle, one ink. The shadow is a good abstract mark at 95 mm and it is the piece's own signature.  ·  Tote gusset or a hem panel where the fan runs as a horizontal band.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** Every VW-bus poster in existence puts the vehicle inside a landscape it is passing through — sunset, road, palms, surf. This does the exact inverse: it takes all the time out of the vehicle and puts it in the ground. The bus is the only stationary thing in a picture entirely made of change, which is a photographic idea (long exposure) executed in the one medium where it is actually achievable on a subject like this. It is also natively CG in a way that is a virtue rather than a tell: no photographer can composite twenty-four registered shadow passes under a fixed subject, and the pipeline already separates exactly that layer — deliver.py writes `combi_<view>_shadow_only.png` per view, measured near-black (max channel < 12/255) against a vehicle mean of 143, and visibility_budget ranks the contact shadow as the SINGLE LARGEST item in the delivery frame at 3.83e6 px². The biggest object in every frame this project has ever made has never once been designed with. And it contains no VW mark and no VW silhouette in its most reproducible form — the beer-mat version is pure shadow.

**PRODUCTION.** Render the VEHICLE ONCE and never again — every shadow pass must be composited under that single sharp master, or the accumulated fan will carry the render's own noise floor (~2.04 % of pixels moving >8 levels at 1600×1100/96 spp; no Cycles seed is set) and the bus will boil while the shadow builds. Normalise the accumulation rather than straight-summing it, or the centre clips to solid and you lose the density gradient that IS the picture. Keep the shadow warm — a neutral or cool shadow reads as studio, and the whole point is that it reads as a day. Resist the urge to add a sun, a sky or a horizon: the moment the frame explains where the light comes from, the sundial reading evaporates and it becomes an ordinary product shot with a long shadow. For the gallery tier, print at 200 dpi minimum on the long edge, which at 1000 mm wants ~7900 px — well beyond anything this chain has ever produced, so either commission the proving run or cap the honest edition at A1.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** **T1_ALPHA MUST ACTUALLY BE RUN — it has been un-exercised for thirteen revisions.** Every PNG in `out/` today is IHDR colortype 2 (RGB, no alpha) and `deliver.py` correctly refuses them: `CONTROLS: 4 checked, 1 FAILED -- C1` / `PACKAGE IS NOT TRUSTWORTHY`. No LEDGER contains the string. Run it, watch C1 go green, and name the frame.  ·  **DO NOT ROUTE ANY OF IT THROUGH post.py.** Measured: RGBA in (colortype 6), colortype 2 out — `post.py` reads `rgba[..., :3]` and saves three channels, so the documented optics chain silently destroys the exact alpha this concept is built on. Optics and cut-out are mutually exclusive from this repo and nothing says so.  ·  **THE STUDIO HAS NO DIRECTIONAL SUN AND THIS CONCEPT NEEDS ONE.** `studio.py`'s own docstring records the finding: "NO SUN. The brightest ground patches sit only +5 L* over their shadows ... db* +2.4: the SHADOWS ARE WARMER. A sun/skylight pair has the opposite sign by construction, so there is no sun/skylight pair." A movable directional key must be added. F259 licenses precisely this — the owner ruled "Relight the studio and fix the ground if necessary" — and its stated constraints travel with it: the studio stays the delivery environment, and DO NOT change RED.  ·  The shadow passes must move the key WITHOUT moving the vehicle's own lighting — otherwise the composite is a lie and the bus's exposure will drift against its own master. Either render shadow-catcher-only passes with the vehicle's beauty lighting held, or accept and declare that this is a composite rather than a single render.  ·  `T1_SCENE=playa` is NOT the route: F57 records it renders no vehicle at all — "a dark cylinder on a grey plane", reported and never diagnosed. Build the fan in the studio and composite.  ·  Render budget: twelve passes ≈ 66 min sequential at preview settings, twenty-four ≈ 2.2 h. Sequential only — do not fan out Blender.

**SCREENER.** Best-grounded concept in the set — the contact shadow really is rank 1 at 3.83e6 px^2 and really has never been designed with, and the gnomon inversion is elegant and natively CG in a virtuous way. But accumulated sun-path shadow fans are a known trope, it carries the same thesis as HORARIO more prettily and less specifically, and strip the bus out and it is a handsome poster of any object. Needs the sun studio.py says does not exist.

---

## Monumento a la Combi, 10:1 — Cast the bus in raw concrete at exactly ten times size — 45.8 metres long, four wheels 6.6 metres tall, a plaza continuing underneath it — and present it as a real civic competition entry, with no red, no folk art and no VW roundel anywhere on the board.  ·  screen 7.0  ·  `t16-2`

**THE IMAGE.** An architectural competition board, A1 portrait, printed in ONE INK: a flat concrete grey. Top two-thirds is the side elevation, orthographic, at 1:200 — the monument 203 mm long on the sheet, drawn as an unpainted concrete monolith. Every surface texture of the vehicle survives at ten times size and becomes architecture: the louvre block reads as a brise-soleil, the V-swage as a shadow gap raked across the flank, the three serving apertures as three vast openings 13.07 metres up, the propped roof lid as a 31.3-metre cantilevered canopy. Four figures, 1.75 m tall, stand at 8.75 mm on the sheet: two under the belly in the 3.18 m of shade beneath the rocker, one at the front wheel which rises 6.649 m over their head, one alone in the plaza. Beneath the elevation, a section cut through the front axle showing the paving running unbroken under the vehicle, and a plan at 1:400. Down the right margin, dimension lines carrying figures that are just STATE.md's own numbers with the decimal point moved one place — 40.650 / 17.500 / 19.833 / 24.000 / 6.649 / 31.320. The ONLY colour anywhere on the board is a second pass in warm amber: the festoon string, drawn as a continuous 45-metre line of 250 mm globes tracing the drip rail, over the rear roof dome, down the propped board's free edge. Title block, Spanish first: MONUMENTO A LA COMBI · ESCALA 10:1 · HORMIGÓN VISTO · PLAYA DEL CARMEN. Beneath it, small: 'Motor: ninguno.' If it moves at all: one four-second beat at dusk, the light going, and the amber line igniting nose to tail. Nothing else in the frame changes. It never drives, because it cannot.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** Awe first, held just long enough to be sincere, then the laugh. The board is played completely straight — the register is competition-submission deadpan, the drawing is real orthographic projection, the numbers are real — so for four or five seconds a viewer believes they are looking at a proposal, and feels the actual thing a monument is for: something enormous, permanent, and civic, made out of a working object that ordinary people used. Then they read 'Motor: ninguno' and get it. What survives the laugh is the argument: this is the brand's own origin story taken absolutely literally. A vehicle that was driven once, had its engine scrapped and its transmission sold, and stopped so completely that it became a building. The monument is not a joke about the bus. It is the bus's actual biography, at a size where you can walk under it.

**FORMATS.** Competition board, A1 594 × 841 mm portrait, TWO screens on heavy uncoated 300 gsm rag: concrete grey for everything, warm amber for the bulb line alone. Screen-print rather than litho because a flat solid grey laid down as ink on uncoated rag has the exact matte density of fair-faced concrete, which four-colour process cannot reach.  ·  Folded A3 proposal document, 8 pp, 100 gsm uncoated or newsprint — plan, four elevations at 1:200, one section through a wheel, and a page of nothing but the dimension schedule. Newsprint because a proposal is ephemeral and a monument is not, and that tension is the whole piece.  ·  Single-ink tote, 380 × 420 mm print area, concrete grey on natural canvas: the side elevation at 1:200 with the 1.75 m figure at the front wheel, 8.75 mm tall. The joke lands at bag scale precisely because the human being is smaller than the maker's mark.  ·  A2 420 × 594 mm print of the SECTION alone — the plaza running unbroken beneath the monument, 3.18 m of clearance under the rocker, four 6.649 m wheels standing in it. This is the sheet that ships today: at 3840 px the chain already gives 232 dpi at A2, where A1 wants 4677 px and one proving run nobody has done.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** There are giant VW bus sculptures in the world and every one of them is PAINTED, cheerful, and unmistakably a VW. This is grey, silent, unbranded and slightly forbidding. Rendered in bare concrete with no paint, no folk art, no script and — with the roundel objects switched off — no Volkswagen mark of any kind, a 45-metre monolith with three doorways cut in its flank and a canopy overhead stops reading as a van at all and starts reading as brutalist civic architecture. That is simultaneously the creative argument and the trademark argument: it is the least VW-forward artefact the program can make, and the most ownable, because the only things left in the drawing are the three hatches, the counter, the lid, the louvres and the bulb line — none of which Volkswagen ever built. It also inverts the category's entire emotional stock in one move. Every other bus in this market is going somewhere; ours weighs four thousand tonnes.

**PRODUCTION.** THIS IS THE RISKY ONE AND A CAUTIOUS TEAM KILLS IT ON SIGHT, for three predictable reasons, all of which should be met head-on rather than designed around. (1) 'Where is our red?' — it is deliberately absent, and its absence is what makes the piece land; the brand's colour returns everywhere else in the program. (2) 'People will think we are actually building it.' Yes, for about five seconds, and that credulity IS the mechanism; the title block should not wink. (3) 'It is grandiose for a taco company.' It is grandiose ABOUT A FOOD TRUCK, which is the opposite thing, and the deadpan register is what keeps it on the right side of that line — no superlatives, no manifesto copy, nothing but drawings and dimensions. Choose 10:1 and nothing else: at exactly ten, every figure on the board is STATE.md's own machine-written measurement with the decimal moved, so there is no arithmetic anywhere in the piece and nothing can go stale silently. Do not print any figure that is not in that file. And do not put the tail board's WIDTH on the board — t1_shell.py carries `TB_WIDTH = 0.5500  # POSE CHOICE, NOT MEASURED`, with an admissible upper bound of 0.59 m and no lower bound at all, so dimensioning it at 5.5 m would publish a pose as a measurement on a sheet whose entire authority is that it does not do that.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** FIX THE ORTHOGRAPHIC CLIPPING FIRST — this blocks the whole elevation series. studio.views() sets front and rear to ortho=3.55 with sensor_fit HORIZONTAL, so at 1600×1100 the vertical extent is 3.55 × 1100/1600 = 2.4406 m, covering roughly z 0.30 to 2.74, while the vehicle spans 0 at the tyres to STATE.md's 'bbox top 3.132' with the lids up. Both ends are cut. Derive ortho_scale from build.py's own printed bbox plus a stated margin, and add a guard row asserting zero ink on all four border rows of any ortho frame — watched going RED at 3.55 before it is believed passing.  ·  RENDER `rear` AT ALL AND LOOK AT IT. It has zero rendered-file mentions across LEDGER_rev66..75. A four-elevation board cannot be designed against a camera nobody has ever produced a frame from.  ·  Use T1_CLAY — it exists and is wired: studio.py's clay_all(rgb=(0.62, 0.62, 0.63)) at studio.py:838 and 863. Note this DELIBERATELY INVERTS the standard warning that clay-shading 'hands back a grey van': here the grey van is the artefact. (T1_BAREMAT is not a clay switch — build.py uses it to reproduce a rev-48 defect. Do not reach for it.)  ·  ADD A SWITCH THAT REMOVES THE VW MARK, and note that none exists today: build.py runs `vr, vd = D.roundel(...)` and appends the glyph bars unconditionally, and T1_VW_NOARC / T1_VW_TRACED / T1_VW_FREE / T1_VW_CAPMIN all change how the mark is CONSTRUCTED — not one of them removes it. The hubcap glyphs (capvw, 8 objects) have no switch either.  ·  Draw the human figures. There are no figures anywhere in the tree.  ·  For A1: export T1_FX=0 by hand before any strip render — grep -c T1_FX hq_render.py returns 0, where the chain it replaced forced optics off per strip precisely because they band at the seams — and READ stitch.py's printed exit code, which is 2 on a seam. The MARGIN chain has been proven exactly once, at 3840×2640, at rev 57. A2 ships today; A1 is one proving run away; B1 is not currently supported by anything.  ·  The bulb line is drawn, not rendered, so F144's 'the festoon bulbs read as pearls, not lights' does not block this piece — but do not claim the render produced it.

**SCREENER.** Impressive and completely committed — the decimal-shift trick means every dimension is machine-written with no arithmetic in the piece, and grey/silent/unbranded is the correct inversion of a category where every giant bus sculpture is painted and cheerful; but scale-joke-as-monument is a familiar art-director move, the laugh actively undercuts the awe it spends four seconds building, and it is the biggest lift by far (ortho clip fix, human figures that exist nowhere in the tree, and a VW-removal switch it correctly notes does not exist).

---

## LA APERTURA — The Unfolding — A repeat in which almost nothing is finished: six frames of the bus opening, tiled as a field, so that a wall or a sheet of paper is the same building unfolding over and over — and printed on the vehicle's own 1.02° rake, so the whole pattern is very slightly, deliberately, crooked.  ·  screen 7.0  ·  `t18-2`

**THE IMAGE.** A chronophotographic repeat. Six poses of the same orthographic side elevation, in order: (1) SHUT and unlit — lids down, tail board stowed, bulbs dark, a plain two-tone van; (2) lid cracked ~15°, a black wedge appearing along the roof; (3) lid at ~40°, the mural's edge just catching; (4) lid at the shipped 76°, mural fully presented, tail board still down; (5) tail board propped out at its measured 38.0° chord 0.7110 m, rear hatch swung to its declared 64°; (6) BULBS LIT — the festoon running the drip rail, over the rear roof dome and along the tail board's free edge, the only warm light on the sheet.

Rows read left to right and wrap: a flipbook that is also a field. Frame 1 is nearly black-and-quiet; frame 6 glows. So the pattern has a slow luminance pulse across it, and at four metres you see rhythmic bands of dark and warm rather than any vehicle at all.

THE CROOKED PART, AND IT IS THE POINT: the entire lattice sits on the vehicle's own measured rake — `rake 17.75 mm/m (locked 17.75)` = 1.02°. Not five degrees, not a jaunty tilt. One point oh two. It is exactly the amount that reads as a trimming error rather than a decision, and holding it is what makes the sheet feel hand-laid instead of stepped-and-repeated. Every row descends 17.75 mm per metre travelled. A print buyer will flag it as a fault. It is not a fault.

LIGHT: frames 1–5 flat and shadowless like the other concepts. Frame 6 is the exception and must be lit for it — the bulb string pushed above post.py's `BLOOM_THR = 0.94` so it actually flares, key dropped, everything else falling to near-silhouette. One warm tile in six.

PALETTE: the sequence is a value ramp before it is a colour scheme. Ground `W_DUST_COL_UP` sRGB(189,165,133). Bodies in RED sRGB(196,49,36) and CREAM sRGB(206,208,200) throughout. The mural's own inks arrive progressively as the lid rises — `lid_gen.ORANGE = (163, 66, 21)`, `GOLD = (172, 119, 24)`, `CREAMV = (232, 224, 198)` over `DARKLINE = (72, 26, 16)` — so the pattern's chroma literally opens with the roof. Frame 6 adds one bulb amber, and that ink appears nowhere else on the sheet.

SCALE: 1:60, so a fully deployed unit is 76.3 × 52.2 mm. Six frames per row on a 500 mm sheet at an 82 mm pitch.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** ANTICIPATION, MULTIPLIED — and it is the only concept here that puts TIME in the paper. Wrapping a present in a pattern of something being opened is a small, precise joke that a child gets instantly and an adult gets a beat later. Under that sits the tenderness of watching a shut, ordinary van become a lit room with a painted ceiling, and then watching it happen again, and again, all over the sheet.

It is also the only artefact in the programme that says the brand's actual founding fact out loud without a caption. Tacombi's own account, graded S in SPEC §0.1: "The engine was scrapped, transmission sold, seats swapped for cooking equipment, and the roof was reeled back." This object cannot drive. Its entire motion vocabulary is OPENING. Every VW-bus pattern on earth animates the wheels; this one animates the roof, because that is the only thing on this vehicle that still moves.

And the 1.02° is what makes it feel like a thing rather than a file — that faint, wrong, hand-cut lean that says a person laid this out.

**FORMATS.** Wrapping paper, 500 × 700 mm, 1:60 — unit 76.3 × 52.2 mm, six frames per row at 82 mm pitch, row advance 62 mm, whole lattice on 1.02°. THE PRIMARY FORMAT: skew is native to wrapping, so the rake reads as charm rather than as a mis-trim.  ·  Tissue paper, 500 × 750 mm, and shipper void-fill — the pattern is at its best crumpled, where the sequence breaks into fragments.  ·  Silk or modal scarf, 900 × 900 mm, 1:45 — the sequence spiralling from the centre outward, so the bus opens as the scarf unfolds. The single most desirable object in the whole programme.  ·  Garment lining and jacket facing, 1:70 — the pattern only its wearer sees, which is the strongest version of the insider test.  ·  Book endpapers, menu inside covers, bag lining, gift-box interior — every one a surface revealed by opening something.  ·  Kraft paper tape, 48 mm, one frame per 82 mm, so a taped seam is a strip of animation.  ·  EXPLICITLY NOT WALLPAPER. At 1.02° over a 2.7 m drop the pattern walks 48 mm across the wall, which on a fixed vertical butt joint reads as a hanging fault and will be rejected on site. If a wall version is wanted, it is a different concept with the rake set to zero — and it loses most of what makes this one good.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** The VW-bus merchandise market has no temporal patterns of anything. Not one of the documented tropes — sunset silhouette, surf, flower-power, 70s wash, minimal line art, blueprint, boho, pop-art — has a time axis; every one of them is a static object repeated. This is a repeat whose subject is a VERB.

More than that, it is a verb no other bus can perform. A stock VW bus drives; that is the only motion available to it, and it is the motion the whole category sells. Ours has no engine and no gearbox, so the only thing it can do is open — a fact that is published, sourced, and unfalsifiable. The pattern is therefore literally uncopyable: a competitor drawing the same sequence would be drawing a lie about their own product.

It also breaks the two conventions that make patterns look like stock. Stock repeats show the product in its hero state; five of our six tiles show it unfinished. Stock repeats are square to the sheet; ours is 1.02° off. Both are exactly the moves a cautious team removes, and removing either one collapses it back into an ordinary tile.

The emblem and the trademark exposure are absent for the same reason as the other two concepts — side elevation only, no nose in frame.

**PRODUCTION.** THIS IS THE RISKY ONE AND HERE IS WHY A CAUTIOUS TEAM KILLS IT. Five of six tiles show the product NOT in its hero pose. The lattice is deliberately 1.02° off square. And it is the only concept requiring a source-code fix to a constant nobody has touched. Any one of those is enough for a nervous approval to sand it flat — into six identical open buses, square to the sheet, which is concept 1 done worse. Ship it whole or don't ship it.

THE FIXED GRAIN SEED IS FATAL HERE AND MUST BE HANDLED. post.py's `grain(srgb, amount=1.0, sigma=0.0042, seed=7, ...)` uses `rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)` — a constant. Six tiles through that path carry byte-identical noise, which in a tiled field becomes a visible ghost lattice at the tile pitch, cross-cutting the intended 1.02° one. For flat print the right answer is to skip post.py entirely: no bloom, no chromatic aberration, no vignette, no grain. The one exception is frame 6, whose bulb flare is the whole point — bloom that tile alone, or better, draw the glow.

DO NOT PASS ANY TILE THROUGH post.py IF YOU NEED THE CUT-OUT: measured, RGBA in / colortype 2 out. Optics and silhouette are mutually exclusive from this repo and nothing documents it.

COLOUR: as concepts 1 and 2 — declare the palette, never sample the render (salmon, G/R 0.422 against 0.114), never average the photographs (2.3× span, prohibition printed by the probe itself).

PRINT: 6 spot flats plus one bulb amber. Frame 6's glow should be a printed halo, not a screened gradient — a gradient at 1:60 will break up. On tissue, drop to 4 inks and let frames 1 and 2 go to a single dark.

SEQUENCE ORDER IS A CONTENT DECISION, NOT A LAYOUT ONE. Left-to-right ascending reads as opening. Descending reads as closing time, which is a different and colder product. Pick deliberately.

A CHEAPER v1 IF THE LID FIX IS NOT AUTHORISED: run the sequence on the TAIL BOARD and the BULBS only — board stowed → propped at 38.0° → bulbs lit — a three-frame ladder that needs no LID_W work at all. It is less spectacular and it is genuinely good, and it lets the concept prove itself before anyone touches the shell code.

NOT F18, NOT THE DELIVERY RENDER. Say so explicitly. F191/F193 hold the delivery render; F18's trigger holds the sticker; neither covers a wrapping paper.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** THE LID_W COUPLING — THE ONE HARD BLOCKER, AND IT IS ONE LINE. t1_shell.py: `LID_W = (LID_X0 - LID_X1) / LID_ASPECT / math.sin(math.radians(LID_OPEN_DEG))`. The lid's WIDTH is derived from its opening ANGLE as 1/sin, because the width was solved from the photographed projected width. Computed from the live constants (LID_X0 0.9640, LID_X1 −1.0700, LID_ASPECT 1.7130, numerator 1.18739): 76° → 1.2237 m (shipped), 72° → 1.2485, 60° → 1.3711, 45° → 1.6792, 30° → 2.3748, 10° → 6.8379, and 0° is a ZeroDivisionError. So a naive `T1_LIDDEG` sweep does not close the lid — it INFLATES the mural board until it is longer than the vehicle. FIX: freeze LID_W at its shipped 76° value of 1.2237 m as a constant, add `T1_LID_WCOUPLE=1` to restore the coupling, and add a guard row that READS THE MESH (not a grep) asserting the built panel's width does not move when the angle does. WATCH IT FAIL on the coupled build before believing it green.  ·  AND IT FAILS EVEN SOONER THAN THAT — THERE IS A HARD ASSERT. Immediately below the derivation sits `assert LID_W <= _LID_W_MAX + 1e-9` with the message "LID_W = %.4f m runs the roof aperture %.1f mm PAST the roof edge". The source's own historical failure string quotes "LID_W = 1.7469 m runs the roof aperture 467.2 mm PAST the roof edge", which implies a bound near 1.2797 m — and 1.18739/1.2797 gives sin(a) = 0.9279, i.e. a ≈ 68.1°. CEILING ON THAT NUMBER: I computed the coupling from the live constants but did NOT run the build, so 68.1° is inferred from a quoted historical message, not measured this session. What is certain is that the shipped 76° sits close to the bound and the build will REFUSE long before the lid is shut. Measure the actual bound before budgeting the sequence.  ·  THE 'LEANS OVER THE COUNTER' ASSERT IS DEGENERATE NEAR FLAT. `assert _top.co.y > LID_Y_HINGE + 0.010` picks `_top = max(main.data.vertices, key=lambda v: v.co.z)`. Near 0° the panel is flat and which vertex wins is float ordering. The guard is correct for a POSED still and wrong for a SWEPT sequence: it needs a closed branch with the cause named in the row's own label, plus a companion row — not a relaxation.  ·  T1_ALPHA ON EVERY FRAME OF THE LADDER, and none of them through post.py. All out/ frames are colortype 2 and the switch has not been run since rev 62.  ·  FRAME 6 NEEDS THE BULBS TO ACTUALLY READ AS LIGHTS. OPEN_FINDINGS F144 stands: "THE FESTOON BULBS READ AS PEARLS, NOT LIGHTS." The emitters exist (STATE.md `bulb_string | 1`, `tb_bulbs | 1`, `tb_bulbflex | 1`) with `T1_BULB_STR` and `T1_BULB_BASEV` as live ablations, and t1_mats has an `emissive(name, colour, strength=8.0, ...)` builder — so the lever is there and unused. Alternatively draw the glow in 2D, which for a flat print is the better answer anyway and needs no model work at all.  ·  THE THREE-FRAME FALLBACK NEEDS NOTHING BUT AN ALPHA RENDER: board stowed / propped at TB_TILT_DEG 38.0 / bulbs lit. Prove the concept there first.

**SCREENER.** A repeat whose subject is a verb is a genuinely fresh pattern structure, and 'the only motion this vehicle has left is opening' is true, sourced and uncopyable; the 1.02° rake taken from the vehicle's own measured rake is exactly the right size of deliberate error. Fatal friction: the LID_W 1/sin coupling inflates the board to 6.84 m at 10° and hits a hard assert long before shut, five of six tiles are off-hero so a nervous approval sands it into concept t18-0, and it honestly flags its own 68.1° bound as inferred rather than measured.

---

## BAJA DEFINITIVA — Certificate of Permanent Retirement — A deadpan vehicle-deregistration document for a bus that was killed so a restaurant could live, with the date of death and the date of opening printed as the same day.  ·  screen 7.0  ·  `free1-2`

**THE IMAGE.** One sheet, portrait. Heavy cream laid stock, warm — the colour of an old logbook, never white. The whole thing is set as a FORM: thin dark-maroon box rules, field labels in small condensed caps (the vehicle's own civic voice, the hand that wrote '267 / ELIZABETH STREET / NUEVA YORK' on the counter fascia), and every entry filled in BY HAND in dark ink, uneven baseline, one genuine crossing-out.

The fields, down the sheet:
CLASE DE VEHÍCULO — Volkswagen Type 2 · 1963
COLOR AL INGRESO — verde
PRECIO — $3,000 USD
ESTADO — faltan tres ventanas / missing three windows
MOTOR — desguazado / scrapped   ← struck through with ONE ruled red line
TRANSMISIÓN — vendida / sold   ← struck through with ONE ruled red line
ASIENTOS — retirados; sustituidos por equipo de cocina
TECHO — abierto
ÚLTIMO VIAJE — Ciudad de México → Playa del Carmen
FECHA DE BAJA — 06.02.2006
FECHA DE ALTA — 06.02.2006   ← the same date. That is the poster.
USO ACTUAL — taquería

Top left, in the box where a registration document carries the vehicle photograph, a 60 mm orthographic side elevation, flat, printed in the single dark ink, no colour, reproduced badly the way documents reproduce photographs. It is the only picture on the sheet.

Bottom right, a real rubber stamp in red, physically stamped, slightly off-register, riding over the ruled line: BAJA DEFINITIVA / PERMANENTLY RETIRED, with 1963 in the middle. Beneath it a blind emboss — three rounded rectangles in a row, no words, no ink, just pressure.

Footer, 7 pt, the only sentence on the sheet: «Sigue abierto.» / 'Still open.'

Three colours and an absence: cream, one dark ink, one red, one emboss.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** You laugh, and then it lands about two seconds later when you notice the two dates are identical. It is a document about a machine being destroyed, issued cheerfully by the people who destroyed it. That reframes the entire brand away from nostalgia — this is not a memory of a bus, it is the paperwork of a decision. Something died so lunch could happen. And it is FOUND paper, which is the most keepable object class there is: people frame documents they would never frame a poster of. A person who has eaten there reads it as a private joke about a place they love; a stranger reads it as a beautiful piece of officialese. Nobody reads it as an advert, because adverts do not admit their hero is dead.

**FORMATS.** Poster 400 × 560 mm, litho or letterpress on 300 gsm cream laid, hand-stamped in red, blind-embossed chop, edition of 250, hand-numbered in pencil, NOT signed — there is no hand to sign a document  ·  A6 card 105 × 148 mm, one ink, no stamp, tipped free into every merchandise order  ·  Garment hang-tag 40 × 70 mm carrying only the two dates and the struck ENGINE line  ·  Inside-neck print, 60 mm: MOTOR: DESGUAZADO, struck through. Nobody sees it but the wearer  ·  Table card / menu reverse, 120 × 200 mm, at every location  ·  10-second social film: the form fills field by field on a static sheet at typewriter pace, the red line strikes ENGINE, then TRANSMISSION, then the stamp lands and the sheet jumps two pixels. Cut to black. No bus, no music, one sound — the stamp

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** Every VW-bus artefact on the market sells the same promise: that the bus still drives, and could take you somewhere. Sunset, surf, palms, the open road. This is a legal notice that the bus will never drive again, and it is the only thing in this entire program that a competitor cannot fake, because every field on it is Tacombi's own published account — SPEC §0.1 grades it S: 'The engine was scrapped, transmission sold, seats swapped for cooking equipment, and the roof was reeled back'; the founder's own $3,000, green, 'missing three of its windows'. A stock seller's whole business model is the opposite claim. It is also the anti-CGI move in a program built on a 3D asset: the sheet is 90% type, and the render appears once, small, in one ink, badly — which is exactly how much a document trusts a photograph.

I'll name the contradiction rather than hide it: this is NOT the F18 sticker and must never be offered as it. F18 is locked to 'cartoon with rendered depth' and 'nothing but the bus, die-cut tight, plus the sun and the papel picado', for children at the restaurant. This is the adult artefact, for a different person in a different room, sharing none of those locks and gated by none of F18's trigger.

**PRODUCTION.** THIS IS THE RISKY ONE AND HERE IS WHY A CAUTIOUS TEAM KILLS IT: it is a death certificate issued by a food brand; it is almost all type, on a program whose premise is a photoreal model, so somebody will ask why we built the model at all; it is Spanish-first with English small; mock official documents make legal people uneasy; and it puts the word 'scrapped' into a customer's hands. Every one of those is true and none of them is a reason not to make it. It is the single most ownable object in the brief.

DO NOT PRINT A SINGLE DATE OR FIGURE WITHOUT THE OWNER CONFIRMING IT. SPEC §0.1 has an internal tension of its own — it records the bus bought in Mexico City and 'Opened 6 February 2006' in the same passage — and public web summaries contradict it outright (one says founded in a Kombi in Mexico City in 2005). A document poster with a wrong date is the worst artefact this program could produce, and the entire concept rests on two dates being identical. Confirm the pair with him, in his own words, before art is begun. Note also that the Spanish field labels are my construction, not the vehicle's — the bus's own Spanish is 'Gracias por su Preferencia', '100% Calidad', 'BIENVENIDOS'; have a native Mexican signwriter or copywriter set the form's language, because a form is exactly where a non-native register shows.

The blind emboss chop is the authentication, not a signature and not a certificate card: an authorless work should be authenticated by pressure, and it gives the buyer something to run a thumb over.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** One orthographic side elevation, flat, single ink, 60 mm at 300 dpi ≈ 710 px — well inside everything that exists. Use the `side` view (ortho 5.90) with the border-ink guard from the 1:1 concept applied, because at this size any framing error is proportionally enormous.  ·  DECISION, and I'd take the second option: a certificate of retirement implies a CLOSED bus, and the closed bus has never been rendered in 76 revisions. Rendering it means fixing `LID_W = (LID_X0 - LID_X1) / LID_ASPECT / math.sin(math.radians(LID_OPEN_DEG))`, which couples the lid's WIDTH to its angle as 1/sin — the lid inflates from 1.2237 m at the shipped 76° to 2.3748 m at 30° and raises ZeroDivisionError at 0°. Freeze LID_W at its shipped value behind an ablation (`T1_LID_WCOUPLE=1` restores), add a guard that the panel's width does not move when the angle does, and watch it fail on the coupled build first. The `assert _top.co.y > LID_Y_HINGE + 0.010` will also fire near closed and needs a closed branch with the cause named.  ·  OR — and this is better — use the OPEN elevation and let the copy carry it: 'USO ACTUAL: taquería' printed beside a bus that is plainly serving is the sharper joke, and it removes the LID_W dependency entirely. Ship this version; fix LID_W anyway, because every other concept in the program wants it.  ·  No alpha, no stitch, no gloss work, no red decision, no emblem. This concept is deliberately almost free of model dependencies — which is both its production virtue and, fairly, the reason someone will argue it does not belong in this program.

**SCREENER.** The two identical dates — FECHA DE BAJA and FECHA DE ALTA — is a real beat, and a death certificate issued by the people who did the killing reframes the brand off nostalgia in one move. But deadpan mock-officialese is a well-worn craft-brand genre, and the concept concedes it barely uses the 3D asset, which in a program premised on the model is a fair strike. Correctly flags that SPEC §0.1 contradicts itself on the founding date and that the whole poster rests on two dates being identical, so it must be confirmed with the owner before art.

---

## THE COUNTER IS THE HORIZON — Shoot the bus from one metre off the ground — a child's eye — where the counter is a ceiling, the wordmark is at face height, and the flowers were painted exactly where a four-year-old is looking.  ·  screen 7.0  ·  `free2-0`

**THE IMAGE.** Camera at z ≈ 1.00 m, level, no tilt, 50–65 mm, standing about 1.6 m off the flank on the serving side. What that gives you, and every one of these heights is off STATE.md or the source constants, not off a render: the frame is filled bottom-to-top by RED. The brass-nosed counter slab (CNT_ZT 1.2540 un-dropped, which the source comment says reads 1.240 m above ground) crosses the top quarter as a hard bright horizontal with the whole world happening above and behind it — a forearm, a steel caddy, the bottom 30 mm of a lit bulb — all sliced off by the counter's own edge. The belt line (1.207 AG) sits a hair below that, so the cream is a sliver at the top of frame and the picture is essentially a two-tone: a warm bright bar across the top, everything else vermillion. Dead centre, at the optical middle of the frame, is the silver 'Señor Tacombi' lockup: build.py places it at z 0.4453–0.9896 in the geometry frame, roughly 0.40–0.95 m above ground once the rake drop is taken off, so its midline lands at about 0.68 m — precisely the eye height of a toddler and the chest height of a six-year-old. Around it, gold folk art, which t1_mats binds over the red only, i.e. entirely below 1.207 m. At the left edge, a rear tyre as a black wall: TYRE_D 0.6649, so its crown is at 0.665 m — above the child's shoulder. Its hubcap is a 274 mm red dome centred at 0.332 m (CAP_EMBLEM_D/2 = 0.043429 → CAP_D = 0.2740), which at this camera reads like a face looking back. Light: warm open shade with a pale upward bounce off paving — ref_playa_34.png is the one white-balance-neutral frame we hold (paving 116,119,120), so grade to it, and never to the warm ref_side.jpg. Depth of field shallow enough that the counter's upper world is soft and only the flank is sharp. Palette: authored RED sRGB(196,49,36) as the field, GOLD (0.8600,0.5400,0.0600) as the ornament, script silver measured by script_gen as neutral RGB(127,122,125) at saturation 0.080, black tyre, one warm cream bar. Nose, roundel, headlamps, mural lid: all out of frame by construction. A companion set of five more stations along the same 1.00 m line — the wheel arch, the underside of the brass nosing, a hubcap square on, the V-swage apex at 0.340 m, the tail board's diagonal seen from beneath — makes it a series rather than a picture.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** Being small, and being about to be handed something. The counter stops being furniture and becomes a horizon — a line above which the adult world is transacting and below which you can only wait, and look. The catch in the throat is the measurement itself: the wordmark and the entire folk-art field are painted between 0.4 and 1.2 metres, which is to say a signwriter in Playa del Carmen decorated the exact band of that vehicle that a child stands in front of. Nobody has ever said that out loud because nobody has ever put a camera there. Adults get a memory of queueing for food they couldn't see; kids get a picture where the good stuff is at their height for once. And there is a second, quieter feeling underneath: everything above the line is happening without you, and you can hear it.

**FORMATS.** Restaurant PLACEMAT / tray liner, 420 × 297 mm, litho on uncoated — the flagship. The child sits down and the picture is at their height twice over.  ·  Board book, 200 × 200 mm, 12 spreads, one child's-eye station per spread, no text or four Spanish words a spread. This is the 'something families keep' object and it is not a sticker.  ·  Kids' menu cover, A4 folded.  ·  In-restaurant wall band, printed at 1:1, hung with its centreline at 1.00 m so it continues the real bus's own band around the room at the height it actually occupies.  ·  Large print for the wall, 700 × 1000 mm portrait — one station, cropped hard.  ·  Postcard set, 148 × 105 mm, six stations. Cheap, giveaway, collectable.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** Every VW-bus image in the merchandise ocean is shot at or above adult eye level, three-quarter front, whole vehicle, sunset behind. This one is below the counter, cropped so hard the vehicle is unrecognisable as a vehicle, and taken from a height no photographer bothers with. It also removes the two most generic and most legally exposed elements — the nose silhouette and the VW roundel — not as a compromise but because a metre off the ground you genuinely cannot see them. Verified against the tree: all 14 declared cameras in studio.views() sit at z ≥ 1.52 m, so in 76 revisions this project has never once looked at its own vehicle from below adult eye height. The seam is real, not rhetorical. NOTE ON F18: this is a DIFFERENT ARTEFACT from the die-cut sticker. It shares F18's audience — 'for children at the restaurant', 'should spark joy and be something families keep' — and nothing else. It is photoreal, not 'cartoon with rendered depth'; it is a cropped POV, not 'nothing but the bus, die-cut tight'. It does not consume F18's trigger and must not be offered as F18.

**PRODUCTION.** Grade to ref_playa_34.png only. Do NOT sample the brand red off any render — flank_compare on out/r75_side.png reads the render at median (196,81,67), G/R 0.422, against the photograph's (158,16,6), G/R 0.114; it images salmon, and the red is two-thirds of every frame in this series. Equally, do not average the photographs: probe_rev71_red.py prints its own prohibition, 'the photograph's red G/R is 0.0149 .. 0.0344, a 2.3x span. DO NOT QUOTE A SINGLE FIGURE FOR IT.' Declare one brand red as a design ruling with provenance and note that the tree currently holds four unreconciled reds (t1_mats sRGB(196,49,36), lid_gen (159,45,22), cal_gen (214,46,30)). For print, litho on uncoated stock or five-ink spot — AUDIT_rev43 establishes the vehicle as 'ONE 70° hue wedge plus four neutrals', so a spot separation hits chroma CMYK cannot reach and, usefully, carries no gloss, which sidesteps the fact that gloss_compare reads the render's paint spread at 0.412 of the photograph against a 0.60 bar. Eye-height figures for children are standard growth arithmetic, mine, not measured here; the vehicle heights are all from STATE.md and the source. The 1.240 m AG counter figure is quoted from t1_detail's own comment, not re-derived.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** Add a `child` camera to studio.views() at z ≈ 1.00, plus four or five stations along that line. Nothing below 1.52 m exists — I checked all 14 dicts.  ·  FIX THE WORDMARK'S MISSING KEYLINE. It is the hero object of this concept and SPEC §3 requires 'SILVER with a dark keyline and drop shadow'. tex/senor.png has no dark ink at all: among pixels with alpha>200 the 1st-percentile luma is 168.3 and the fraction below luma 80 is 0.0000. Fix in script_gen.py using lid_gen's own authored DARKLINE (72,26,16) — and REGENERATE BY HAND, because script_gen.py is not called by build.py.  ·  RESOLVE THE LOCKUP'S PLACEMENT before shooting it at hero scale. flank_compare says the render's lockup must move +76.2 mm forward and −47.6 mm down; an independent route reads +83/+80 mm aft. Two routes agree on direction; settle the magnitude.  ·  REDRAW THE FLANK FOLK ART. At this crop the art IS the picture, and the shipped tile is measurably more fragmented than the photograph: 196 gold components with the top three carrying 66.4 %, against folk_gen's own photograph census of 66 components with the top three carrying 84.4 % (COMP_TOP = {1: 47.8, 2: 73.7, 3: 84.4}). The generator's own header says coverage is a marginal and 'the same 10 % gold over the same flank can be one bouquet or five hundred commas'. Redraw from ref_side.jpg at magnification.  ·  F16 is open and lands here: 'every flank rosette is drawn at the diameter of its gold core' — i.e. systematically undersized. Visible at this crop.  ·  THE HUBCAP IS A HERO OBJECT AND ITS BADGE IS UNVERIFIED. STATE.md: 'hubcap badge is SELF-CONSISTENCY ONLY -- CAP_EMBLEM_WFRAC has never been compared to a frame; see PHOTOS_WANTED item 7.' Either close item 7 with one close square-on hubcap photograph, or crop the badge out and shoot the rim.  ·  The salmon red is the whole lower two-thirds. Per the record this is a grade/light decision, not a shader fix, and F259 ('Relight the studio and fix the ground if necessary') licenses the rig — with its constraint attached: DO NOT change RED.

**SCREENER.** Contains the single best insight in the batch — the signwriter decorated exactly the 0.4-1.2 m band a child stands in front of, an emotional claim derived from measurement rather than asserted over it. Verified the seam is real: no camera in the tree sits below 1.52 m. But free3-0 executes the same low-eyeline idea harder and with one committed image where this diffuses into six stations, and this one carries more expensive prerequisites (folk-art redraw, lockup placement unresolved between two disagreeing routes).

## NINE TIMES — Print the project's own failure: the nose at near-life size, overlaid with every measurement window ever painted on it — including the wrong ones — and the nine dates the owner said it still isn't right.  ·  screen 7.0  ·  `free3-2`

**THE IMAGE.** One very large sheet, 700 × 1000 mm, portrait. GROUND: the nose in flat orthographic projection, cream, no perspective, no gloss, printed at roughly 1:1.5 so the 0.2800 m roundel lands at 187 mm — big enough that a viewer can see the strokes disagree. It is deliberately dead: no highlight, no shadow, no atmosphere, matte litho on uncoated cream stock. OVER IT, screen-printed in one hit of 0.3 mm fluorescent-magenta hairline, every measurement window the project has ever painted on this object, all at once, overlapping and not agreeing: the ring-band annulus, the interior mask, the six cream cells with their elongation axes, the pose-fit bounding box, the C4 landmark ladder with its L1–L6 tick marks — AND THE WRONG ONES, given equal weight and equal ink: the 'roundel' window that landed on the V and W strokes, the 'emblem' window that landed on the rim ring, the box that clipped 14 % of the mark off the bottom. Each window carries a 6 pt label: its revision, its date, and its number.
