THE COUNTER NOSING RIBBON is the sheet's only structural rule and it is 2.5 m long in reality — at 700 mm sheet width that is 1:3.6, so its brass profile stays fully legible. Use it as the baseline everything else aligns to.

STATE THE CEILING IN THE COLOPHON, same as Section AA: fore-aft and vertical positions are measured; depths are the model's choice. On this sheet the note costs almost nothing, because the objects are shown out of the vehicle anyway.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** REAL FORM FOR THE STAND-IN OBJECTS — approximately eight to ten of the twenty-eight are currently primitive boxes/slabs that will not survive being shown alone at poster scale. Named: `gal_tool` ×6, `gal_can_u` ×3, `gal_can_l` ×2, `gal_stack` ×2, `gal_caddy_fill` ×2, `gal_appliance`. This is new geometry and it is the concept's real cost.  ·  AN EXPORT PATH, AND THIS IS THE CONCEPT THAT FINALLY JUSTIFIES WRITING IT. There is no 3D interchange export anywhere in the tree — grep for export_scene / gltf / usd_export / fbx returns nothing. The only persistence is build.py's `if os.environ.get("T1_SAVE"): bpy.ops.wm.save_as_mainfile(...)`, `.blend` is gitignored, and T1_SAVE appears in ZERO handoff documents. A flat lay needs every object individually posed and lit, which means either a .blend to work in or a per-object render harness. Write the export, and record it in a carrier by name so it is not lost again.  ·  A PER-OBJECT RENDER HARNESS: each named mesh isolated, re-posed to lie flat, lit by one shared soft top source, rendered with alpha and a soft contact shadow as a separate layer. deliver.py already proves the shadow can be separated cleanly — `combi_%s_no_shadow.png` / `combi_%s_shadow_only.png`, with the vehicle side measured "97.1 % fully opaque and 99.99 % ONE connected component" — so the mechanism exists; it needs pointing at objects instead of at the whole vehicle.  ·  `T1_ALPHA=1` EXERCISED AND VALIDATED, as for Section AA. Twenty-eight cut-out objects is precisely the case the switch was built for and it has not been run since rev 62.  ·  A DIMENSION EXTRACTOR: a small script that imports the constants and prints each object's bounding box in millimetres, so the captions regenerate rather than rot. Two dozen typed figures on a printed sheet is two dozen chances to publish a stale number.  ·  NOTHING ELSE. Notably: no emblem work, no nose work, no red-colour work, no stitch-chain proving at delivery size (the objects are small and can be rendered individually at high resolution), and no dependency on the held delivery render.

**SCREENER.** The tea towel is the right physical object and the inversion — putting the vehicle's objects onto an object rather than the vehicle onto one — is a genuinely good line. But knolling and the captioned finds tray are exhausted trends, and the proposed dodge (loose bands, warmth, dry captions) is a temperature change, not a different idea. It is also the worst value for money: it honestly flags that gal_tool ×6, gal_can_u ×3, gal_can_l ×2, gal_stack ×2, gal_caddy_fill ×2 and gal_appliance are boxes and slabs that read correctly at 1.5 m behind a 515 mm aperture and will read as boxes at 200 mm on white paper — a large new-modelling bill to serve a mid-strength concept, even granting the spillover to the two section pieces.

---

## STILL OPEN — A near-black plate in which the bus is small, parked, and the only light in the world — a picture about being fed late by people who have not gone home yet.  ·  screen 6.0  ·  `t2-0`

**THE IMAGE.** FORMAT 3:1 landscape. Ninety per cent of the frame is empty near-black — not pure black, a warm-neutral floor around #0B0908 so ink and screen both have somewhere to sit. The vehicle occupies roughly the lower-right third, small, seen from the SERVING side (+Y) at rear-three-quarter, camera at ~1.55 m eye height — the same height REF_MEASUREMENTS.md independently derives for the reference photograph ('a pinhole model with camera height ~ 1.55 m, horizon y ~ 340') and the same height AUDIT_rev43 specifies. No horizon, no sky, no street, no palms, no people. FRAMING LOGIC: three lines carry the whole composition and nothing else is allowed in — (1) the drip-rail festoon, a 3.5 m horizontal of ~115 warm beads at a measured 28.6 ± 1.0 mm pitch (t1_detail.py: 'FFT along the string in ref_side.jpg … pitch 28.6 +/- 1.0 mm, ~115 bulbs'); (2) the counter's brass nosing, one hard horizontal specular, the only cool-bright element in the frame; (3) the propped tail board's 38.0° diagonal (STATE.md: 'tail board pose … principal-axis chord 0.7110 m, angle 38.0 deg') running out of the mass top-left, carrying its own lit string (tb_bulbs). LIGHT: every lit thing in the frame is lit BY THE VEHICLE. The festoon is the key, at its authored emission colour (1.000, 0.760, 0.442). Behind the three serving apertures the galley glows a paler, cooler warm — steel and cream bouncing a practical, not a lamp aimed from outside — and spills three soft trapezoids onto the ground beneath the bays. PALETTE INVERSION, and this is the point: at this exposure the body RED collapses to a dark maroon mass, roughly #3A0F0A, and the CREAM becomes the only large surface that reads, taking a strong amber cast from the string above it — call it #C9A97B rather than its daylight (206,208,200). Four values, one hue family, which is AUDIT_rev43's measured 'ONE 70° hue wedge plus four neutrals' still holding at night. MOTION ARM (optional, 6 s, static camera): black; one bead catches; the string ignites left-to-right over ~1.2 s RAGGEDLY, two or three lamps out of order, because that is what a festoon on an inverter actually does; the bays come up half a second later; hold. Nothing else moves — no wheels, no drive-by, ever.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** The relief of finding somewhere still open. Not nostalgia, not escape — arrival, and being fed. The category's entire emotional stock is a bus going somewhere; this one has stopped, and the feeling is what stopping is for. It also inverts the object's own hierarchy in a way that is quietly moving: in daylight this vehicle is a red thing you look at; at night it is the reason you can see anything at all.

**FORMATS.** POSTER, 1000 × 340 mm (3:1) or B1 trimmed to 3:1. SUBSTRATE: do NOT four-colour litho this image. A large soft dark gradient is the classic banding case and this chain is 8-bit end to end (studio.py sets color_depth='16'; stitch.py and post.py both read through PIL, which truncates silently). Spec it as a screen print — two or three inks, warm amber + pale cream (+ optional brass) — on a heavy uncoated BLACK stock, so the black is paper, not ink. Matte throughout; gloss on a near-black field mirrors the room and destroys the dark.  ·  WEB HERO 2400 × 800 and a 4:5 social crop at 1080 × 1350 with the bus SMALLER still — resist every instinct to enlarge it on mobile. The emptiness is the product.  ·  BLACK COTTON TEE, chest width 200 mm, the vehicle reading at ~120 mm. Print ONLY the lit parts: two inks (warm amber, pale cream) on black, no body red on the garment at all, no white underbase. Small and constellation-like rather than a picture of a van. This is also the cheapest good screen job in the whole programme — two screens, soft hand.  ·  6-SECOND LOOP, 1080 × 1350 and 1920 × 1080, silent, for social and for a screen above the actual counter.  ·  NOT A STICKER. A die-cut of a dark object on a pale laptop lid inverts the entire idea — the black field IS the artwork and a sticker cannot carry it. Say no to this one deliberately.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** The category's night image is a sunset silhouette: the light is BEHIND the bus and outside it, the sky is the subject, and the vehicle is a black cut-out on a gradient. Here there is no sky at all and the light is INSIDE the object. Second: the bus is small. Every VW-bus poster on the market fills the frame with the vehicle because the vehicle is the merchandise; making it small and letting 90 % of the sheet be dark says the object is a place, not a possession. Third and most specific: it is stationary and cannot be read otherwise — Tacombi's own account, graded S in SPEC.md §0.1, says 'The engine was scrapped, transmission sold, seats swapped for cooking equipment, and the roof was reeled back to produce a taco serving stand.' A parked bus lit from within is the only honest picture of this object, and it is the one picture the trope-ocean never makes. Finally it carries no VW roundel and no nose: at rear-three-quarter the five nose objects (vw_ring, vw_disc, vwbar ×2) are simply not in frame, which removes both the owner's ninth-report defect and the most trademark-exposed element from the flagship image.

**PRODUCTION.** POSITION IT AS A RELIGHT, NOT A SCENE — this is what keeps it inside the rulings. F259 (owner, rev 71) is 'Relight the studio and fix the ground if necessary'; the rig is OPEN and only RED is closed. The environment routes are both shut: F92 parks the Playa hero ('DEPRIORITISED, NOT CANCELLED', licences nothing to do now) and F57 records that T1_SCENE=playa 'RENDERS NO VEHICLE … a dark cylinder on a grey plane'. So: dark cyclorama, vehicle-lit, inside the studio. THE KEY IS ALREADY ONE SWITCH — studio.rig_from_env reads `key=float(os.environ.get("T1_KEY", "1.0"))` and every softbox in lighting() is scaled by it, so a first nocturne is `T1_KEY` low plus `T1_WORLD=0` and needs no new code to look at. BUT T1_KEY ALSO SCALES THE PRACTICALS (cabin_fill's `p = T1_CABFILL * key`, and the galley fills at `_gw[_i] * _gk * key`), so the interior dies with the exterior — decoupling them is the first build item. BLOOM IS THE HIDDEN WIN. post.py's `_FLOATS` now defaults `"bloom": 0.0`, and its own comment says why: at BLOOM_THR = 0.94 'the entire backdrop [is] its own bloom source … paper white and a blown specular' are indistinguishable. On a dark frame that threshold selects ONLY the filaments. Bloom becomes admissible here for the first time — pass `--bloom 1.0` explicitly and count the mask's pixels before believing it. Two warnings that must travel with every figure. (a) post.py's live default is `BACKDROP = "headroom"` at BACKDROP_PEAK = 252.0, which on a nocturne is catastrophic — a 252 field behind a black image. Pass an explicit backdrop or render RGBA. (b) A DARK BACKDROP IS A DECLARED DEPARTURE FROM SPEC §6, which locks the backdrop to pure white, so flank_compare, gloss_compare, probe_rev70_tyre and deliver.py's controls all become uninterpretable on these frames — do NOT score a nocturne with the daylight gates, and say so in the row rather than quietly re-basing anything. AND THE TRAP THAT WILL CATCH THE UNWARY: F260. Every gain rev 71 measured from darkening the frame was 'a clipping artefact' in the cream denominator, read three times running as a lighting win. Any nocturne claim must go through photometry.py — read linear, refuse clipped, median not mean, paint the window first.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** DECOUPLE THE VEHICLE'S OWN LIGHTS FROM T1_KEY. Today cabin_fill and fill_galley1/2/3 are all multiplied by the same `key`, so turning the studio down turns the bus off. Add a separate multiplier for the practicals (and keep T1_KEY bit-identical at 1.0 so nothing regresses).  ·  BUILD REAL INTERIOR EMITTERS. The galley fills are cheats aimed from OUTSIDE — `_gy = _envf("T1_FILLG_Y", 1.35)`, which the source itself annotates '0.48 m off the flank'. At night that reads as someone shining a torch in through the hatches. Put emission on geometry that exists: gal_warmer, gal_warmer_tap, plancha, gal_worktop, galley_top, and a strip under gal_rack_hi.  ·  MEASURE F144 ON THE NOCTURNE FRAME, WITH ITS OWN INSTRUMENT. F144 is the reason this territory exists: the bulbs read as pearls at a within-frame saturation ratio of 1.38 against the photograph's 4.96 (ref_side.jpg) and 3.82 (ref_rear34.jpg), BOTH model-side levers ablated and refuted, and its own conclusion is 'A saturated amber bead requires the SURROUND to be dimmer relative to the bulb.' The nocturne is exactly that condition. Re-run the ratio on a low-key frame before anything is tuned — and if it does not move, say so, because F260 is the precedent for a darkened frame producing a false gain.  ·  THE STRING IS SHOW-SIDE ONLY AND THAT IS AN INFERENCE, NOT A MEASUREMENT. `bulb_string(side=1)` is called once, and the source says 'Pitch, bulb size and the fact that it is show side only are INFERRED.' The composition is therefore locked to +Y; a mirrored or off-side nocturne has no lights in it at all. Do not silently build a second string to fix a crop.  ·  HEADLAMPS AND TAIL LAMPS ARE NOT LIGHTS. `emissive()` is called exactly ONCE in t1_mats.py — M['bulb'] is the model's only self-luminous material. hl_lens/hl_bowl use M['lens'] and the amber/ruby lenses are plain `simple()` constants; STATE.md further says the bowls are 'NOT GRADEABLE -- all 64 back-face rays fall through the headlamp bore; no skin behind it', i.e. the reflector has nothing behind it to be a lamp. The Nolita frames show the headlamps lit. If any nocturne wants them on, that is new geometry plus a new material, and CLAUDE.md's own defect list ('a disc of body red at the centre of every tail lamp') says to render it and look at a crop before believing it.  ·  CHECK THE BOBBLE FRINGE AT NIGHT. It is built as three matte white rings (`bobble_fringe()`, material 'capwhite', FRINGE_R 0.0090 at FRINGE_PITCH 0.0260). Lit only by galley spill it may read as three grey scallops or vanish entirely — either is a composition problem, and it is decided by rendering it and looking, not by reasoning.  ·  RUN T1_ALPHA=1 ONCE. Every PNG in out/ is colortype 2 and deliver.py refuses them ('CONTROLS: 4 checked, 1 FAILED -- C1' / 'PACKAGE IS NOT TRUSTWORTHY'); no LEDGER contains the string T1_ALPHA, so the capability is a rev-62 result carried thirteen revisions unexercised. Note the collision: post.py reads `rgba[..., :3]`, so alpha and the optics/bloom pass are currently mutually exclusive — and this concept needs bloom. Either fix post.py to carry alpha, or composite onto the dark ground before post and accept no cut-out.

**SCREENER.** The most immediately affecting and the least surprising. 'The relief of finding somewhere still open' will land on anyone, and making the bus small in a 3:1 dark field is a genuine refusal of the category's fill-the-frame instinct — but a food truck glowing in the dark is what every food truck already photographs, so the picture arrives pre-seen. Strong technical reasoning (T1_KEY currently scales the practicals with the key, so the bus goes out with the studio; bloom becomes admissible for the first time on a dark field; F260 is the right precedent for distrusting any gain measured by darkening a frame), and it correctly declines to score a nocturne with daylight gates locked to SPEC §6's white backdrop. Well-made version of a familiar image.

---

## APERTURA — the three holes are the logo — Abstract the CONVERSION, not the vehicle: three unequal openings, a louvre rule, a wheel bullseye and one 38° diagonal — a complete identity system containing no bus, no folk art and no Volkswagen.  ·  screen 6.0  ·  `t3-1`

**THE IMAGE.** Three rounded rectangles in a row, one ink, on a flat field. Each is 515.5 × 403.0 mm at 1:1 — aspect 1.2792 — with a 55.0 mm corner radius. The gaps between them are NOT equal: 109.5 mm, then 129.5 mm, because the vehicle's bay centres sit at +672 / +47 / −598 mm on a 515.5 mm bay. DRAW THAT ASYMMETRY. Do not correct it; the wrongness is the whole point. Optionally, round each opening's inner edge, a scalloped row of white bobble fringe at even pitch — the one texture that identifies this vehicle instantly and that no stock VW asset carries. The three apertures then work as a MASKING DEVICE and a LAYOUT GRID: photographs, colour fields, menu panels and video get cut into them, always three, always at 109.5/129.5. The rest of the set, every member drawn from a measured constant: THE LOUVRE RULE — ten strokes at 21.111 mm pitch over a 190 mm block, used as divider, table rule, progress bar, hem stripe; THE BULLSEYE — four concentric bands, cap 274 mm with the badge at exactly 0.317 of it, the only circle the system allows and its sole counter-form; THE DIAGONAL — 38.0° from horizontal, the tail board's measured prop angle, the system's ONE permitted angle, for banners, corner cuts and price flashes; THE LINE — a single continuous stroke tracing the vehicle's whole outline the way the lit festoon does, used as connective rule and as the animated signature. Discipline: never more than two inks in one application. Red on cream, cream on red, dark on brass.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** Recognition without being sold to. Three holes and a fringe: a person who has eaten there knows instantly, and a person who hasn't sees a well-made abstract mark and is not being advertised at. That asymmetric gap — 109.5 then 129.5 — is what makes it feel FOUND rather than styled, and found things are what people keep. It also carries the founding fact silently: the $3,000 bus arrived missing three of its windows and they left them that way, so the signature feature is an absence, and the mark is literally the absence. Generosity made out of damage, stated as geometry.

**FORMATS.** 12 mm woven neck label, one ink — at this size drop the fringe and the radius, keep only the three-plus-two-gaps rhythm; it is the only mark in this brief that survives here  ·  25 mm enamel pin and 25 mm hang-tag; matchbook and coaster  ·  45 mm embroidered left chest — three satin-outlined rectangles at a 1.4 mm column, comfortably above the 1.2 mm satin floor; four needles, no fringe  ·  Business card and gift card, die-cut through one aperture so the card carries a real hole  ·  App icon, favicon, avatar — the asymmetry survives to 32 px where a symmetric triad would read as generic  ·  Window vinyl at 900 mm and interior signage band at 3 m, single ink; the apertures cut through to the street or the wall behind  ·  Paper cup: the three apertures wrap the body, the fringe scallop becomes the lid line  ·  Video and motion: the three openings as a wipe/masking device, the 38° diagonal as the only transition angle, the bulb line as the ignite

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** It contains no Volkswagen whatsoever — not the silhouette, not the split screen, not the roundel, not a bus at all. Every stock VW-bus mark on the market is the SHAPE, which is also the shape Volkswagen claims as trade dress and enforces on merchandise. This one is the CONVERSION: three holes cut in a flank, a bobble fringe, a louvre block, a hubcap ring, a 38° prop angle. Volkswagen made none of them. So the most ownable available mark and the lowest-exposure available mark are the same mark, which almost never happens. It is also the only concept here that works at 12 mm and at 3 m without redrawing, because it is proportion rather than picture — and it sidesteps the sunset/palm/daisy/blueprint saturation entirely by having no picture to be derivative with.

**PRODUCTION.** Build it as a PROPORTION SET, not a literal drawing: aspect 1.2792, radius 0.1365 of the height, gaps 0.2124 and 0.2512 of the bay width. Then it scales with no decision at any size. One ink everywhere; no gradient, no drop shadow, no outline — the moment it gets an outline it stops being a hole. Reduction ladder: fringe below ~60 mm, radius below ~15 mm, and at 12 mm the corners go square. Trademark posture, and it is design-side only, not legal advice: this system is the reason to build the identity here rather than on the nose — but a lawyer signs off per product and per market, and the record contains nothing about whether Tacombi holds any VW arrangement, which is a question for the owner before any unit is ordered.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** NOTHING is required to draw the mark — every constant is in source and STATE.md, and the mark needs no render at all. That is its main advantage over the other two.  ·  THE FRINGE IS UNDER-BUILT BY ONE APERTURE. It is built on the three serving bays only (STATE.md: fringe | 3), while the photographs also show it round the WINDSCREEN, which is not modelled. If a render is ever used as the fringe reference, that gap shows.  ·  DO NOT BUILD A REPEAT ON THE TYRE TREAD. TREAD_LUGS = 64 is carried in source as DECLARED inside a measured 48..84 bracket, and the probe REFUSES to publish a count. A countable tread rule would publish a specific claim the project cannot defend at any size where lugs are countable.  ·  DO NOT DIMENSION TB_WIDTH. The tail board's 38.0° angle and 0.7110 m chord are measured and quotable; its 0.5500 m width is labelled in source a POSE CHOICE, NOT MEASURED, with an admissible upper bound of 0.59 m and NO lower bound. Use the angle; never publish the width.  ·  IF THE SYSTEM IS EVER RENDERED RATHER THAN DRAWN: no cut-out asset exists today. Every frame in out/ is RGB with no alpha, T1_ALPHA has not been exercised since rev 62, and post.py measurably discards alpha — so optics and silhouette are currently mutually exclusive.

**SCREENER.** Genuinely ownable and the lowest-exposure mark available (contains no Volkswagen at all), and the 109.5/129.5 asymmetry is a real tell that survives to 32 px — but 'our identity is the building's apertures abstracted' is a staple of cultural-institution branding, and its own emotional claim tops out at 'not being advertised at', which is also not being moved.

---

## MISREGISTRO — The flank as a four-colour risograph in fluorescent pink and yellow — separations cut geometrically exact from the model, then deliberately printed 1.2 mm wrong.  ·  screen 6.0  ·  `t8-1`

**THE IMAGE.** The orthographic side elevation, cropped hard to the aft ~60% so the vehicle is CUT at the B-pillar and the nose is off the page entirely. In frame: two of the three serving apertures with the bobble fringe running their inner edges, the pillars with their menu cards, the counter's slab and its brass nosing running the full width and bleeding off both sides, the Señor Tacombi lockup, the rear quarter's folk-art mass, the ten-slot louvre block, the rear wheel bullseye, and the mural lid entering the top-right corner at its diagonal, bled off. No horizon, no ground, no sky, no vehicle-as-a-whole.

FOUR PASSES, AND NONE OF THEM IS THE BUS'S COLOUR:
— Riso FLUORESCENT PINK where the red is
— Riso YELLOW where the gold folk art is
— Riso FEDERAL BLUE (or black) carrying the occlusion layer ONLY — under the counter, inside the apertures, the wheel arch, the louvre slots
— and the PAPER is the cream. The upper body takes no ink at all.
Overprints do the rest: pink + yellow reads orange, pink + blue reads a deep plum. Seven apparent colours from four drums, and the plum is what the mural lid's dark ground becomes.

THE MISREGISTRATION IS SPECIFIED, NOT TOLERATED, and it is the entire aesthetic. Yellow offsets +1.2 mm right / +0.6 mm down. Blue offsets −0.8 mm left / +1.4 mm down. Pink sits on register. So the gold folk art floats proud of the red it is painted on: a hairline of bare paper down one side of every acanthus stroke, a doubled dark edge down the other. The occlusion layer slides off the aperture openings and leaves a soft ghost beside each one. An object measured to a tenth of a millimetre, printed 1.2 mm wrong, on purpose.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** Warmth by way of imprecision. A person feels a HAND — this was printed by a machine that doesn't quite care, on paper with tooth, in inks that are too loud for the subject. It is the exact visual opposite of an automotive advert, where registration is perfect and the paint is deep and the whole image whispers 'expensive'. This one says: small business, printed a hundred, taped to a wall. And the fluoro pink does something the actual vehicle cannot — it makes the object read as JOYFUL rather than as documented, which after 76 revisions of measurement is the more useful feeling to sell.

**FORMATS.** A2 poster, 420 × 594 mm, four-colour riso on 120 gsm natural/oatmeal. The flagship. A2 is the size at which a 1.2 mm offset is legible at reading distance and invisible from across a room, which is the right ratio — you notice it when you lean in, and leaning in is the point.  ·  A3, 297 × 420 mm, as the shop and edition size. Scale the offsets DOWN proportionally or the print reads broken rather than printed.  ·  Screen-print for apparel: three plates only, drop the blue. 250 × 190 mm chest on a NATURAL or ecru garment, so the shirt does the paper's job and the cream body needs no ink and no white underbase — soft hand, three screens, no ink slab. Offset the yellow plate 1.5 mm on press.  ·  305 × 305 mm album format using the mural lid alone, two inks (pink + yellow, overprinting to orange). The lid is already a flat-colour illustration; this is the cheapest thing in the whole programme to produce.  ·  The four separations sold as a set of four A3s, one ink per sheet, for the people who like that sort of thing. It also doubles as the honest documentation of the method.  ·  Sleeve, tote gusset and cup band as a single-ink yellow repeat lifted from the folk-art plate.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** Every VW-bus print on the market is in the bus's own colours, because it is a portrait of a vehicle. This is not a portrait — it is a print of a print, and it is in the wrong colours on purpose. The category's palette is sunset: orange, purple, teal, gold-hour. Fluoro pink and yellow on oatmeal reads Mexico City street poster and lucha billing, not California coast. That single substitution moves the object out of the American counterculture reading of the body shape and into the reading Tacombi's own name commits to.

And the crop removes the bus. There is no nose, no roof line front-to-back, no VW roundel, no recognisable Type 2 profile in frame — what is in frame is a SHOPFRONT: holes, a counter, a fringe, signage. It is unmistakably not a vehicle print, which is both why it escapes the ocean and why it carries essentially no trade-dress or mark surface.

One honest note: it contradicts F18's scene lock, 'nothing but the bus, die-cut tight'. It is a different artefact from the F18 sticker and should be offered as one.

**PRODUCTION.** Specify the offsets as PLATE offsets in the delivered file, not as press tolerance, or the printer will fight them. Riso F-pink over yellow needs pink LAST or it goes muddy. Keep any single layer under roughly 70% area coverage or the drum rollers mark. Paper must be uncoated natural — riso on anything coated offsets onto the next sheet.

THE PALETTE IS A DESIGN DECISION AND MUST BE DECLARED AS ONE, WITH PROVENANCE. Do NOT sample the brand red: `flank_compare` on `out/r75_side.png` reads the render's red at median (196,81,67), G/R 0.422, against the photograph's (158,16,6), G/R 0.114 — it images salmon. And the photograph cannot supply a target either; `probe_rev71_red.py` prints its own prohibition — the photograph's red G/R spans 0.0149 to 0.0344, 'a 2.3x span. DO NOT QUOTE A SINGLE FIGURE FOR IT.' The tree also holds four unreconciled authored reds: `t1_mats` sRGB(196,49,36), `lid_gen` (159,45,22), `cal_gen` (214,46,30). Fluorescent pink sidesteps every one of those arguments by not being red at all, which is a real strategic advantage and not a dodge.

THE SIMPLIFICATION IS ALREADY MEASURED, so use it rather than eyeballing it: `folk_gen.COMP_TOP = {1: 47.8, 2: 73.7, 3: 84.4, 5: 87.8, 10: 93.5}` — the largest THREE gold components carry 84.4% of the ink — while `COMP_HIST`'s first three bins sum to 48 of 66 components carrying 2.29% between them. Delete those 48 on the yellow plate. Riso cannot hold them anyway, and this is a licence to simplify with a number under it rather than a taste call.

Proof the offsets on the actual stock before committing. Misregistration that reads as charming at 1.2 mm reads as a mistake at 2.5 mm, and the threshold moves with paper tooth.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** T1_ALPHA=1, EXERCISED FOR THE FIRST TIME IN THIRTEEN REVISIONS. Every frame in `out/` is PNG colortype 2 — no alpha — and `deliver.py` correctly refuses them: 'CONTROLS: 4 checked, 1 FAILED -- C1' / 'PACKAGE IS NOT TRUSTWORTHY'. No LEDGER contains the string `T1_ALPHA`, so 'transparency works' is a rev-62 result carried on paper. Run it, and watch C1 go green.  ·  DO NOT ROUTE THE RESULT THROUGH `post.py`. Measured: fed an RGBA file it returns colortype 2 — `rgba[..., :3]` at the top, a three-channel array at the save. The optics chain and the cut-out are mutually exclusive and nothing in the tree documents it. For a flat print you want neither grain nor CA anyway, but the silhouette must survive.  ·  A MATERIAL-ID / OBJECT-INDEX PASS so each ink layer is a geometrically exact mask rather than a colour-key off a tone-mapped frame. No `pass_index` exists anywhere. Drive it off `build.py`'s own `A(obj, key)` assign list — that gives roughly 17 stable material keys (paint, chrome, glass, tyre, countercream, capred, lidmural, calidad, script…) and maps the ID islands onto design intent for free.  ·  A DIFFUSE-COLOUR PASS, to carry the artwork (folk art, mural, script, Calidad) into the pink and yellow plates WITHOUT carrying the lighting. This is the pass that makes reduction possible at all: the artwork is the vehicle's identity and it must survive the flattening untouched.  ·  THE SEÑOR LOCKUP NEEDS ITS MISSING KEYLINE BEFORE IT GOES ON A PLATE. Measured on `tex/senor.png`: among pixels with alpha > 200 the 1st-percentile luma is 168.3 and the fraction below luma 80 is 0.0000 — there is NO dark ink in the shipped asset — while SPEC §3 requires 'SILVER with a dark keyline and drop shadow'. On oatmeal paper a light plate with no keyline vanishes. `lid_gen.py` already authors the convention as `DARKLINE = (72, 26, 16)`. Note `script_gen.py` is NOT called by `build.py` — regenerate `tex/senor.png` by hand or the render silently keeps the old file.  ·  PLACEMENT, in the same edit: `flank_compare` reports 'the render's lockup has to move +76.2 mm forward and −47.6 mm down', with an independent route agreeing at +83 / +80 mm aft. Placement survives every downscale and is judged against the rest of the vehicle, so it is visible on an A2 at 11 mm of lateral shift.  ·  NOT REQUIRED: gloss, roughness, environment, the emblem (the crop excludes the nose), the tyre tread (the wheel is a flat bullseye at this reduction). A flat plate has no specular, so `gloss_compare`'s failure — render spread 0.412 of the photograph's against a 0.60 bar — is simply not a property of this artefact.

**SCREENER.** One genuinely smart move — cropping the bus out of its own frame so it reads as a SHOPFRONT, not a vehicle — and one genuinely smart dodge: fluoro pink sidesteps four unreconciled authored reds and a photograph the record forbids quoting a single figure for. But riso-with-specified-misregistration is the most saturated aesthetic in independent graphic design right now; every art-school poster for a decade. This is a competent treatment applied to the object, not an idea that could only exist for it.

---

## LA CARTA — the menu is the artwork, and the bus is not in it — A two-ink screen print in the menu strip's own measured yellow and ink, setting the food words this project has already measured character-run by character-run — GOURMET · TACOS · TORTAS · FRESH · JUICES · CEVICHE · TOSTADAS · SHRIMP & FISH — as a tall banner with painted food vignettes between the words, and no vehicle anywhere on the sheet.  ·  screen 6.0  ·  `t10-1`

**THE IMAGE.** FRAMING: a tall narrow banner, the proportion of the mural board's own side strip. lid_gen.py measures it: STRIP_L_F = 0.0835 and STRIP_R_F = 0.0794 of board width, STRIP_T_F = 0.0923 of board height. Take the right strip's proportion and print it at roughly 3:1 of life size — a vertical field of strip yellow with a thin dark rule inboard and out.

THE TYPESETTING IS ALREADY MEASURED AND NOBODY HAS EVER USED IT AS TYPOGRAPHY. lid_gen.py records the right strip's ink runs as fractions of its own length: GOURMET at 0.001–0.041 ARCHED, in the corner block; TORTAS 0.204–0.244 ARCHED; JUICES 0.523–0.555 ARCHED; CEVICHE / TOSTADAS 0.590–0.637, TWO STRAIGHT LINES AND SMALLER; SHRIMP 0.731–0.755. The top strip in a 2400-wide rectified frame gives JUICES at 589–926 and TORTAS at 1807–2145, with the intervening gap measured to accommodate '13.0 ± 0.6 characters' and a cap height of '64 px = 0.46 of the strip height'. That is a complete signwriter's grammar: which words arch and which sit straight, which shrink to fit two lines, how tall a cap runs against its band, and how tight the letterspacing packs. Set the poster to those rules exactly.

COLOUR, both measured, both quoted from lid_gen.py: ground STRIP = (172, 144, 17), recorded as 'strip yellow, ink excluded', hue 48.0, saturation 0.88–0.93. Ink = (72, 46, 6), with the note that the measured median (91,59,7) is blur-limited and the core is darker — so print the darker one, which is what the source itself concluded. TWO INKS. Nothing else on the sheet.

COMPOSITION: bands, foot to head, alternating word and vignette. Between the words go the small painted food illustrations SPEC §4 already attributes to the strips — a fish, a shrimp, a tostada, a cut lime, a glass of agua fresca — drawn in the SAME two inks, flat, in a rotulista's hand: tapered brush strokes, hairline terminals, no gradients, no outline weight below the ink's own stroke minimum. The arched words get their arcs; the straight ones are dead level. Deliberate irregularity in the letterspacing — this is a hand, not a font.

MOTION: the words arrive one band at a time on a beat, bottom to top, like a board being lettered. No easing, no fades: a word is either painted or it is not. Ends on the full strip, held. That is a 4-second title card and it costs nothing to make.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** APPETITE, and specifically the appetite you get from a hand-painted board rather than from a photograph of food. Food photography sells you a specific meal; a lettered menu strip sells you the whole idea of the place, and it does it faster — a person reads CEVICHE · TOSTADAS · SHRIMP & FISH in yellow and dark red and is already at the counter. It also carries a second feeling the vehicle cannot: SOMEONE PAINTED THIS BY HAND, ON A TUESDAY, WITH A BRUSH. The arched words prove it — a machine would have set them straight.

**FORMATS.** SCREEN PRINT, 2 inks, 300 × 900 mm on heavy uncoated — the strip at roughly 3:1 life size. Uncoated is right: the real strip is painted board, and coated stock would make it look like a reproduction of a painting instead of a painted thing.  ·  PAPER TRAY LINER / FOOD-CONTACT WRAP, 300 × 300 mm, one ink on natural greaseproof. THIS IS THE BEST FORMAT ON THE LIST AND IT CLOSES A LOOP: the menu strip that lives above the counter becomes the paper the taco is served on. The customer eats off the artwork. Food-safe single-ink flexo, and it costs almost nothing per unit.  ·  TABLE TENT, 100 × 200 mm, and a COASTER at 95 mm — the two vignettes alone, no words, so the fish and the shrimp become standalone marks.  ·  TISSUE / SHOPPING BAG all-over: the words as a repeating vertical rhythm, one ink on kraft.  ·  TOTE PANEL, 380 mm — one strip, one ink, on natural canvas. The wearer carries a menu, not a logo.  ·  ANIMATED TITLE CARD, 1080 × 1920 and 1920 × 1080 — the word-by-word paint-on, reusable across every social post the brand makes.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** It contains no vehicle. That single decision walks the whole program out of the VW-bus merch category — sunset, surf, palms, peace signs, blueprint, line art — because none of those tropes can attach to a piece with no bus in it. What it attaches to instead is Mexican rotulismo, which the English-language merch market has barely touched. And it is not pastiche: every letterform decision on the sheet comes from measurements taken off this specific vehicle's own board at 8–22× magnification. Nobody else has those runs. It is also the one concept in this brief that is unambiguously FOOD-FIRST — the words are all food words, the vignettes are all food, and the vessel does not appear.

**PRODUCTION.** THIS SHIPS WITH THE RENDER PIPELINE ENTIRELY BROKEN. No Blender, no alpha frame, no stitch chain, no 16-bit question, no seam check, no T1_ALPHA, no owner hold. It is flat art derived from measurements that are already in the repository. It is the only concept in this brief that could be on paper this week, and it is the one I would put in front of him first for exactly that reason.

⚠ A CONTRADICTION I FOUND AND CANNOT SETTLE, AND IT MUST BE SETTLED BEFORE PRINTING. OPEN_FINDINGS F74(a) (MEASURED-rev58, OPEN) says 'tex/lidmural.png is CROPPED AT THE BOTTOM — the bottom row of flowers is sliced mid-bloom and the yellow side columns are truncated, where ref_side.jpg shows a board framed on all four sides.' lid_gen.py's own measured note says the opposite about the strips: 'BOTTOM strip NONE. Confirmed again: the mural runs to the bottom edge and the side strips run down past it.' Those two are compatible on whether a bottom strip exists and INCOMPATIBLE on whether the side columns are truncated. One of them is wrong and it is 18 revisions old. Resolve it against the frame before setting a strip's length, because the strip's length is the poster.

THE INK COLOUR CARRIES A STATED CEILING FROM ITS OWN SOURCE: lid_gen.py annotates the measured median (91,59,7) as 'blur-limited; core darker', and its authored constant INK = (72,46,6) is already the corrected value. Use the constant, not the median, and say which in the print spec. Do not sample either off a render — flank_compare reads the render's red at G/R 0.422 against the photograph's 0.114 on out/r75_side.png, and probe_rev71_red.py prints its own prohibition on quoting a single figure for the photograph's side.

THE VIGNETTES ARE INVENTED. SPEC §4 attributes 'small painted food illustrations' to the strips but they are not resolved at any magnification we hold, and lid_gen's own audit stops at word runs. Draw them; do not claim them as replication. Note that the owner's 'ABSOLUTE REPLICATION OF ALL ARTWORK' hard bar names seven parts of the VEHICLE's artwork and food is not among them — so new food vignettes are outside that bar, but a redrawn menu strip is inside it. Keep the two clearly separated in the file and in the conversation.

lid_gen.py IS NOT CALLED BY build.py. Any regeneration is a hand step or the render silently keeps the old texture — which also means fixing the strip artwork for print does NOT automatically improve the model, and vice versa.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** NONE for the print itself. This is the concept's whole point.  ·  OPTIONAL, and it is the cheapest fidelity win in the food territory: regenerate tex/lidmural.png's strips from lid_gen's own measured runs at print resolution, which improves the model's loudest graphic surface at the same time. Hand-run, since build.py does not call lid_gen.py.  ·  RESOLVE F74(a) vs lid_gen.py's 'BOTTOM strip NONE — confirmed twice' before either the print or the regeneration. This is a finding, not a task: one of two live records of the same board is wrong.  ·  IF the strip is ever to be rendered ON the vehicle rather than printed flat, note the LID_W coupling first — t1_shell.py sets LID_W = (LID_X0 − LID_X1) / LID_ASPECT / sin(LID_OPEN_DEG), so the board's width is tied to its opening angle and any lid-angle change rescales the strips. Not this concept's problem; it will be the next one's.

**SCREENER.** The safest idea here and it knows it. The tray liner is the one inspired format — the menu that hangs above the counter becomes the paper the taco is served on — and zero render dependency means it could print this week. But the differentiator is invisible in the artifact: the measured character runs and arch/straight grammar produce something that reads simply as a nice hand-lettered Mexican menu poster, a look thoroughly worked over in food branding. The vignettes are invented, so the most eye-catching content is the least grounded.

---

## Tres Pasadas — The lettering builds itself in three passes — body, keyline, outline — the way a rotulista actually paints it, released as a set of loops and as a three-plate overprint poster where you can see the registration.  ·  screen 6.0  ·  `t11-1`

**THE IMAGE.** MOTION. A locked, flat, front-parallel frame of one red panel — no vehicle edges, no horizon, no perspective; just red filling the frame like a wall. Light is a single soft raking source from upper left so the paint has a faint sheen and the panel reads as metal, not as a colour field. Then, in stepped time at 12 fps with holds, not a smooth 24 fps tween: PASS ONE, the silver body strokes land one glyph at a time in painting order, left to right — Señor small and raised, then T with its swash ribbon entering at the rolled left terminal, cresting near x 90 and rolling clockwise over itself above the c, then a c o m b i, each glyph appearing whole on a beat rather than being drawn stroke-by-stroke (a signwriter loads the brush once per letter). PASS TWO, the dark keyline lands around every stroke at once, in one frame, and the whole lockup snaps forward off the red — this is the single most satisfying frame in the piece and it is the frame the shipped asset currently cannot produce. PASS THREE, the drop shadow lands down-right, sign_gen.py's measured offset, and the letters lift off the panel. Two-second hold. Cut to black. Then the same three-pass structure runs on the other three hands the vehicle carries, each as its own loop: LA SANTA in red brush script — the tight spiral eye top left of the S, the full bowl, the long descender swinging back down-LEFT below and BEFORE the S's own start, the red five-point star landing point-up above and just left of the S's apex — and here the passes invert, because lid_gen.py measures the real build as red ink, then a LIGHT keyline (adjacent pixels reach luma 233 against a cream ground of 221), then a dark warm outline. Then 100% Calidad: the spiky sunburst rays fire outward from the centre, then the white bold italic type lands on two lines at exactly -19.7 degrees (cal_gen.py's ANG), then the pink star (232,96,122). Then the menu strip caps, arched, landing word by word in the measured yellow (172,144,17) with their ink at (72,46,6). PRINT COMPANION: the same three passes as three overprinted spot plates on one A2 sheet, deliberately printed with a visible 0.4 mm registration offset on the shadow plate, so the object confesses how it was made.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** The pleasure of watching a craft resolve — the same thing that makes sign-painting videos the most-watched craft content on the internet, and it works with the sound off. Specifically: relief. Pass one is silver on red and reads flat and slightly disappointing; pass two arrives and the letters suddenly have an edge and a body; you feel the picture become correct. That is a real perceptual event, not a transition, and it lands in one frame. Underneath it is a claim about the brand: nobody typed this. A person mixed a colour, cut a keyline and cast a shadow by hand, four separate times, on four separate parts of one vehicle.

**FORMATS.** 1:1 and 9:16 social loops, 4-6 seconds each, four in the set (Señor Tacombi / La Santa / 100% Calidad / the menu strip). 12 fps stepped, no motion blur, no easing on the landings — a brush lands, it does not ease.  ·  A 20-frame logo reveal for video end-cards and app splash: passes one, two, three, hold. It works at 32 px because the keyline pass is a contrast event, not a detail event.  ·  A2 three-plate screen-printed poster, 420 x 594 mm on heavy uncoated, with the registration offset deliberate and visible. Silver, dark keyline black, and the red as the stock's own printed ground — three pulls, and the third is the joke.  ·  Window vinyl in three physical layers on clear PVC: silver body, dark keyline, shadow. Applied to a restaurant window in three passes by hand, which means the installation IS the piece.  ·  A short-run 'signwriter's kit': three separate transparent sheets in a sleeve, so a person can stack them and make the lockup themselves. This is the family-keeps object.  ·  Apparel: the lockup at 240 mm chest in three screens. At 1:5.3 the p10 stroke is 0.74 mm and every stroke prints; the keyline is what stops silver-on-red reading as mud.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** Every VW-bus motion asset in existence drives, spins on a turntable, or pans across a sunset. This one has no vehicle in it and nothing moves except paint arriving. It also cannot be reverse-engineered from stock: the three-pass structure is not an animation convention I invented, it is lid_gen.py's own measurement of how the real signs are built — 'each stroke is red with a LIGHT KEYLINE and then a dark warm outline' — read at 10x off ref_rear34.jpg. And it is the only concept in this set where the project's biggest lettering defect becomes the subject: the missing keyline is the money frame.

**PRODUCTION.** 12 fps stepped with holds, not 24 fps interpolated — an eased tween reads as software, a hold reads as a hand. Do NOT add motion blur. Grain must be seeded per frame: post.py's grain() is fixed at seed=7, so an unmodified export puts identical grain on every frame, which reads as dirt on the lens and instantly betrays a still-image pipeline. If any frame comes from the render chain rather than from vector, note that the render is not deterministic — roughly 2.04 percent of pixels move by more than 8 levels between two identical renders at 1600x1100/96 spp, and 7.90 percent on the body — so a per-frame re-render would boil; build these loops in vector and composite, not in Blender. The La Santa loop carries a hard scope warning that must be written into the brief: F164 is an owner ruling — 'The forward one is separate from the Bus' — and t1_shell.signboard() says in terms 'it is not on the vehicle at all. It is a detached sign that happens to fall behind the combi in ref_rear34.jpg.' So La Santa may be used as a Tacombi LETTERING artefact and must never appear on the vehicle in any render. Ceiling on the La Santa loop, stated rather than hidden: lid_gen.py records the word as CONSISTENT and UNREAD — perpendicular cuts across it at four heights give 6, 5, 5 and 4 separated dark runs, and 'the count is not resolvable letter by letter'; its ink chroma is explicitly NOT MEASURABLE because the word is 74 x 105 px with 4-6 px strokes, below the JPEG's 2x2 chroma subsampling. Anything we animate there is an interpretation and should be labelled one.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** THE KEYLINE, AGAIN, AND IT IS THE SAME EDIT AS CONCEPT 1 — this concept's central frame does not exist until script_gen.py emits the keyline and shadow that sign_gen.py already had. Regenerate tex/senor.png by hand.  ·  EXPORT THE LOCKUP AS VECTOR. There is no vector of any lettering in this repository — the only committed .svg is vw_canonical_2019.svg, which F168 records as a DIFFERENT OBJECT from the 1955-67 pressing. script_gen.py builds every glyph as a union of variable-width strokes swept along a measured medial axis, so the outlines exist as geometry inside the generator; add an SVG writer beside the PNG writer. Everything in this concept, and every apparel and vinyl deliverable across the whole programme, is blocked on that one output.  ·  REGENERATE tex/lidmural.png FOR THE MENU-STRIP LOOP — AND RESOLVE A LIVE CONTRADICTION FIRST. OPEN_FINDINGS F74(a) says the asset is 'cropped at the bottom -- the bottom row of flowers is sliced mid-bloom and the yellow side columns are truncated'. But lid_gen.py's own section D says the opposite, twice: 'BOTTOM strip NONE. Confirmed again: the mural runs to the bottom edge and the side strips run down past it.' Both are live and they disagree about the same edge of the same asset. Look at the board in ref_side.jpg before regenerating anything, and record which one is wrong.  ·  NO RENDER REQUIRED for the four loops if they are built in vector, which is the recommendation — this ships under the F191/F193 hold because it is lettering artwork, not a delivery render.

**SCREENER.** Pass two is a real perceptual event — the keyline lands in one frame and the letters snap off the red — and it is derived from lid_gen's own measurement of how the signs are actually built, not from animation convention. The three-plate poster with deliberate 0.4 mm misregistration and the three-transparent-sheet kit are good objects. But sign-painting process video is the single most saturated craft genre on the internet, and the concept cites that saturation as a strength, which is the tell. Well-observed, technically careful, not strange.

---

## PÁTINA — The same vehicle at five ages — factory, donor, plain, peak, and worn — in which the object visibly gets WORSE, which is the one thing no VW-bus print on earth will show you and which directly contradicts a live owner ruling.  ·  screen 6.0  ·  `t14-2`

**THE IMAGE.** Five orthographic side elevations in a horizontal strip on one wide sheet, or five separate prints boxed as a set. Identical camera, identical registration, identical scale. Left to right:

**1963** — the vehicle before anyone touched it. Three bay windows GLAZED. No counter, no tail board, no mural lid, no folk art, no script, no Calidad decal, no bulbs. Two-tone cream and red, nothing else. Almost monochrome as a picture: one cream, one red, one grey ground. Silent.

**2006** — the $3,000 donor, in green, as bought. Bare painted rims with no hubcaps. No headlamps fitted. Script only, in silver outline. And the three windows already GONE — the holes are there before the business is. This frame is the one that tells the story: the signature feature arrived as damage.

**2012** — red, plain. TACOMBI.COM on the flank. No folk art, no script, chalkboard lid. Clean, commercial, a little anonymous.

**2019** — the full folk-art livery at maximum chroma and maximum ornament. Gold scrollwork, silver Señor Tacombi, flower mural lid propped, Calidad burst, bulbs lit, counter dressed, tail board out. This is the loudest cell on the sheet by a distance, and the composition is built so that it is: it is the middle, everything else is quieter, and your eye lands there first and stays.

**20XX** — the same bus, further on. Nothing removed, nothing broken. The cream has gone chalky and blotchy; the gold has pulled toward ochre; the red has pulled toward brick; the tyres have greyed; the shadow has softened; three or four bulbs in the string are dark. It is still completely legible and completely itself. It is just older.

Composition: a wide sheet, generous white above and below, the strip sitting low. Under each cell, the year in figures — 1963 · 2006 · 2012 · 2019 · 20XX — and nothing else. No captions, no arrows, no timeline rule. The strip's chroma envelope IS the narrative: quiet, quiet, warmer, LOUD, warm-and-dimming.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** Tenderness with an ache in it. Every restaurant sells 'serving you since'; this one shows what serving cost. The middle frame is the peak and you can see that it is the peak, which means you can also see that the last frame is after it — and the last frame is not sad, it is EARNED. A person feels the same thing they feel about a good chef's knife with a worn-down blade: that the wear is the evidence of the work, and that they would not want it new. That is why someone keeps it on a wall for twenty years, and it is the only piece in this program that admits the object is mortal.

**FORMATS.** Strip print, 1400 × 400 mm, limited edition, on heavy uncoated rag. Hand-numbered, blind-emboss chop, no signature. A 6 pt colophon in the foot naming the render's own provenance — revision, resolution, sample count, year — because unfakeable machine provenance is the correct authentication for an authorless work and it is exactly this project's real asset.  ·  Boxed set of five A4 prints (210 × 297 mm each), matte-coated, in a plain sleeve. The set structure is the product: you cannot buy the 2019 frame alone, which is the point.  ·  Five-panel scroll or rail-hung sequence for the restaurant wall, ~2.5 m overall.  ·  The 2019 frame ALONE as the shop's t-shirt and the shop's tote — the loud one is the wearable one, and the customer who has seen the wall knows what it is the middle of.  ·  Slow five-frame dissolve, ~30 s per state, for the in-store screen. No motion within a frame; only the cross-fade.  ·  Postcard set, 148 × 105 mm, five cards — the giveaway tier, and the one that gets kept in a drawer.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** The VW-bus merchandise category is built on restoration and eternal golden hour: a perfect bus, at sunset, forever. Nobody sells the same bus getting more worn, because for everyone else the bus is a fantasy and a fantasy cannot age. Tacombi owns the one version of this object that is a real, specific, working thing with a documented biography — bought green for $3,000, missing three windows, engine scrapped — so it is the only VW bus in the world that has anything to age FROM. The series is also a direct inversion of the category's emotional grammar: the escape fantasy runs away from consequence, and this runs straight at it. And the two hardest frames to make are the two nobody else could make at all: 1963 requires putting the windows back IN, which is a thing only the owner of the model can do.

**PRODUCTION.** **THIS CONCEPT CONTRADICTS A LIVE OWNER RULING AND MUST BE PROPOSED AS SUCH, NOT SLIPPED THROUGH.** At rev 61 he said, in his own words in LEDGER_rev61 §6: *"I want this 3d model to look like new. Enhanced from the photo."* SPEC §3 simultaneously locks the finish as WEATHERED, and the record's standing instruction on the collision is explicit — *"Live tension with SPEC §3's WEATHERED lock — surface it, do not silently pick a side"* — with F230 naming it as one of two owner questions that gate the delivery render and that cannot be answered by code. My position, stated plainly and offered as a choice rather than a decision: **he is right that the MODEL should be new, and this is a different artefact that deliberately ages a copy of it.** Put it to him as a picture — render three rungs of the wear ladder as three crops of the same cream panel and ask which is his bus — not as a paragraph. That asking closes an open owner question either way, which is worth more than the print is.

On production: age the LAST frame with the existing parameter chain and publish the settings in the colophon, so the wear is a stated quantity rather than an illustrator's mood. Do NOT invent damage — no rust, no dents, no cracked glass; the object in the photographs is worn, not broken, and inventing decay would be as false as inventing bunting. Hold the five frames at ONE exposure and one ground; if the light changes between cells the eye reads a lighting change instead of an ageing. And render the five states as FIVE SEPARATE RENDERS — SPEC §7.1 records the owner's ruling that folk art, script, aperture glazing and roof-board treatment *"may never be carried across states."* Any single frame that blends two liveries is a fake and this project has already killed three findings on exactly that.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** **THE 1963 FRAME NEEDS THE THREE SERVING APERTURES GLAZED SHUT, AND THAT IS REAL GEOMETRY, NOT A SWITCH.** STATE.md reads `open serving apertures on +Y: 3` and the bays are cut into the shell at `band 1.372-1.775 un-dropped, bay widths 0.516 0.515 0.516`. Putting stock windows back in means new panes, new seals and a closed shell — the single most expensive item across all three of my concepts, and it should be costed honestly before the set is promised.  ·  The 1963 and 2006 frames also need the counter, counter_nosing, counter_top, tail_board, tail_board_stay, tb_bulbs, lid_board and the folk art all ABSENT. `build.py` binds the folk art, the script and the Calidad decal unconditionally — there is no livery switch anywhere in the tree. This wants a `T1_LIVERY` with named states, built once and guarded by rows that read the mesh and the material bindings rather than grepping for names.  ·  **THE AGEING LADDER ITSELF IS THE CHEAP PART AND IT ALREADY EXISTS.** `t1_mats.py` carries `MOTTLE_AMP = float(os.environ.get("T1_MOT_AMP", 0.55))`, the WEATHER group behind `T1_WEATHER` (shipped 1.0, and watched moving at 4.535 % of pixels >8 levels against F228's 2.441 % floor), `W_DUST_COL = (0.4400, 0.3900, 0.3100)` — the pale limestone road film — and `T1_TYRE_FILM` (ships 0.15; `=1.0` restores the heavier film rev 69 removed). Five rungs of that chain is a parameter sweep on switches with existing watched ablations, not new shading work.  ·  The green states need paint that does not exist in the shader at all. `ref_workshop.jpg` and `IMG_2073.jpeg` give the green (a reading of roughly sRGB #5E7A50 on the workshop frame) and the 2006 state's own details — bare rims, no hubcaps, no headlamps fitted, script in silver outline only — are recorded as a VERIFIED table in LEDGER_rev51.md, so the reference work is already done and the authoring is not.  ·  `tex/lidmural.png` must be regenerated first: F74(a) is open and the shipped master is CROPPED AT THE BOTTOM — the bottom flower row sliced mid-bloom and the yellow side columns truncated, where ref_side.jpg shows a board framed on all four sides. The 2019 frame is the loudest cell on the sheet and the mural is the loudest thing in it. Note `lid_gen.py` is NOT called by `build.py`, so regeneration is a hand step or the render silently keeps the old file.  ·  Same LID_W blocker as HORARIO if any frame in the set shows the lid at a non-shipped angle, and the same missing keyline on `tex/senor.png` — measured, of pixels with alpha > 200 the fraction below luma 80 is 0.0000, i.e. the shipped wordmark contains no dark ink at all against SPEC §3's requirement of "SILVER with a dark keyline and drop shadow." On the 2019 print at 1400 mm the wordmark is the second-largest graphic on the sheet and silver with no keyline will read as mud.

**SCREENER.** The 2006 frame is the single best observation across all nine — the signature feature arrived as damage, the holes are there before the business is — and ageing as EARNED rather than sad inverts the category correctly. But five-panel evolution strips are stock, it contradicts a live owner ruling so the hero is gated on an unresolved question (named honestly, which is right), and glazing three apertures back in plus a livery switch plus green paint plus mural regeneration is by far the heaviest build proposed.

---

## Thirty-Seven Per Cent — The bus rendered perfectly, and every person who was ever in the photograph punched out of it as a flat hole of pure brand gold — because across seventy-five revisions the customers are the only thing this project measured and could never build.  ·  screen 6.0  ·  `t15-0`

**THE IMAGE.** GROUND LAYER: the true orthographic side elevation, photoreal, full bleed, registered to ref_side.jpg. Use studio.views()['side'] — `lens=None, ortho=5.90`, camera at z 1.52 — because it is a genuine parallel projection, not a photograph's projection, which is what makes the overlay honest. Bus lit exactly as it ships (white cyc, contact shadow present, T1_SHADOW at its default 9.0).

FIGURE LAYER: every region the source code masks out as a human being is REMOVED from the render and replaced by a flat, unshaded field of a single ink — warm folk-art gold (t1_mats `GOLD = (0.8600, 0.5400, 0.0600)`) on the day version, cream sRGB(206,208,200) on the night version. Not silhouettes drawn on top: holes punched through, hard-edged, zero gradient, zero shading, and — this is the whole picture — ZERO CONTACT SHADOW. The four-and-a-half-tonne machine touches the ground; the people float free of it.

FOUR FIGURES, all sourced from live constants and comments, all on this one elevation:
(1) `probe_rev39_flank.py`: "the man in the white cap and jacket occludes the lower-forward flank. He is a known occluder and the exclusion is PRICED, never quietly trimmed" — `MAN_ROWS, MAN_COLS = (420, 768), (90, 300)`. On a 1024×768 frame that box is 210 × 348 px: 20.5 % of the frame's width, 45.3 % of its height, running clean off the bottom edge. He stands forward of the front wheel, three-quarters as tall as the bus's belt line.
(2) `t1_shell.py`, on why the front wheel arch is still a plain circle: "in ref_side.jpg a man stands directly in front of it, and every attempt to measure the front arch here locked onto his red shirt instead." `t1_core.py` prices him: "The front wheel is 54 % unoccluded." So he is a gold shape covering just under half a wheel.
(3) `studio.py`, on serving bay 1: "BAY 1 IS NOT A TARGET: a man in a white shirt fills its window in the photograph — his forearm crosses the full width and the shirt covers the right two thirds." He is a gold rectangle-and-arm INSIDE the aperture, at the printed window u[332,422] v[330,399].
(4) `compare_script.py`, rejecting pixels from the wordmark measurement: "the counter and the man's hand." One hand, alone, resting on the brass counter nosing.

COMPOSITION: full-bleed elevation across the top three-quarters. Deep bottom margin in cream. One hairline rule in dark maroon (#501010). Under it, small condensed caps, one line: 37 % OF WHAT HAPPENS HERE IS A PERSON WE COULD NOT MEASURE. Below that, at 6 pt, a four-line colophon of the actual source lines — the poster's second read, for the person who leans in.

PALETTE: five inks and no more — body red, cream, gold, dark maroon, and the render's own black. The figures are ONE ink. Never two.

MOTION (10 s, stepped 8–12 fps, no easing): elevation alone, held two seconds. Then one gold hole fills with the actual photographic crop from ref_side.jpg — a real man, grainy, 1024 px, unmistakably a photograph inside a render. Then the next. Then the next. All four fill. Hold. Then they drain back to flat gold in reverse order and the caption types on. The cut between flat colour and photograph must be a HARD cut. No dissolve.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** Two reads, four seconds apart. The first is warmth and legibility — a beautiful measured object with human-shaped warmth standing around it, and you understand instantly that this is a place with people in it. The second is the ache: this company built a flawless replica of its own restaurant and the one thing it could never build was the customers. The bus is the part you can measure. The reason it exists is the part you can't. That lands because it is literally true of the file it came from, and because everyone who has ever queued for a taco knows which half mattered.

It also puts the customer at the centre of the artwork without photographing a single identifiable person — no releases, no dated haircuts, no one aging out of the campaign. The hole is the reader.

**FORMATS.** POSTER, A2 (420 × 594 mm) — ship this size first and honestly. Nothing above 1600×1100 exists on disk (`out/` is untracked and starts empty) and the current stitch chain has been proven exactly once, at 3840×2640 (rev 57, one view, 106.8 min). 3840 px is 325 mm at 300 dpi and 488 mm at 200 dpi, so A2 lands at ~232 dpi and is defensible today. CRITICAL ADVANTAGE OF THIS CONCEPT: the figures are flat vector, so only the bus needs pixels — the resolution ceiling binds a smaller fraction of this image than of any other in the program.  ·  POSTER, A1 (594 × 841 mm) — needs ONE proving run of hq_render.py at ~4700 px with T1_FX=0 exported by hand, and stitch.py's exit code actually read (F49: "a guard whose exit code nobody reads is not a guard"). One afternoon of machine time, not a revision.  ·  SCREEN PRINT, 5 spot inks on heavy uncoated 300 gsm, edition of 100–250, hand-numbered in pencil, blind-embossed chop rather than a signature (there is no artist's hand on a render; a forged signature on CGI is embarrassing). Uncoated because the gold figures must read as ink, not as process yellow.  ·  T-SHIRT, chest print 280 mm wide, garment-dyed heavy cotton in body red so the shirt carries the vehicle's own red and the print is cream + gold + dark only — three screens, no white underbase, soft hand. The figures survive reduction perfectly because they are solid shapes; the bus reduces to its two-tone and its three apertures.  ·  WINDOW VINYL at the restaurant, 1800 × 620 mm, printed on clear with the FIGURES LEFT UNPRINTED — so the actual people inside the taqueria fill the holes, moving, all day. This is the version that justifies the whole idea and it costs one extra ink channel (confirm the vendor has a white channel before designing).  ·  ANIMATED LOOP, 10 s, 1080 × 1350 for social and 1920 × 1080 for in-store screens.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** THE ENTIRE VW-BUS MERCHANDISE CATEGORY SELLS AN EMPTY BUS. Sunset, palms, surfboard, open road, nobody in it — because the fantasy on sale is escape, and other people ruin escape. Every recurring trope the market survey named (sunset silhouette, surf, hippie flower-power, 70s wash, minimal line art, blueprint) is an unoccupied vehicle in a landscape. This is, as far as I can establish, the only VW-bus artwork whose subject is the crowd, and it gets there by inverting the category's own emotional premise: Tacombi's bus is the one that STOPPED, has no engine and no gearbox, and exists to have people standing around it.

And the mechanism is structurally uncopyable. The four figures are not art-directed poses; they are the exact occlusion masks a seventy-five-revision measurement project cut out of its own reference photographs, quoted from live source. Nobody who has not spent seventy-five revisions measuring THIS vehicle has these coordinates. A competitor can copy the look in an afternoon and it will be a decoration; here it is a document.

It also carries no VW roundel at all — the side elevation contains only four hubcap badges, roughly 1.6 mm each at sticker scale — so it is simultaneously the most emotionally loaded and the least trademark-exposed image in this set.

**PRODUCTION.** RISK, STATED PLAINLY: this is the one a cautious team kills. It is conceptual, it is partly about the making of the thing, and read badly it looks like airing the project's laundry on a poster. I am proposing it anyway, because the two-level construction protects it — a stranger reads warmth and people; only the person who leans in reads the colophon. If the caption frightens the room, cut the colophon and keep the headline; the picture still works. Do not cut the headline.

THIS IS NOT THE F18 STICKER and must not be offered as it. F18's scene is LOCKED by the owner to "nothing but the bus, die-cut tight, plus the sun and the papel picado", and this deliberately puts people in the frame. Different artefact, said out loud in the proposal — at which point F18's "build it after the model is done" trigger, and F191/F193's holds on the DELIVERY render, stop applying to it.

COLOUR: choose and declare ONE brand red as a design ruling and do NOT eyedropper it off the render. flank_compare on out/r75_side.png reads the render at median (196,81,67), G/R 0.422, against the photograph's (158,16,6), G/R 0.114 — it images salmon, and the register calls it the largest colour error in the delivery frame. Nor can it be averaged from the photographs: probe_rev71_red.py prints its own prohibition, "the photograph's red G/R is 0.0149 .. 0.0344, a 2.3x span. DO NOT QUOTE A SINGLE FIGURE FOR IT." Take it from the authored constant (t1_mats `RED = (0.5520, 0.0294, 0.0176)  # = sRGB(196,49,36)`) or from ref_playa_34.png, the one frame whose white balance is neutral on the paving (116,119,120), and record which.

REGISTRATION: the render and the photograph must sit in one frame. probe_rev39_flank.py already builds that warp and prints "warp covers %.1f %% of the reference frame" — reuse it rather than eyeballing a homography.

CEILINGS THE ARTWORK MUST NOT PRETEND AWAY: the masks in the source are RECTANGLES, not silhouettes. Real human outlines have to be hand-traced from a 1024×768 JPEG where features are 2–4 px. That is a DRAWING, and it should be labelled one — the figures are honest as flat abstractions and dishonest as claimed likenesses. And nobody in this program has seen current Tacombi merchandise, brand guidelines, official colours or the logo lockup (every first-party page returned EGRESS_BLOCKED); do not set any type in a face attributed to the brand until the owner supplies it.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** RUN T1_ALPHA=1 ONCE, AND WATCH IT. Every PNG in out/ today is colortype 2 (no alpha), and `deliver.py` correctly refuses them: "CONTROLS: 4 checked, 1 FAILED -- C1" / "PACKAGE IS NOT TRUSTWORTHY". No LEDGER_rev*.md contains the string T1_ALPHA, so the capability is a rev-62 result carried forward thirteen revisions un-exercised. Render `side` with T1_ALPHA=1 and watch C1 go green — that is the watched kill this capability has never had on a live tree.  ·  DO NOT ROUTE THE RESULT THROUGH post.py. Measured: RGBA in (colortype 6), colortype 2 out — `rgba[..., :3]` at the top, 3-channel array at the save. Optics and cut-out are mutually exclusive from this repo and nothing in post.py's docstring says so.  ·  A `side` RENDER AT DELIVERY SCALE, registered to ref_side.jpg via probe_rev39_flank's existing warp. No new camera needed — studio.views()['side'] is already the right one and is the best-instrumented view in the tree (27 rendered-file mentions across LEDGER_rev66..75).  ·  NOTHING HERE NEEDS THE EMBLEM FIXED. The side elevation carries no nose roundel, so F191 ("Keep holding — fix the emblem first") does not gate this image, and the owner's ninth-report defect is out of frame by construction. Rank this concept ABOVE any front or three-quarter artwork for that reason alone.  ·  OPTIONAL, AND IT WOULD IMPROVE THE PICTURE: fix `tex/senor.png`'s missing dark keyline before rendering. SPEC §3 requires the wordmark "SILVER with a dark keyline and drop shadow"; measured on the shipped 4096×1738 master, among pixels with alpha>200 the 1st-percentile luma is 168.3 and the fraction below luma 80 is 0.0000 — there is no dark ink in the asset at all. Silver on red with no keyline reads flat, and this poster puts the wordmark at large scale. Regenerate BY HAND: script_gen.py is not called by build.py.  ·  DO NOT 'FIX' THE THINGS THE CAPTION IS ABOUT. The front arch is circular because a man's red shirt defeated the fit; bay 1 has no photographic target because a man in a white shirt fills its window. If a future revision closes those, the poster's headline stops being true. That is a real cost and it should be a stated, deliberate trade, not a surprise.

**SCREENER.** The construction is arresting — people as flat gold holes with no contact shadow while the four-tonne machine touches the ground — and the window vinyl with the figures left unprinted, so real customers fill the holes all day, is the best single execution idea in the set. Fatal flaw is the headline: '37 %' is nowhere a claim about the frame; the only 37 in the tree is a ceiling on bay 1's dressing VARIANCE. Publishing an unsourced statistic at poster scale, in the one piece that claims measurement as its authority, is self-inflicted. It also needs the defects it is about to stay unfixed.

---

## The Brass Line — Eye height, 1.4 m from the counter: two hands and a paper-wrapped taco crossing 19.9 mm of brass — the vehicle reduced to a soft red field behind the only hard line in the frame.  ·  screen 6.0  ·  `t15-1`

**THE IMAGE.** CAMERA: a customer's view, not a bystander's. 1.55 m eye height (which is also the reference photograph's own derived camera height — REF_MEASUREMENTS.md recovers the ground line with "a pinhole model with camera height ≈ 1.55 m"), standing 1.3–1.5 m back from the counter's outer lip, 85 mm, f/2.8. Landscape, 3:1.

THE LINE: the brass nosing runs dead horizontal across the lower third, edge to edge, uncropped and unbroken. The measured facts that make this composition work, all from live source: `CNT_ZT, CNT_ZB = 1.2540, 1.1470  # 107 mm thick` in the un-dropped frame — with the ~48 mm ride drop that is a serving surface at about 1.21 m above the ground, and t1_detail's own comment records the photographed nosing at "1.189-1.205 AG". `CNT_Y_IN, CNT_Y_OUT = 0.8450, 1.1660  # 321 mm plan depth` — so the slab reaches 291 mm outboard of a flank that is 1.7497 m wide, and it cantilevers on five brackets. `CNT_NOSE_F = 0.1860` caps the slab's outer edge in brass: t1_detail computes it as "19.9 mm of gold in elevation". Nineteen point nine millimetres. That is the hero of the picture.

WHAT IS SHARP: the brass line, and two hands on it. One hand comes out of the soft dark of serving bay 3 (the cook's — sleeve, forearm, no face); one comes in from the bottom edge of frame (the customer's — no face, no watch, no jewellery, nothing datable). Between them, a paper-wrapped taco, half-transferred. The moment chosen is the half-second BEFORE the release, when both hands are still on it.

WHAT IS SOFT: everything. Above the brass line, at f/2.8, the 107 mm cream slab edge, then the two-tone break, then the bottom of bay 3's aperture with the white bobble fringe scalloping across the top of frame like a party. The folk art is a gold blur. The bus is unrecognisable as a vehicle and that is deliberate.

LIGHT: warm and low, keyed from the serving side, ~35° above and slightly behind camera so the brass takes a specular run along its whole length and the cream goes ivory rather than paper. The galley behind the hands sits 1.5–2 stops down, so bay 3 is a warm cave and the cook's hand emerges from it. This is the relight the owner explicitly licensed — F259, rev 71: "Relight the studio and fix the ground if necessary." His one constraint travels with it: DO NOT change RED.

PALETTE, five values and no more: brass sRGB(201,186,150) (t1_mats `COUNTERTAN`), body red as a soft unfocused field, cream ivory, two skin tones, kraft paper. No sixth colour enters the frame.

COMPOSITION: the brass line at exactly one third from the bottom, running off both edges. The counter is over four metres of continuous edge and the picture must feel like a slice cut out of something much longer. All copy sits BELOW the line, small, in the cream, never above it.

MOTION, 4 s, seamless: hand in from the bottom, hand out from the bay, the exchange, release, both hands withdraw, and then a full second of EMPTY BRASS LINE before it loops. The empty second is the piece. Do not cut it for length.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** Recognition of one specific bodily moment: the half-second when a stranger hands you food across a bar and the two of you are, briefly, exactly the same height. The counter is 1.21 m above the ground — an adult's forearm rests on it, an adult's chest clears it, and a child's chin is under it. That single measurement is why a queue at this bus looks the way it does: adults leaning, children being lifted. It is a fact about the object, and the picture is about what the fact does to people.

The absence of faces is what makes it a keeper rather than an advert. There is no one in the frame to be — so the viewer is the hand. Nobody ages out of it, nobody's haircut dates it, and it works identically in Playa, Nolita and any city the brand opens in next.

**FORMATS.** BANNER / IN-STORE, 1200 × 400 mm on uncoated stock. Uncoated is not a preference: process gold on coated stock reads yellow, and the entire picture rests on the brass reading as METAL. If budget allows one spot metallic, spend it here and nowhere else.  ·  COUNTER-FRONT VINYL AT THE ACTUAL RESTAURANT, 1000 × 120 mm, applied to the fascia so the printed brass line sits directly below the real brass line the customer is leaning on. The artwork and its subject in the same field of view, at 1:1. This is the version worth building the concept for.  ·  CUP WRAP, 230 × 100 mm — the brass line runs the full circumference at drinking height, so the line meets the customer's actual mouth. Two colours plus the line.  ·  NAPKIN, 300 × 300 mm, single colour: the brass line at one third and nothing else at all. The cheapest object in the program and the one that reads as confidence.  ·  WEB HERO, 2400 × 800 (3:1). The aspect is chosen so the line survives every responsive crop — a 3:1 strip degrades to 2:1 and 16:9 without ever losing the horizontal.  ·  POSTCARD, 148 × 105 mm, given with the order. Test 2 from the strategy pass: it does an independent job (it is a postcard) before it does ours.  ·  ANIMATED LOOP, 4 s, 1080 × 1350 and 1920 × 1080.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** The VW-bus merchandise ocean is, without exception, an EXTERIOR THREE-QUARTER OF A VEHICLE IN A LANDSCAPE. This frame has no wheels, no silhouette, no roof line, no horizon, no sky, no road and no VW roundel. You cannot tell from it that the object is a Volkswagen — you can only tell it is a place where somebody hands you food. That is the categorical opposite of every listing in the survey, and it is also, not coincidentally, the lowest trademark exposure of anything in this program: nothing in frame was made by Volkswagen. The counter, the brass nosing, the bobble fringe and the folk art are all Tacombi's.

It is also the only concept here that could not be made from a stock 3D bus model at all. A purchased VW asset has glass where this has holes and has no counter, no nosing, no fringe and no galley behind. The picture is 100 % conversion and 0 % donor vehicle — which is exactly where this brand's ownable content lives.

**PRODUCTION.** NOT OFFERED AS THE F18 STICKER (photoreal, and F18's style is LOCKED to "cartoon with rendered depth"), and not offered as the delivery render (F191 and F193 both stand, held four times). This is exploratory look-development that happens to be shippable as its own artefact. Say that in the proposal and the holds stop applying.

A CAMERA-NAMING TRAP WORTH FLAGGING, BECAUSE IT IS RULE 34's SHAPE: the view in studio.views() whose comment reads "rev 8b: standing AT the counter, eye height. This is the shot that carries the place rather than the specification — a person's view, looking slightly up at the mural board with the counter lip in the near field. Wider aperture than the studio heroes: at f/3.5 the tail and the background go soft the way an eye does" is named `playa` — `loc=(3.15, 5.75, 1.60), tgt=(-0.30, 0.45, 1.40), lens=42, fstop=3.5`. Its INTENT is precisely this concept. But F92 parks the Playa ENVIRONMENT ("DEPRIORITISED, NOT CANCELLED"; "do not act on it either") and F57 records that `T1_SCENE=playa` renders no vehicle at all. A camera dict rendered in the shipped white studio is a different object from the parked Playa hero. State that distinction and let the owner rule it rather than assuming either way — a ruling inherits its object, and this project has already published one twelve-revision misattribution of exactly this kind.

DEPTH OF FIELD IS ALREADY INSTRUMENTED AND UNUSED: `aim()` reads T1_FSTOP from the environment, logs measured near / far / hyperfocal on every frame, and sets 9 aperture blades with an 11° rotation so out-of-focus speculars render 9-sided rather than as perfect discs. This concept is the first thing in the program with a reason to use any of it — hero34f at 78 mm, f/8, ~15 m has a hyperfocal around 25 m and essentially everything sharp, so its DoF is decorative.

COLOUR: as with every artefact here, choose the red as a spot ink and declare it. This crop is roughly 60 % body colour by area, so it is the frame most exposed to the render's salmon.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** A NEW CAMERA.
