The existing `counter` view — `loc=(3.40, 5.20, 1.98), tgt=(-0.55, 0.75, 1.26), lens=90, focus=(0.20, 1.05, 1.22), fstop=6.3`, commented "the shot that says taqueria" — has the right subject but the wrong body: ~6.0 m out at 1.98 m camera height is a bystander watching a transaction, not a customer in one. Add an entry at ~1.4 m stand-off, 1.55 m eye height, 85 mm, f/2.8. AND RENDER THE EXISTING ONE FIRST AND LOOK AT IT: `counter` has ZERO rendered-file mentions across LEDGER_rev66..75. Nobody has ever seen it.  ·  HANDS DO NOT EXIST IN THIS TREE. A grep for person / human / figure / mannequin returns nothing structural — there is no human geometry anywhere in 229 mesh objects. So hands are either photographed and composited (which needs T1_ALPHA exercised, and a model release) or drawn. Decide before the camera is built, because the two routes want different light.  ·  THE GLOSS DEFICIT BINDS HARDEST HERE, AND THIS CONCEPT IS THE ARGUMENT FOR FIXING IT. gloss_compare on out/r75_hero34f.png reads render spread 0.495 against the photograph's 1.202 — 0.412 of it against a 0.60 bar, FAIL — and, more to the point for a close crop, HEADROOM 0.120 against 1.008, an 8.4× miss on 'is there a highlight at all'. A close crop of a painted cantilevered slab is exactly where matte paint reads as vinyl. F259 licenses the relight; use headroom as the objective and publish spread beside it so a falling spread is not misread as a regression.  ·  THE BRASS NOSING HAS NEVER BEEN INSPECTED AT DETAIL SCALE. It is 19.9 mm of gold in elevation, stored deliberately as a fraction (`CNT_NOSE_F = 0.1860`) rather than a depth so it cannot silently stop meaning what it was measured to mean. It is the hero line of this picture and nobody has looked at it at close range. Render and crop before designing.  ·  WHAT IS BEHIND THE HANDS IS UNGATED. F45 (roof-aperture interior, 2.91e5 px², dead centre of the hero frame) and F15 (unlit roofed body, 6.92e5 px²) have never been measured against a photograph, so the galley behind bay 3 may simply render dead. Everything a customer sees through the bay IS built — roughly thirty `gal_*` meshes, plancha, galley_top, six hooks — but built is not lit. Render one frame and LOOK through the bay before committing the composition.  ·  T1_ALPHA if the hands are composited; and NOT through post.py, which discards the alpha channel (measured).

**SCREENER.** Excellent restraint and the best human noticing here — a serving surface at ~1.21 m is why adults lean and children get lifted — plus 100% conversion and 0% donor vehicle, no roundel, lowest trademark exposure, and a director's instinct in the empty second before the loop. But two hands exchanging food across a counter at f/2.8 in warm light is the single most-photographed frame in food and beverage; the bobble fringe and the running-off-both-edges brass do not lift it clear of that ocean. Needs hands that exist nowhere in 229 meshes, a new camera, and the relight.

---

## The toy that was never made — A 1:64 die-cast Combi on a peg-hook blister card, complete with sprue nub, tampo slop and a baseplate that reads MADE IN MEXICO — for a toy that does not exist and never will, of a bus that has no engine.  ·  screen 6.0  ·  `t16-1`

**THE IMAGE.** A carded toy, photographed dead-on and flat-lit, filling a 108 × 165 mm card. The bus lies in a clear vacuum-formed blister at 71.6 mm overall (1:64 of the propped 4580 mm silhouette), three-quarter rear, lid up and counter out — so the blister has a deep, awkward bulge at one end where the raised mural board sits, which is exactly the tell of a toy that fought its own packaging. Style: FLAT VECTOR FILL WITH SAMPLED OCCLUSION — hard flat red, hard flat cream, a single multiply pass of real contact shadow and ambient occlusion lifted straight off the 3D asset, so it reads as painted metal rather than as a render. The folk art is reduced to three gold masses and nothing else. The toy tells are the whole craft: a pale sprue nub under the rear valance, one tampo print sitting 0.3 mm off register on the flank, a hairline mould-parting seam down the roof, a wheel that sits a degree out of true. Card art: cream field, red header band, the wordmark in silver with its dark keyline, and a small black-and-white photograph of the real bus bottom-right. Header reads 'TACOMBI · No. 01 · 1963'. Under the blister, in 6 pt: 'ESCALA 1:64 · MOTOR: NINGUNO · TRANSMISIÓN: VENDIDA'. Bottom corner, a price sticker that has been peeled and put back crooked. No motion; if animated, one beat only — the blister lifts and the toy is not underneath.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** Delight, then a small pang. Everyone has held this card. The body knows the weight of the boxboard and the crack of the blister before the eye finishes reading it, and that recognition arrives free and instantly — which is exactly the currency a restaurant giveaway needs. The pang is the second beat: you cannot buy it. There is no toy. It is a picture of an object that was never manufactured, of a vehicle that was itself dismantled — engine scrapped, transmission sold — so the whole card is about a thing that stopped existing twice. Children take it because it is a toy. Adults keep it because it is a joke about wanting things.

**FORMATS.** Blister card, 108 × 165 mm, litho on 350 gsm folding boxboard, with gloss UV on the header band ONLY and a matte body — the exact finish split real peg-hook cards use, and the thing that makes the fake read as genuine at arm's length. Die-cut hang hole, top centre.  ·  Kids' postcard, 108 × 165 mm, 300 gsm uncoated, handed out with a meal: the card at 1:1 with the blister flattened to a drawn highlight. Uncoated so it takes a crayon — a keepsake a child can add to is a keepsake a family keeps.  ·  Six-up display panel, A2 420 × 594 mm, matte litho, for the wall: six cards on one board — the four documented liveries (green-and-bare from the conversion, green in service, red plain with TACOMBI.COM, red folk-art) plus two invented seasonal lids. Four SEPARATE drawings, never a composite.  ·  Kiss-cut sticker sheet, A6 105 × 148 mm: the carded toy as one sticker, the bus die-cut free of its blister as another, and the SAME drawing repeated at 1:64, 1:43 and 1:24 in a row with a scale bar under each — a scale joke nested inside a scale concept, and the cheapest thing in the program to produce.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** Die-cast VW bus toys are the single most saturated physical object in this category — this is the one concept that walks straight at the ocean rather than around it, and the escape has to be exact. It is not a toy of a VW bus; it is a toy of a TAQUERIA, and every tell says so: three holes in the flank, a counter cantilevered past the tail, a lid propped like an awning, and the card copy 'MOTOR: NINGUNO'. No real die-cast maker would ever tool a model whose whole selling point is that it cannot be pushed along a carpet. There is also a genuine format advantage the program should exploit deliberately: at 1:64 the nose roundel is 4.4 mm, and a tampo-printed roundel at 4.4 mm is a blob on EVERY die-cast ever made — so the one defect the owner has reported nine times is invisible by genre here, not by concealment. This is the only artefact in the program that ships correct while the emblem is still wrong.

**PRODUCTION.** Draw the toy tells in 2D on the render — the sprue nub, the parting seam, the off-register tampo, the crooked price sticker — do not model them; only the baseplate would repay actual geometry and it is not worth a revision. Reduce the folk art BY MEASUREMENT, not by eye: folk_gen.py's own census reads `COMP_TOP = {1: 47.8, 2: 73.7, 3: 84.4, 5: 87.8, 10: 93.5}` with `COMP_HIST` summing to 48 of 66 gold components carrying 2.29 % of the ink, so keeping three masses and deleting forty-eight loses almost nothing — at 1:64 those forty-eight are sub-0.02 mm anyway. Two things die at this scale and both are useful: the tread pitch falls to 0.51 mm (no lug count published, which is what we want) and the louvre pitch to 0.33 mm (draw as a tone block, not as ten lines). The one thing that must NOT die is the wordmark: at 1:64 the lockup is 20.0 × 8.5 mm, and silver on red with no dark edge is mud at that size. This is a DIFFERENT ARTEFACT from the F18 die-cut sticker — it borrows F18's rendering technique and honours none of its scene lock, and the proposal must say so plainly so it is not read as walking through F18's 'build it after the model is done' trigger.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** Run T1_ALPHA=1 for the silhouette (no frame in out/ has alpha; the switch is thirteen revisions un-run).  ·  Use deliver.py's existing layers/ output as the style's two inputs — it already writes combi_<view>_no_shadow.png and combi_<view>_shadow_only.png, which are literally the flat-fill reference and the occlusion pass this treatment needs.  ·  RESTORE THE OWNER'S OWN LOST CLAUSE INTO A CARRIER. NEXT_CONTEXT_PROMPT_rev39.md §7 item 3 reads 'Style LOCKED by me: cartoon with rendered depth — vector line and flat colour, shading and occlusion sampled from the 3D asset.' Everything after the em-dash was dropped at rev 43 and has been missing from every handoff for thirty-two revisions. It is a buildable pipeline, not a mood word, and this concept is built on it.  ·  FIX tex/senor.png's MISSING KEYLINE. SPEC §3 requires the wordmark 'SILVER with a dark keyline and drop shadow'; the shipped master has no dark ink at all. lid_gen.py already authors the convention as DARKLINE = (72, 26, 16). script_gen.py is NOT called by build.py, so regenerate the texture by hand or the render silently keeps the old file.  ·  For the six-up panel: add a livery switch. Only the red folk-art state is authored today; the three others are new artwork, and SPEC §7.1 forbids carrying folk art, script, glazing or roof-board treatment across states — four separate drawings, never one composite.  ·  DO NOT fix the emblem for this format. At 4.4 mm it is a tampo blob by genre, and touching it here would be spending the project's hardest open problem on the one artefact that does not need it.

**SCREENER.** Real charm — 'MOTOR: NINGUNO / TRANSMISION: VENDIDA', the blister bulging where the mural board fights its own packaging, and the smartest production insight in the whole set (at 1:64 a tampo roundel is a blob on every die-cast ever made, so the nine-times-reported emblem defect is invisible by genre, not by concealment). But fake-vintage carded blister packaging is a fully exhausted design meme done by every brand as self-promo; the escape is content inside a wholly borrowed form, and the six-up panel needs a livery switch that does not exist.

---

## THE CROSSING — A loop in which the bus never moves a single pixel and the world travels around it — Playa, then darkness, then New York.  ·  screen 6.0  ·  `t17-2`

**THE IMAGE.** One locked orthographic side elevation. The vehicle is centred, filling roughly 78 % of frame width, and it does not shift, rotate, scale or re-pose for the entire duration — not one pixel, not one frame. Everything that changes is LIGHT and GROUND. STATE A, PLAYA: warm open shade, the cream reading as sun-bleached ivory rather than white, a soft palm-frond shadow crawling slowly across the upper body from left to right, warm-neutral paving under the tyres, the folk art at full gold, the counter shelf out, the bus closed and quiet. STATE B, THE CROSSING: hard cut to black. Not a fade — a cut. For a full beat, roughly a third of the loop's duration, the frame is almost entirely dark and the ONLY thing visible is the festoon bulb string, still lit, tracing the vehicle's whole silhouette in one continuous warm amber line — drip rail, over the rear roof dome, along the propped tail board. No ground. No horizon. Nothing to stand on. Just the outline of a bus, drawn in light, hanging in nothing. STATE C, NUEVA YORK: cut up into cool grey-blue market fill — high indoor fluorescent, concrete floor around #5D6C70, breeze-block wall behind — with the headlamps now ON as practicals, warm against the cold room, and the bulb string reading differently because it is competing with room light instead of owning it. Hold. Loop back to Playa. CADENCE: stepped, 8–12 fps, with holds — not a 24 fps tween. Each state gets a hold long enough to read as a photograph before the cut. The cuts are hard on the beat. Palette: State A is warm neutrals plus gold; State B is one amber and black; State C is cool blue-grey plus one warm amber accent. The red is the ONLY constant across all three and it must be graded to sit still — that is what makes the vehicle read as the fixed point. Composition of the final beat: leave the near-counter third of frame slightly open and unpopulated, so the viewer occupies the position a customer stands in.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** Tenderness, and then a small awe. You are watching a thing be carried a very long way by people who cared enough to keep it. The dark beat is the whole piece — you sit with a bus that is nowhere, suspended, asleep, and the only sign it is still itself is a string of bulbs somebody left on. It is the exact feeling of watching something you love go into a shipping container. Then the light comes back and it is colder and harder and there are people in it, and it is fine — better than fine — and you feel the relief of arrival because you were made to sit through the crossing first. Nothing in this category has ever asked a viewer to sit in the dark for a beat, and that is precisely why it will hold a thumb on a feed.

**FORMATS.** Social loop, 1080 × 1080 and 1080 × 1920, 4–6 s, seamless, silent-first (designed to read with sound off; the audio is a bonus, not a crutch).  ·  Print triptych — three panels, identical camera, identical pose, three lights: A3 landscape 420 × 297 each, hung as a set of three, or one long 1200 × 300 mm strip. THE PIECE DEGRADES GRACEFULLY: if the motion never gets made, the still triptych is already a complete and beautiful object, and it is a MAP — three places in one frame — with no coastline and no dotted line anywhere.  ·  Store-window LED loop, 16:9, running slow at the physical location — the bus in the window, looping through its own history, next to the actual bus.  ·  Three-frame animated favicon / app icon: warm, black, cold. Reads at 32 px because the bulb line is a single continuous stroke.  ·  Postcard set of three, 105 × 148 mm, sold banded together — the cheapest way to own the whole idea.  ·  Title sequence bed for any brand film, 6 s, with type landing on the dark beat where there is room for it.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** Every motion piece in this category moves the bus. Drive-bys, rolling wheels, road loops, a bus crossing a sunset — it is the entire vocabulary, and it is also, for THIS vehicle, a factual lie: the engine was scrapped and the transmission sold before it ever served a taco, so a rolling wheel is the brand contradicting its own About page, and it would put motion blur on a tyre tread whose lug count the project explicitly refuses to publish. Nailing the bus to the centre of frame for the entire duration and moving the world instead is simultaneously the truth, a stronger graphic device, and a decision no competitor can arrive at because no competitor's bus is immobile. The dark middle beat is the second thing nobody does — merch loops are relentlessly bright, and a piece that goes black for a third of its length reads as confidence. And it delivers a ROUTE with no map, no arrow, no dotted line and no road: three lights in one frame is the map.

**PRODUCTION.** THIS IS THE TECHNICALLY RISKY ONE and it is worth being blunt about why. FIRST: there is NO OWNER RULING ON ANIMATION ANYWHERE IN THE RECORD — grep -rli 'animat' across every .md and .txt returns one file and one hit, a persona description in PANEL_rev61.md. Motion is unmandated territory and must be put to him as multiple choice with the still triptych attached, not inferred. SECOND: the three environments must be delivered as COMPOSITES over T1_ALPHA frames, not as in-scene environments — that route is doubly blocked (F57 records that T1_SCENE=playa renders no vehicle at all, 'a dark cylinder on a grey plane', reported and not diagnosed; and F92 parks playa_env.py as 'DEPRIORITISED, NOT CANCELLED' and licenses nothing to do now). Compositing sidesteps both, and it is honest about what it is: a cut-out on a ground, not a lit object in a scene — say so rather than implying a relight we did not do. The one thing that IS licensed is the rig itself: F259, 'Relight the studio and fix the ground if necessary', with the single binding constraint 'DO NOT change RED' — that is the option he declined. THIRD: the pose is FIXED across all three states, and that is a deliberate engineering decision, not laziness. It dodges the project's worst animation blocker outright — t1_shell.py couples LID_W to the lid angle as 1/sin(LID_OPEN_DEG), so a naive T1_LIDDEG sweep inflates the mural board from 1.22 m to 6.84 m at 10° and throws ZeroDivisionError at 0°. Nothing hinges here, so none of that applies. FOURTH: FLICKER. The render is not run-to-run deterministic and the floor is measured — ~2.04 % of pixels differing by >8 levels whole-frame, 7.90 % IN THE BOX ON THE BODY (F308). For a still that is noise; for a sequence that is a visible boil on the largest flat panels, and OpenImageDenoise is spatial-only with no temporal pass. The stepped 8–12 fps cadence is chosen partly to absorb it — a hand-drawn cadence wants a boiling line — but that must be a DECIDED choice with the floor measured in the same window, not a discovery in dailies. FIFTH: post.py's grain seed is hardcoded (`seed=7`), so identical grain lands on every frame and reads as dirt on the lens, not film grain. One line, seeded from the frame index; nothing in the tree has a frame index today. BUDGET: no animation infrastructure exists (zero frame_start / keyframe_insert; every hinge bakes its rotation into vertices), so one pose is one full rebuild at roughly 5.8 min a frame, and CLAUDE.md forbids fanning out Blender. A 3-state stepped loop of 24–36 frames is 2.5–3.5 hours sequential. That is affordable. Twenty-four fps is not.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** FIX F144 — 'THE FESTOON BULBS READ AS PEARLS, NOT LIGHTS.' This is not a nice-to-have; it is the concept's load-bearing element, because the dark beat is nothing but the bulb line. In every reference frame the string reads as a continuous glowing amber bead, not a row of spheres. Push the emission above post.py's BLOOM_THR = 0.94 so it actually flares, and look at a crop before and after. The emitters exist (STATE.md: bulb_string | 1, tb_bulbs | 1, tb_bulbflex | 1) and T1_BULB_STR / T1_BULB_BASEV are already ablation switches, so nothing needs modelling.  ·  RUN T1_ALPHA=1 AND VERIFY THE SHADOW SURVIVES IN PARTIAL ALPHA. F159 measured it once on a proof frame — 184 distinct alpha values, 57.9 % transparent / 25.5 % opaque / 16.6 % partial, with the contact shadow living in the partial band — and it has not been re-run since rev 62. The whole three-ground composite depends on it. Do NOT route through post.py, which returns colortype 2.  ·  THREE GROUND TREATMENTS, which is the half of F259 the record keeps naming and nobody has acted on: warm paving, nothing at all, cool concrete. Ladder T1_SHADOW for the two lit states — it ships at 9.0 and was set to the WEAKEST of four photographed readings, which is right for a fidelity target and wrong for a picture — and watch control C3 (it fires at T1_SHADOW=20).  ·  MEASURE THE FLICKER AS FLICKER BEFORE COMMITTING RENDER HOURS: render the same pose twice and diff, then render two adjacent lighting states and diff, both in the SAME painted window on the body panel where the recorded floor is 7.90 %. Publish both figures side by side. A difference with no floor under it is not a measurement, and the floor must be taken in the box being read.  ·  SEED post.py's GRAIN FROM THE FRAME INDEX. One line plus threading an index through judge_set.sh. Do it before anything is graded.  ·  LIVERY DISCIPLINE, and it is a rule-11 trap this concept walks straight toward: the honest version renders the FOLK-ART bus in all three lights, which SPEC §7.2 supports ('the RED, folk-art bus is the one standing in Nolita, having been shipped from Mexico', graded R). It must NEVER show the folk-art flank under a chalkboard lid, or the Nolita glass lettering on a Playa body — that is a fake, the owner has ruled on it, and it has already killed F99, F100 and F140. If the New York beat is wanted in the plain-red Nolita state, that is a whole new livery build (build.py binds folk art, script and Calidad unconditionally) and it should be scoped as such rather than faked.  ·  NOT REQUIRED, and worth stating: no hinge animation, no new geometry, no export path, no large-format chain. The fixed pose is what buys all of that.

**SCREENER.** The dark middle beat — a bus nowhere, asleep, with only the bulb line still lit — is a real and unusual piece of nerve, and nailing the vehicle still while the world moves is both truthful and graphically stronger than a drive-by; but three-lighting-states-of-one-object is standard motion design otherwise, the whole concept rests on a bulb string F144 grades exhausted (T1_BULB_STR=60 measured WORSE, 1.40 → 0.88) while the brief calls that lever 'there and unused', and the 7.90% in-body flicker floor with no temporal denoise is a live hazard.

---

## LA BASE — The Rank — One bus, printed forty times on a true-scale metric grid, every one parked and every one open: a fleet register for a company that owns a single vehicle.  ·  screen 6.0  ·  `t18-0`

**THE IMAGE.** Orthographic side elevation only — no perspective, no vanishing point, no ground plane, no shadow. The `side` camera in studio.py is already a true parallel projection (`lens=None, ortho=5.90`), which is the whole point: an advert photograph is always a projection, a plate is not. One frame, rendered once, tiled. Every bus is IDENTICAL and every bus is DEPLOYED — lid up at the shipped 76°, tail board propped at its measured 38.0°, counter cantilevered, bulbs lit, folk art and silver script on the flank. Serving side (+Y) presented, so the nose roundel never appears and only the four hubcap bullseyes carry any VW content.

LAYOUT: half-drop. Wallpaper spec, 1:20 on a 520 mm roll — the deployed silhouette measures 4.580 × 3.132 m off the machine (STATE.md `x range [-2.157, 2.160]` with the tail board reaching x −2.420; `bbox top 3.132`), so at 1:20 each unit is 229.0 × 156.6 mm. Horizontal pitch 260 mm gives exactly two across a 520 mm roll with a 31 mm gutter; vertical pitch 200 mm, alternate columns offset 100 mm. The gutters are deliberately mean — this is a rank of buses parked nose-to-tail at a base, not a scatter.

LIGHT: flat, frontal, shadowless. Nothing on the sheet should suggest a time of day. The buses are objects in a catalogue, not scenes.

PALETTE (5 flats, and every value is quoted, none sampled from a render): body red `t1_mats.RED = (0.5520, 0.0294, 0.0176)  # = sRGB(196,49,36), hue 5.0, sat 0.816`; cream `CREAM = (0.6172, 0.6308, 0.5776) # = sRGB(206,208,200)`; folk-art gold `GOLD = (0.8600, 0.5400, 0.0600)` (linear — no sRGB is written in the source for this one; convert and declare it, don't guess it); dark maroon keyline `lid_gen.DARKLINE = (72, 26, 16)`; silver for the script, which script_gen.py measured off the real vehicle as "neutral silver, not white and not gold: median RGB (127,122,125), saturation 0.080". GROUND: `W_DUST_COL_UP = (0.5077, 0.3775, 0.2340)     # settled ochre, sRGB(189,165,133)` — the vehicle's own road-film ochre, so the paper is the colour of the yard it parks in.

GUTTER FURNITURE: a 0.25 pt hairline rule runs the full width between every row, broken once per repeat by a 5 pt caption in the gutter: `1:20 · 4 580 mm · 1963`. The wallpaper is therefore also a ruler — hold anything against the wall and the rule tells you its length.

CHASE (v2, not v1): one bus in twelve is SHUT and unlit. Never in the same column twice, never within one unit of the sheet trim.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** ABUNDANCE, AND THE JOKE UNDERNEATH IT. A company with one bus prints wallpaper as though it had forty, and does it deadpan, on a metric grid, like a livestock recognition chart. The feeling is a full lot at midday — everything present, nothing going anywhere. Every VW-bus pattern on the market sells you departure; this one sells you the relief of having arrived and parked. And it rewards proximity twice: at four metres it is a warm ochre field with red bands; at forty centimetres you find the counter, the three holes, the propped board and a wordmark, and you realise every single bus is the same bus.

**FORMATS.** Wallpaper, 520 mm roll, 1:20, half-drop, horizontal pitch 260 mm (exactly 2 across), vertical pitch 200 mm, drop offset 100 mm. Uncoated non-woven, matte. Restaurant back-of-house, WCs, stairwells — spaces where people stand close.  ·  Wrapping paper / tissue, 500 × 700 mm sheet, 1:50 — unit 91.6 × 62.6 mm, pitch 100 × 80 mm (5 across the sheet exactly), 8.4 mm gutter. Deliberately denser than the wallpaper; at this scale the hubcap badge falls to 1.7 mm and stops resolving as a glyph at all.  ·  Tissue for takeaway wrap and shipper boxes, 500 × 750 mm, same 1:50 tile.  ·  Tea towel / apron, 500 × 700 mm, 1:20 single course of two buses plus the dimension rule — a strip, not a field.  ·  Tote gusset and lining fabric, 1:33 continuous.  ·  Shipper tape, 48 mm, one 1:60 course.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** The stock VW-bus repeat is a doodle scatter: different buses, different colours, different three-quarter angles, surfboards, sunsets, a peace sign, drawn with a wobbly outline. Ours breaks every one of those rules at once — ONE bus, IDENTICAL every time, ORTHOGRAPHIC, on a strict metric grid, stationary, and open for service. It reads as a fleet register or a plane-spotter's chart, not as merchandise. Structurally it is closer to a Becher typology than to a Redbubble tile.

It is also the only VW-bus pattern that could be printed by someone who measured the vehicle: the 1:20 claim is honest because the source camera is a genuine parallel projection and the length is machine-written (STATE.md `dims L=4.065`, and 4.580 with the board propped). A photograph-derived tile cannot make that claim and no competitor has the mesh to make it either.

And it dodges both live hazards by construction: the side elevation contains no VW nose roundel — the owner's ninth reported defect and the highest trademark-exposure element on the object — while showing the counter, the three serving hatches and the propped board, none of which Volkswagen ever built.

**PRODUCTION.** COLOUR IS A DECLARED DECISION, NOT A SAMPLE. Do not eyedrop the render: flank_compare on out/r75_side.png reads the render's flank at median (196,81,67), G/R 0.422, against the photograph's (158,16,6), G/R 0.114 — it images salmon. And do not average the photographs either: probe_rev71_red.py prints the prohibition itself — "the photograph's red G/R is 0.0149 .. 0.0344, a 2.3x span. DO NOT QUOTE A SINGLE FIGURE FOR IT." The tree currently holds FOUR unreconciled reds (t1_mats sRGB(196,49,36); lid_gen (159,45,22); cal_gen (214,46,30); plus the render's). Pick one, write it into SPEC with its provenance line, and use it everywhere.

GRAIN IS A PATTERN HAZARD NOBODY HAS NAMED. post.py's `grain(srgb, amount=1.0, sigma=0.0042, seed=7, ...)` with `rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)` is a FIXED seed. Tile a post-processed frame and every unit carries byte-identical noise, so the grain itself repeats at the tile pitch and reads as a ghost lattice or a dirty plate. Either skip post.py entirely (recommended — this is flat print, it wants no bloom, no chromatic aberration and no vignette) or re-seed per tile.

DO NOT ROUTE THE MASTER THROUGH post.py FOR ANOTHER REASON: it destroys alpha. Measured — RGBA in (colortype 6), colortype 2 out (`rgba[..., :3]` at the top, 3-channel array at the save). You need the cut-out.

PRINT: 5 spot flats, no process build, no halftone on the body. Screen for the wallpaper, flexo or litho for the paper. On uncoated stock the vermillion will need a double hit. Refuse any separation that halftones the red field — a screened flat at this area is where the sheet starts looking cheap.

RESOLUTION: nothing above 1600×1100 exists on disk and `out/` is untracked. At 1:20 the unit is 229 mm, so a 300 dpi master wants ~2705 px on the long edge — above anything currently in the tree. The MARGIN stitch chain has been proven ONCE, at 3840×2640 (rev 57), and hq_render.py does not export `T1_FX=0` where the chain it replaced explicitly did. Export it by hand or the strips band at the seams and then post.py lays a second optics pass over the join. Read stitch.py's printed worst-z and its exit code (2 on a seam), not the redirect's rc.

SECONDARY MARK, IF THE FIELD NEEDS BREAKING: the flank rosettes, whose measured gold-core areas are 3632 / 3564 / 1838 / 1816 / 1524 / 1435 / 1076 / 740 mm² (folk_gen.FLANK_ROSETTES) — equivalent diameters 68.0 / 67.4 / 48.4 / 48.1 / 44.1 / 42.7 / 37.0 / 30.7 mm. CARRY THE CAVEAT: OPEN_FINDINGS F16 is open and says "every flank rosette is drawn at the diameter of its gold core" — i.e. those are the CORES and the real rosette, with its cream petals and maroon ring, is LARGER. Drawing to these numbers reproduces a known undersizing error at wallpaper scale.

NOT F18. This is not the die-cut sticker and is not gated by F18's "build it after the model is done" trigger, nor by F191/F193 which hold the DELIVERY render. Say so in the proposal or the hold will read as blocking work it does not cover.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** THE SCRIPT'S MISSING KEYLINE — the one blocking defect, and the cheapest fix in the inventory. SPEC §3 requires the wordmark "SILVER with a dark keyline and drop shadow"; the shipped tex/senor.png (4096×1738 RGBA) contains NO dark ink at all — among pixels with alpha>200 the 1st-percentile luma is 168.3 and the fraction below luma 80 is 0.0000, and grep finds no keyline/shadow path in script_gen.py or t1_mats.silver_script. At 1:20 the lockup is 63.9 mm wide on a wall (build.py `SCR = dict(x0=0.784, x1=-0.494, ...)` = 1278 mm on the vehicle); silver on red with no edge is mud at that size. script_gen.py is NOT called by build.py — regenerate by hand or the render silently keeps the old texture. Guard it in the same edit with a row that reads the asset's own sub-luma-80 fraction.  ·  THE SCRIPT'S PLACEMENT — flank_compare on out/r75_side.png: "the render's lockup has to move +76.2 mm forward and −47.6 mm down", with an independent route agreeing at +83 / +80 mm aft and the ink top +61.1 mm high. 3.8 mm on the wall at 1:20. Two routes agree; it is free to fix.  ·  ONE ALPHA SIDE RENDER. Every frame in out/ is colortype 2 (no alpha) and `T1_ALPHA` appears in ZERO ledgers — it has not been exercised since rev 62. Run `T1_ALPHA=1 T1_FX=0` on `side`, then run deliver.py and watch control C1 go from FAILED to PASS. Do not send the result through post.py.  ·  MARK-NEUTRAL HUBCAPS FOR WALLPAPER SCALE. At 1:20 the hubcap is 13.7 mm and its VW badge 4.3 mm (CAP_D 274 mm from `CAP_EMBLEM_D = 0.3170 * CAP_D` against STATE.md's `4 rings R 0.043429`). No switch exists to omit or repaint it — the 8 `capvw` objects and their `capwhite` material are built unconditionally. A three-state `T1_VW_MARK` (ship / repaint to plate colour / not built) would let each product pick its own posture. At 1:50 wrapping scale the badge is 1.7 mm and this does not arise. Also note STATE.md: "hubcap badge is SELF-CONSISTENCY ONLY — CAP_EMBLEM_WFRAC has never been compared to a frame", so at wallpaper scale you would be publishing an unverified proportion.  ·  THE CHASE VARIANT IS THE ONLY PART THAT NEEDS NEW CAPABILITY, AND v1 SHOULD SHIP WITHOUT IT. A shut bus needs the LID_W fix (see concept 3). A livery chase needs three unbuilt paint states. Neither is required for the wallpaper to be excellent.

**SCREENER.** 'A fleet register for a company that owns one bus' is a good joke and the Becher-typology framing is apt — the unequal gutters and the 1:20 dimension rule in the gutter are real craft; but strip the argument and it is still a repeat pattern of a VW bus, which is the single most crowded object in this market, and 'ours is different because it's orthographic and identical' is a distinction the four-metre viewer will never make.

---

## Service Temperature — Merchandise printed in thermochromic ink: cold, the bus is shut, dark and parked; warm, the shut bus vanishes and the open, lit, folk-art bus is underneath — the vehicle opens for service when the object gets hot.  ·  screen 6.0  ·  `t19-2`

**THE IMAGE.** TWO REGISTERED LAYERS, ONE CAMERA. Both drawn from the orthographic side elevation so they align pixel-for-pixel with no perspective drift.

LAYER A — PERMANENT, printed first, underneath: THE BUS OPEN AND WORKING. Mural lid propped up on its struts, tail board out, counter dressed, three hatches open with the galley behind them, bulb string lit along the drip rail and the lid frame, folk art full on the red, silver wordmark, Calidad burst. Warm, loud, generous. Three to five spot inks.

LAYER B — THERMOCHROMIC, printed over it, opaque cold and clear above activation: THE BUS SHUT AND UNLIT. Lid down flat on the roof. Tail board folded. Apertures blanked. No bulbs, no counter dressing, no folk art. Just the two-tone shell, the belt-line split, the wheels, and a long horizontal cream mass — a parked vehicle at 5 a.m. Printed in a single deep near-black/charcoal leuco ink so cold it reads as night.

COLD STATE: you see a quiet, dark, shut bus on a pale ground. It looks like a closed shop. It is deliberately a little sad.

WARM STATE: the charcoal clears from the bottom up as the heat travels, and the colour, the bulbs, the flowers and the food come up through it. The lid appears to lift because the dark lid-down shape dissolves and the lid-up shape was always there.

THE TRANSITION IS THE DESIGN. Leuco dyes do not switch cleanly — they clear over a 3–5°C band, so the reveal is a soft irregular front that follows the actual heat in the object. On a mug filled with coffee the bus opens from the base upward over about four seconds. Nobody has to design that; the physics does it. Specify a 45°C ink on the mug so ONLY a hot drink opens it, and a 31°C body-heat ink on the cloth and stickers so a child's hand opens it.

PALETTE: cold, one ink — near-black on cream. Warm, the full five: red, cream, gold, dark maroon, silver.

SCALE: the shut bus at 90–110 mm long on a mug wrap; 60 mm on a jar label; 130 mm on a sticker sheet panel.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** Wonder, then ownership. This is not a picture of a thing that happens — it is the thing happening, in your hands, on demand, every time. A child pours horchata into the mug and the bus opens. That is not a graphic; it is a trick the child gets to perform on other people, which is the exact mechanism by which an object becomes something families keep rather than something they use.

And the trick MEANS something, which is what separates it from a novelty. The brand's whole founding sentence is that this bus drove once, stopped, got cut open and started feeding people. Heat is the thing that opens it — heat is what the vehicle exists to make. So the object's state change is the company's own story, told without a word of copy, by a mug going warm.

There is also a quieter adult version of the feeling: you own an object that is shut and dark most of the day, and you can open it. That is a small daily kindness to keep on a desk.

**FORMATS.** ENAMEL OR CERAMIC MUG, 300 ml, wrap print, 45°C activation. Only a hot drink opens the bus — coffee, chocolate, atole. This is the flagship and the one that justifies the tooling.  ·  HOT-SAUCE / SALSA JAR LABEL, 60×90 mm, 45°C. Opens in the pan, or in the hand of anyone who has just eaten. A label that responds to what the jar is for.  ·  A6 STICKER SHEET (105×148 mm) with ONE thermochromic panel at 31°C — body heat. The rest of the sheet is conventional: the aperture triptych, a wheel bullseye, one rosette, the '1963' plate, the bulb strip. Kiss-cut, 4 mm web, no concave notch under 3 mm so the negative lifts in one pull.  ·  TORTILLA-WARMER CLOTH, ~260 mm round, screen print on heavy cotton, 31°C. The most correct object in the whole program: it is warm exactly when it is doing its job, so the bus is open exactly while you are eating.  ·  NOT apparel. Leuco on a garment fatigues under UV and washing and will fail visibly within a season. Refuse it.  ·  LIMITED, HONEST TEST ONLY — a microencapsulated scent varnish on the sticker sheet's plume element. One SKU, 200–300 units, not a range. Scent print is the highest-risk element in this concept and it should be allowed to fail small.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** The entire VW-bus merchandise category — every trope in it, sunset through blueprint — is PRINTED IMAGES OF A VEHICLE. This is not an image. It is an object with two states and a physical input, and there is nothing like it on the shelf.

More than that: it makes the derivativeness question irrelevant. Nobody assesses whether the drawing is generic, because the drawing is not the product — the CHANGE is the product. A competitor can trace our silhouette and cannot copy a mug that opens.

And it converts the brand's most-owned fact into a mechanism. Everyone else's bus is going somewhere; ours arrived and opened. Here the object literally cannot open unless it is hot. The strategy is not printed on it, it is enacted by it.

**PRODUCTION.** THIS IS A SPOT-COLOUR JOB END TO END. Thermochromic leuco is an opaque spot ink — no process build, no gradients, no halftone under it. That constrains the permanent layer to 3–5 flat inks, which is exactly what AUDIT_rev43's surviving colour row says the vehicle is: 'ONE 70° hue wedge plus four neutrals. Seven inks printed; five if cut.'

TWO ACTIVATION GRADES, DELIBERATELY: 45°C for the mug and jar (only a hot drink or a warm pan opens it), 31°C for the cloth and stickers (a hand opens it). Do not use one grade for everything — the difference between 'I can do this' and 'only heat can do this' is the whole emotional split of the range.

HONEST FAILURE MODES, STATED UP FRONT. Leuco dyes fatigue with cycle count and degrade under UV — a mug left on a sunny windowsill will lose contrast in months. They are not dishwasher-safe above their range. The clear state is never perfectly clear; expect a faint ghost of the shut bus over the open one, so design the permanent layer to survive a 5–10% grey veil and do not put fine detail where the shut-bus lid sits. Prototype ten units and cycle them 200 times before any order.

REGISTRATION IS THE OTHER RISK. Two layers must align within about 0.3 mm or the ghost reads as a mis-print rather than an after-image. Use the orthographic elevation for both — it is the only camera whose two states cannot drift — and give the printer registration marks outside the trim.

DESIGN THE COLD STATE PROPERLY. It is what the object looks like most of the time and it is the half nobody has ever seen: the shut bus has not been built or rendered in 76 revisions. It must be a good, quiet, complete picture in its own right — not a placeholder waiting for the reveal.

SAY WHAT THIS IS NOT. It is NOT the F18 die-cut sticker. F18's style is locked to 'cartoon with rendered depth', its scene to 'nothing but the bus, die-cut tight, plus the sun and the papel picado', and its trigger — 'build it after the model is done' — has not fired. This is a different artefact, on a different substrate, and offering it as F18 would be walking through a gate the owner has held four times. Say so in the proposal and the hold stops applying.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** FIX THE LID WIDTH COUPLING BEFORE ANYTHING ELSE — THE SHUT BUS CANNOT BE BUILT TODAY. t1_shell.py carries LID_W = (LID_X0 - LID_X1) / LID_ASPECT / math.sin(math.radians(LID_OPEN_DEG)): the mural board's WIDTH is coupled to its opening angle as 1/sin. Closing the lid INFLATES the board (76°→1.2237 m shipped, 30°→2.3748 m, 10°→6.8379 m, 0°→ZeroDivisionError). Freeze LID_W at its shipped value, add T1_LID_WCOUPLE=1 to restore the coupling, and add a guard row that the built panel's width does not move across at least three angles — WATCHED FAILING on the coupled build before it is believed passing.  ·  GIVE THE NEAR-CLOSED CASE A BRANCH IN THE LID ASSERT. t1_shell's assert _top.co.y > LID_Y_HINGE + 0.010 picks the topmost vertex, which is degenerate when the panel is flat. It is correct for a posed still and wrong for a swept state; it needs a closed branch with the cause named in the row's own label, not a relaxation.  ·  RENDER THE SHUT STATE AND LOOK AT IT. It has never existed in 76 revisions and it is half the product. Two frames — shut and open, same camera — is about twelve minutes of machine time and it is the proof the whole concept turns on.  ·  USE THE side ORTHO (lens=None, ortho=5.90) AND NOT front/rear. Side is measured clean at 77.8% × 77.5% frame fill with no clipping. The front/rear orthos at 3.55 cover z 0.2997–2.7403 against a vehicle reaching z 0 and a bbox top of 3.132 — out/r75_front.png has ink running off both borders, so those views would print the clipping.  ·  FIX F144 FOR THE OPEN STATE — 'THE FESTOON BULBS READ AS PEARLS, NOT LIGHTS.' The revealed bus's lit bulb string is the payoff of the reveal; if it reads as a row of dull beads the warm state has no reward. M['bulb'] is emissive at (1.000, 0.760, 0.442) with T1_BULB_STR as the lever.  ·  SEPARATE TO 3–5 SPOT INKS AND DECLARE THE RED. Four authored reds exist in the tree and the render's images salmon (render (196,81,67) G/R 0.422 vs photograph (158,16,6) G/R 0.114). Both layers must share one declared ink.  ·  ADD T1_LIDDEG / T1_TB_TILT AS CLEAN POSE SWITCHES once LID_W is frozen, so the shut and open states are one render queue and not two hand-edited trees.

**SCREENER.** The reveal genuinely MEANS something — heat is what this vehicle exists to make, so the object's state change is the founding sentence enacted rather than printed — and splitting 45°C (only a hot drink) from 31°C (your hand) is a considered emotional distinction; but thermochromic heat-reveal is the most worn gimmick in the promotional-products catalogue and a jaded AD has seen a hundred, it is hard-blocked by the same LID_W coupling, and it leans on F144's refuted bulb lever for its payoff. Best observation in it: the shut bus has never been built or rendered in 76 revisions, and it is half the product.

---

## LA VENTANA — the view from behind the counter — The only VW bus picture in existence taken from INSIDE the bus, looking out through the serving hatch at the customer.  ·  screen 6.0  ·  `free1-0`

**THE IMAGE.** Camera inside the galley, at the middle serving bay. There is no such camera in this project — all 14 entries in `studio.views()` sit outside the vehicle, the nearest at 3.4 m. Put one at roughly x = 0.0470 (`BAY_CX[1]`, the middle bay's own measured centre), y just inboard of the flank, z ≈ 1.50, a 35 mm lens at f/2.8, looking straight out through the aperture.

The aperture IS the composition: `BAY_W = 0.5155` wide by `Z_HEAD - Z_SILL` = 0.403 m tall, `BAY_R = 0.0550` corner radius — a rounded rectangle mask, 1.28:1, filling the frame. Read the picture near to far. BOTTOM EDGE: the counter slab running left to right, 107 mm thick (`CNT_ZT, CNT_ZB = 1.2540, 1.1470`) with 321 mm of plan depth (`CNT_Y_IN, CNT_Y_OUT = 0.8450, 1.1660`), its brass nosing catching one hard specular line — the only crisp bright edge anywhere in the frame. TOP EDGE: the bobble fringe hanging INTO shot, backlit — 18 mm white balls at 26 mm pitch (`FRINGE_R, FRINGE_PITCH, FRINGE_INSET = 0.0090, 0.0260, 0.0130`), soft, glowing, scalloping the top of the picture. LEFT: the pillar between bays 0 and 1 cut by the frame edge, a menu card on it, the plancha's steel intruding at bottom-left. THROUGH THE HOLE: three to four stops over. Blown paving, one moving palm shadow, and — if anyone at all — a forearm, a hand on the counter lip, a paper plate. Never a face.

LIGHT: one dominant source, the sun outside the hole. Everything inside is bounce. VALUE STRUCTURE: a dark warm ring around a white void, roughly 0.2 : 1.0. PALETTE: interior collapses to warm greys, brass and steel with the red shell reading near-black; exterior is a single blown near-white plus one band of paving grey and one green frond. Two values, three hues. Nothing else.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** You are on the working side of the window. Not being sold to — serving. The feeling is the specific calm inside a busy place: cool, dark, cluttered, competent, with a hot bright world pressing at the hole. Every VW bus image ever made is a picture OF the bus, made by someone standing outside wanting to own it. This is a picture FROM the bus, made by someone who works there. That inversion is the entire payload, and it turns a customer into staff for one second, which is the most flattering thing hospitality can do to a person.

**FORMATS.** Panoramic print 900 × 300 mm (3:1), hung above a real counter — the aperture's own 1.28:1 opened out  ·  Triptych, one bay each, 3 × 400 × 500 mm — uses the measured 0.516 / 0.515 / 0.516 equal bays as a real rhythm  ·  Email header / site hero 1600 × 500 px: the blown hole is the type area, black type straight on white, no box, no scrim  ·  Phone lock screen 1170 × 2532, single-bay vertical crop — the fringe at the top, the counter at the bottom  ·  Printed on the INSIDE LINING of a tote. Only the person carrying it ever sees it  ·  12-second loop, 1:1 square: the fringe swings once, a frond crosses the light, the plancha steams. The bus never moves because it cannot — the engine was scrapped

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** It is structurally impossible to make this image with any other asset on earth. A stock VW bus has no galley, so there is nothing to stand in. This repository has roughly thirty `gal_*` meshes plus `plancha`, `counter`/`counter_nosing`/`counter_top`, three `menucard`s and three `fringe` objects — an interior that exists because someone modelled a kitchen. It also carries ZERO trademark exposure: no roundel, no hubcap, no bus silhouette anywhere in frame. It is a hole, a counter and a fringe. And it is immune to the project's two loudest measured defects — the paint's gloss deficit (gloss_compare 0.412 of the photograph against a 0.60 bar) and the salmon red (flank_compare render G/R 0.422 vs photograph 0.114) — because there is almost no bodywork and almost no red in the picture.

**PRODUCTION.** Shoot it as one frame, not a cut-out — this concept never needs alpha, so it walks past the fact that `T1_ALPHA` has not been run since rev 62 and that `post.py` destroys alpha (measured: colortype 6 in, colortype 2 out). Optics ON is correct here; a slightly veiled bloom through the hatch is the point.

Honest ceiling on the interior: the register's F45 prose ("untextured white blocks") is REFUTED by F98 with numbers — interior/exterior cream value ratio render 0.705 against the photograph's 0.774 (the render's interior is DARKER, not brighter), relative std inside the bay 0.161 vs 0.060, edge density 0.231 vs 0.200. So the galley is closer to usable than the prose says. But F45's roof-aperture half is still "UNGATED and never measured", and nothing has ever been rendered from in there. Render one frame and look at a crop before committing a single day of art direction.

Do not fan out Blender — 4 cores, CPU-bound, ~5.5 min a frame at 1600×1100/96 spp. One sequential background queue.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** A camera inside the vehicle. Add `hatch` and `galley` entries to `studio.views()`. Then check `_cull_foreground(loc, tgt, margin=2.20)` does not cull the shell we are standing inside — it exists to hide geometry between camera and subject, and here the subject surrounds the camera.  ·  Interior key light. The rig today is a 13 × 8.5 m white softbox outside; it will flood through the aperture and flatten everything. Needs a practical — the warmer, a work lamp — which is new. F259 is the licence: the owner ruled 'Relight the studio and fix the ground if necessary', the rig is open, and only RED is closed.  ·  F72, which is dead centre of this frame and OPEN: the stainless warmer reads mean 186.6, the caddy 185.4 and painted cream 187.8 — within 2.4 DN, where `ref_side.jpg` shows a 60+ DN spread — and `gal_caddy` has no dark frame member at all. In an exterior hero that is a nit; at counter level it is the subject.  ·  F14 — `gal_end_f` sees past its own end (OPEN, INHERITED-rev52, the oldest live row, 260 mm show side). An interior camera looks straight down the galley's long axis and will see exactly the sight line nobody has ever re-measured.  ·  Depth of field discipline: `aim()` reads `T1_FSTOP` and prints measured near/far/hyperfocal every frame. At f/2.8 with fringe at ~0.3 m and the outside at infinity, read those printed numbers rather than eyeballing the falloff.  ·  Nothing else. No emblem work, no gloss work, no red work, no alpha, no stitch.

**SCREENER.** Well-observed and honestly grounded — correctly cites F98 refuting F45's 'untextured white blocks' prose rather than inheriting it, and correctly notes the frame dodges both the gloss deficit and the salmon red because almost no bodywork is in it. But shooting out through the pass from the working side is standard hospitality editorial; the POV inversion is the whole payload and it is not strange enough to carry a program. F14 (oldest inherited row) and F72 both sit dead centre of frame, so it is not as cheap as it reads.

---

## AQUÍ — the 1:1 shadow — Not a picture of the bus: the bus's cast shadow, printed at full size on the restaurant floor, so customers queue standing inside the footprint of a vehicle that is two thousand miles away.  ·  screen 6.0  ·  `free5-1`

**THE IMAGE.** A single warm near-black shape on the existing floor, 4.58 m long with the tail board propped and 1.75 m across the body, oriented so the three serving-hatch positions face the real counter. Not a silhouette — a SHADOW: soft-edged and lighter where it is far from the object it falls from (the roof line, the raised lid), hard and dense where the object touches ground (four tyre contact patches on the measured track — TRACK_F 1.3690, TRACK_R 1.3590, wheelbase 2.4000 — and the tail-board stay's single foot). Density around 78% coverage, one ink, no process black. Inside the shadow, three things and nothing else: (a) a bright UNPRINTED rectangle where daylight falls through the open roof — physically true, STATE.md reads 'roof aperture: open, and solid fore / aft / both sides', so a high sun genuinely drops a patch of light onto the ground inside the bus's own shade; (b) four small hairline circles on the tyre contact patches; (c) one line of 8 pt type running along the tail, reversed out: '1963 · 4.065 m · $3,000 · aquí'. No drawn wheels. No drawn bodywork. No wordmark larger than that hairline. The whole graphic is the negative of an object that is not in the room.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** The uncanny, then recognition, then belonging. You notice you are standing in something. Then the three hatch positions line up with the counter you are queueing at and you realise the restaurant is a drawing of a bus — that you are inside the thing, not looking at it. It is a register that does not exist in food branding: quiet, faintly haunted, and generous, because the brand tells you where it came from without a single photograph or logo. And it does the one thing merchandise structurally cannot — it makes the PLACE the artefact, and the customer's own body the figure that completes it.

**FORMATS.** 1:1 anti-slip floor vinyl, 4.58 x 1.75 m, R10/R11 rated with a matte laminate, tiled as five panels with 20 mm overlaps. One per location, laid to the counter's orientation, not the room's.  ·  The outdoor arm: a 1:1 stencil, screened or chalked onto the pavement in front of a pop-up. It is a queue marker that IS the bus — and it is free every time it rains off and gets redone.  ·  1:20 framed print — 230 x 90 mm image on a 300 x 400 mm cream sheet, warm black, huge margin. A shadow with nothing casting it is a genuinely strong picture and it is the retail version of the idea.  ·  Coir doormat at 1:4 (1145 x 440 mm), shadow debossed rather than printed. The one place a shadow underfoot is already the convention.  ·  90 x 55 mm gift/loyalty card carrying only the shadow silhouette and the three light rectangles — the whole identity at wallet scale, with no wordmark on the face.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** Nothing in the VW-bus merchandise ocean is an ABSENCE. Every trope in the category — sunset silhouette, surf, line art, boho watercolour, blueprint — DRAWS THE OBJECT. This draws the hole the object leaves. It is also the only concept in this set that literally cannot be faked without a measured model: a true 1:1 cast shadow correct to the track, the wheelbase, the 39.7/40.7 mm arch-to-tyre gaps and the tail board's 38.0° is a MEASUREMENT, and at full size a person standing on it can feel when it is wrong. And it turns what the pipeline already emits into the product — deliver.py writes combi_%s_shadow_only.png as its own layer, with the split measured (shadow-catcher pixels near-black under 12/255 against a vehicle mean of 143).

**PRODUCTION.** ONE INK. A 4.5 m² area of 4c on a floor shows every registration error and every head-strike band. Proof the density twice: at ~78% it reads as a shadow and people walk over it; at 100% it reads as a hole and people step around it — which may be the more interesting failure and is worth seeing before deciding. Budget replacement every 9–12 months in a high-traffic taqueria and treat the wear as intended: a shadow that fades where feet land is better than one that doesn't. DO NOT put a wordmark on it — the moment it carries a logo it becomes a floor advert and the entire idea dies. THE RESOLUTION PROBLEM AND ITS FIX: at 1:1 and 4.58 m even 150 dpi wants ~27,000 px on the long edge, which this chain has never come near — so do not print a raster. Trace the shadow's alpha to a VECTOR path and print vector; trace_outline.py already exists and self-tests 'on shapes whose area and perimeter are known BY CONSTRUCTION'. A shadow is a flat shape and does not need pixels. THIS IS THE RISKY ONE and I am proposing it knowingly: a cautious team rejects floor vinyl as unsellable, conceptual, and a slip liability, and every one of those objections is answerable except the first, which is a nerve question rather than a design one.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** ADD A SUN. There isn't one. The studio rig is an AREA-lamp softbox, and studio.py's own analysis of the reference scene records 'NO SUN … a sun/skylight pair has the opposite sign by construction, so there is no sun/skylight pair.' A cast shadow with a legible edge needs a directional light at a stated elevation and azimuth, and that elevation is a DESIGN CHOICE that must be declared as one rather than smuggled in as a measurement. F259 licenses exactly this — 'Relight the studio and fix the ground if necessary' — and its one constraint, 'DO NOT change RED', is untouched here because the concept has no colour in it.  ·  ADD AN ORTHOGRAPHIC PLAN CAMERA. `topdown` exists (loc 2.60, 4.60, 6.40, lens 62, f/11) but it is a PERSPECTIVE view and has never been rendered by any preview list. A 1:1 floor graphic needs a true parallel projection from directly above, which does not exist in views() at all. Derive its ortho_scale from build.py's own printed bbox with a declared margin — do not type a number.  ·  RUN T1_ALPHA. It has not been exercised since rev 62 — no LEDGER contains the string. Every frame in out/ is RGB with no alpha (IHDR colortype 2) and deliver.py correctly refuses them: 'CONTROLS: 4 checked, 1 FAILED -- C1' / 'PACKAGE IS NOT TRUSTWORTHY'. The shadow layer this concept IS does not exist on disk. One env var, one render, and do NOT route it through post.py, which discards alpha (measured: colortype 6 in, colortype 2 out).  ·  DECIDE WHAT TO DO ABOUT TB_WIDTH BEFORE PRINTING AT FULL SIZE. The tail board's angle (38.0° ±2.3) and chord (0.7110 ±0.028) are measured, but t1_shell labels the width 'POSE CHOICE, NOT MEASURED' inside a 0.59 m upper bound with NO lower bound, and the source says flatly that the width 'is not measurable from anything we hold'. At 1:1 that unmeasured width gets printed on a floor at full size. Either crop the board's shadow out of the graphic, or keep it and make sure the caption claims nothing about it.  ·  VERIFY THE ROOF-APERTURE LIGHT PATCH BY RENDERING IT, NOT BY REASONING. STATE.md says the aperture is open and solid on all four sides, so the patch should be real — but F45 (roof-aperture interior) is ungated and has never been measured against anything. Render it with the new sun, look at the crop, and if the patch is muddy say so and author the rectangles as a deliberate graphic rather than publishing them as physics.

**SCREENER.** Standing inside the footprint is a strong bodily idea and the hatch-to-counter alignment is lovely, but it is free4-2's insight at another scale and loses the comparison — one location, an operational and slip liability, retail floor-decal precedent, and it prints TB_WIDTH at full size, a constant t1_shell.py itself labels 'POSE CHOICE, NOT MEASURED' with an upper bound of 0.59 m and no lower bound.

---

## GRADIENTE — the pattern that empties — A directional folk field whose density is a measurement of the actual painted vehicle: dense at one end, bare at the other, so the repeat has a horizon and leaves you somewhere to put things.  ·  screen 5.5  ·  `t3-0`

**THE IMAGE.** An unrolled horizontal band, read right-to-left. At the dense (aft) end: one enormous gold acanthus mass, ~305 mm equivalent diameter, a fat C-curve trunk whose stroke runs 12–18 mm and tapers to a 1.5 mm hairline, every terminal a tight rolled volute or a hook, with 6–9 secondary fronds combing off its convex side. Scattered across and around it, dotted rosettes at 30.7 to 68.0 mm — a solid gold core disc, a maroon scalloped inner ring, and a ring of 10–14 cream/white dots set outside the core. Threading between them, dark maroon comma-tendrils 37 to 123 mm long: each an S-curve with a bulbous head and a whip tail, carrying 2–4 dots along its concave side. Then, moving left, the field THINS along a measured profile — 27.86 % ink, then 13.93 %, then 0.29 % — until the last third is nearly bare red carrying three isolated commas and one 30 mm rosette. FLAT THROUGHOUT: no light, no gradient inside a mark, no outline, every stroke a hard-edged flat colour the way loaded enamel actually lays down. Palette, four inks on the body ground: red sRGB(196,49,36); gold sRGB(194,146,6) with a single shaded arm at 0.88 of its luma; rosette cream (198,164,105) plus the white dot ring; dark ink (75,17,13). Two masses, four tendrils, eight rosettes, five dark commas per full run — the census, not a scatter.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** Relief. Wallpaper is an anxious surface — it fills everything, edge to edge, and competes with whatever you put on it. This one breathes, and it breathes in a direction. A person feels a room that is decorated where they are standing and calm where they are not, which is what a good sign-painter actually does and what a tiling engine never does. The emptiness is not absence, it is hospitality: it is where the price goes, where the photograph goes, where the hand goes. And because the density profile is copied off a real hand-painted panel rather than invented, it has the specific unevenness of a person who knew when to stop — the quality that makes people run a thumb over a printed bag.

**FORMATS.** Wrapping/tissue, 500 mm roll — the gradient runs along the roll so a torn sheet is never the same composition twice, and the sparse end is where the fold lands  ·  Single-wall paper cup, 90 mm tall wrap — orient the gradient so the SEAM falls in the sparse zone; no motif is ever bisected by the join, which is the failure that makes printed cups look cheap  ·  Take-away bag, 260 × 320 mm kraft or red — dense at the base, empty at the top third where the wordmark sits, so the pattern hosts the logo instead of fighting it  ·  Apron and tote lining, 1:1 element scale — at life size the median gold stroke is 13 mm and nothing reduces, so the line quality survives intact  ·  Menu backer and placemat, A3 — reduced set: delete the 48 smallest components (2.29 % of the ink), keep the three masses  ·  Web/app background, gradient running off the right edge; 3 m interior wall band at 1:1, painted or printed on uncoated  ·  Two-ink reduction (gold + dark on red) for embroidery, one-colour vinyl and any small application

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** Every floral in the VW-bus market is one of two things: uniform wallpaper, or a bouquet centred on a bus. This is neither. It is a DIRECTIONAL field whose density profile (0.29 / 13.93 / 27.86 % across three zones) and whose element inventory (2 masses at 73 213 and 30 285 mm², 4 tendrils, 8 rosettes, 5 dark commas, all at measured areas) are a census of the real vehicle, not a decorative choice — nobody else can copy it because nobody else measured a bus for seventy-five revisions. It is also the wrong country's flowers for the trope to catch: the grammar here is Mexican rotulismo — tapering gold acanthus, dotted rosette, maroon comma-with-dots — which reads as a sign-writer's hand, not as a hippie daisy. The trope is real and this bus is exposed to it, because it genuinely carries painted florals; the defence is the specific three-ink vocabulary and the directional density, both of which flower-power has no equivalent for.

**PRODUCTION.** Four spot inks, no halftone, no white underbase when printing on red stock. SPECIFY EVERY STROKE WIDTH IN FINAL MILLIMETRES PER SKU — never scale one master. A 0.6 mm hairline scaled from a 500 mm run to a 150 mm cup becomes 0.18 mm and dies silently: the file still opens, the proof still previews, the print comes back as mud. Use the measured licence to simplify rather than eyeballing it: folk_gen's own component census records 48 of 66 gold components carrying 2.29 % of the ink while the largest three carry 84.4 %, so below roughly 200 mm delete all 48 and lose almost nothing. CEILING ON THE PALETTE, and it must travel with the artwork: those four values are AUTHORED CONSTANTS in the source, not colorimetry, and the project holds four unreconciled reds (t1_mats RED sRGB(196,49,36), lid_gen RED (159,45,22), cal_gen RED (214,46,30)). One brand red has to be CHOSEN and declared. Do not sample it from a render — flank_compare reads the render's red at G/R 0.422 against the photograph's 0.114, i.e. it images salmon — and do not average the photographs, because probe_rev71_red.py prints the prohibition itself: the photograph's red spans 0.0149 to 0.0344, a 2.3× range.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** FIX F16 BEFORE HARVESTING ANYTHING FROM tex/swirl.png. The register records it OPEN: every flank rosette is drawn at the diameter of its GOLD CORE, so the built rosettes are systematically undersized against the photograph. A pattern harvested off the tile inherits that error at every scale.  ·  RECONCILE THE COMPONENT CENSUS, or state that it is unreconciled. folk_gen.py's own table records rev 11 at 30 gold components against ref_side.jpg's 66; the grounding pass counted 196 on the shipped tile. Three numbers for one quantity. I did not resolve them and neither should the artwork pretend to.  ·  HARVEST FROM THE PHOTOGRAPH, NOT THE TILE, per SPEC §10.10's own stated method — read at 4–5× magnification, redraw at 2–4K, measure the result back against the crop. I composited the shipped tile over the body red and looked at it: the marks are correct in grammar but the drawing is thinner and smaller-scale than ref_side.jpg's cab door, which carries one bold acanthus with broad tapering strokes.  ·  folk_gen.py IS NOT CALLED BY build.py. Any regeneration is a hand step; skip it and the render silently keeps the old texture.  ·  NO VECTOR OF ANY OF THIS EXISTS. The only .svg in the tree is vw_canonical_2019.svg, which the record grades a DIFFERENT OBJECT. Every mark above has to be redrawn as vector before it can be screen-printed, embroidered or cut.

**SCREENER.** The most commercially producible and the least distinctive. Its claim to originality is that the density profile is a census of the real panel — but that provenance is invisible in the artefact, which is fatal to the argument, since every other concept here makes the measurement legible. Gradient repeats are everywhere in textiles and packaging, and this is the one concept sitting directly in the flower-power trope's blast radius, defended only by the Mexican rotulismo vocabulary. The specify-every-stroke-in-final-millimetres note is correct and hard-won. Two live defects it inherits rather than solves: F16 is OPEN in the register ('every flank rosette is drawn at the diameter of its gold core'), so harvesting off tex/swirl.png carries the undersizing at every scale, and the component census stands unreconciled at three values.

---

## NUNCA DOS VECES — the pattern that is never the same twice — The asset is not a file, it is a grammar: every cup, bag and napkin carries a different generated fragment of one enormous folk field, seeded and stamped, and the brand book's central rule is a refusal to lock the pattern.  ·  screen 5.5  ·  `t3-2`

**THE IMAGE.** Two paper cups side by side, same run, same press, same four inks. The first carries one gold acanthus trunk running clean off the top edge, half a rosette cut by the seam, two complete maroon commas, and a lot of bare red. The second, from the same batch: three rosettes clustered low, a dark comma bisected by the rim, no gold trunk at all, a different silence. Nothing on either is centred. No motif on either is whole. The grammar is identical — the same tapering gold acanthus with rolled volutes, the same dotted rosette (gold core, maroon scalloped ring, cream dot ring), the same bulbous-head/whip-tail comma with 2–4 dots on its concave side, the same four flat inks, the same density feel — and the composition is unrepeatable. At the base of each, 6 pt, one line: SEED 04-B7 · PLAYA · 2026. Mechanically: the generator is handed the inventory as a RATIO SET rather than a fixed list — two dominant masses carrying ~84 % of the ink at 305 and 196 mm equivalent diameter, four tendrils, eight rosettes from 30.7 to 68.0 mm, five dark commas from 37 to 123 mm — plus the density gradient, and it solves SIZES AND POSITIONS, never counts. Then production takes a RANDOM WINDOW onto the result, so every object is a crop and the field is always implicitly larger than the thing it landed on.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** The feeling of a thing that was PAINTED rather than printed. A sign-writer never repeats a panel; a brand system exists to insist that everything is repeated exactly. This one makes the origin literally true again — and it gives away for free the two things brands spend fortunes faking: scarcity and specificity. Your cup is yours. Nobody at the table has the same one. Children compare them across a table, which is precisely the behaviour that turns packaging into a keepsake, and the seed line is the insider mark: meaningless to a stranger, and everything to the person who first notices no two are alike. It also quietly refuses the category's core assumption — that a beloved image should be reproduced as many times as possible.

**FORMATS.** Digitally printed cups, napkins, sleeves, bags, wrap and tissue — the whole short-run consumable range, where variable data costs nothing per unit  ·  Loyalty and gift cards, each a unique field with its seed printed — the cheapest possible collectible mechanic  ·  In-store screen, 9:16 or 16:9: the field PAINTS ITSELF ON in the signwriter's own measured order — largest mass first, then tendrils, then rosettes, then the dark commas last, which is the real hierarchy (the first three strokes lay 84.4 % of the ink)  ·  Web and app background generated per session, seed shown in the footer  ·  A LOCKED two-ink version (gold + dark on red), fixed and unchanging, for screen-printed apparel, litho, embroidery and vinyl — the system splits deliberately and says so  ·  Brand-book spread showing the GRAMMAR plus twelve example seeds captioned "none of these is the pattern"

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** It is the only idea here that cannot be screenshotted and resold, because there is no file to steal — the asset is a program. The entire VW-merch category is built on reproducing one image at volume; this refuses to have an image. It also converts the single genuinely unusual property of this project into public brand behaviour: the vehicle's artwork is already PROCEDURAL, authored by a generator that exists, that was built against a measured census, and that solves sizes rather than counts. Nobody in the category has a generator, because nobody in the category measured anything. And it is the strongest possible answer to "is this an advert or a thing people want": an advert is identical every time by definition.

**PRODUCTION.** THIS IS THE RISKY ONE AND HERE IS EXACTLY WHY A CAUTIOUS TEAM KILLS IT: it only works on digital print. Spot-screen, flexo and litho all want one locked plate, so the system must formally SPLIT — a locked two-ink version for screened and stamped goods, a generated version for anything digitally printed — and the guidelines have to say that plainly rather than pretending it is universal. Second refusal, and it is the one that gets argued about in the room: the brand book CANNOT SHOW THE PATTERN. It shows the grammar — mark inventory, area ratios, density gradient, four-ink triad, minimum stroke in final millimetres — and then twelve seeds explicitly labelled as not-the-pattern. That is an unusual document and some people will experience it as the absence of a deliverable. It is not; it is a stricter deliverable, because a grammar can be violated in ways a JPEG cannot. Third: every generated field still obeys the same reduction ladder as concept 1 — stroke widths declared in final millimetres per SKU, 0.5 mm positive minimum, and the smallest components dropped below ~200 mm. A generator that emits sub-0.2 mm hairlines at cup scale will do it a hundred thousand times.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** REFACTOR folk_gen.py TO TAKE A SEED AND EMIT SVG. Today it emits a 4096² raster bound to the vehicle's flank. The cheap half is already done: it is NOT called by build.py, so it is already a standalone generator with no render dependency — this is the single highest-leverage piece of work in this brief and it needs no Blender at all.  ·  FIX F16 FIRST, BEFORE GENERATING ANYTHING. The rosettes are drawn at the diameter of their gold CORE and are systematically undersized. Generating a hundred thousand undersized rosettes multiplies one open finding by a hundred thousand, across physical objects that cannot be recalled.  ·  RECONCILE THE COMPONENT CENSUS BEFORE TRUSTING THE GRAMMAR: 30 (folk_gen's own rev-11 row), 66 (ref_side.jpg), 196 (the grounding pass on the shipped tile). A generator tuned to the wrong one produces a family-consistent field that is consistently wrong.  ·  DECLARE ONE BRAND RED AS A DESIGN RULING WITH ITS PROVENANCE, before a single unit prints. Three authored reds disagree in the tree; the render images salmon (G/R 0.422 against the photograph's 0.114); and the photographs cannot supply one either — the probe prints its own prohibition against quoting a single figure. This must be chosen, written down and signed off, not sampled.  ·  NOT F18. This is deliberately a different artefact from the die-cut sticker, whose style and scene the owner locked and whose build trigger has not fired. It also runs against the instinct behind a locked brand asset, and I am proposing it anyway — the refusal to lock is the idea, not a side effect of it.

**SCREENER.** The grounding is unusually good — a real procedural generator built from a measured census, plus the spiky refusal to let the brand book show the pattern — but the core mechanic, every unit uniquely generated, has been the standard variable-data packaging move for over a decade, and the concept's own kill list (fix F16 first, reconcile 30 vs 66 vs 196 components, declare one red) says it would multiply an open finding across a hundred thousand unrecallable objects.

---

## THE FILAMENT — One festoon bulb at 27:1 after dark — glass, brass crimp and tungsten coil sharp, the rest of the chain dissolving into nine-sided amber bokeh with the flex sagging black across it.  ·  screen 5.0  ·  `t7-1`

**THE IMAGE.** LANDSCAPE 3:1. Camera at the drip rail, 200 mm equivalent, 250 mm standoff, WIDE OPEN at f/2.0. Time: the twenty minutes after sunset — the sky gone, the cream body still holding a trace of cool ambient, the bulbs the only warm source in frame.

The rail runs lower-left to upper-right on a shallow diagonal. ONE bulb is sharp, third from the left, sitting on the lower-left thirds intersection — and you can see INTO it: the glass envelope, the brass cap's crimp, and the tungsten coil, a tight helix maybe 4 mm long, the single brightest thing in the picture, blooming just past clipping so it flares rather than clips flat. Everything in front of and behind it dissolves by distance: the next bulb is a soft amber lozenge, the one after a clean 9-SIDED DISC, and by the fifth they are overlapping bokeh the size of oranges, stacking into a warm field at the upper right.

The black flex sags between them in slack catenaries — out of focus, a soft dark line drawn across the amber. It is the only line in the picture and it does all the drawing.

Underneath, the cream body takes the SPILL: a warm wash falling off with distance, so the bottom third is a gradient from lit ivory to cool grey. Above the rail: nothing. Dark.

PALETTE IS TWO COLOURS AND A BLACK LINE. Emission at the material's own authored (1.000, 0.760, 0.442) — saturation 0.558, which the render currently loses entirely; cream lit warm; flex at true black. No third hue anywhere in frame. No red, no gold, no folk art, no bus.

MOTION VERSION — 2.4 s seamless loop. The chain ignites LEFT TO RIGHT, one bulb at a time, on an 8 fps STEPPED cadence: 25 bulbs on the tail board is 25 steps, which is the run length the geometry already computes. Each bulb flares past the bloom threshold and settles back one step later, so the leading edge of the ignition is a travelling overshoot. Ends held lit. Loops by cutting to black for two frames — an inverter dropping out, not a fade.

**EMOTIONAL CLAIM.** ARRIVING SOMEWHERE WARM AFTER DARK. Not nostalgia — APPETITE. A bulb chain at this distance is pre-verbal: it means the place is open, someone is inside, you can sit down and be fed. That is the entire emotional business of a taqueria compressed into one out-of-focus amber node, and it arrives before the viewer identifies a single object. The person who has eaten at Tacombi recognises it instantly; the person who has not simply wants to be wherever this is. It is also the only asset in the whole programme that would go on a wall in a home rather than a shop.

**FORMATS.** PANORAMIC PRINT, 1200 x 400 mm, on SATIN or GLOSS. This is the one image in the programme that WANTS the glare — a real specular sheen on the paper makes the bokeh read as light rather than as printed orange. Matte would kill it.  ·  PORTRAIT PRINT, 900 x 1200 mm, for the single-bulb-plus-catenary crop. Big enough that the coil is life-size-plus and the viewer leans in.  ·  WALLPAPER SET — phone lock screen (1290 x 2796), desktop (3840 x 2160). Free, and it is the piece that does the marketing for everything else. A lock screen is worn in public more often than a t-shirt.  ·  LOOP, 1080 x 1350, 2.4 s, silent. Social and in-store screens. The ignition is the whole content; no type on the frame.  ·  LIMITED EDITION: same panoramic, screen-printed on deep-black uncoated with SPOT FOIL OR SPOT UV ON THE IN-FOCUS BULB ONLY, so the one sharp light catches the actual light in the actual room. One extra pass, one extra plate, and it makes the print physically responsive to where the buyer hangs it.  ·  APPAREL — the bokeh chain as a PRINTED hem stripe or side-seam tape, 200 x 25 mm. Printed, never embroidered: a needle cannot make bokeh, and a satin-stitched version of this is a row of orange ovals, which is the failure it is trying to avoid.

**WHY NOT DERIVATIVE.** The category's night imagery is bus-at-sunset with a warm gradient behind it — the single most saturated frame on the shelf. THIS HAS NO BUS, NO SUNSET, NO HORIZON AND NO SILHOUETTE. It is a light source photographed at 27:1; the vehicle is present only as a cream surface catching spill in the bottom third, unidentifiable. It sits closer to contemporary photography than to merchandise. And the specific object being photographed — a festoon string clipped to a food truck's drip rail — belongs entirely to the taqueria: zero VW shape content, zero VW mark content, nothing a factory ever made. It passes the logo-removal test completely, because there is no logo in it to remove.

**PRODUCTION.** THE ONE-BULB CROP IS 27.3:1 AT A 600 mm PRINT AND 54:1 AT 1200 mm. That is not a crop of anything — it is a dedicated camera, which is good news because it means the resolution ceiling never binds. A 1200 x 400 mm panoramic at 300 dpi is 14173 px on the long edge, far above anything ever stitched, SO SHOOT THE HERO BULB AS ITS OWN FRAME AND COMPOSE THE CHAIN, rather than rendering one enormous stitched plate. The out-of-focus bulbs carry almost no high-frequency detail, so they can be rendered small and upscaled without penalty; only the sharp bulb needs real pixels.

THIS CONCEPT TAKES THE OPTICS CHAIN AND GIVES UP THE CUT-OUT, DELIBERATELY. post.py reads `rgba[..., :3]` and saves three channels, so alpha and optics are mutually exclusive in this tree. A macro bokeh print wants bloom and grain and does not want a silhouette. Say that in the ledger rather than discovering it.

Seed the grain from the frame number for the loop. post.py's `grain(..., seed=7)` is fixed, so identical grain on every frame reads as dirt on the lens — the single most amateur tell available and one line to fix.

DUSK IS A RELIGHT, NOT A REPAINT, AND THE OWNER HAS ALREADY OPENED IT. F259, rev 71: "Relight the studio and fix the ground if necessary." The rig is open; "DO NOT change RED" is the constraint and this concept never touches RED — there is no red in the frame.

**MODEL WORK REQUIRED.** THE BULB HAS NO FILAMENT. THIS IS THE HEADLINE ITEM AND THE CONCEPT IS NAMED AFTER IT. `_ball(verts, faces, c, r, nu=7, nv=4)` builds a SOLID 7x4 UV sphere, and `emissive()`'s docstring says exactly what it is: "A lit festoon bulb: warm emission over a pearl-white envelope." There is no glass, no cap, no gas, no coil — the whole bulb is one opaque ball with emission on it. BUILD: a transmissive glass envelope, a brass/bakelite cap with its crimp, and a coiled tungsten filament as the ONLY emitter inside it.
